`CaseInsensitiveMap`, which holds the message headers, now treats two keys that differ only in the case of a letter
outside the Basic Multilingual Plane (such as U+10400 and U+10428) as the same key, as `String.equalsIgnoreCase` does;
before, they were usually kept as two entries. Keys without such letters are not affected.

=== camel-core - ThrottlingExceptionRoutePolicy runs the half open check on a thread pool of Camel

The half open check of `ThrottlingExceptionRoutePolicy` now runs on a thread pool created through the
`ExecutorServiceManager` (one thread, named `ThrottlingExceptionRoutePolicy` and managed like the other thread pools of
Camel), instead of a new `java.util.Timer` thread each time the circuit opens. The thread is created when the circuit
opens the first time, and it ends when the last route of the policy is removed or the policy is stopped.

`halfOpenAfter` must now be greater than 0: adding a route with a policy whose `halfOpenAfter` is 0 or less fails,
and `setHalfOpenAfter` (also over JMX) rejects such a value with an `IllegalArgumentException`. With 0, a check that
keeps the circuit open (`keepOpen`, or a `halfOpenHandler` that is not ready) scheduled the next check without delay, a
busy loop. To half open the circuit almost at once, as 0 did, use a small value such as 1.

An exception thrown by the `isReadyToBeClosed` method of a `halfOpenHandler` is now logged (by the exception handler of
the policy), and the circuit is kept open and checked again after `halfOpenAfter`; before, the timer thread died and the
circuit stayed open.

=== camel-core - Split EIP keeps the original message when maxFailedRecords or errorThreshold stops it

When the Split EIP stops because `maxFailedRecords` or `errorThreshold` is reached, the exchange now keeps its original
input message, headers, exchange properties and variables, and only gets the exception, as with `stopOnException`.
Before, the message, headers, exchange properties and variables of the split item that reached the threshold, as the
steps inside the split had changed them, were copied onto the exchange.

So a `doCatch` around the split, an `onException`, an `onCompletion`, a dead letter channel or the caller of the route
now receive the input that was split, instead of that one split item. A route that needs to know which items failed can
read the `CamelSplitResult` exchange property, set as before: `SplitResult.getFailures()` has the index and the
exception of the failed items.

=== camel-core - Split EIP processes the items again when the watermark cannot be stored

When the resume strategy of a split with watermark tracking fails to store the watermark, the split now fails with that
exception, and the next split processes the same items again. Before, with `synchronous()`, the split also failed, but
the watermark was kept in memory anyway, so the next split skipped those items although the watermark was never stored.
Without `synchronous()`, the exchange never completed (fixed by the same change).

A `watermarkExpression` that throws on an item now fails that item, so `stopOnException`, `maxFailedRecords` and
`errorThreshold` apply as for any failed item.

=== camel-core - shareUnitOfWork and the topic load balancer: a part sent to seda no longer takes the on completions of the parent exchange

Since Camel 4.14 the parts of a Split, Multicast or Recipient List with `shareUnitOfWork` use the unit of work of the
parent exchange. A part that sent to an asynchronous endpoint without waiting for a reply (such as `seda` with an
`InOnly` exchange) handed over all the on completions of that unit of work to its copy, including the commit or
rollback of the consumer of the parent exchange (such as moving the file, or deleting the SQS message). They then ran
when that copy was done, with the outcome of that copy: a file was moved to its done folder although a later part
failed the split, or a message was rolled back although the split succeeded.

Now the on completions of the parent exchange stay with the parent exchange, and run when it is done, with its outcome
(as the Camel 4.14 upgrade guide describes). Such a part still hands over the on completions it added to its own
exchange (this is unchanged from earlier versions), so a resource the part itself opened, such as a file it polled, is
still released when its copy is done. On completions added to the unit of work directly, such as by a route
`onCompletion` that the part reaches, now stay with the parent exchange too (before, they went with the seda copy): they
are done with the shared unit of work when the split is done, as for a part that does not send to seda.

As the consumer now commits when the split is done, it can commit before the seda copies have been processed. A seda
copy that still reads from the input after that must get the content first. This mainly hits Multicast and Recipient
List, where each part carries the whole message body (such as the file), while the parts of a Split usually carry
content that was already read. For example, when the file consumer moves or deletes the file, convert the body of a
file that was not loaded yet with `convertBodyTo(String.class)` before sending to `seda`. Or wait for the copy with
`waitForTaskToComplete=Always` on the `seda` endpoint, which is also what to use when the message must not be committed
(moved, deleted, acknowledged) before the asynchronous processing is done: a failure of the seda route then fails the
part, and so the split. The previous behaviour, where the consumer commits with the outcome of one seda copy, cannot be
configured.

The same applies to the copies that the topic load balancer (`loadBalance().topic()`) sends to each of its endpoints:
they use the unit of work of the exchange too, and the first one sent to `seda` took the on completions of the exchange
(such as the commit of the consumer), so they ran with the outcome of that copy. They now stay with the exchange.

=== camel-core - Tokenize: the token is literal text unless regex is true

The token of the xref:components:languages:tokenize-language.adoc[Tokenize] language is now matched as literal text, as the
`regex` option (default `false`) describes. Before, the token was always compiled as a regular expression, so a
delimiter such as `|`, `.`, `$` or `*` split every character, did not split, or failed with a `PatternSyntaxException`.
The end token of the pair mode (`endToken`) is also literal text now.

A route that relies on a regular expression as token, such as `\s+` or `(\W+)\s*`, must now set `regex=true`:

[source,java]
----
.split().tokenize("\\s+", true)
----

In the XML and YAML DSL, a token with the escapes `\n`, `\r` or `\t` (for example `\r\n`) is turned into the new line,
carriage return and tab characters, so it works as before. Before, only a leading `\n` was turned into a new line.

=== camel-core - Idempotent Consumer: an exchange marked rollback only removes its message id

The xref:components:eips:idempotentConsumer-eip.adoc[Idempotent Consumer] now treats an exchange marked with
`markRollbackOnly()` or `markRollbackOnlyLast()` (without an exception) as failed, as the SQL consumer already does:
with `removeOnFailure=true` (the default) its message id is removed from the idempotent repository, so a redelivered
message is processed instead of being dropped as a duplicate. Before, the message id was kept.

With a consumer that does not redeliver a rollback only exchange, such as the file consumer, a later message with the
same id is now processed instead of being dropped as a duplicate.

To keep the message id, set `removeOnFailure=false`. Note that this also keeps the message id of an exchange that
failed with an exception.

=== camel-cyberark-vault - credentials are checked at endpoint start, and a rejected token is retried only once

A `cyberark-vault` endpoint now fails to start when it has no usable credentials, with a message that names the
options to set: the `authToken` option, the `username` and `apiKey` options, or the `username` and `password` options,
unless the endpoint has a `conjurClient`. Before, an endpoint with an `apiKey` and no `username` started, and its first
message failed with a `NullPointerException`.

When CyberArk Conjur answers HTTP 401, the endpoint and the CyberArk Conjur Vault Property Function authenticate again
and retry the call once, and only when they have an `apiKey` or a `password` to authenticate with. With only an
`authToken`, the call now fails with the 401 response, instead of retrying with the same token until a
`StackOverflowError`. Configure the `username` with an `apiKey` or a `password`, instead of an `authToken`, to let the
component get a new token when the current one expires: when the `authToken` is set, the component uses only that
token.

=== camel-seda - receive without a timeout returns when the polling consumer stops

A `receive()` without a timeout on a seda endpoint, such as `consumerTemplate.receive("seda:foo")` or a `pollEnrich`
from seda without a timeout, waited for a message for ever, also after the CamelContext, the consumer template or the
route of the `pollEnrich` was stopped. It now returns `null` (no message) within about a second once the polling
consumer is stopped, as `EventDrivenPollingConsumer`, used by most other endpoints, does. This also happens when the
consumer cache of a consumer template evicts the polling consumer (see `maximumCacheSize`). A message that is in the
queue is still returned. A polling consumer that was never started still waits for a message, as before. A caller that
loops on `receive()` should stop on a `null` result.

=== camel-main - a failure to start is logged

A Camel Main application that fails to start now logs why, at ERROR: `Error starting Camel: ...` with the stack trace,
as `camel run` already did. Before, the failure left `main()` and only the JVM printed it, on stderr
(`Exception in thread "main" ...`), so the log file of the application did not say why it stopped.
The JVM no longer prints that same failure a second time. When the application logs nothing at ERROR (such as without
a logging implementation), the JVM prints it as before.

=== camel-report-maven-plugin:validate / configuration-validate

The catalog now accepts all `camel.*` groups defined in `camel-main-configuration-metadata.json`
(previously only 7 of the 36 groups were recognized, besides `camel.component.*`, `camel.dataformat.*`
and `camel.language.*`). As a result,
`camel-report-maven-plugin:validate` and the camel-jbang MCP `camel_configuration_validate` tool
now validate 29 additional `camel.*` groups (including `camel.server.*`,
`camel.management.*`, `camel.vault.*`, `camel.ssl.*`, and others).

If your project uses Spring Boot and has options such as `camel.metrics.uriTagEnabled` (from a
Camel starter) that are not in the camel-main schema, `camel-report-maven-plugin:validate` may
report them as "Unknown option", which fails the build when `failOnError=true`. The
`camel_configuration_validate` MCP tool with `runtime=spring-boot` reports such keys as
`checked=false` instead.

Group names are now matched with dash- and case-insensitive normalisation, matching how
camel-main binds keys at runtime. For example, `camel.error-registry.enabled` and
`camel.ai-observability.enabled` are now accepted alongside their camel-case equivalents.

If your project uses `camel-report-maven-plugin:validate` with `failOnError=true`,
a key that was silently ignored before (e.g. a misspelled option under one of the newly accepted
groups) may now cause the build to fail. Review the validation output and correct any misspelled keys.

The `camel_configuration_validate` MCP tool response format has changed:

* `PropertyValidationInfo.accepted` is renamed to `checked`.
* `ConfigurationValidateSummary` gains a new `unchecked` integer field counting lines whose
  namespace is not recognized as a Camel property (e.g. `server.port=8080` or `quarkus.*` keys).
  Before, such lines were returned with `accepted=false` and `valid=true`; they are now
  `checked=false` and are left out of `summary.valid` and `summary.errors`.

If you call `camel_configuration_validate` programmatically and read the JSON response, update
field references from `accepted` to `checked` and handle the new `summary.unchecked` field.

=== camel-semantic - expert artifacts

TypeSafe AI Semantic applications must add `org.apache.camel:camel-semantic-typesafe-ai`.
The `camel-typesafe-ai` component and TypeSafe AI language remain independently usable and no longer
bring in `camel-semantic`. The new expert artifact includes both dependencies transitively.
Existing bean declarations and imports for
`org.apache.camel.component.typesafeai.TypeSafeAiSemanticAdapter` remain valid.

Spring Boot applications using this expert must keep `camel-typesafe-ai-starter` and add
`org.apache.camel:camel-semantic-typesafe-ai` at the matching Camel version. The existing starter
continues configuring the component and TypeSafe AI language; register the Semantic expert explicitly
as a named Spring bean. Applications using only the component or TypeSafe AI language need no new dependency.

Replace `org.apache.camel:camel-wolf-defender` with `org.apache.camel:camel-semantic-wolf-defender`.
The Wolf Defender Java packages, classes, model provisioning and configuration properties are unchanged.
Its documentation page is now `semantic-wolf-defender.adoc`.

Update Maven dependencies and Camel JBang `--dep` options to the new expert artifact names.
Each Semantic evaluation still selects an explicit named registry expert. Provider identities and
operation names remain `typesafe-ai` and `wolf-defender`; only the reported adapter artifact changes.

TypeSafe AI now rejects explicit media parts nested in structured input, instructions or criteria,
which previously could be serialized and sent to the provider. Text and JSON-compatible `Map`/`List`
state remain supported. Binary objects and image, audio or video parts are rejected before HTTP;
plain text and ordinary application field names do not imply media. Records such as
`{type: image, url: ..., sku: 42}` and MIME metadata with a URL alone remain application state.
Direct Semantic adapter calls
and batches use the same validation. Wolf Defender remains text-only with its existing limits.

=== camel-semantic - explicit experts

Every evaluation in the preview semantic API now requires an explicit `expert` registry bean name.
Move block-level `expert` values onto each evaluation in YAML and XML; in Java, move `.expert(...)`
from `SemanticEvaluationsBuilder` to each `SemanticEvaluationBuilder`.

The `camel.language.semantic.default-expert` and `camel.language.semantic.adapter` options,
`SemanticLanguage.setDefaultExpert` / `setAdapter`, automatic selection of a sole expert, and
`META-INF/services/org/apache/camel/semantic-adapter` discovery are removed. Register an expert
bean explicitly, including its initialization and destruction methods where needed, and reference
it from each evaluation. Expert references accept property placeholders, but do not fall back to
class loading. The default state selector is unchanged.

The semantic metadata response no longer includes `defaultExpert`, `defaultError` or
`defaultErrorCode`. Operation inspection and completion require an explicit expert reference.

=== camel-semantic - evaluation-only auditing

The preview semantic audit API no longer records route actions. `SemanticAuditDecision`,
`SemanticAudit.get(context).decision(...)` and `SemanticObserver.decision(...)` have been removed.
Remove the corresponding decision beans and processor calls from routes. Evaluation auditing is
automatic under the existing audit configuration; normal application routing remains unchanged.
Previously persisted version-1 decision records remain readable and exportable as legacy data.
Semantic audit browsing no longer accepts route-action, namespace or application-correlation filters.
The console's `target` filter is also removed. Newly captured evaluation records use `definition` instead of
`target`; update consumers and queries to read `definition`. Previously persisted records retain their original fields.
Event-ID detail responses no longer expand a decision's
`evidence` references automatically; use `caseEventId` for a bounded associated case or `eventIds` for explicit
bulk retrieval. The original persisted record remains available in `record`.
Use automatic evaluation provenance, including entry/executing route and Camel breadcrumb identifiers.
The internal `CamelSemanticAuditReferences` exchange property is no longer populated.
In audit health responses, `capturing` replaces `decisionsEnabled`; it indicates that some expert enables
evaluation capture. For external readers, `retained` is `unknown` instead of a count from the unrelated memory store.

`SemanticAuditReader.getAll` no longer imposes a 100-ID evidence limit. Callers must bound requests
and responses; backend implementations may split retrieval into their own batches. The 100-reference
limit on legacy persisted evidence records remains unchanged.

=== Vert.x 5

Camel now uses Vert.x 5. This affects `camel-vertx`, `camel-vertx-http`, `camel-vertx-websocket`,
`camel-platform-http-vertx`, `camel-platform-http-main`, `camel-platform-http-jolokia`, `camel-knative-http`
and `camel-oauth`. Application code that uses the Vert.x API directly, for example to create a custom `Vertx`
instance, a custom `Router` handler, or an authentication provider for platform-http, must be migrated as
described in the https://vertx.io/docs/guides/vertx-5-migration-guide/[Vert.x 5 migration guide]. Notably,
the callback variants of asynchronous methods are removed in favour of the methods returning a `Future`.

A metrics or tracing factory can no longer be set on the `VertxOptions` given to a component (the
`MetricsOptions.setFactory` and `TracingOptions.setFactory` methods are removed). Either create the `Vertx`
instance with `Vertx.builder()` and set it on the component (or bind it to the registry), or register the factory
as a `io.vertx.core.spi.VertxServiceProvider` service.

`VertxPlatformHttpRouter.mountSubRouter` is removed, as it is no longer part of the Vert.x `Router` API.
Use `router.route(mountPoint + "*").subRouter(subRouter)` instead.

When the connection of a WebSocket is lost without a close frame, the `camel-vertx-websocket` consumer no longer
passes the resulting exception to the consumer exception handler, and no longer routes it as an `ERROR` event. It is
handled as a WebSocket closed with a close frame: a consumer with `fireWebSocketConnectionEvents=true` routes a `CLOSE`
event, and a consumer with `consumeAsClient=true` reconnects. With the default `fireWebSocketConnectionEvents=false`,
the lost connection of a peer is therefore no longer logged as a warning.

When the WebSocket of a consumer with `consumeAsClient=true` reports any other error, the consumer exception handler
now receives that exception. Previously it received the cause of that exception, which could be `null`.

When the WebSocket that the `camel-vertx-websocket` producer sends to (a server not managed by Camel) reports an error
other than a lost connection, the exchange now fails with that exception. Previously it failed with the cause of that
exception, which could be `null`.

`camel-arangodb` still uses Vert.x 4, as the ArangoDB Java driver does not support Vert.x 5 yet
(see https://github.com/arangodb/arangodb-java-driver/issues/625[arangodb-java-driver#625]). An application that
uses `camel-arangodb` together with one of the components above resolves a single version of the `io.vertx`
artifacts, so either the ArangoDB driver or the other components fail at runtime. The workaround suggested by the
driver maintainers is to replace `com.arangodb:arangodb-java-driver` with `com.arangodb:arangodb-java-driver-shaded`,
which embeds its own relocated copy of Vert.x. The `vertx` option of `camel-arangodb` cannot be used in that case.

=== camel-kubernetes - Kubernetes client 8

`camel-kubernetes` now uses the fabric8 Kubernetes client 8.0, which brings these changes for applications:

* The client uses its Vert.x 5 HTTP client (`io.fabric8:kubernetes-httpclient-vertx-5`) instead of the Vert.x 4 one
  (`io.fabric8:kubernetes-httpclient-vertx`). An application that excludes the default HTTP client, for example
  to use OkHttp or the JDK HTTP client, must now exclude `kubernetes-httpclient-vertx-5`.
* The client and its model use Jackson 3 (`tools.jackson`). Jackson 2 does not apply the serializers of the
  model, so Kubernetes model objects serialized with Jackson 2 come out in a different shape. For example, a
  `targetPort` of `8080` is written as `{"value":8080,"intVal":8080}`, and a CPU limit of `500m` as
  `{"amount":"500","format":"m"}`. This affects routes that marshal the result of a `camel-kubernetes` producer
  with the `camel-jackson` data format (`marshal().json()`), and application code that uses its own Jackson 2
  `ObjectMapper`. Replace the `camel-jackson` dependency with `camel-jackson3`, which provides the same `jackson`
  data format on Jackson 3 (see xref:components:dataformats:jackson3-dataformat.adoc[JSON Jackson 3]), so the route does not
  change. In Java code, use Jackson 3 or `io.fabric8.kubernetes.client.utils.Serialization`.
* Jackson 3 uses the Jackson 2 `com.fasterxml.jackson.core:jackson-annotations` artifact, and needs version 2.21
  or later. An application that pins Jackson 2 to 2.20 or older, for example through an older Spring Boot BOM,
  fails on the first use of the client with
  `NoClassDefFoundError: com/fasterxml/jackson/annotation/JsonSerializeAs`. Use `jackson-annotations` 2.21 or
  later; Camel uses 2.22.

See the https://github.com/fabric8io/kubernetes-client/blob/main/doc/MIGRATION-v8.md[Kubernetes client 8 migration guide]
for the other changes.

=== camel-kubernetes - the namespace option, custom resources and scale

* The producers of the namespaced `kubernetes-*` components (config-maps, cronjob, deployments, events, hpa, job,
  persistent-volumes-claims, pods, replication-controllers, resources-quota, secrets, service-accounts and services)
  use the `namespace` endpoint option when the `CamelKubernetesNamespaceName` header is not set; the header still
  wins. Before, these producers ignored the option: the get, create, update and delete operations failed without
  the header, and the list operations listed all namespaces. A route that sets the `namespace` option on a producer
  endpoint and lists without the header now lists that namespace only.
* The `kubernetes-custom-resources` producer uses the `crdName`, `crdGroup`, `crdScope`, `crdVersion` and
  `crdPlural` endpoint options. Before, it checked them but then built the custom resource definition from the
  headers only.
* The `kubernetes-custom-resources` producer takes the namespace from the `CamelKubernetesNamespaceName` header,
  or else from the `namespace` endpoint option, like the other producers. Before, the option won over the header.
* `createCustomResource` and `updateCustomResource` fail the exchange with the `KubernetesClientException` of a
  409 Conflict (the instance already exists, or the update is based on an old resource version). Before, the
  conflict was logged at INFO and the body was an empty `GenericKubernetesResource`.
* `scaleDeployment` and `scaleReplicationController` return the replicas of the spec of the scaled resource, the
  number that was asked for. Before, they returned the replicas of its status, which lags behind until the pods are
  started or stopped, and failed with a `NullPointerException` when the resource had no status yet.
* `createNode` and `updateNode` of `kubernetes-nodes` take the labels of the node from the
  `CamelKubernetesNodesLabels` header, the header that `listNodesByLabels` uses. Before, they read the
  `CamelKubernetesPodsLabels` header; a route that sets that header for a node must set
  `CamelKubernetesNodesLabels` instead.
* The `openshift-builds`, `openshift-build-configs` and `openshift-deploymentconfigs` producers take the namespace
  from the `CamelKubernetesNamespaceName` header, or else from the `namespace` endpoint option, in every operation.
  Before, they ignored the option (used by the `openshift-deploymentconfigs` consumer only), `listBuilds` and
  `listBuildConfigs` listed all namespaces even when a namespace was set, and `listDeploymentConfigs`,
  `listDeploymentConfigsByLabels` and `getDeploymentConfig` always used the namespace of the Kubernetes client.
  Without a namespace, `listBuilds` and `listBuildConfigs` still list all namespaces, and the deployment config
  list and get operations still use the namespace of the client.
* `listBuildsByLabels`, `listBuildConfigsByLabels` and `listDeploymentConfigsByLabels` without the labels header fail
  with an `IllegalArgumentException` that says the labels are required, instead of a `NullPointerException`.
* `scaleDeploymentConfig` returns the replicas of the spec of the scaled deployment config, the number that was asked
  for. Before, it returned the replicas of its status, which lags behind until the pods are started or stopped, and
  failed with a `NullPointerException` when the deployment config had no status yet.

=== camel-langchain4j-embeddingstore - auto-embedding a TextSegment, a List, a Map or a POJO body, and the REMOVE id

With an `embeddingModel` and no `CamelLangChain4jEmbeddingsEmbedding` header, ADD and SEARCH now embed a `TextSegment`
body as is, with its metadata. Before, they embedded and stored its `toString()`.

They now fail the exchange with an `InvalidPayloadException` for a body that is not text, bytes, a stream, a file or a
`TextSegment`, such as a `Map` or a POJO. Before, such a body was embedded and stored as its `toString()`, for example
`{text=...}`. Convert the body to text before the endpoint, with `convertBodyTo(String.class)` or a marshal to JSON.

ADD now embeds a `List` of `String` or `TextSegment` as a batch, one entry per element, and returns the `List` of the
IDs. Before, the whole `List` was embedded and stored as one text (such as `[a, b]`), and the body was one ID. A `List`
with another element type fails. SEARCH fails on a `List` body.

REMOVE now fails when the body is not text (a `String`, bytes or a stream) or a `Collection` of `String` IDs. Before, a
`Map` or a number body removed the ID made of its `toString()`, which usually removed nothing.

=== camel-jbang - camel run uses MariaDB Connector/J 4

Camel is upgraded to MariaDB Connector/J 4.0 (`org.mariadb.jdbc:mariadb-java-client`), which is the version
`camel run` downloads for a route or application that uses the MariaDB JDBC driver. The driver has these breaking
changes:

* TLS is enabled by default: `sslMode` defaults to `verify-full`. A server without TLS, or with a certificate the
  client does not trust (such as a self-signed one), now fails the connection. Configure the trust store, or set
  the `sslMode` option in the JDBC URL, for example `sslMode=trust` for a test database with a self-signed
  certificate, or `sslMode=allow`.
* `PreparedStatement.executeQuery()` rejects a statement that returns no result set. The `permitNoResults=true`
  option in the JDBC URL restores the former behavior.
* `setSavepoint()` throws in auto-commit mode.
* AWS IAM authentication moved to the separate `org.mariadb.jdbc:mariadb-java-client-aws` artifact, which an
  application using it must now add.
* Java 17 is the minimum, and the `useReadAheadInput` option is removed.

See the https://github.com/mariadb-corporation/mariadb-connector-j/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md[MariaDB Connector/J changelog]
for the other changes.

=== camel-jbang - the Camel CLI uses aesh instead of picocli

The Camel CLI parses its command lines, prints its help and completes its commands with
https://github.com/aeshell/aesh[aesh] instead of picocli. The commands and their options are the same. The help looks a
little different: the default value of an option is on a line of its own (`Default: ...`).

A plugin of the Camel CLI (an implementation of `org.apache.camel.dsl.jbang.core.common.Plugin`) must be changed and
built for Camel 4.24:

* `Plugin.customize` takes a `CommandTree` instead of a picocli `CommandLine`. A command is added with
  `commandLine.addSubcommand("mycommand", new CommandTree(new MyCommand(main)))`.
* The command classes use the aesh annotations: `@CommandDefinition` instead of `@Command` (the `footer` of the help
  is an attribute of it; aesh wraps the lines of the footer at 80 columns, except indented lines such as examples, so
  keep a line of text within 80 columns), `@Option(name = "my-option")` instead of `@Option(names = "--my-option")` (with
  `hasValue = false` for a flag), `@OptionList` for an option with several values, `@Argument` or `@Arguments` instead
  of `@Parameters`, and the aesh `@Mixin`.
* A class with the completion candidates of an option extends `org.apache.camel.dsl.jbang.core.common.CompletionCandidates`,
  and is set as the `completer` of the option.
* A test sets the options of a command with `CommandLineTestHelper.parse(command, args...)` (in the test jar of
  `camel-jbang-core`) instead of `CommandLine.populateCommand`.

=== camel-jbang - plugins declare which commands they contribute to

A CLI plugin can now declare which built-in commands it participates in beyond its own command, using the
`appliesTo` attribute on `@CamelJBangPlugin`. For example, a plugin that provides a `PluginExporter` should declare
`@CamelJBangPlugin(name = "...", firstVersion = "...", appliesTo = {"export"})` so it is loaded during `camel export`
but not during `camel run`.

Existing plugins that do not declare `appliesTo` are loaded for `camel run` and `camel export` as before (backward
compatible). A plugin opts in to filtering by listing at least one command in `appliesTo`; in the plugin configuration
file, an empty `appliesTo: []` array means the plugin is only loaded for its own command.

When adding a plugin via `camel plugin add`, the `appliesTo` value from the list of known Camel plugins is stored in
the configuration. After a plugin is downloaded for the first time, its `appliesTo` is learned from its
`@CamelJBangPlugin` annotation and cached for subsequent invocations.

NOTE: The annotation default (`appliesTo = {}`) is not equivalent to `appliesTo: []` in the config file.
An unset annotation value means no filtering (backward compatible — the plugin is loaded for `run` and `export`),
whereas an explicit empty list in the config restricts the plugin to its own command only.

=== camel-jbang - camel run exits with 1 when the integration fails to start

When `camel run` cannot start the integration, such as for a route that cannot be created or started, it now exits
with code `1` (fix the route) instead of `70` (internal error), and prints the error as one `ERROR:` line on stderr.
The stack trace is in the log of the integration, and is no longer printed a second time on stderr (add `--verbose`
for it). Scripts that checked for `70` after a failed start should check for `1`.

=== camel-jbang - a name of a running integration matches only that integration

A name given to `camel stop`, `camel get`, `camel cmd`, `camel trace` and the other commands for running integrations
is still a prefix, but when an integration has exactly that name, the name now matches only that integration. With
`orders` and `orders-v2` running, `camel stop orders` stops only `orders` (before, it stopped both), and
`camel cmd send orders` sends to `orders` (before, it failed because the name matched two integrations). The same
applies to the CamelContext name, and to the `nameOrPid` argument of the runtime tools of the MCP server. To act on
all integrations whose names start with `orders`, use `orders*`.

=== camel-resilience4j, camel-microprofile-fault-tolerance - a circuit breaker in a doTry

A circuit breaker in the steps of a `doTry` no longer ends that `doTry` for the steps after it. Before, when the
circuit breaker was done, a later routing slip, dynamic router, multicast or recipient list of the same `doTry` used
the error handler of the route (redelivery and the dead letter channel) instead of the `doCatch`. And after the
fallback of the circuit breaker had recovered from a failure, no `doCatch` of that `doTry` caught a later failure:
the failure was thrown from the `doTry`. Now such failures go to the `doCatch`, as for the other steps of the `doTry`.
A failure of the fallback itself is now also caught by the `doCatch` (before, it was thrown from the `doTry`).

To have those steps use the error handler of the route, move them out of the `doTry` block.

=== camel-aws2-kinesis - each endpoint uses its own client

Each `aws2-kinesis` endpoint now uses the Kinesis client of its own configuration (`region`, credentials,
`uriEndpointOverride`, or `amazonKinesisClient` / `amazonKinesisAsyncClient`). Before, all endpoints of the component
used the client of the endpoint that started first, and stopping one endpoint (for example by removing its route)
closed that client for every other endpoint.

Camel now closes only the clients it created. A client set with `amazonKinesisClient` or `amazonKinesisAsyncClient`, on
the component or on an endpoint, is no longer closed when Camel stops: close it yourself when you no longer need it.

=== camel-aws2-kinesis - failed records of the KCL consumer

With `useKclConsumers=true`, a record whose exchange fails (the route ends with an exception) is now reported to the
exception handler of the consumer, as the other consumers do. It is still checkpointed with its batch, so it is not
consumed again: handle it in the route, for example with a dead letter channel.

The new option `kclStopCheckpointOnFailure` (default `false`) keeps the checkpoint of a shard before the first failed
record instead, so the next holder of the lease of the shard consumes the records from that one on again. See the
component documentation for its cost: the records after the failed one can be routed twice.

=== camel-aws2-kinesis - the batch producer sends the failed records again and splits at 10 MiB

When the body is an `Iterable`, the producer now splits the batch into `PutRecords` requests of at most 500 records
and 10 MiB (the data and partition keys of the records). Before, only the number of records was limited, and a request
above 10 MiB was rejected by Kinesis. A record above 10 MiB now fails the exchange with an `IllegalArgumentException`
before any request is sent.

When some records of a request are not written, the producer now sends only those records again, up to 3 times with a
delay of 100 to 400 milliseconds (plus a random part of up to a quarter of it), and then sends the next requests of the
batch. Before, the exchange failed at once and
the next requests were not sent. If records are still not written, the exchange fails after the last request, with a
`RuntimeCamelException` (a `RuntimeException`, as before) whose message gives the number of records written and not
written. The `CamelAwsKinesisRecordCount` and `CamelAwsKinesisFailedRecordCount` headers are now also set when the
exchange fails, including when a `PutRecords` call throws: the exchange then fails with the exception of the call, as
before, and the records of that call and of the requests that were not sent are counted as not written.
Because of the retries, a batch whose records all fail now blocks the caller longer before it fails (700 to 875
milliseconds per request, 14 to 17.5 seconds for 10,000 records), and a record that is sent again is written after the
next records of its request. A redelivery of the exchange still sends the whole batch again: see the Batch Producer
section of xref:components::aws2-kinesis-component.adoc[AWS Kinesis].

=== camel-azure-storage-blob - copyBlob validates the source account and container name

`copyBlob` now rejects invalid source account or container names (headers `CamelAzureStorageBlobSourceBlobAccountName`
and `CamelAzureStorageBlobSourceBlobContainerName`) with an `IllegalArgumentException`.

=== camel-stitch - a response with an empty body, and the default sequence of a message

A response of Stitch with an empty body now sets the `CamelStitchCode`, `CamelStitchStatus` and `CamelStitchHeaders`
headers, and the body is an empty message. Before, the exchange kept the request body and got none of these headers.
An error response (HTTP status 300 or above) with an empty body now fails the exchange with a `StitchException`, as
any other error response does. Before, it was ignored.

A `StitchMessage` without a `sequence` now gets the time it is built (in millis) as its sequence. Before, every message
got the same sequence: the time the `StitchMessage` class was loaded.

=== camel-stripe - the CamelStripeOperation header chooses the operation

The `CamelStripeOperation` header now overrides the operation of the endpoint, as documented. Before, the header was
ignored and the operation of the endpoint was always used. Remove the header from a message that should use the
operation of the endpoint.

=== camel-telemetry - the span of an asynchronous step ends when the step is done

This applies to `camel-opentelemetry2`, `camel-micrometer-observability` and `camel-telemetry-dev`. The span of a step
that calls back from another thread (such as `delay`, which is asynchronous by default, `threads`, `throttle` with
`asyncDelayed`, or a `bean` that returns a `CompletableFuture`) now ends when the step is done, and the steps after it
are children of the span that contains the step (the route span, or the span of an EIP such as `choice`). Since 4.17
(since 4.21 with the default options, which trace the core processors) the following steps were children of the span
of the asynchronous step, and that span lasted until the end of the route. Around a called route the spans were ended
by the wrong events, and with a `recipientList` or `enrich` to such a route some spans were never ended. In particular:

* The trace tree of such routes changes, and the span of the asynchronous step is shorter.
* A failure in a later step no longer marks the span of the asynchronous step as failed: the error is on the span of
  the failing step and on the spans that contain it.
* Steps of an `onException` that run after the asynchronous step, and custom spans created in a processor after it,
  are no longer nested under its span.
* An asynchronous step that fails and is then handled by an `onException` with `handled(true)` now has its span marked
  as an error, because the span ends before the error handler runs. A synchronous step already behaved that way.

This is the tree of Camel 4.16 and older, so there is no option to keep the 4.17 tree. A dashboard that read the time of
the rest of the route from the span of the asynchronous step can use the route span instead.

=== camel-telemetry, camel-tracing - the http.url, url.path and server.address span attributes are presented like camel.uri

This applies to `camel-opentelemetry2`, `camel-micrometer-observability`, `camel-telemetry-dev`, `camel-opentelemetry`
and `camel-observation`. The `http.url`, `url.path` and `server.address` attributes of a span are now presented the same
way as the `camel.uri` attribute, which shows the endpoint URI as `Endpoint.toString()` does:

* `http.url`, when the message has neither a `CamelHttpUrl` nor a `CamelHttpUri` header, is now the HTTP address the
  endpoint sends to, with the query parameters it sends but without the Camel endpoint options. Before, it was the
  endpoint URI from the last `http:` onwards, with all the endpoint options. A `camel-http` endpoint with an `https`
  address now sets the attribute as well, and for a `camel-vertx-http` endpoint with an `https` address the value no
  longer starts with `http://https://`.
* `url.path` no longer includes the user info in front of the host of the endpoint URI.
* `server.address`, and `http.url` taken from the `CamelHttpUrl` or `CamelHttpUri` header, are presented as `camel.uri`
  is.

Dashboards or queries that use the old values may need a change.

=== camel-oaipmh - the CamelOaimphOnlyFirst header applies to its message only, and the CamelOaimphIgnoreSSLWarnings header is removed

The `CamelOaimphOnlyFirst` header now applies only to the message that has it. Before, the producer wrote it onto the
endpoint, so after one message with `CamelOaimphOnlyFirst=true` every later message sent to that endpoint returned only
the first page. A message without the header uses the `onlyFirst` option of the endpoint.

The producer no longer reads the `CamelOaimphIgnoreSSLWarnings` header, and the `OAIPMHConstants.IGNORE_SSL_WARNINGS`
constant is removed. Use the `ignoreSSLWarnings` option of the endpoint instead; when only some repositories need it,
send to them through an endpoint URI that sets the option.

=== camel-clickhouse - a query in a binary format returns a byte[] body

A `query` with a binary `format` (such as `RowBinary`, `Native`, `Parquet`, `Arrow` or `Avro`) now returns the result
as a `byte[]` body. Before, the bytes were decoded as UTF-8 text into a `String`, which replaced the bytes that are not
valid UTF-8, so the result could not be read. A query with a text format (such as `JSONEachRow`, `CSV` or `TSV`) still
returns a `String`.

Inserting a `List` of `Map` now fails with a `ClickHouseException` that says why, and so does a `List` of objects when no
class is registered on the `Client` for the table. Before, both failed with a `NullPointerException` from the ClickHouse
client.

=== camel-clickup - an event of another kind than taskTimeTrackedUpdated reaches the route

A webhook event of another kind than `taskTimeTrackedUpdated`, such as `taskCreated`, now reaches the route as a
`GenericEvent` (`getEvent()` gives the kind, `getProperties()` the other fields of the JSON). Before, it failed before
the route with 500, and ClickUp kept sending it again. A route that expects only `TaskTimeTrackedUpdatedEvent` can check
the type of the body, as the example in the ClickUp component page does.

=== camel-cm-sms - the from of an SMSMessage is the sender of the SMS

An `SMSMessage` with a `from` is now sent with that sender. Before, the sender was set only when `from` was empty (to
`defaultFrom`), so a message with its own `from` was sent with an empty sender. A message without a `from` is still sent
with `defaultFrom`.

When the validation of an `SMSMessage` fails, the `InvalidPayloadRuntimeException` now names the invalid fields in its
message, and its cause is an `org.apache.camel.ValidationException`.

=== camel-coap - a body that does not convert to a byte array fails

The `coap` producer sent a `POST` or `PUT` body that has no conversion to `byte[]`, such as a `Map`, a POJO or a
number, as an empty payload, without an error, so the data was lost. It now fails the exchange with an
`InvalidPayloadException`. A message without a body is still sent with an empty payload.

The `coap` consumer answered such a reply body with `2.05 Content` and an empty payload. It now answers
`5.00 Internal Server Error`, as the CoAP component page documents. A route without a reply body still answers
`2.05 Content` with an empty payload.

Marshal such a body first (for example to JSON) or convert it to a `String`.

=== camel-cometd - stream, file and null bodies are published

The cometd producer, and the reply of an InOut cometd consumer, now publish a stream, a `Reader` or a file body
(`File`, `Path` or the body of the file consumer) as its content as text. Before, the clients received the text of its
`toString`, such as `ByteArrayInputStreamCache@...` or the path of the file. A cometd consumer in the same JVM now
receives such a body as a `String`.

A `null` body is now published as a JSON `null` that the subscribed clients receive. Before, the message had no data,
so the clients took it for the reply of a publish and never received it, while the exchange succeeded.

=== camel-dapr - the producer keeps the message headers, and sends text and stream bodies as bytes

The `dapr` producer replaced all the headers of the message with the headers of the Dapr response, so every header set
before the call was gone afterwards. It now keeps the headers of the message and adds the headers of the response. A
`CamelDapr*` header set before a `dapr` call therefore also applies to the next `dapr` call of the route; remove it
when the next call must use the options of its endpoint.

The `invokeService`, `pubSub`, `invokeBinding` and `state` `save` operations now convert a text body (`String`) and a
stream body (`InputStream`, `Reader`) to bytes, which the Dapr client sends as they are. Before, the Dapr client
serialized a text body as a JSON string, so `hello` was sent as `"hello"` with the quotes, and a JSON text was sent
as a quoted, escaped JSON string; a stream body failed. A route that relied on the quoted text must now send the JSON
it wants, for example with `marshal().json()`. Other bodies, such as a `Map` or a POJO, are still serialized as JSON.
The workflow operations give the body to the workflow client as before.

=== camel-dapr - the secret store and key headers need the allowSecretStoreAndKeyFromHeaders option

The `secret` operation of the `dapr` producer took the secret store, the key and the metadata from the
`CamelDaprSecretStore`, `CamelDaprKey` and `CamelDaprMetadata` headers when they were set, over the `secretStore` and
`key` options of the endpoint, so a message could read another secret, or use another secret store, than the endpoint
is configured with. The `secret` operation now takes the secret store, the key and the metadata from the endpoint,
unless the endpoint sets the new `allowSecretStoreAndKeyFromHeaders=true` option (disabled by default), or the
component sets `camel.component.dapr.allowSecretStoreAndKeyFromHeaders=true`. A route that relies on these headers
must set this option.

The metadata of the endpoint is set with the new `metadata.` options, such as `metadata.namespace=production`. A
`secret` route that sent the metadata in the `CamelDaprMetadata` header can move it to these options. The other
operations also send the metadata of these options, and the `CamelDaprMetadata` header still takes their place for
one message.

A `secret` endpoint without a `key`, which took the key from the `CamelDaprKey` header, now fails with an error that
names the option, instead of reading all the secrets of the store. A `secret` endpoint without a `secretStore`, which
took it from the `CamelDaprSecretStore` header, now fails because the secret store is missing.

The other operations do not change: the `state` operations still take the key from the `CamelDaprKey` header, the
other operations still take the metadata from the `CamelDaprMetadata` header, and the other `CamelDapr*` headers still
take the place of the options of the endpoint, as before.

=== camel-dapr - the CamelDaprConfigKeys header takes the place of the configKeys option

The `configuration` operation of the `dapr` producer ignored the `CamelDaprConfigKeys` header and always sent the keys
of the `configKeys` option of the endpoint. It now sends the keys of the header when it is set: a comma-separated String
or a `Collection` of keys. A route that set this header, but relied on the keys of the endpoint, must remove the header.

=== camel-consul - no Optional in the body, and the CamelConsulResult header of every action

The `GET_SESSIONS` action of `consul:kv`, the `RENEW` action of `consul:session` and the `GET` action of
`consul:preparedQuery` set the `java.util.Optional` of the Consul client as the body, and the `CamelConsulResult`
header was always `true`. The body is now the value itself: the session id as a `String`, the `SessionInfo` or the
`StoredQuery`. When there is none, the body is unchanged and `CamelConsulResult` is `false`, as with the `INFO` action
of `consul:session`.

The actions of `consul:agent` and `consul:status` now set the `CamelConsulResult` header, as the other actions do.
`REGISTER` and `DEREGISTER` of `consul:catalog` now set it to `true` instead of `false`.

The `FIRE` action of `consul:event` with a message without body failed with a `NullPointerException`. It now fires the
event without payload.

=== camel-aws - the HTTP client is no longer looked up on the classpath

The camel-aws components now set the HTTP client of the AWS SDK clients they create for their endpoints: the Apache 5
client (`software.amazon.awssdk:apache5-client`) for synchronous clients, and the Netty client
(`software.amazon.awssdk:netty-nio-client`) for asynchronous clients. These are the clients the AWS SDK selects itself
with the Camel dependencies. Before, unless a proxy or `trustAllCertificates` was configured, the AWS SDK looked the
HTTP client up on the classpath. That lookup fails with `java.util.ServiceConfigurationError` (`... not a subtype`) when
the classpath registers an HTTP client from another copy of the AWS SDK, as on a Kafka Connect worker.

If you replaced these dependencies to use another HTTP client implementation, name it with the AWS SDK system property
`software.amazon.awssdk.http.service.impl` (synchronous clients) or `software.amazon.awssdk.http.async.service.impl`
(asynchronous clients): Camel then leaves the choice of the HTTP client to the AWS SDK.

=== camel-aws-bedrock - retrieval pagination

The `retrieve` operation of `aws-bedrock-agent-runtime` now removes the
`CamelAwsBedrockAgentRuntimeNextToken` header when the response has no continuation token.
Previously, an incoming token could remain on the final page. Pagination loops should stop
when this header is absent.
The `CamelAwsBedrockAgentRuntimeRetrieveGuardrailAction` header is also removed when the response
has no guardrail action, so an action from an earlier page cannot survive.

=== camel-aws-bedrock - the invoke operations send the body, not its toString()

The invoke operations of `aws-bedrock` now convert the message body to bytes, so a byte[], an InputStream or a file is
sent as its content, and a `Map` or a Jackson `JsonNode` is marshalled to JSON. Before, the body was sent as its
`toString()`, so only a String body worked. A message with no body now fails with an `InvalidPayloadException`, where
before the text `null` was sent to the model.

Other changes of `aws-bedrock`:

* `invokeTextModel` with `amazon.titan-embed-text-v2:0` now fails with an `IllegalArgumentException` that points to
  `invokeEmbeddingsModel`, as with the other embedding models. Before, it gave the JSON of the embedding as text.
* The streaming operations fail with an `IllegalArgumentException` for an image generation or embedding model, which
  do not stream. Before, `invokeImageModelStreaming` and `invokeEmbeddingsModelStreaming` gave an empty text.
* With no `operation` option and no `CamelAwsBedrockOperation` header, the producer fails with an
  `IllegalArgumentException` that says so, instead of a `NullPointerException`.
* The `CamelAwsBedrockGuardrailTrace` header is a `GuardrailTraceAssessment`, as `converse` always set it, and
  `converseStream` now sets it too. `ConverseStreamHandler.StreamMetadata` keeps it as a `GuardrailTraceAssessment`
  instead of a `GuardrailTrace`.
* `converseStream` with `guardrailTrace=true` now asks for the guardrail trace.
* `invokeTextModel` with an AI21 Jamba model gives the `choices` of the response, which was null before.

In the same way, `aws-bedrock-agent-runtime` now sends a `Map` or a Jackson `JsonNode` body as JSON text for
`retrieveAndGenerate`, `retrieve`, `invokeFlow`, `invokeAgent` and `invokeInlineAgent`. Before, a `Map` was sent as
its `toString()`, such as `{question=What is Camel?}`, and a `JsonNode` as pretty-printed JSON.

=== camel-aws-bedrock - invokeImageModelStreaming and invokeEmbeddingsModelStreaming are deprecated

The `invokeImageModelStreaming` and `invokeEmbeddingsModelStreaming` operations of `aws-bedrock` are deprecated, and
will be removed in a later release. The image generation and embedding models do not stream their response, so these
operations fail for such a model, and with a text model they are the same as `invokeTextModelStreaming`. Use
`invokeImageModel`, `invokeEmbeddingsModel` or `invokeTextModelStreaming` instead.

=== camel-aws2-sts - the secret access key and the session token are no longer copied into headers

The `assumeRole`, `getSessionToken` and `getFederationToken` operations of `aws2-sts` no longer set the
`CamelAwsStsSecretKey` and `CamelAwsStsSessionToken` headers. The body is the response of the AWS SDK, such as an
`AssumeRoleResponse`, and its `credentials()` hold them: use `${body.credentials().secretAccessKey()}` and
`${body.credentials().sessionToken()}` instead of the headers. The `CamelAwsStsAccessKeyId`, `CamelAwsStsExpiration` and
`CamelAwsStsAssumedRoleArn` headers are still set.

The `STS2Constants.SECRET_KEY_ID` and `STS2Constants.SESSION_TOKEN` constants are deprecated.

=== camel-weather - the CamelWeatherLocation header is the location that is queried

The latitude and longitude of the current geolocation (a location of `current`, or no location) are no longer written
onto the configuration of the endpoint. Before, they were, so every later query on that endpoint, including one with a
`CamelWeatherLocation` header such as `Paris`, requested the weather of the current geolocation. An endpoint without a
`location` already did the geolocation when its producer was created, so its `CamelWeatherLocation` header was never
used. The `lat` and `lon` options of an endpoint are still used instead of the location.

=== camel-geocoder - Nominatim sets ZERO_RESULTS when it finds no place

With `type=NOMINATIM`, an address that Nominatim does not find (it answers `[]`) now sets the `CamelGeoCoderStatus`
header to `ZERO_RESULTS`. Before, the exchange failed with an `IllegalArgumentException`. A latitude and longitude with
no place (Nominatim answers `{"error":"Unable to geocode"}`) now sets `ZERO_RESULTS` too, and another error answer of
Nominatim sets `ERROR`. Before, both set `OK` with no other headers.

=== camel-xchange - an endpoint with a method of another service fails

An `xchange` endpoint now fails when it is created if it has no `service` or no `method`, or if the method belongs to
another service, such as `service=marketdata&method=currencies`. Before, its producer did nothing and left the message
unchanged. The `Currency` and `CurrencyPair` headers and the body can now be text such as `BTC` and `BTC/USDT`; text
with a currency that XChange does not know fails the exchange.

=== camel-gson, camel-jsonb - the unmarshal type header needs allowUnmarshallType

The Gson and JSON-B data formats now use the `CamelGsonUnmarshalType` and `CamelJsonbUnmarshallType` headers to choose
the class to unmarshal to only when the new `allowUnmarshallType` option is enabled, as the Jackson data formats do with
`CamelJacksonUnmarshalType`. By default the headers are ignored, and the configured `unmarshalType` is used (a `Map` for
a JSON object when none is configured). A route that sets the header must enable the option, for example with
`.unmarshal(new JsonDataFormat(JsonLibrary.Gson).allowUnmarshallType(true))` or
`<json library="Gson" allowUnmarshallType="true"/>`.

=== camel-google-calendar-stream

With `syncFlow=false` and `considerLastUpdate=true`, the consumer now follows all result pages in each poll.
Previously it only read the first page, which could prevent later events from ever being delivered.
In this mode, `maxResults` controls the API page size; a poll can now deliver more events than this value.
Non-sync polling with `considerLastUpdate=false` continues to read only one page per poll.

=== camel-google-mail, camel-google-drive, camel-google-calendar, camel-google-sheets - one API client per endpoint

The `google-mail`, `google-mail-stream`, `google-drive`, `google-calendar`, `google-calendar-stream`, `google-sheets`
and `google-sheets-stream` components now create one API client per endpoint, from the options of that endpoint
(`clientId`, `clientSecret`, `refreshToken`, `accessToken`, `serviceAccountKey`, `delegate`, `scopes` and
`applicationName`, plus `emailAddress`, `p12FileName` and `user` on the two calendar components), instead of a single
client per component that every endpoint of the component shared. Each endpoint keeps its client for its lifetime. The
`clientFactory` option of a `google-drive` endpoint is now used to create the client of that endpoint. In addition, a
`google-mail-stream` endpoint configured with neither `clientSecret` nor `serviceAccountKey` now fails with an
`IllegalArgumentException` when its client is created, instead of getting a `null` client and failing at the first API
call. The `google-mail:update-message-labels` data type transformer now uses a client created from the component-level
configuration (the `camel.component.google-mail.*` options), kept until the component stops. Before, it used whichever
client the component had already created, for example the one of a `google-mail:messages/modify` endpoint, so
credentials set only on an endpoint URI no longer reach the transformer: set them on the component as well.

=== camel-grpc - the consumer reply must be the response message of the method

The `grpc` consumer sent the message body at the end of the route to the client as is. A body of another protobuf
message type, such as the unchanged request, was serialized and decoded by the client as the response, with wrong field
values and no error. The consumer now converts the reply (each element of a `List` reply) to the response message type
of the called method, and when that is not possible it sends nothing and ends the call with an `INTERNAL` status error.
A route must set the response message of the method as the body. A reply without a body still sends an empty response
message.

The same applies to the replies that a producer with `toRouteControlledStreamObserver=true` sends for a consumer with
`routeControlledStreamObserver=true`: a reply that does not convert ends the call with an `INTERNAL` status error and
fails the exchange. Such a producer also sends each element of a `List` body as a message, where a `List` failed
before. A `CamelGrpcEventType` header with a value other than `onNext`, `onCompleted` or `onError` now fails the
exchange, where it sent nothing before. An `onError` event with a body that is not a `Throwable` now ends the call with
an `INTERNAL` status error and fails the exchange with an `IllegalArgumentException`, where it failed with a
`ClassCastException` or a `NullPointerException` and the client waited until its deadline.

=== camel-grpc - routeControlledStreamObserver applies to streaming requests with the PROPAGATION strategy

With `routeControlledStreamObserver=true` and the default `PROPAGATION` consumer strategy, the route now sends the
replies of client streaming and bidirectional streaming calls, as it already did for unary and server streaming calls.
The consumer passes the `grpcResponseObserver` exchange property to the route, and no longer sends the body at the end
of the route or ends the call itself. Before, a producer with `toRouteControlledStreamObserver=true` failed with an
`IllegalArgumentException`, and the consumer sent the body at the end of the route as the reply. Such a route must now
send its replies with a producer with `toRouteControlledStreamObserver=true` and end the call, for example on the event
forwarded with `forwardOnCompleted=true`. The events of a call are still routed one at a time, in order, which is the
difference with the `DELEGATION` strategy.

=== camel-hashicorp-vault - cloud without a namespace, the createSecret body, and the secret version

With `cloud=true` and no `namespace`, the `hashicorp-vault` producer now works on the secrets engine itself, as the
secret refresh task already did. Before, `getSecret` failed with `Path must not be empty` and `listSecrets` returned an
empty list. A `hashicorp:` property with `camel.vault.hashicorp.cloud=true` and no namespace now also reads the secret
from the engine itself. With `cloud=true` and a `namespace`, `createSecret` and `deleteSecret` now work on the secret
in the namespace (`<namespace>/<secretsEngine>`), as `getSecret` and `listSecrets` do. Before, they worked on the
engine outside the namespace.

`createSecret` now writes a body of text, bytes, a stream or a file that holds a JSON object as the fields of the
secret; before, such a body failed. A body that is not a JSON object, and a message without a body, now fail the
exchange. Before, a message without a body wrote a new, empty version of the secret.

The `CamelHashicorpVaultSecretVersion` header of `getSecret` must be a version number; any other value now fails the
exchange with an `IllegalArgumentException`.

=== camel-hashicorp-vault - the token is renewed, and requests to Vault time out

The `hashicorp-vault` endpoint, the `hashicorp:` properties function and the secret refresh task now renew their token
before it expires. With a token, Camel now looks the token up in Vault (`auth/token/lookup-self`) at its first request,
and renews it (`auth/token/renew-self`) when the token is renewable and has a time to live. Vault's `default` policy
allows both. A token that cannot look itself up logs a warning, and is used as before, without renewal. Camel never
revokes a token it is given; a token it gets from one of the new logins, chosen with the `authentication` option, is
revoked when the endpoint, the function or the task stops.

Requests to Vault now time out after 5 seconds to connect, and after 15 seconds to read the response, which are the
defaults of Spring Vault. Before, a request could wait forever.

The `hashicorp:` properties function now uses the `VaultTemplate` it is created with. Before, it replaced it with a
client of the `camel.vault.hashicorp` configuration, and failed to start when that configuration had no token. A
`hashicorp-vault` endpoint without a `vaultTemplate` now fails when it is created if its host, port or scheme is
missing, or a setting of its login is missing. Before, a missing host failed only at the first request to Vault. The
`camel.vault.hashicorp.token` option is no longer marked as required, as only the token login uses it.

The `hashicorp-secrets` dev console now shows the login (`Token`, `AppRole`, `Kubernetes` or `JWT`) instead of
`OAuth Token`.

=== camel-hivemq - a body that does not convert to a byte array fails the exchange

The `hivemq` producer published a message body that has no conversion to `byte[]`, such as a `Map`, a POJO or a
number, as an empty payload, without an error, so the data was lost. It now fails the exchange with an
`InvalidPayloadException`. Marshal such a body first (for example to JSON) or convert it to a `String`. A message
without a body is still published as an empty payload.

=== camel-jcache - a JCacheManager closes only what no other manager uses

`JCacheManager.close()`, which a `jcache` endpoint calls when it stops, closed its cache, its `CacheManager` and the
`CachingProvider`, which is shared by the whole JVM, and so the caches of every other endpoint, repository and
CamelContext of that provider. It now closes each of them only when no other `JCacheManager` uses it. A
`JCacheManager` that was closed no longer opens its cache: `getCache()` then throws `IllegalStateException`; before,
it returned the closed cache, whose operations throw `IllegalStateException`. This matters for code that calls a
`JCacheManager` (for example one created by a custom `JCacheManagerFactory`) after it was closed.

=== camel-jdbc, camel-spring-jdbc - plain ? placeholders take their values only from the CamelJdbcParameters header

With `useHeadersAsParameters=true` and a query with only plain `?` placeholders (no named parameters `:?name`), the
`jdbc` and `spring-jdbc` producers bound the values of all the message headers by position, or of the
`CamelJdbcParameters` header when it was set. Which value went to which `?` then depended on the headers of the
message and their order (which changed in 4.21 from alphabetical to the order they were added). The values of plain
`?` placeholders now come only from the `CamelJdbcParameters` header: a `List` with the values in the order of the
placeholders (or a `Map`, whose values are taken in the order of its entries, or a single value for a query with one
`?`, such as a `String` or a `byte[]`). When the header is not set, or has more or fewer values than the query has
`?` placeholders, the exchange fails with a `RuntimeExchangeException` (before, extra values were ignored). Set the
values in that header, or use named parameters.

Named parameters take their values from the `CamelJdbcParameters` header when it is a `Map`, or else from the message
headers, as before. A `CamelJdbcParameters` header that is not a `Map` (such as the `List` left by an earlier query
with plain `?` placeholders in the same route) is now ignored for named parameters, where before it made the exchange
fail with `Cannot find key`.

=== camel-jgroups-raft - a body that does not convert to a byte array fails the exchange

The `jgroups-raft` producer skipped a message body that has no conversion to `byte[]`, such as a `Map`, a POJO or a
number, without an error, so nothing was set in the replicated log and the exchange succeeded. It now fails the
exchange with an `InvalidPayloadException`. Marshal such a body first (for example to JSON) or convert it to a
`String`. A message without a body is still skipped.

=== camel-keycloak - a failed create or delete fails the exchange, and a pojoRequest body is converted

The producer operations that give the JAX-RS `Response` of Keycloak as the body, such as `createUser`, `deleteUser`,
`createGroup`, `createClient` and `createOrganization`, now fail the exchange with a
`jakarta.ws.rs.WebApplicationException` when Keycloak answers with a status other than 2xx, such as 409 (already
exists) or 404 (not found). Before, the exchange completed and the route had to check the status of the `Response`.
`deleteOrganization`, `removeOrganizationMember` and `unlinkOrganizationIdentityProvider` fail the same way. The
`Response` body is now closed: its status and headers, such as `Location`, can still be read, but not its entity. The
bulk operations count such a status as a failed item.

With `pojoRequest=true`, a body that is not the Keycloak representation of the operation is converted: a `Map` or
JSON text is mapped to it. A body that does not convert fails with an `InvalidPayloadException`; before, the operation
did nothing and the body was kept. The elements of the `List` of `bulkCreateUsers` and `bulkUpdateUsers` are
converted the same way; an element that does not convert fails the exchange before any call to Keycloak.

=== camel-keycloak - JWKS public key resolution rate limiting

`KeycloakSecurityPolicy` now rate limits the refresh of the public keys from the JWKS endpoint of Keycloak
(`/protocol/openid-connect/certs`), so that a burst of tokens with unknown `kid` headers, or a key rotation, no longer
contacts the endpoint once per request.

The new option `minJwksRefreshInterval` is the minimum interval in seconds between two refresh attempts, `10` by
default. A token with an unknown `kid` triggers a refresh only when no attempt was made within that interval. Otherwise
the token is rejected, because its key is not in the cache, and the endpoint is contacted again only once the interval
has elapsed. A failed attempt counts as an attempt.

The cached keys are refreshed on a request once they are older than `jwksRefreshInterval`, in seconds and `300` by
default. There is no background refresh.

=== camel-splunk-hec - bytes, stream and file bodies are sent as text

The producer now sends a byte array, an `InputStream`, a `Reader` or a file body (`File`, `Path` or the file of the
file consumer) as text in the event. Before, a byte array was sent as a base64 string, a `File` as its path, and a
stream failed after part of the request had already been sent.

The JSON of the event is now written before the request is sent, with a `Content-Length` instead of a chunked request
body. A body or a header that cannot be written as JSON fails the exchange without sending anything to Splunk.

=== camel-spring-ai-embeddings - a List body always gives a List of vectors, and a Map or POJO body fails

A `List` body with one text now gives a `List<float[]>` with one vector, as a `List` of more texts does, and sets the
`CamelSpringAiEmbeddings` and `CamelSpringAiEmbeddingInputTexts` headers. Before, it gave a single `float[]` and set the
`CamelSpringAiEmbedding`, `CamelSpringAiEmbeddingIndex` and `CamelSpringAiEmbeddingInputText` headers, as for a `String`
body. A `String` body still gives a single `float[]`.

Each element of a `List` body is now read as text, and a `null` element fails with an `IllegalArgumentException`.
Before, a `List` of other objects failed with a `ClassCastException` in the embedding model. An empty `List` now gives
an empty `List` without calling the embedding model.

The body, and each element of a `List` body, must now be text, bytes, a stream, a `Reader`, a file or a Spring AI
`Document` (its text is embedded). Any other value, such as a `Map`, a POJO or a number, fails the exchange with an
`InvalidPayloadException`. Before, such a body was embedded as its `toString()`, for example `{a=1, b=2}`, and a
`Document` too. Convert the body to text before the endpoint, with `convertBodyTo(String.class)` or a marshal to JSON.

This also applies to bodies that Camel can convert to text but that are not in the list above, and that were embedded
as their text before: a `char[]`, a Netty `ByteBuf` (for example from netty-http with `disableStreamCache=true`), and a
custom type with its own type converter to `String`. A `List` with such an element fails too, as do a `Set` and an
array of texts (only a `java.util.List` is embedded as a batch). Add `convertBodyTo(String.class)` before the
endpoint for such a body, or convert each element of the `List` to a `String`.

=== camel-spring-ai-vector-store - DELETE takes the ids from the body first, and the embeddings headers go with a vector body

The DELETE operation now takes the ids from the body: an id, a `Document`, or a List of ids or of `Document`. The
`CamelSpringAiVectorStoreDocumentIds` header is used only when the message has no body. Before, the header won over
the body, and as ADD and SIMILARITY_SEARCH set this header to their own output ids, a later DELETE in the same route
deleted those documents instead of the ids in the body. A route that sets the header and sends a body that is not the
ids should remove the body (for example `setBody(constant(null))`) or put the ids in the body. Deleting what
SIMILARITY_SEARCH found still works, as its body is the List of `Document` found. A List with an element that is not a
String or a `Document` now fails.

The ADD operation now uses the `CamelSpringAiEmbeddingInputText` and `CamelSpringAiEmbeddingInputTexts` headers of
spring-ai-embeddings only with a `float[]` or a List of `float[]` body. Before, a header left over from an earlier
spring-ai-embeddings call replaced a `Document` or text body. A `float[]` body without the text header, a List of
`float[]` with a different number of texts, and a List with an element that is not a `Document` or a String now fail
with an `IllegalArgumentException`. Before, a vector without its text was stored as the text of `float[].toString()`,
extra vectors or texts were dropped, and other elements were skipped.

SIMILARITY_SEARCH with a `float[]` body (after spring-ai-embeddings) now searches for the text of the
`CamelSpringAiEmbeddingInputText` header, and fails without it. Before, it searched for the text of
`float[].toString()`.

=== camel-spring-batch - the CamelSpringBatchJobName header needs jobFromHeader=true

The producer now takes the job name from the `CamelSpringBatchJobName` header only when the endpoint has
`jobFromHeader=true`. Before, the header chose which job was launched also with the default `jobFromHeader=false`,
instead of the job named in the endpoint URI. Set `jobFromHeader=true` on the endpoint to keep picking the job from
the header.

A message header with a `null` value is no longer turned into a job parameter. Before, such a header failed the job
launch, because Spring Batch does not accept a job parameter without a value.

=== camel-salesforce - a Map or POJO body is sent as JSON, and raw fails on a body it cannot send

The REST operations (such as `createSObject`, `updateSObject` and `upsertSObject`) now marshal a `Map` or another
object that is not an SObject DTO as JSON. Before, they sent its `toString()`, for example `{Name=x}`, which is not
JSON. Text, bytes, a stream or a file in the body are sent as is, as before.

The `raw` operation now fails with an `InvalidPayloadException` when the body cannot be converted to an `InputStream`,
for example a `Map`. Before, it sent the request with an empty body.

=== camel-spring-rabbitmq - confirm=auto waits for simple publisher confirms

With the default `confirm=auto`, an InOnly producer now also waits for the publisher confirm when the connection
factory has publisher confirms of type `SIMPLE` (for example `spring.rabbitmq.publisher-confirm-type=simple`), as it
did for type `CORRELATED`. Before, it sent such messages without waiting, so a message that RabbitMQ negatively
acknowledged, or did not confirm, was lost without an error. Each send now waits up to `confirmTimeout` (5 seconds by
default). It fails with a `java.util.concurrent.TimeoutException` when RabbitMQ negatively acknowledges the message,
and with an `org.springframework.amqp.AmqpTimeoutException` when RabbitMQ does not confirm it within `confirmTimeout`.
Set `confirm=disabled` to send without waiting, as before.

=== camel-cyberark-vault - verifySsl and certificatePath are applied, and getSecret no longer sets the CamelCyberArkVaultSecretValue header

The `verifySsl` and `certificatePath` options of the `cyberark-vault` endpoint, and the
`camel.vault.cyberark.verifySsl` and `camel.vault.cyberark.certificatePath` properties of the CyberArk Conjur Vault
Property Function, now apply to the HTTPS connections to CyberArk Conjur. Before, they were ignored, and the default
trust store of the JVM was always used.

* With `certificatePath`, the certificates in that file (PEM or DER) are trusted instead of the default trust store of
  the JVM, and the endpoint or the function fails to start when the file cannot be read. Remove `certificatePath` to
  keep the default trust store of the JVM.
* With `verifySsl=false`, neither the certificate nor the host name of the server is checked. The option is now marked
  as an insecure SSL setting, so the security policy reports `camel.component.cyberark-vault.verifySsl=false`, which
  fails the startup with `camel.main.profile=prod`. Set `certificatePath` to the certificate of the server or of its
  CA instead.

The `getSecret` operation now sets the secret only as the message body, and no longer sets the
`CamelCyberArkVaultSecretValue` header. Before, a `createSecret` later in the same exchange stored the value of that
header instead of its own body. A route that reads the secret from the `CamelCyberArkVaultSecretValue` header after
`getSecret` has to read the body instead, or copy the body into a variable right after `getSecret`, for example with
`.setVariable("mySecret", body())`, and read it later with `${variable.mySecret}`. A variable stays in the exchange,
while producers such as HTTP, JMS or Kafka send the headers on to the next system. The `createSecret` operation still
stores the `CamelCyberArkVaultSecretValue` header when the route sets it.

=== camel-rocketmq - the headers are named CamelRocketMQ, and waitForSendResult is true by default

The header names of `camel-rocketmq` were misspelled `CamelRockerMQ...`; they are now `CamelRocketMQ...`. The Java
field names of `RocketMQConstants` are unchanged; only the header string values have changed.

[options="header"]
|===
| Previous value | New value
| `CamelRockerMQTopic` | `CamelRocketMQTopic`
| `CamelRockerMQTag` | `CamelRocketMQTag`
| `CamelRockerMQKey` | `CamelRocketMQKey`
| `CamelRockerMQOverrideTopicName` | `CamelRocketMQOverrideTopicName`
| `CamelRockerMQOverrideTag` | `CamelRocketMQOverrideTag`
| `CamelRockerMQOverrideMessageKey` | `CamelRocketMQOverrideMessageKey`
| `CamelRockerMQBrokerName` | `CamelRocketMQBrokerName`
| `CamelRockerMQQueueId` | `CamelRocketMQQueueId`
| `CamelRockerMQStoreSize` | `CamelRocketMQStoreSize`
| `CamelRockerMQQueueOffset` | `CamelRocketMQQueueOffset`
| `CamelRockerMQSysFlag` | `CamelRocketMQSysFlag`
| `CamelRockerMQBornTimestamp` | `CamelRocketMQBornTimestamp`
| `CamelRockerMQBornHost` | `CamelRocketMQBornHost`
| `CamelRockerMQStoreTimestamp` | `CamelRocketMQStoreTimestamp`
| `CamelRockerMQStoreHost` | `CamelRocketMQStoreHost`
| `CamelRockerMQMsgId` | `CamelRocketMQMsgId`
| `CamelRockerMQCommitLogOffset` | `CamelRocketMQCommitLogOffset`
| `CamelRockerMQBodyCrc` | `CamelRocketMQBodyCrc`
| `CamelRockerMQReconsumeTimes` | `CamelRocketMQReconsumeTimes`
| `CamelRockerMQPreparedTransactionOffset` | `CamelRocketMQPreparedTransactionOffset`
|===

Routes that use the constants (for example `setHeader(RocketMQConstants.OVERRIDE_TOPIC_NAME, ...)`) work without
changes. Routes that use a header by its literal name (for example `CamelRockerMQOverrideTopicName` in an XML or YAML
route, or `${header.CamelRockerMQKey}` in Simple) must be updated to the new name; the previous names are no longer
read. The generated Endpoint DSL header accessors are renamed accordingly (`rockerMQTopic()` -> `rocketMQTopic()`,
`rockerMQOverrideTopicName()` -> `rocketMQOverrideTopicName()`, and so on).

The `waitForSendResult` option is now `true` by default. Before, it was `false`, but the producer still waited for the
send result and failed the exchange when the send failed: it only reported the exchange as done synchronously while
calling the callback later, which breaks the asynchronous routing contract. The default behaviour is therefore
unchanged. With `waitForSendResult=false` an InOnly exchange now continues as soon as the message is handed to the
RocketMQ client, and a failed send is only logged (at WARN) and no longer fails the exchange.

=== camel-azure-cosmosdb - the consumer starts the change feed processor again when its start fails

When the change feed processor of an `azure-cosmosdb` consumer cannot start (Cosmos DB cannot be reached, the lease
container does not exist yet, a role assignment is not applied yet), the consumer now tries again, after 1 second and
then with a backoff of up to 30 seconds, until it starts or the consumer stops; before, the route stayed started and
never received a change. The first failure of each start still goes to the `ExceptionHandler` of the consumer (with
`bridgeErrorHandler=true` it is routed to the error handler once), and each further failed attempt is logged as one
WARN line.

Its readiness health check (`consumer:<routeId>`, with the consumer health checks of Camel enabled, the default) is now
the initial state (`camel.health.initial-state`, DOWN by default) while the first attempt to start runs, and DOWN while
the change feed processor cannot start, with the failure of the last attempt; before, it was UP. To leave
the check out, as before, set `camel.health.exclude-pattern=consumer:<routeId>`.

=== camel-vertx-websocket - consumers on the same host and port must use the same SSLContextParameters
