parameter, such as `directoryName` when the `uri` is only `file`: an `&` does no harm in the path. A route dump, such as
`camel validate normalize`, writes such a value in RAW.

Camel sees the `&` only when it is written in the YAML as the value of an option. When the `&` comes from a property
placeholder, or is in a value of a map option (such as `additionalProperties`), write the value in RAW yourself, such
as `+RAW({{name}})+`.

=== camel-jbang - camel get bean, get route-controller and top source

* `camel get bean --dsl` shows the configuration of each property next to that property: it paired them by position,
  which showed the configuration of another property, or failed with an internal error (`70`) with `--nulls=false`.
  An unknown `--scope` exits with `2` (it showed every bean).
* `camel get route-controller` shows back-off times and attempts that do not fit in an int (it showed other numbers),
  and no longer shows `Starting Routes: false` for the default route controller, which does not tell.
* `camel top source --json` has the counters `total`, `mean`, `min`, `max` and `last` as numbers instead of text, as
  the other listings.

=== camel-jbang - a boolean option given without a value is on

A boolean option that is on by default is now on when it is given without a value, like any other boolean option:
`camel export --maven-wrapper` exports the Maven wrapper, `camel config set --global key=value` writes the global
configuration, `camel cmd receive --show-body` shows the body, and `camel kubernetes run --dev --cleanup` removes the
deployment when it stops. Such a flag turned the option off before (picocli sets a flag to the opposite of its default).
Turn the option off with `=false`, such as `--maven-wrapper=false`, which works as before.

=== camel-jbang - an unknown sort key is a usage error

The commands that sort their rows by `--sort`, such as `camel ps`, `camel get route`, `camel cmd thread-dump` or
`camel catalog component`, exit with `2` and list their keys when the key is unknown: `camel ps --sort=nmae` printed
the integrations unsorted. A row without the value of the key now comes first, instead of failing the command.

=== camel-jbang - camel catalog kamelet

`camel catalog kamelet --filter` no longer fails with an internal error (`70`) when a Kamelet has no description. An
unknown `--type` exits with `2` (it showed nothing), a `--filter` that matches nothing says so on stderr, and `--json`
prints the Kamelets as JSON, as the other `camel catalog` commands do.

=== camel-jbang - camel init does not overwrite a file

`camel init` exits with `1` when the file it creates exists already, and leaves the file as it is (it overwrote the
file before). Use `--force` to overwrite it. The same holds for the files `camel init` downloads from GitHub or a gist,
of which it writes none when one of them exists. When the template is picked in the terminal (`camel init` without a
file), the command asks there whether to overwrite the file.

=== camel-jbang - camel get history

* `camel get history --diagram` and `--it` exit with `2` when several integrations have a message history, and ask for
  the name or pid of one (they printed a message on stdout and exited with `1`). The errors of `--diagram` are printed on
  stderr, and a graphical `--theme` in a terminal without graphics exits with `2`.
* `camel get history --json --mask` masks the secrets of the endpoints in the JSON too, as in the table.

=== camel-jbang - exit codes of config set, dependency list, jolokia and cmd memory-leak

* `camel jolokia` with a name or pid that matches no running integration, or more than one, exits with `3`, as
  `camel hawtio` does (it exited with `1`, and printed the second case on stdout).
* `camel config set` without a key (`=value` stored a value with an empty key) or without a value exits with `2`, and
  prints the error on stderr.
* `camel dependency list` with an unknown `--output` exits with `2`.
* `camel cmd memory-leak --min-size` takes a size in bytes such as `10B`, and a value that is not a size exits with `2`
  (both turned the filter off).
* `camel cmd send --reply` to a route that fails with an exception without a message, such as a `NullPointerException`,
  prints the failed exchange and exits with `1`, instead of failing with a `ClassCastException`.
* `camel get source` of an integration with routes without a source location, such as routes added by a
  `RouteBuilder`, no longer fails.
* Versions with four parts, such as the Quarkus platform version `3.27.3.1`, compare as versions in the version checks
  of the CLI (`3.27.3.1` came after `3.27.4`), and a `-SNAPSHOT` always comes before its release, as in Maven
  (`4.24.10-SNAPSHOT` came after `4.24.10`).

=== camel-jbang - camel dependency copy, update run and get error

* `camel dependency copy` deletes the JAR files an earlier copy left in `--output-directory`, and keeps its other files:
  it deleted the whole directory (with `--output-directory=.` the project). It exits with `1` when a dependency cannot
  be resolved or copied (it printed the error and exited with `0`), and with `2` when `--output-directory` is a file.
* `camel update run --runtime=quarkus --download=false` without `--quarkusMavenPluginVersion` exits with `2`, and a
  version without upgrade recipes exits with `1`: both failed with an internal error (`70`).
* `camel get error --last` shows the newest error of all the integrations (it showed the newest error of the integration
  with the lowest pid). An `--ago` that is not a duration, and a `--handled` that is not `true` or `false`, exit with
  `2`: the first showed every error, the second none. The errors of `--diagram` are printed on stderr, and a graphical
  `--theme` in a terminal without graphics exits with `2`.

=== camel-jbang - camel version list

* `camel version list` prints the `Last updated` note on stderr, so stdout has the table only.
* A `--date-format` that is not a date pattern, and a `--from-date` or `--to-date` that is not a date in the
  `yyyy-MM-dd` format (such as `2025-01-31`), exit with `2`: the first showed the dates unformatted, and the others were
  ignored.

=== camel-jbang - the shared action file is deprecated for plugin commands

`CamelCommand.getActionFile` and `CamelCommand.getOutputFile`, the action and output files that all the clients of an
integration share, are deprecated. A plugin command sends an action with `ActionClient` instead, which gives each action
a file of its own, so that two clients cannot overwrite each other's action or answer, and uses the shared files only
for an integration before Camel 4.21. `camel debug` sends its commands this way too.

=== camel-jbang - camel log, trace, cmd receive, cmd send, transform message and cmd memory-leak

* `camel log`, `camel trace --action=dump` and `camel cmd receive --action=dump` exit with `3` when the name or pid
  matches no running integration, and they do not follow (in a script or an AI agent, or with `--follow=false`): they
  printed nothing and exited with `0`.
* `camel cmd receive --action=dump` without a name shows the messages of the running integrations (it showed nothing).
  With `--output=json` it prints each received message once, as one JSON document per line with its `exchangeId`: it
  printed the batch of messages a line of the received file holds once for each message in it. `--since`, `--grep` and
  `--mask` apply to the JSON too.
* `camel trace` and `camel cmd receive` exit with `2` for an unknown `--sort` key, as the other listings do.
* `camel trace --tail` shows the end of the last exchange as completed, not as returning from its route.
* `camel cmd send` and `camel transform message` print the error and exit with `1` when the integration fails the action
  itself (they failed with an internal error, `70`).
* `camel cmd memory-leak` exits with `1` when the integration fails the action, such as when it cannot record, or has no
  `jfr-memory-leak` dev console: it printed nothing and exited with `0`. `--status` shows the progress of a recording
  (it said the recording started), and after a recording that its results can be queried (it said that no samples were
  captured).

=== camel-jbang - camel cmd eval with quotes and slashes

`camel cmd eval`, and the tools that evaluate an expression in a running integration (`camel ask`, and
`camel_eval_expression` of `camel mcp` and `camel tui --mcp` with an integration selected), send the expression and the
body as they are. They escaped them, which the integration does not undo: an expression with a double quote failed,
such as `+${body} == "hello"+`, and a slash (as in every XPath) or a quote in the body became part of the text.

=== camel-jbang - the runtime tools of camel ask and the MCP servers

* `send_message` of `camel ask`, and `tui_send_message` of `camel tui --mcp`, send the headers given as `key=value`
  lines, as their description says: the integration got the lines as one text, which it could not read as headers, and
  failed the message. A line that is not `key=value` is an error of the tool.
* `get_circuit_breakers` has the calls of the fallback, the calls that timed out and the calls a full bulkhead
  rejected, as `camel get circuit-breaker` shows them.
* `get_route_analysis`, `get_eip_stats` and `detect_config_drift` have the answers of the integration as JSON, instead
  of as JSON text inside their JSON.

=== camel-jbang-mcp - migration and dependency tools

The `camel_migration_compatibility` tool no longer takes the `camelComponents` argument, which it never used. It
now takes the Java requirement from the JDK versions of the target release in the catalog (the same data as
`camel_version_list`): Camel 4.19 and later no longer report Java 17 as a blocker, and `javaCompatibility` also
lists the `supportedVersions`. A target version newer than every release in the catalog has no Java requirement: the
tool says so in `warnings` instead of taking the one of the newest release.

`camel_migration_recipes` returns commands that run: the Spring Boot command activates
`org.apache.camel.upgrade.CamelSpringBootMigrationRecipe`, the recipes artifact is the `<major>.<minor>.0` release
of the target version, the OpenRewrite plugin is no longer pinned to a version, and the Quarkus command runs the
`quarkus-maven-plugin` at the Quarkus platform version of the new optional `quarkusPlatformVersion` argument (or the
latest one), with `-Drewrite=true` for a full run.

In `camel_dependency_check` and `camel_dependency_security_audit` the dependencies are the declared ones: a
component only in `dependencyManagement` or in the dependencies of a plugin no longer counts, and Spring Boot starters
and Camel Quarkus extensions count as the component they provide. The version status of `camel_dependency_check`
compares the project with the latest Camel release, added as `latestVersion`, instead of the catalog version of the
server. The security audit no longer audits the server's catalog version when the `pom.xml` sets no Camel version:
it audits the `camelVersion` argument, else lists every advisory of the declared components, and says so in
`warnings`. Its `reachable` is null when no routes are given, advisories without components are returned as
`uncheckedAdvisories`, and `clean` is true only when none of them apply.

=== camel-jbang-mcp - camel_openapi_mock_guidance is removed

The `camel_openapi_mock_guidance` MCP tool is removed. Call `camel_openapi_scaffold` with
`missingOperation=mock` instead: its answer explains the mode and lists the `camel-mock/` files, one per path
of the spec. The scaffold and `camel_openapi_validate` now follow the rules of camel-rest-openapi: an operation
without `operationId` is routed to `direct:GENOPID_<METHOD><path>` with `/` replaced by `.` and the braces of
path parameters by `_` (`direct:GENOPID_GET.pets._petId_` for `+GET /pets/{petId}+`), and a mock file has the
request path as its name, without the HTTP method (`camel-mock/pets/42.json` for `GET /pets/42`). The
security scheme diagnostics of `camel_openapi_validate` are now all warnings with a `SECURITY_<type>` code
(`SECURITY_APIKEY`, `SECURITY_HTTP`, ...), as camel-rest-openapi enforces none of them, and `OPENAPI_31` is
reported as information instead of a warning.

=== camel-jpa, camel-hazelcast - an exchange marked rollback only is not consumed

When a route marks the exchange rollback only (for example with `markRollbackOnly()` or `markRollbackOnlyLast()`)
there is no exception on the exchange, and these consumers treated it as a success. The JPA consumer deleted the entity
(or called its `@Consumed` method) and committed the poll, and the transacted (`transacted=true`) Hazelcast SEDA
consumer committed its transaction, so the message was removed from the queue. Now a rollback only exchange is not
consumed: the JPA consumer leaves the entity in the table, so the next poll consumes it again, as long as it still
matches the query. With `transacted=false` the other entities of the poll are still consumed (without logging a WARN),
and so is what a `@PreConsumed` method changed before routing; with `transacted=true` the whole poll is rolled back,
as for a failed exchange. The Hazelcast SEDA consumer rolls back its transaction, so the message stays in the queue.

A message that the route marks rollback only every time was removed before, and is now consumed again at every poll
(JPA), or taken from the queue again at once, without a delay (transacted Hazelcast SEDA, as for a failed exchange).
Handle such a message in the route instead, for example by moving it aside, rather than marking it rollback only
every time.

=== camel-jpa - a consumer query that selects values delivers them

A JPA consumer whose query selects values that are not entities, such as a column (`select x.address from Customer x`),
now delivers them with the default `consumeLockEntity=true`. Before, the lock of each value failed and the value was
skipped, so the route received no message, with only a DEBUG log. Such a value cannot be deleted or marked with
`@Consumed`, so each poll delivers the same values again, whatever `consumeDelete` is: a route that received nothing
now gets the whole result on each poll. Make the query exclude the rows already handled, or select the entity instead.
With the default `consumeDelete=true` a WARN is logged once; set `consumeDelete=false` to avoid it.

=== camel-a2a - caller profile and task owner

* When a security requirement of the agent card combines several schemes, such as an API key and an OAuth token, the
caller profile (`CamelA2AUserProfile`) is now the one of the token, whatever the order of the schemes. Before, it was
the profile of the first scheme, so a requirement that listed the API key first only gave `{"scheme": "apiKey"}` to
the route, and the tasks belonged to the API key.
* A token without a `sub` claim is now identified by the `username` that a token introspection response gives, else by
its `client_id` or `azp` claim (such as a client credentials token), so the tasks of different users and clients are
kept apart. A valid token that has none of these claims is now rejected with HTTP 401.
* The `CamelOAuthTokenValidationResult` exchange property is now set on the exchange that the route processes, as
documented. Before, it was only set on the incoming HTTP exchange.

=== camel-hazelcast - the transacted SEDA consumer waits for an exchange that continues on another thread

A Hazelcast transaction can only be committed or rolled back by the thread that began it. When the route of a
transacted (`transacted=true`) Hazelcast SEDA consumer continued on another thread (an asynchronous producer, or
`threads()`), the consumer tried to commit or roll back the transaction on that thread, which failed with
`Transaction cannot span multiple threads!` (logged as a WARN and ignored). The message was neither removed nor
put back: a failed message was not taken again, and the messages, also the processed ones, were put back into the
queue when the application left the Hazelcast cluster, so they were consumed again. Now the consumer thread waits for
the exchange to complete and ends the transaction itself, so it takes the next message only then; use
`concurrentConsumers` to process several messages at the same time. The exchange must complete within the Hazelcast
transaction timeout (2 minutes by default, `TransactionOptions`): when it takes longer, the commit fails with
`TransactionTimedOutException`, which is logged at ERROR, the transaction is rolled back and the message is consumed
again, also when the route succeeded.

=== camel-hazelcast - the reliable topic consumer goes on after it lost messages

A `hazelcast-topic` consumer with `reliable=true` that fell behind the ringbuffer of the reliable topic (messages
overwritten, or expired by its time to live, before the consumer read them) stopped receiving messages for good,
while the route stayed started: Hazelcast removed its listener. The consumer now skips the lost messages, logs a WARN
with their number, and goes on with the oldest message still in the ringbuffer. When Hazelcast cancels the listener
for another reason, such as a destroyed topic, the consumer reports it to its exception handler (logged at WARN by
default); restart the route to receive messages again. A listener cancelled because the Hazelcast instance or the
CamelContext is shutting down is only logged at DEBUG.

=== camel-hazelcast - HazelcastRoutePolicy gives up the leadership when its route is removed

A `HazelcastRoutePolicy` kept the Hazelcast lock until the CamelContext stopped, also after its route was removed (with
`removeRoute`, or by a route reload), so no other node could become the leader and the route ran nowhere in the
cluster. The policy now releases the lock when the last route that uses it is removed, so another node takes over, and
it competes for the lock again when a route that uses it is added again (with the same or a new policy instance). So a
route reloaded on the leader can now move to another node.

=== camel-sql, camel-mybatis, camel-jooq - an exchange marked rollback only last is not consumed either

The SQL, MyBatis and jOOQ consumers already left a message unconsumed when the route marked the exchange rollback
only with `markRollbackOnly()`. They now leave it unconsumed when the route marks it rollback only last with
`markRollbackOnlyLast()` too, as the JPA and Hazelcast SEDA consumers do: the SQL consumer runs its `onConsumeFailed`
statement instead of `onConsume` (and no statement when `onConsumeFailed` is not set), the MyBatis consumer does not
run its `onConsume` statement, and the jOOQ consumer does not delete the entity, so the next poll reads the row
again. With `transacted=true` the SQL and MyBatis consumers break out of the batch and roll back.
`markRollbackOnlyLast()` reaches the consumer only when no `transacted()` EIP has consumed the flag, so the
consumer's own transaction is the last one.

=== camel-sql - sql and sql-stored with batch=true: the update count and a single Map body

With `batch=true` the `CamelSqlUpdateCount` (sql) and `CamelSqlStoredUpdateCount` (sql-stored) headers no longer add
the negative values a JDBC driver returns for an item whose update count it does not know
(`Statement.SUCCESS_NO_INFO`, which HSQLDB returns for stored procedure calls). Such a batch reported a negative count
before, for example `-4` for two items; it now reports the sum of the known counts, which can be `0`.

A single `Map` body is now one batch item, and so is a single `Map` in the `CamelSqlParameters` header with
`useMessageBodyForSql=true` (sql) or the `CamelSqlStoredParameters` header with `useMessageBodyForTemplate=true`
(sql-stored). Before, its entries were used as the batch items and the call failed (sql: no value found for the named
parameters, sql-stored: an `SQLException`).

=== camel-sql - JdbcKeyValueRepository: deleteIfExpiredString, and putIfAbsent after a duplicate key

When `JdbcKeyValueRepository` reads an entry that has expired, it deletes the row with the new `deleteIfExpiredString`
statement (`DELETE FROM CAMEL_KEYVALUE WHERE ITEM_KEY = ? AND EXPIRES_AT > 0 AND EXPIRES_AT <= ?`) instead of
`deleteString`, so that an entry another node stored for the key in the meantime is kept. If you set `selectString`,
`insertString` and `deleteString` for a table with other column names, set `deleteIfExpiredString` as well; otherwise
reading an expired entry fails with an SQL error. With `tableName` it is derived from the default statement, as the
other statements are.

When the INSERT of `putIfAbsent` fails with a duplicate key, the key is read again in a new transaction, which returns
the value stored by the other caller (before, the key was read again in the failed transaction, which could return
`null` although nothing was stored). `putIfAbsent` now throws the `DuplicateKeyException` when the INSERT fails three
times, or at once when it runs in a transaction of the caller, as that transaction cannot be used to read the key
again.

=== camel-sql - JdbcKeyValueRepository: replace and delete(key, expected) do not match an expired entry

`JdbcKeyValueRepository.replace(key, expected, newValue, ttl)` and `delete(key, expected)` now match only an entry that
has not expired. Before, they matched an entry whose TTL had passed and returned `true`, although `get` returns `null`
for it, and `replace` gave that entry a new TTL. Now they return `false` and leave the row as it is.

For this, the default `updateIfValueString` and `deleteIfValueString` statements end with
`AND (EXPIRES_AT = 0 OR EXPIRES_AT > ?)`, the condition of `selectKeysString`, and the current time is passed as their
last parameter:

[source,sql]
----
UPDATE CAMEL_KEYVALUE SET ITEM_VALUE = ?, EXPIRES_AT = ?
    WHERE ITEM_KEY = ? AND ITEM_VALUE = ? AND (EXPIRES_AT = 0 OR EXPIRES_AT > ?)
DELETE FROM CAMEL_KEYVALUE WHERE ITEM_KEY = ? AND ITEM_VALUE = ? AND (EXPIRES_AT = 0 OR EXPIRES_AT > ?)
----

If you set `updateIfValueString` or `deleteIfValueString` yourself, for example for a table with other column names,
add the same condition with a parameter for the current time at the end. Otherwise the repository passes one more
parameter than the statement has placeholders: most JDBC drivers then fail with an SQL error, while some drivers may
ignore the extra parameter, so the statement still matches an expired entry. With `tableName` the statements are
derived from the default statements, as before.

=== camel-sql - JdbcOrphanLockAwareIdempotentRepository keeps confirmed keys

`JdbcOrphanLockAwareIdempotentRepository` now confirms a key by setting its `createdAt` to null and no longer keeping
it alive, so a confirmed key stays a duplicate after its instance stops, as in `JdbcMessageIdRepository`. Before,
another instance took it over `lockMaxAgeMillis` later and processed the message again. Keys that are only added
(the file consumer with `idempotentEager=false`) and keys written before the upgrade are still taken over. With
`readLockRemoveOnRollback=false`, a failed file is confirmed too and is no longer retried by another instance.

The `createdAt` column must allow null. The repository checks this when it starts (in a transaction that it rolls
back); with a `NOT NULL` column, or a MySQL/MariaDB `TIMESTAMP` with `explicit_defaults_for_timestamp=OFF`, it logs a
WARN and keys stay locks as before, and a confirm sends no statement. Confirmed keys have no age, so housekeeping by
`createdAt` does not delete them: to purge them by age, add a column of your own (such as
`insertedAt TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`) or set a `confirmQuery` that also stores the time of the confirm,
and purge by that column. With the `idempotent` read locks of the file consumer and the default
`readLockRemoveOnCommit=false`, the name of every processed file stays in the table; when the key is only a read
lock, set `readLockRemoveOnCommit=true` with `readLockIdempotentReleaseDelay`. With a custom table set by custom
queries instead of `tableName`, set `confirmQuery` too; set it to null or empty to keep the old behaviour. All
instances sharing the table must run this version: older ones see a confirmed key as absent (before 4.14.6 and 4.18.2
they take it over).

=== camel-jta - an exchange marked rollback only last rolls back its transaction

In a `transacted()` route that uses a JTA transaction manager, `markRollbackOnlyLast()` now rolls back the
transaction of that `transacted()` block, as it already did with the camel-spring transaction error handler. Before,
the transaction was committed, and the marker was then cleared, so nothing showed that the rollback was skipped.
The marker is still cleared afterwards, so an outer `transacted()` block does not see it.

If the `transacted()` block in which the exchange is marked rollback only last joins the transaction of an outer block
(for example a `PROPAGATION_MANDATORY` block inside a `PROPAGATION_REQUIRED` block), that shared JTA transaction is now
marked rollback only, so the commit of the outer block fails (in Camel Quarkus with `CamelException: Unable to commit transaction`) where it used
to commit, as with camel-spring (`UnexpectedRollbackException`). To roll back only the inner block, use
`PROPAGATION_REQUIRES_NEW` for it.

=== camel-keycloak - the CamelKeycloakOperation header needs the allowOperationHeader option

The `CamelKeycloakOperation` header took the place of the `operation` of the `keycloak` endpoint, so a message could
make the endpoint run another operation than the one it is configured with. The header is now ignored unless the
endpoint sets the new `allowOperationHeader=true` option (disabled by default), and the endpoint runs its configured
`operation`. A route that picks the operation with the header must set `allowOperationHeader=true` on the endpoint, or
on the component (`camel.component.keycloak.allowOperationHeader=true`) for all its endpoints.

An endpoint without an `operation`, which took the operation from the header, now fails with an
`IllegalArgumentException` that asks for the operation, unless it sets `allowOperationHeader=true`.

=== camel-thrift - SSL/TLS settings of the consumer, and errors sent to the client

* A consumer configured with `negotiationType=SSL` and the default `synchronous=false` now fails to start. It used to
start a server without SSL/TLS. SSL/TLS requires `synchronous=true`, as on the producer.
* The `SASL` negotiation type, which is not implemented, now makes the producer and the consumer fail to start. They
used to behave as with `PLAINTEXT`.
* The SSL/TLS consumer now creates its server socket from the SSL context of `sslParameters`, so all of its settings
apply, including the trust managers and the server parameters. For example, `serverParameters.clientAuthentication=REQUIRE`
now requires a client certificate. Before, the consumer only used the key store, the protocol and the cipher suites,
and never asked for a client certificate. The key managers are required, and as in the other components the key is
read with their `keyPassword`: set it when the key is protected by a password. Before, the consumer read the key with
the password of the key store.
* A new `muteException` consumer option, enabled by default, sends a generic `TApplicationException` of type
`INTERNAL_ERROR` ("Internal error processing <method>") to the client instead of an exception that the Thrift method
does not declare. Before, the asynchronous consumer sent the message of the exception, and the synchronous consumer
sent a `TApplicationException` as it was, such as one returned by another Thrift service that the route called. An
exception declared in the IDL is still sent as it is. Set `muteException=false` to send the exception as before.

=== camel-mina - sync=false is one-way, and the reply of a textline consumer

A consumer with `sync=false` no longer writes the body the route ends with back to the client. Before, it wrote it back
(over TCP and UDP), so a client that only sends got its own message echoed. Use `sync=true` (the default) when the client
expects a reply.

A producer with `sync=false` now leaves the body and headers of an `InOut` exchange as they were. Before, the exchange
ended with an empty message.

A consumer with `textline=true` now converts the reply to a `String` before writing it, as the producer does with the
request. Before, a `byte[]` or a stream reply was written as its `toString()`.

A consumer with `disconnectOnNoReply=false` now keeps the session open when the route ends with no body, and logs it at
`noReplyLogLevel` (`WARN` by default). Before, the consumer always closed the session and logged at `DEBUG`.

=== camel-thrift - the reply of the consumer must convert to the return type of the method

For a method with a return type, the consumer now fails the call with a `TApplicationException` when the route leaves
no body (type `MISSING_RESULT`), or a body that does not convert to the return type (type `INTERNAL_ERROR`). Before,
the synchronous consumer sent `0` (or `false`) for a method that returns a number (or a `bool`) and the route left no
body, and the asynchronous consumer sent a null result, or no response at all for a method that returns a number. The
body is still ignored for a `void` method.

=== camel-netty - errors sent to the client

A new `muteException` consumer option, enabled by default, makes a consumer with `sync=true` write a generic exception
(`java.lang.Exception` with the message `Exchange processing failed` and no stack trace) back to the client when the
exchange failed, instead of the exception of the route. With `textline=true` the client now receives the line
`java.lang.Exception: Exchange processing failed`, where it used to receive the class name and the message of the
exception of the route. With an encoder that accepts any object, the serialised exception no longer carries the class,
message, cause or stack trace of the exception of the route. Set `muteException=false` to write the exception as before.
`transferExchange=true` is not affected.

=== camel-netty - replies with the default codec, useByteBuf and sync=false

With the default codec (no `textline`, no custom `encoders` or `decoders`) a consumer with `sync=true` now converts a
reply that is not a `String` (or a `ByteBuf`) to a `String` before writing it, as with `textline=true`. Before, such a
reply, including the exception of a failed exchange, could not be written and the client got no reply. A failed exchange
now replies `java.lang.Exception: Exchange processing failed` (see `muteException` above). When the reply cannot be
written, the consumer closes the TCP channel instead of leaving the client waiting.

With `useByteBuf=true` a body that cannot be converted to a `ByteBuf` (such as a `File`, a `Map` or a POJO) now fails
the exchange with an `InvalidPayloadException`, where nothing was sent before. A consumer with `useByteBuf=true` now
also converts a reply set as the out message to a `ByteBuf`. `NettyPayloadHelper.getIn` and `getOut` declare
`InvalidPayloadException`.

With `sync=false` the producer now ignores a message the remote server sends back. Before, that reply was set on the
exchange after it had already continued routing. The producer now also leaves the body and headers of an `InOut`
exchange as they were. Before, the exchange continued with an empty message. This also applies to `netty-http`.

=== camel-netty - a reply that cannot be written, and allowSerializedHeaders on the reply

When writing the reply fails (for example the encoder cannot encode it), the consumer now closes the channel (not for
UDP), so the client does not wait for a reply that never comes. Before, the failure was only logged.

With `transferExchange=true` and `allowSerializedHeaders=true`, the consumer now also includes serializable header,
property and variable values in a reply set as the out message, as it already did for any other reply. The decoder of the
other side must accept their classes.

With `transferExchange=true`, the producer and the consumer no longer transfer the headers and exchange properties that
hold Netty transport objects of their own side: `CamelNettyRemoteAddress`, `CamelNettyLocalAddress`,
`CamelNettySSLSession`, `CamelNettyChannelHandlerContext`, `CamelNettyMessageEvent` and `CamelNettyChannel`. Before,
with `allowSerializedHeaders=true`, the socket address headers (`java.net.InetSocketAddress`) were transferred, and the
default deserialization filter of `ObjectDecoder`, which rejects `java.net` classes, made the other side fail to decode
the message unless the route removed them.

=== camel-netty - with transferExchange the consumer keeps its own connection headers

With `transferExchange=true` the connection headers `CamelNettyRemoteAddress`, `CamelNettyLocalAddress`,
`CamelNettyChannelHandlerContext`, `CamelNettySSLSession` and the `CamelNettySSLClientCert*` headers (and the
`CamelNettyRemoteAddress` and `CamelNettyChannel` exchange properties) are now always the ones of the receiving side:
the connection it accepted is the source of truth. Before, the headers of the transferred exchange replaced them. Such
a header in the transferred exchange is no longer used, also when the receiving side has no value for it (for example
the `CamelNettySSLClientCert*` headers on a connection without a client certificate). This also applies to the reply
the producer receives. Send any such value of the sender under a header name of your own.

=== camel-netty - transferExchange requires explicit endpoint configuration

`NettyPayloadHelper` now checks `transferExchange=true` on the endpoint configuration before reconstructing exchange data from a `DefaultExchangeHolder` payload.

Before, a receiver with an object decoder silently reconstructed the exchange even when `transferExchange=false`. Now, when `transferExchange` is disabled (the default), the incoming `DefaultExchangeHolder` is left as an opaque body (both on the consumer side and for the producer's reply via `setOut`).

In addition, the legacy two-argument methods `NettyPayloadHelper.setIn(Exchange, Object)` and `setOut(Exchange, Object)` are deprecated and no longer unmarshal `DefaultExchangeHolder` payloads. Callers should migrate to the three-argument overloads passing the `NettyEndpoint`.

=== camel-netty-http - a response that cannot be built

When no HTTP response can be built from the reply of the route (for example the body cannot be converted), the consumer
now answers with HTTP Status 500 and closes the connection. The body is empty, or the stack trace of the exception with
`muteException=false`. Before, no response was sent and the client waited until it timed out.

A `List` reply is now written as its `toString()`, as a `Map` or a POJO already was. Before, no response was sent.

=== camel-odata - result headers

The OData producer no longer adds an `Authorization` header to the result when authentication comes from the
endpoint's `authMethod`, `authUsername`, `authPassword` or `authBearerToken` options. A caller-supplied
`Authorization` header keeps its existing precedence and remains on the exchange.

Routes that need an `Authorization` header after the OData step should set it explicitly in the route.

The `CamelHttpUri`, `CamelHttpQuery` and `CamelHttpMethod` headers and the `Accept` header that the producer sets for
its own HTTP request are no longer copied to the result either. Before, a later HTTP step in the same route reused
them and sent its request to the OData target, with the OData method. A value of these headers that the caller has on
the exchange is kept.

=== camel-odata - the body of CREATE and UPDATE, and a response that is not a JSON object

`CREATE` and `UPDATE` send JSON text given as a byte array, a stream or a file as it is, as they do for a `String`.
Before, a byte array was sent as a JSON array of numbers, and a stream or a file failed.

`CREATE` and `UPDATE` now fail with an `IllegalArgumentException` when the body is null or empty. Before, they sent a
`POST` or `PATCH` request with no content. Set the body to `{}` to send an empty JSON object.

A response that is a JSON array or a plain value is now the body as it is parsed (a `List`, a number, a `String` or a
boolean). Before, the producer failed with `Expected an OData JSON object response`.

=== camel-telegram - webhook requests are verified with a secret token

In webhook mode, Camel now registers the webhook with a secret token, which Telegram sends in the
`X-Telegram-Bot-Api-Secret-Token` header of every webhook request, and rejects a webhook request that does not carry
the token with HTTP 403. The token is the value of the new `webhookSecretToken` option or, when that option is not
set, a value derived from the authorization token. A client that calls the webhook endpoint directly, for example in
a test, must send the header too.

When Camel registers the webhook at startup (`webhookAutoRegister=true`, the default), nothing needs to change: each
upgraded instance registers the webhook with the token, and the instances that are not upgraded yet ignore the header.

When the webhook is registered in another way, through the `WebhookRoutePolicy` or outside Camel, the registration
made with the previous version has no secret token, and the upgraded instances reject every update until the webhook
is registered again with it. Register the webhook again first, then upgrade the instances that receive the updates:
instances of the previous version ignore the header. With the `WebhookRoutePolicy`, run the registration with the new
version. Outside Camel, set `webhookSecretToken`, and pass the same value as the `secret_token` parameter of
`setWebhook`.

The default webhook path is a hash of the endpoint URI, so adding an option to the URI, such as `webhookSecretToken`,
changes the path. When the webhook is not registered by Camel at startup, set `webhookPath` to keep the path that is
registered.

To restore the previous behaviour, set `webhookSecretTokenEnabled=false`: the webhook is then registered without a
secret token, and webhook requests are not verified.

=== camel-whatsapp - webhook events are verified by default

In webhook mode (`webhook:whatsapp:...`), the `X-Hub-Signature-256` signature of every event that WhatsApp posts is
now verified with the `webhookSecret` option, the app secret from the Meta app dashboard. A webhook route without
`webhookSecret` now fails to start. Set `webhookSecret` to the app secret, or set the new `webhookVerification` option
to `false` to accept the events without verifying their signature, for example in development. An event without a
valid signature is answered with HTTP 403 and does not reach the route. A client that posts events to the webhook
endpoint directly, for example in a test, must sign them too.

An event that reaches the route is now answered with HTTP 200 and an empty body. Before, the reply carried the event
back to the caller. The reply to the verification request (`GET`) is still the `hub.challenge`.

=== camel-paho-mqtt5 - a failed message with manualAcksEnabled is delivered again or acknowledged

With `manualAcksEnabled=true`, a message whose exchange failed was left unacknowledged. For a QoS 1 message the
broker never sent it again during the connection, and after `receiveMaximum` such messages it sent the consumer
nothing more (the client completes the QoS 2 flow on its own, so QoS 2 messages did not stop the consumer). Now, for a
QoS 1 message on a session that the broker keeps after the disconnect (`cleanStart=false` and `sessionExpiryInterval`
greater than 0), the consumer connects again after a failed exchange (after one second, doubled for each failed
exchange in a row up to `maxReconnectDelay`, and back to one second after any exchange that succeeds; logged at WARN),
and the broker sends the message again. In all other
cases (a clean session, which is the default, a QoS 2 message, or `cleanStart=false` without `sessionExpiryInterval`,
where the session ends with the connection) the broker never sends the message again, so the failed message is
acknowledged and the failure is left to the Camel error handler. A consumer that uses a client set with the `client`
option leaves a failed QoS 1 message on a session that the broker keeps unacknowledged, as before; as the consumer does
not connect that client, set the `cleanStart` and `sessionExpiryInterval` of the endpoint to match its connection
options.

=== camel-iggy - messageExpiry is sent to Iggy in microseconds

The `messageExpiry` option is in seconds, but its value was passed unchanged to Iggy, which reads the message expiry
of a topic in microseconds, so a topic created by Camel (`autoCreateTopic=true`) expired its messages a million times
too early (`messageExpiry=3600` gave 3.6 ms instead of one hour). The value is now converted to microseconds. Topics
created before keep their expiry: change it on the Iggy server. If you set `messageExpiry` in microseconds to work
around this, set it in seconds now, or newly created topics keep their messages a million times longer than intended.

=== camel-iggy - binary payloads, and the body of the consumer

The `iggy` producer now sends a `byte[]`, `InputStream`, `ByteBuffer` or file body as its bytes. Previously it
converted it to text first, which corrupted a payload that is not valid UTF-8. Other bodies are still sent as UTF-8
text. A `null` body now fails with an `InvalidPayloadException`, a `List` body with a `null` element fails with a clear
message, and an empty `List` body sends nothing (all three failed inside the Iggy client before).

The `iggy` consumer now sets the payload as a `byte[]` body. Previously it was a `String` decoded with the default
charset of the JVM, which corrupted binary payloads. Camel converts the `byte[]` to a `String` where a `String` is
needed, with the charset of the exchange (`CamelCharsetName`) if set, but code that reads the body with `getBody()`
and expects a `String` must now use `getBody(String.class)` or add `convertBodyTo(String.class)` to the route.

=== camel-jira - the newIssues consumer reads every new issue

A poll of the `newIssues` consumer read only the newest `maxResults` (default 50) of the issues created since the
previous poll, and the older ones were never consumed. A poll now reads every page of the search and can route more
than `maxResults` issues; `maxResults` is the page size of that search (at most 50). There is no upper bound, so the
first poll after a long outage or a large import can send many search requests and route many issues in one batch.

On Jira Cloud the Jira consumers now read the pages of a search with the next page token, as Jira Cloud cannot page by
start index: with `maxResults` above 50, a search with more than 50 results failed on the second page with
`UnsupportedOperationException`.

=== camel-langchain4j-ingest - a body that is not text fails

In the default text modality, the `langchain4j-ingest` producer now ingests the text of a LangChain4j `TextSegment` or
`Document` body, and fails the exchange with an `InvalidPayloadException` for a body that is not text, bytes, a stream,
a `Reader` or a file, such as a `Map` or a POJO. Before, such a body was ingested as its `toString()`, for example
`TextSegment { text = ... }`. Convert the body to text before the endpoint.

=== camel-netty-http - an ambiguous request path is rejected when a security constraint is configured

A `netty-http` consumer configured with a `securityConstraint` now rejects, with HTTP 400, a request whose path
contains `.` or `..` segments (including their percent-encoded `%2e` form). Such a path is ambiguous: the security
constraint, the consumer dispatch and the route may each interpret it differently, so with `matchOnUriPrefix=true` and a
specific inclusion such as `/admin/*`, a request like `/foo/../admin/x` (or its encoded `/foo/%2e%2e/admin/x` form)
could be matched against the inclusions and exclusions in a different form than the one the route handles. Rejecting
it up front keeps the constraint decision and the routed path consistent. A well-behaved HTTP client normalizes
dot-segments before sending, so normal traffic is unaffected.

For the same reason, such a consumer also rejects with HTTP 400 a request whose path contains an empty segment (`//`),
a `;` path parameter (also as `%3b`) or a `\` (also as `%5c`). A backend the route forwards the request to may collapse
the empty segment, strip the path parameter or treat the backslash as a `/`, and so act on a different path than the one
the constraint was checked against. A route that needs matrix parameters (`;`) must not configure a
`securityConstraint` on the same consumer.

This applies only when a `securityConstraint` is configured; a consumer without one is unchanged, and continues to
receive the request path as sent.

=== camel-hashicorp-vault - properties without engine use the `secret` engine

A `hashicorp:` property without an engine, such as `{{hashicorp:mysecret}}` or `{{hashicorp:mysecret#field}}`, now
reads the secret from the `secret` engine. Before, it read from the engine of the `hashicorp:` property resolved
before it (or from a `null` engine), and `{{hashicorp:mysecret#field}}` always resolved to no value. Properties with
an engine, such as `{{hashicorp:secret:mysecret#field}}`, are not affected.

A `hashicorp:` property without an engine and with a version, such as `{{hashicorp:mysecret@2}}`, now reads version 2
of the secret `mysecret` in the `secret` engine. Before, the `@2` was kept as part of the secret name.

A `hashicorp:` property with a field of a secret that does not exist, such as `{{hashicorp:secret:missing#field}}`,
now resolves to no value, as it already did without a field, so its default value or an optional placeholder
(`{{?hashicorp:secret:missing#field}}`) applies. Before, without a default value, it failed with a
`NullPointerException`.

=== camel-jetty, camel-servlet - mapHttpMessageBody=false leaves the message body null

With `mapHttpMessageBody=false` on a `jetty` or `servlet` consumer, the HTTP request body is not read and the message
body is `null`, also when the route accesses it. Before, the body was not read up front, but it was still read from
the request when the route first accessed it. As before, no attachments, Java serialized objects or form fields are
mapped.

A route that needs the request body must keep `mapHttpMessageBody=true` (the default). A route that sets no reply
body now replies with an empty body, instead of with the request body.

=== camel-aws2-ddb - aws2-ddbstream reads every shard to its end

The `aws2-ddbstream` consumer now reads a closed shard to its end before it reads its child shards, and uses
`streamIteratorType` only for its start position. Before, records left in a closed shard could be skipped, and after a
shard rotation the consumer started again from `streamIteratorType`: with `FROM_START` it routed records of the stream
again, and with `FROM_LATEST` it skipped records already written to the new shards.

=== camel-bean - a VirtualMachineError thrown by a bean method is no longer given to the error handler

A bean method that throws a `VirtualMachineError` (such as `OutOfMemoryError`, `StackOverflowError` or
`InternalError`) is now handled as a bean that is a `Processor`: Camel's error handling does not get it (the JVM may no
longer be in a consistent state, so the application should not handle it). It is not set on the exchange, but thrown on
after the callback of the bean was called. Depending on the error handler, the `Error` reaches the caller (and the
exchange may stay inflight), or it is only logged and the route continues as if the bean method had succeeded.

Before, the bean component set any `Error` of a bean method on the exchange, so `onException`, `doCatch`, redelivery
and a dead letter channel also handled an `OutOfMemoryError` or a `StackOverflowError`. Any other `Error` of a bean
method, such as an `AssertionError`, still fails the exchange and goes to the error handler, as before.

An application with strict reliability needs should not rely on Camel to react to a `VirtualMachineError`. For an
`OutOfMemoryError` that the JVM raises, it can let the JVM stop with `-XX:+ExitOnOutOfMemoryError` or
`-XX:+CrashOnOutOfMemoryError`.

=== camel-beanio - BeanIOSplitter decodes files and streams with the charset of the exchange or its encoding

`BeanIOSplitter` read a file body with the default charset of the JVM, and a stream or byte array body with the
charset of the exchange or the default charset of the JVM; its `encoding` option was not used. A file consumed with
`charset=ISO-8859-1`, or a splitter with `setEncoding(StandardCharsets.ISO_8859_1)`, had every non-ASCII character
replaced. The splitter now decodes a file, stream or byte array body with the charset of the exchange (the
`CamelCharsetName` header or exchange property, as set by the `charset` option of the file consumer), else with its
`encoding` (default: the default charset of the JVM). A `String` or `Reader` body is read as before, and other text
(such as a `StringBuilder`, which failed before) is read as text.

=== camel-beanio - BeanIOSplitter keeps the records after a record that the error handler handled

When a custom `BeanIOErrorHandler` calls `handleErrorAndAddAsResult` for an invalid record, the BeanIO reader goes on
and returns the next record in the same read. `BeanIOSplitter` replaced that next record with the result of the error
handler, and of two records handled in a row it kept only the last result. Those records were not split, and nothing
was logged. The splitter now returns the results of the error handler in their order, followed by the record that the
reader returned, as the `beanio` data format does when it unmarshals into a list. A route that splits data with handled
invalid records now gets every valid record.

The splitter also sets its error handler before it reads the first record. Before, an invalid, unexpected or
unidentified first record failed the split even with `ignoreInvalidRecords`, `ignoreUnexpectedRecords`,
`ignoreUnidentifiedRecords` or a custom error handler; now the first record is handled as the others are.

=== camel-bindy - a number field without precision keeps all its decimals when marshalled

A `BigDecimal`, `Double` or `Float` field whose `@DataField` (or `@KeyValuePairField`) has no `precision` and no
`pattern` was marshalled with at most three decimals, rounded: `1.23456` was written as `1.235` and `0.000123` as `0`
(since Camel 4.22, CAMEL-24168; up to Camel 4.21 the default `precision = 0` wrote these fields without any decimals).
It is now written with all its decimals, so a record that is unmarshalled and marshalled again is the same text. A
`Double` that is the result of a calculation is written with all its digits too (`0.1 + 0.2` as `0.30000000000000004`,
before `0.3`). Set `precision` to get a fixed number of decimals, as before.

=== camel-cxfrs - an unchanged request body as reply, and a List body of the HTTP API producer

With the `Default` binding style, a route that does not set a reply body replied with the request
`MessageContentsList`, which failed with HTTP 500 "No message body writer has been found for class
MessageContentsList" (or was written as a one element array by a JSON provider that writes any object). Now a
`MessageContentsList` reply of one element is the response entity, and an empty one replies with no content (204).
When the resource method returns a collection, the `MessageContentsList` is still written as that collection. A
`MessageContentsList` of several parameters (a resource method with more than one parameter) still fails with HTTP 500:
set the reply body in the route.

The producer with the HTTP centric client API (`httpClientAPI=true`) sent only the first element of a `List` body. Now
a `List` body is sent as a whole as the request entity, also a `List` of one element. Of a `MessageContentsList` (such
as the body from a `cxfrs` consumer with the `Default` binding style) the first element is still sent. To send one
element as before, set that element as the body.

=== camel-stax - the text mode of xtokenize gives the text as an XML parser reads it

With mode `t`, `xtokenize` returned the characters between the tags of the element as they are written: entity and
character references were not decoded (`AT&amp;T` instead of `AT&T`), and the content of a CDATA section was left
out. The text is now decoded and includes the content of CDATA sections, so a route that decoded the entities itself
must stop doing so. With a `group` greater than 1, the texts in the `<group>` element are escaped as XML text, so the
group is still well-formed XML.

=== camel-smooks - allowExternalEntities applies to Smooks Source bodies and to a reader declared without a class

The Smooks component and `SmooksProcessor` now parse a message body that is already a Smooks `Source`
(`org.smooks.io.source.StringSource`, `ReaderSource`, `StreamSource`, `ByteSource` or `URLSource`) with the same XML
reader settings as the other message bodies, so external entities in it are only resolved when `allowExternalEntities`
is `true`. Before, such a body was handed to Smooks as it was. The bytes of a `StreamSource`, `ByteSource` or
`URLSource` are decoded with the exchange charset, or UTF-8, as before. These bodies are now parsed into a DOM before
Smooks processes them, which uses more memory for very large inputs. `DOMSource` and `JavaSource` bodies are unchanged.

A Smooks configuration whose `<reader>` declares no `class` only sets handlers, features or parameters of the default
XML reader. The Smooks component, `SmooksProcessor` and the Smooks data format now apply `allowExternalEntities` to
such a configuration as they do to a configuration without a `<reader>`, so the handlers and features of that reader no
longer take part in parsing the XML unless `allowExternalEntities` is `true`. Configurations that declare a reader
class, such as EDI, CSV, JSON, DFDL or a custom reader, are unchanged.

A document with a document type declaration (`<!DOCTYPE ...>`) is now processed with these settings for every body
type, `String`, `byte[]` and `InputStream` bodies included, which failed before: the entities of its internal subset are
expanded, and the declaration itself is not passed on to Smooks.

Set `allowExternalEntities=true` on the endpoint or on the data format for documents that rely on external entities or
an external DTD.

=== camel-smooks - a failure of a bean route with a correlation id fails the Smooks processing

A `<camel:route>` of the Smooks configuration with a `correlationIdName` and `correlationIdPattern` ignored a failure of
the endpoint it routes the bean to. The failure now fails the Smooks processing with a `SmooksException`, as it already
did for a bean route without a correlation id.

=== camel-platform-http-main - management server settings on a shared port

When the HTTP server (`camel.server.enabled=true`) and the HTTP management server (`camel.management.enabled=true`)
have the same port (both default to 8080), the HTTP server serves the management endpoints. It cannot apply these
options of the management server, which were ignored without notice:

* `camel.management.host`, when set to another host than the one of the HTTP server
* `camel.management.path`, when set to another path than the one of the HTTP server
* `camel.management.useGlobalSslContextParameters=true`, when the HTTP server does not use the global SSL configuration
* `camel.management.authenticationEnabled=true`, with the basic or JWT authentication options

Camel now fails to start when the management server shares the port and one of them is configured, with a message
that lists them. Set `camel.management.port` to another port, so that the management server runs a server of its own
and applies them. To keep a single port, remove them and configure the HTTP server instead: for example,
authentication configured on the HTTP server for `/*` also applies to the management endpoints.

=== camel-ibm-cos - the producer takes the bucket from the CamelIBMCOSOverrideBucketName header (Breaking change)

The producer used the bucket named in the `CamelIBMCOSBucketName` header instead of the bucket of its endpoint. The
consumer sets that header to the bucket an object was consumed from, so a route from one bucket to another
(`from("ibm-cos:source...").to("ibm-cos:target...")`) stored each object back in the source bucket, and with the
default `deleteAfterRead=true` the consumer then deleted it there. The producer now ignores `CamelIBMCOSBucketName`
and uses the bucket of the endpoint, or the bucket named in the new `CamelIBMCOSOverrideBucketName` header
(`IBMCOSConstants.OVERRIDE_BUCKET_NAME`), as camel-aws2-s3, camel-minio and camel-google-storage do since 4.10. A route
that sets `CamelIBMCOSBucketName` to choose the bucket of a producer operation (for example `createBucket` or
`deleteBucket`) must set `CamelIBMCOSOverrideBucketName` instead.

=== camel-xslt-saxon - external resource access is restricted for a Source message body

`XsltSaxonAggregationStrategy` now configures its Saxon `TransformerFactory` the same way the `xslt-saxon`
endpoint configures the factory it creates: `FEATURE_SECURE_PROCESSING` is enabled and `ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD`
and `ACCESS_EXTERNAL_STYLESHEET` are set to an empty string. Previously the strategy used the Saxon defaults,
so the same stylesheet ran with different settings depending on whether it was used from the endpoint or from
the strategy. On Saxon PE/EE, `FEATURE_SECURE_PROCESSING` also disables reflexive Java extension functions in the
stylesheet.

In addition, both the strategy and the `xslt-saxon` endpoint now parse a message body that is already a
`javax.xml.transform.Source` still to be parsed, for example a `StreamSource` or the body after a
`convertBodyTo(Source.class)`, with a reader that does not resolve references to external resources declared
in the document. A `DOMSource`, a `StAXSource`, or a `Source` that already carries its own reader is left
unchanged, and a declaration internal to the document is still applied. The other body types (`String`,
`byte[]`, `InputStream`) are unaffected, as they already reach the transformer through Camel's StAX/SAX
conversion.

On the `xslt-saxon` endpoint, `secureProcessing` now controls both: setting `secureProcessing=false` (for example so
a Saxon PE/EE stylesheet can call Java extension functions) also restores the previous, unrestricted parsing of a
`Source` body. The option is marked as a security knob (`security=insecure:dev`, `insecureValue=false`), so
`camel.main.profile=prod` reports it when it is turned off. `XsltSaxonAggregationStrategy` has no switch for the
`Source` parsing: `setTransformerFactory(...)` replaces the factory, but the strategy's `XsltSaxonBuilder` keeps
`secureProcessing=true`, so the body is still parsed with the restricted reader.

=== camel-ical - each unmarshal parses with its own calendar builder

The `ical` data format used one ical4j `CalendarBuilder` for all exchanges. Two exchanges that were unmarshalled at
the same time could get the events of each other, or the calendar of the other exchange. Each unmarshal now uses its
own builder.

A builder also has its own time zone registry, so a `VTIMEZONE` of one calendar is no longer used for the calendars
unmarshalled after it. A date with a `TZID` that is not a standard time zone and that its calendar does not define is
now read as a local date-time without a zone (`getDate()` returns a `LocalDateTime`); before, it was read with the
definition of an earlier message, if there was one. A calendar that defines a `TZID` an earlier message has defined
differently is now read with its own definition.

=== camel-flatpack - marshal writes the first row of the list

With the default `ignoreFirstRecord=true`, marshalling a `List<Map<String, Object>>` skipped the first map, so the
first data row was missing from the output, for delimited and fixed-length files alike (the header line of a delimited
file is written from the column names, not from the first map). Every map of the list is now written, and
`ignoreFirstRecord` only applies to unmarshal. A route that set `ignoreFirstRecord=false` to work around this is not
affected; a route that added a dummy first map now gets it in the output too.

=== camel-crypto - verify fails on a message without body, and the key header always wins

`crypto:verify` now fails with a `SignatureException` when the message has no body. Before, a null body was verified
as an empty payload, so a signature of an empty payload passed. `crypto:sign` still signs a null body as an empty
payload.

The `CamelSignaturePublicKeyOrCert` header now always wins over the `publicKey`, `certificate` or `keystore` of the
`crypto:verify` endpoint. Before, a `PublicKey` in the header was ignored when the endpoint had a certificate (or a
keystore with a certificate for the alias); a `Certificate` in the header already won.

A stream body is now cached by `crypto:sign` and `crypto:verify`, so the next step of the route gets the body also
when stream caching is turned off. Before, the stream was read and closed, and the next step got an empty body.

=== camel-csv - the maps of a list are written in the columns of their keys

When no `header` is set (or `headerDisabled=true`), marshalling a `List<Map<String, Object>>` wrote the values of
each map in the iteration order of that map, so a row whose map had its keys in another order, or had no entry for
one of the keys, put its values in the wrong columns. Within one message body, each key now keeps its column: the
columns are the keys of the first map, followed by the keys of later maps that the first one does not have, in the
order they first appear (the rows written before such a key appears have fewer fields). A map without one of these
keys gets a null value in its column (an empty field, or the `nullString` when it is set), where before the following
values moved one column to the left. Each message body starts again with no columns, as before. Set `header` (with
`headerDisabled=false`) to get a fixed set and order of columns.

This also changes the output of a body that mixes record types on purpose, such as a header row
`{type=H, date=d}`, detail rows `{type=D, sku=s, qty=q}` and a trailer row `{type=T, count=n}` (common in flat-file
exchanges): it was written as `H,d`, `D,s,q` and `T,n`, and is now written as `H,d`, `D,,s,q` and `T,,,,n`. To keep
the previous output, convert the rows to `List` before marshalling, or set a custom `marshallerFactory`
(`marshallerFactoryRef` in XML and YAML) whose `CsvMarshaller` returns the values of each map. Every row
now has all the columns seen so far in the body, so rows that each have keys of their own (sparse or pivoted data)
give about N²/2 fields for N rows, where the output was linear in the input before.

=== camel-cxf - message bodies of the POJO producer and of CXF_MESSAGE, and replies that do not convert

The `cxf` producer in `POJO` mode split a body that is not a `List` or an `Object[]` into several parameters, the way
the Split EIP does: a `String` with a comma (`"Hello,World"`) became two parameters, a `byte[]` one parameter per
byte. Now a `List` (or another `Collection`) or an `Object[]` holds the parameters, and any other body, such as a
`String`, a `byte[]` or a `Map`, is the one parameter. A route that relied on a comma-separated `String` (or another
iterable value) to give several parameters must now set a `List` of the parameters as the body.

In `CXF_MESSAGE` mode, the producer and the consumer reply sent a body that is not a `SOAPMessage` or a
`javax.xml.transform.Source` as an empty SOAP envelope. Such a body (for example the envelope as a `String`, `byte[]`,
`InputStream` or DOM `Document`) is now converted to a `DOMSource` and sent; a body that cannot be converted fails the
exchange.

A consumer reply in `PAYLOAD` mode that does not convert to a `CxfPayload`, or in `RAW` mode that does not convert to
an `InputStream`, was sent as an empty `soap:Body` or an empty response. It is now a SOAP fault. A `null` reply is
unchanged.

=== camel-github2 - the commit consumer routes the commits beyond the newest 100

The `commit` consumer read only the 100 newest commits of the branch at each poll, so when more than 100 commits were
pushed between two polls, the older ones were never routed. A poll now reads the commits up to the last routed one
(at most 1000, the number of commits the consumer remembers), and can route more than 100 commits. When the last
routed commit is no longer on the branch, only the 100 newest commits are considered, as before. GitHub lists the
commits by committer date, newest first, so this covers a linear history: the commits that a merge brings in keep
their older dates and can be listed after the last routed commit, and they are not routed, as before.

Each routed commit costs one GitHub API request for its author, plus one for each author or committer not looked up
before, so a poll that catches up on many commits can send about 1000 requests instead of about 100. When the rate
limit is reached, the GitHub client waits inside the poll until the limit resets (its default rate limit handler),
and stopping the route waits for that poll.

=== camel-git - CamelGitCommitId is the sha of the commit

*Breaking change:* the `CamelGitCommitId` header set by the commit consumer (`git:...?type=commit`) is now the sha of
the commit as a `String`, as the header is documented. Before, it was the JGit `RevCommit` object, whose `String` form
is not the sha, so the header could not be given to the `cherryPick` operation. A route that read the header as a
`RevCommit` or an `ObjectId` must now read it as a `String` (`ObjectId.fromString` gives the `ObjectId`).

The `commit` and `commitAll` operations now set the `CamelGitCommitId` header to the sha of the new commit. The body is
unchanged. The header replaces a `CamelGitCommitId` already on the message, such as the one set by the commit consumer,
or one set for a later `cherryPick`.

The `checkout` operation without a `tagName` now checks out a branch that already exists. Before, it always created the
branch, and failed with `RefAlreadyExistsException` when the branch existed. With a `tagName`, it still creates the
branch from the tag.

=== camel-milo - the OPC UA client validates the server certificate

When the endpoint it selects uses a security policy other than `None`, the `milo-client` and `milo-browse` components
now validate the certificate of the OPC UA server. Before, any server certificate was accepted, and no option allowed
to validate it.

Configure the trusted certificates with the new `trustListDirectory` option: put the certificate of the server, or of
the CA that issued it, in the `trusted/certs` directory of the trust list. A server certificate that does not chain up
to a trusted certificate is written to `rejected/certs`, from where it can be moved to `trusted/certs` once it has been
checked. The trust list also refuses a server certificate outside its validity period, revoked by a revocation list of
the trust list, or whose application URI differs from the one of the server. A server certificate issued by a CA is
refused with `Bad_CertificateRevocationUnknown` when the trust list holds no revocation list of that CA: add the CRL of
the CA (an empty one is enough) to `trusted/crl` or `issuer/crl`. A custom validator can be configured with the new
`certificateValidator` option instead.

With neither option, the client does not connect to such an endpoint. It logs a `WARN` message
`Failed to connect: The certificate of the OPC UA server cannot be validated`, followed by the endpoint and its
security policy, and tries again after `reconnectTimeout` (10 seconds by default). A producer fails with
`IllegalStateException: No connected`, caused by the same message. A server certificate refused by the trust list or by
a `certificateValidator` is logged at `WARN` as `Failed to connect, because a certificate or security check failed`. To
restore the previous behaviour, set the new `trustAllCertificates` option to `true`. This is insecure: the client then
does not authenticate the server.

The certificate that the user name and password are encrypted with is checked by the same validator. Endpoints that
use the security policy `None` are not affected.

=== camel-ssh - known_hosts entries are matched as in OpenSSH

When `knownHostsResource` is set, the entries of the `known_hosts` file are now matched as OpenSSH
matches them, against the host name or address the client connects to, written `host` for port 22 and
`[host]:port` for another port:

* A host pattern must match that name as a whole. An entry for another host whose name or address
contains the configured one is no longer used.
* A pattern such as `db.example.com` only applies to port 22. A server on another port needs an entry
such as `[db.example.com]:2222`, or a pattern that matches that form, such as `*`.
* The host is used as it is written: the host configured on the endpoint, or the `HostName` that
`~/.ssh/config` gives for it. An endpoint configured with a host name no longer uses entries listed
only under its address, an endpoint configured with an address no longer uses entries listed under the
name that a reverse lookup of the address returns, and an IPv6 address such as `::1` is matched as
written instead of in its expanded form.
* Every entry of the host is considered, so a host can be listed with several keys. Wildcard, negated
and hashed patterns, comments and `@revoked` entries are supported. `@cert-authority` entries are
ignored.
* A `known_hosts` resource that cannot be read, or that has a line that cannot be read (for example an
unknown `@` marker, a missing key, or a key that is not valid base64), now makes every connection fail,
whatever the value of `failOnUnknownHost`. Previously such a resource was treated as having no entry,
and such a line was skipped.

As before, a server whose host has entries without a marker is rejected when it presents a key that
none of them lists, whatever the value of `failOnUnknownHost`, which only applies to a host without
such an entry: `@revoked` and `@cert-authority` entries do not list a host.
Check that the `known_hosts` file has an entry for the host name or address, and the port, that each
endpoint is configured with.

=== camel-zipfile - ZipAggregationStrategy keeps entries with the same name

`ZipAggregationStrategy` replaced an entry of the zip file when a later message had the same entry name, for example
two files `orders.csv` from different folders of a recursive file consumer (the folders are not kept by default), so
the content of the earlier file was lost. The later entry is now added with a number before its extension:
`orders (1).csv`, `orders (2).csv`, and so on. Entry names can therefore differ from the file names (or the
`CamelFileName` header). Use `preserveFolderStructure=true` to keep the folders in the entry names instead.

=== camel-zip-deflater - gzipDeflater and zipDeflater limit the decompressed size

The `gzipDeflater` and `zipDeflater` data formats have the `maxDecompressedSize` option of the `zipFile` and
`tarFile` data formats, with the same default of 1073741824 bytes (1 GiB). An unmarshal of a message that decompresses
to more than that now fails with an `IOException` ("The InputStream entry being copied exceeds the maximum allowed
size"). Routes that unmarshal larger messages set a higher limit, or `-1` for no limit, for example
`<gzipDeflater maxDecompressedSize="-1"/>` or `unmarshal(dataFormat().zipDeflater().maxDecompressedSize(-1).end())`.
The limit counts the whole decompressed message. For example, when a `.tar.gz` file is unmarshalled with
`gzipDeflater` and then split into its entries, the limit applies to the whole tar archive, not to each entry as the
limit of `tarFile` does.

=== camel-zookeeper - the producer waits for every write and fails on a body it cannot store

The `zookeeper` producer now waits for ZooKeeper to confirm a write or a delete of an InOnly exchange too, and fails the
exchange when it failed. Before, an InOnly exchange was sent without waiting, and a failure, such as a _znode_ that
does not exist without `create=true`, a stale `CamelZooKeeperVersion` header, or a delete of a missing _znode_, was
only logged. An InOnly exchange still keeps its body.

A body that cannot be converted to bytes, such as a `Map`, a POJO or a number, now fails the exchange with an
`InvalidPayloadException`. Before, the _znode_ was overwritten with no data. A null body still stores no data.

A `DELETE` of a _znode_ that does not exist no longer creates it when `create=true` is set; it fails the exchange with
a `NoNodeException`, as it did without `create=true`.

With `listChildren=true`, a failed write or delete of an InOut exchange now fails the exchange, instead of being
replaced by the listing of the child _znodes_.

=== camel-zookeeper-master - MasterRoutePolicy and route removal, and the consumer of a slave

A `MasterRoutePolicy` stayed in the ZooKeeper group until the CamelContext stopped, also after its route was removed
(with `removeRoute`, or by a route reload), so no other node became the master and the route ran nowhere in the
cluster. The policy now leaves the group when its route is removed, so another node takes over, and joins it again when
the route is added again. So a route reloaded on the master can now move to another node.

On a node that is not the master, the policy now stops the consumer of its route again right after the route was
started or resumed (from JMX, the controlbus or a route policy), and starts it when the node becomes the master. Before,
the consumer of such a route kept running next to the one of the master. This includes a route left at
`autoStartup=true`: it now consumes only once its node is the master, also when the CamelContext starts, so it does not
consume while the node cannot reach ZooKeeper. Setting the route to `autoStartup=false` is still recommended, so the
consumer does not run for a moment when the CamelContext starts.

=== camel-zookeeper-master - the zookeeper.password system property, and a group factory of the application

The `zookeeper.password` system property is now used when no `zooKeeperPassword` is configured: the ZooKeeper client
that the component creates then sends the `digest` authorization `fabric:<password>`. Before, the property was read but
not used, so a deployment that set it connected without that authorization.

A `ManagedGroupFactory` bound in the registry, or set with `setManagedGroupFactory`, is no longer closed when a
`zookeeper-master` consumer, a `MasterRoutePolicy` or the component stops. The application that created it owns its
lifecycle, as for a `CuratorFramework` bound in the registry. A group factory and a client that Camel created are
still closed on stop, and created again on the next start.

=== camel-milo - each client connection has thread pools of its own

A `milo-client` or `milo-browse` connection now handles the outcome of its write, method call, read and browse
requests on a thread pool of its own named `MiloClient`, and runs its reconnects on a single thread named
`MiloClientReconnect`. Before, it used the shared scheduled executor of Milo (`milo-shared-scheduled-executor`) for
both. A route that continues after an awaited request now runs on a thread of the `MiloClient` pool. The pool has up
to as many threads as there are processors, which end after they have been idle for the keep-alive time of the
default thread pool profile (60 seconds). With virtual threads enabled and more than one processor, it starts a
virtual thread for each task instead. The `MiloClientReconnect` thread starts with the first reconnect of the
connection, and its reconnects run one after the other. The threads of a connection end when it is closed.

A `MiloClientConnection` created outside an endpoint needs a `CamelContext`, set with `setCamelContext`, before it is
used.

=== camel-mllp - the producer converts a body that is not a String or a byte array

The mllp producer now converts a body that is not a `String` or a `byte[]`, such as an `InputStream` or a file, to a
`byte[]` with the type converter, and encodes a `CharSequence` such as a `StringBuilder` in the `charsetName`
charset. A body that cannot be converted, such as a `Map`, fails the exchange with a `MllpInvalidMessageException`.
Before, such a body was sent as an empty MLLP message: with `InOut` the exchange then failed with a
`MllpAcknowledgementTimeoutException` after the receive timeout, and with `InOnly` it completed without an error.

After sending a `String` body, the `CamelCharsetName` exchange property is now the charset the message was sent in.
Before, it was set to `charsetName` even when the charset of the exchange was used to encode the message.

=== camel-lra - only an LRA hosted by the configured coordinator is joined

The LRA saga service now joins, completes or compensates an LRA only when its URL is under the configured LRA
coordinator (`coordinatorUrl` followed by `coordinatorContextPath`). A message whose `Long-Running-Action` header holds
the URL of another coordinator now fails, instead of the saga service calling that URL.

If your participants reach the same coordinator under different URLs, for example with different host names, list the
other coordinator URLs, including the context path, in the new `allowedCoordinatorUrls` option
(`camel.lra.allowedCoordinatorUrls`).

This also applies to the LRAs the service starts itself: when the coordinator returns LRA URLs under another base URL than `coordinatorUrl`, for example behind a proxy, starting a saga fails until that base URL is added to `allowedCoordinatorUrls`.

=== camel-lra - warning for unsigned participant callbacks, and reserved saga option names

The LRA saga service now logs a warning at startup when no `callbackSecret` is configured: the callbacks that the LRA
coordinator sends to the participant endpoints are then not verified, so these endpoints must only be reachable by the
coordinator. The Saga EIP documentation describes how to sign them.

A saga option named like a parameter of the participant callback URLs, in any case (`Camel-Saga-Compensate`,
`Camel-Saga-Complete`, `Camel-Saga-Signature` or `Camel-Saga-Lra`), now fails the creation of its route, with or
without a callback secret. The value of such an option could not be read back from the callback before.

=== camel-openai, camel-ai-tool - AI events now use CamelEvent.Type.Ai

The events fired by the AI components now share a common contract, `org.apache.camel.ai.AiEvent` (a
`CamelEvent` of the new `CamelEvent.Type.Ai`), instead of each being a component-specific
`CamelEvent.Type.Custom` event. Events that relate to a single exchange implement the exchange-backed subtype
`AiEvent.ExchangeAiEvent` (the root `AiEvent` itself is not an `ExchangeEvent`). This lets an `EventNotifier` recognise AI activity — and route it to metrics or
tracing — by type and by the `AiEvent` sub-interfaces, without depending on an AI component.

The events shipped in 4.23.0 are affected:

* `camel-ai-tool`: `AiToolAuthorizationDeniedEvent` now implements `AiEvent.ToolCallDeniedEvent`;
* `camel-openai`: `OpenAIAgenticLoopStartedEvent`, `OpenAIAgenticToolCallExecutedEvent` and
  `OpenAIAgenticLoopCompletedEvent` now implement `AiEvent.AgentLoopStartedEvent`, `AiEvent.ToolCallExecutedEvent`
  and `AiEvent.AgentLoopCompletedEvent`.
