Their `getType()` now returns `CamelEvent.Type.Ai` rather than `CamelEvent.Type.Custom`, and they are now
`CamelEvent.ExchangeEvent`s (via `AiEvent.ExchangeAiEvent`). An `EventNotifier` that recognised these events by
`event.getType() == CamelEvent.Type.Custom` must match on `CamelEvent.Type.Ai` instead, or — preferably —
on `event instanceof org.apache.camel.ai.AiEvent` (or one of its sub-interfaces), which no longer requires a
dependency on the AI component. The concrete event classes and all their accessors are unchanged, so code that
references them directly is not affected.

Because the shipped AI events are now `ExchangeEvent`s (via `AiEvent.ExchangeAiEvent`), a notifier that filters on
`instanceof ExchangeEvent` now also receives them. AI events are their own category: a new `ignoreAiEvents` flag
(`EventNotifier.setIgnoreAiEvents(boolean)`, default `false`) opts a notifier out, and `ignoreExchangeEvents` does
**not** drop AI events (so an AI-observability notifier can keep `ignoreExchangeEvents=true` for performance). Camel's
own exchange-oriented notifiers (the debugger, the dev-console event ring, the micrometer and OpenTelemetry metrics
exchange notifiers) set
`ignoreAiEvents=true` so AI events do not reach them; a custom exchange-focused notifier that should not see AI events
should do the same.

=== camel-oauth

`OAuthBearerTokenProcessor` no longer creates or updates the OAuth session, and no longer sets the
`camel.oauth.session` cookie nor the `CamelOAuthSessionId` header: a bearer token authenticates the request that
carries it, and nothing else. The authenticated `UserProfile` is available in the `CamelOAuthUserProfile` exchange
property (`OAuth.CAMEL_OAUTH_USER_PROFILE`). A route that read the profile from the OAuth session after
`OAuthBearerTokenProcessor` must read it from that exchange property instead.

When the message comes from platform-http (on Vert.x or on Spring Boot), from a servlet based HTTP consumer
(camel-servlet, camel-jetty) or from camel-netty-http, the bearer token and the session cookie are now read from the
`Authorization` and `Cookie` headers of the HTTP request only. A query or form parameter of the same name, which these
consumers also map into the message headers, is no longer used for them, and neither is an `Authorization` or `Cookie`
message header that the route sets itself. For example
`+.setHeader("Authorization", simple("Bearer ${header.X-Forwarded-Access-Token}"))+` before
`OAuthBearerTokenProcessor` now results in a 401 response. A route that takes the token from elsewhere can validate it
with `OAuthHelper.validateOAuthToken` from camel-support instead.

For any other message, such as one from camel-undertow, the processors keep reading the message headers, but leave out
a value that also appears in the query string of the request.

`InMemorySessionStore` no longer logs session identifiers. Additionally, `OAuthCodeFlowCallback` rotates the session
identifier upon successful authentication, replacing the pre-login session identifier with a new session identifier
and emitting an updated session cookie. The default `rotateSession` method of `OAuthSessionStore` keeps the session
unchanged, so a custom `OAuthSessionStore` must override it to rotate the session identifier on login.

The Jakarta servlet backend (`ServletOAuth`) now validates the `aud` (audience) claim of access tokens evaluated by
`UserProfile` and `OAuthBearerTokenProcessor`. Tokens are validated against configured expected audiences on
`JWTOptions` (via `config.getJWTOptions().addAudience(String)` or `config.getJWTOptions().setAudience(List<String>)`),
falling back to the configured `camel.oauth.client-id` when no custom audience is configured. Tokens whose audience does
not match or tokens without an audience claim are rejected with `OAuthException`.

For OpenID Connect / Keycloak login flows, Keycloak access tokens commonly carry `aud=account` by default unless an
audience protocol mapper is configured on the client. Applications upgrading that use Keycloak for authorization code
login flows should configure an audience mapper on the client in Keycloak or configure the expected audience on `JWTOptions`
(for example, `config.getJWTOptions().addAudience("account")`).

=== camel-platform-http-vertx - the charset parameter of the request Content-Type in any case

The consumer now reads the charset parameter of the request `Content-Type` whatever the case of its name, as
camel-undertow and camel-netty-http do: a body sent with `Content-Type: text/plain; Charset=ISO-8859-1` is decoded as
ISO-8859-1 and `CamelCharsetName` is set. Before, only `charset=` in lower case was read and such a body was decoded
as UTF-8. An empty charset parameter (`charset=` or `charset=""`) now counts as no charset, as in camel-undertow,
camel-netty-http and camel-http-common (CAMEL-25355); before, converting the body to a String failed with 500.

=== camel-platform-http-vertx - a number, a boolean, a character or an enum reply is written as text

A reply body that is a number, a boolean, a character or an enum is now written as its text, in the charset of the
response `Content-Type` (UTF-8 otherwise), as camel-undertow does: `setBody(constant(42))` replies with `42`. Before,
an `Integer`, `Long`, `Short`, `Float` or `Double` was written in its binary form (`42` became the 4 bytes
`00 00 00 2A`), and a boolean, a character or an enum failed with 500. A `StringBuilder` or another `CharSequence` is
now also written in the charset of the response `Content-Type`, as a `String` is, rather than in the charset of the
exchange. To send the binary form of a number, convert the body to `byte[]` first.

=== camel-platform-http-vertx - the HTTP server fails to start when it cannot listen on its port

The Vert.x HTTP server of platform-http now fails to start when it cannot listen on its port, for example because
another process already uses the port, and so Camel fails to start, with the bind error as the cause. This also applies
to the HTTP server and the HTTP management server of camel-platform-http-main (`camel.server.*` and
`camel.management.*`), which `camel run` uses: a second `camel run` with the same `--port` now fails to start. Before,
the failure was only logged at WARN, and Camel started, with its platform-http routes reported as started, although
nothing listened on the port.

=== camel-quickfix - an InOut consumer replies only to application messages, and an InOut producer needs CorrelationCriteria

A consumer with `exchangePattern=InOut` now sends a reply only for an `AppMessageReceived` event, with the body the
route ends with (a new FIX message, or the received message changed in place), and a null body sends no reply. Before,
every event was answered with the body the route ended with, so the `AdminMessageSent` event of the Logon sent the Logon
again until a `StackOverflowError`. A route that does not change the body still sends the received message back, so
set a null body for a message that must not be answered. A reply that is not a FIX message (such as a `Map`) is no
longer sent: the error goes to the exception handler of the consumer (logged at WARN by default), and the request
counts as received. Before, it failed inside QuickFIX/J, which then asked the counterparty to resend the request over
and over. No reply is sent when the exchange failed.

A producer with an InOut exchange now fails before it sends the message when the `CorrelationCriteria` exchange property
is not set, with an `IllegalArgumentException` that names the property. Before, the message was sent and the exchange
failed with `ExchangeTimedOutException`, as no reply could be matched. Send with `InOnly` when no reply is expected.

A producer body that does not convert to a FIX message (such as a stream, a `Map` or no body) now fails with an
`InvalidPayloadException` that names the body type, instead of a `NullPointerException`.

=== camel-report-maven-plugin - Java routes are read with the Java DSL parser

The `validate` and `route-coverage` goals read Java routes with the Java DSL parser of camel-java-io instead of
camel-route-parser (Roaster). XML routes are read as before. The parser reads every `configure()` method of a source
file, including those of a route builder created inside a test, so `validate` can find and validate endpoints it did
not see before. An endpoint uri that is only a method call, such as `from(inboxQueue())`, is not validated (it was
validated as a property placeholder without a component before); with `logUnparseable=true` it is logged.

=== camel-jbang - the route-parser plugin is removed

The `route-parser` plugin (`camel route-parser`) and its `camel-jbang-plugin-route-parser` artifact are removed. Use
`camel cmd route-structure MyRoute.java` instead: it prints the structure of the routes of a Java source, read
without compiling it, and of YAML and XML sources too. If you installed the plugin, remove it with
`camel plugin delete route-parser`.

=== camel-jbang-mcp - the camel_component_properties tool is removed

The `camel_component_properties` MCP tool is removed. It listed the endpoint options of a component as
`camel.component.<scheme>.<option>` keys, which `camel run` and `camel_configuration_validate` reject: only the
component options can be set that way. Use `camel_catalog_doc` instead: each component option in its answer carries
its `application.properties` key as `configKey`.

`camel_catalog_doc` without `optionsFilter` now lists the required options and the first 20 other options of a
component in full, and the rest by name in `moreOptions` (`limit` lists more in full), and an answer is cut at
18 000 characters with a marker. An option of both the endpoint and the component is listed once, with `scope`
set to `endpoint,component` and its `configKey`, instead of twice, and `matchedOptions` counts option names. A name
that only matches a word of an EIP title (such as `retry`) no longer returns that EIP: the answer is a not-found with
suggestions.

A comma in the `optionsFilter` of `camel_catalog_doc` now separates several terms, each matched on its own, instead
of being matched as part of the text; a term that matches no option is listed in `unmatchedTerms`. For an EIP, the
options of its elements that match the filter are now listed under `nestedOptions` also when options of the EIP
itself match, not only when none does.

=== camel-wasm - the endpoint runs WASI command modules (Breaking change)

The `wasm` endpoint now runs WASI Preview 1 command modules (the body is standard input, standard output becomes the
body, the exit code and standard error are in `CamelWasmExitCode` and `CamelWasmStderr`) and no longer supports the
custom `alloc`/`dealloc` and JSON message ABI. The URI path is now the module (`wasm:classpath:upper.wasm`); the
function name and the `module` option are removed, and a header reaches the module only when listed in
`environmentHeaders`. To migrate, turn the function into a program that reads standard input and writes standard
output, built for `wasm32-wasip1` (Rust) or with `GOOS=wasip1 GOARCH=wasm` (Go).

The endpoint runs every exchange on a new instance of the module, so a module can no longer keep state from one
message to the next. The 4.23 note that a failed call discards the Wasm instance no longer applies.

The `wasm` language is removed: the `wasm` expression of the Java, XML and YAML DSLs, `WasmExpression` of the model,
and `WasmLanguage`, `WasmFunction`, `WasmSupport`, `Wasm.FN_ALLOC` and `Wasm.FN_DEALLOC` of `camel-wasm`. Call the
module with the `wasm` endpoint as a route step instead, for example `.to("wasm:classpath:transform.wasm")`, built as a
WASI command module as described above, and use its output (the new message body) in the next steps.

The following API of `camel-wasm` is removed or changed with the old ABI:

* `WasmComponent.getModule()` and `setModule(String)`, and `WasmConfiguration.getModule()` and `setModule(String)`:
the module is the path of the endpoint URI, available as `WasmEndpoint.getModule()`.
* The `functionName` path parameter of the endpoint: the third argument of the `WasmEndpoint` constructor is now the
module.
* `WasmSupport.deserialize(byte[], Exchange)`.
* The constructor `WasmProducer(Endpoint, String, String)` is replaced by `WasmProducer(WasmEndpoint)`.

`camel-wasm` has a new dependency, `run.endive:compiler`, which brings in ASM (`org.ow2.asm`). It is used by the
new `compile` option of the endpoint (default `false`). The module is now loaded once per endpoint and no longer by
every producer, so stopping and starting a route does not read the module again: remove the route and add it
again, or restart the application, to load a module that has changed.

=== camel-google-text-to-speech - listVoices lists the voices of all languages unless languageCode is set

The `listVoices` operation of `google-text-to-speech` now lists the voices of all languages when the `languageCode`
option is not set. Before, it always listed only the voices of `en-US`, the default value of the option, which is meant
for `synthesize`. To keep listing the voices of one language only, set the option on the endpoint, for example
`google-text-to-speech:listVoices?languageCode=en-US`.

With `pojoRequest=true`, the producer now runs the operation of the endpoint, or of the
`CamelGoogleCloudTextToSpeechOperation` header: `listVoices` takes a `ListVoicesRequest` as body and gives the `List` of
the voices. Before, it always called `synthesize` with a `SynthesizeSpeechRequest` body, whatever the operation. A
`listVoices` endpoint with `pojoRequest=true` now fails with an `InvalidPayloadException` for a body that is not a
`ListVoicesRequest`. The path of the endpoint must therefore name the operation, `synthesize` or `listVoices`, unless
the header does: an endpoint such as `google-text-to-speech:speak?pojoRequest=true`, which synthesized before, now fails
with an `IllegalArgumentException`. A `CamelGoogleCloudTextToSpeechOperation` header left on the message by an earlier
step now applies with `pojoRequest=true` too.

=== camel-web3j - the consumer subscribes again after an error

When the subscription of a consumer ends with an error (for example a request to the node that fails inside
`BLOCK_OBSERVABLE` or `TRANSACTION_OBSERVABLE`, or a node that cannot be reached when the route starts), the consumer
now subscribes again every 15 seconds until it succeeds. The error is routed as before, and logged at `WARN`; the
failed attempts that follow are logged at `DEBUG`. Previously the consumer received nothing more while the route
stayed started. A new subscription starts at the latest block, so the blocks between the error and the new
subscription are not routed. `REPLAY_BLOCKS_OBSERVABLE`, `REPLAY_TRANSACTIONS_OBSERVABLE` and an
`ETH_LOG_OBSERVABLE` with `fromBlock` set to a block number are not subscribed again, as that would route the same
blocks or logs again.

=== camel-web3j - ETH_GET_TRANSACTION_RECEIPT gives the TransactionReceipt, and the block options default to latest

The `ETH_GET_TRANSACTION_RECEIPT` operation now sets the `TransactionReceipt` as the body, or `null` when the node has no
receipt for the transaction, like the `ETH_GET_TRANSACTION_BY_*` operations do with the `Transaction`. Before, the body
was an `Optional<TransactionReceipt>`: a route that unwraps the `Optional` must now use the body as it is.

The `atBlock`, `fromBlock` and `toBlock` options now default to `latest`, as documented. Before, they were not set, and
the operations that take a block, such as `ETH_GET_BALANCE` and `ETH_GET_BLOCK_BY_NUMBER`, failed with a
`NullPointerException` unless `atBlock` or the `CamelWeb3jAtBlock` header was set, and so did the
`REPLAY_BLOCKS_OBSERVABLE` and `REPLAY_TRANSACTIONS_OBSERVABLE` consumers without `fromBlock` and `toBlock`.

The `operation` option defaults to `transaction`, which is not an operation of the producer nor of the consumer. A
consumer without an observable operation now fails to start with a message that lists them, and accepts the operation
in lower case, as the producer does. A producer without the option and without the `CamelWeb3jOperation` header now
fails the exchange with a message that asks for one.

=== camel-google-vertexai - generateMultimodal sends the body as media, and fails without a MIME type

When the `CamelGoogleVertexAIMediaData` and `CamelGoogleVertexAIMediaGcsUri` headers are not set, the `generateMultimodal`
operation now sends a body that is not the text prompt as media: bytes, an `InputStream`, a `File`, a `Path` or the file of
a file consumer, read with the type converter. The MIME type is the one of the `CamelGoogleVertexAIMediaMimeType` header or, for a
file, the one of its file name extension. Before, only a `byte[]` body with that header was used: a stream, a file, and
bytes without the header were dropped without an error, and the model got the prompt alone.

Media without a MIME type now fails the exchange with an `IllegalArgumentException`, for the body as well as for the
`CamelGoogleVertexAIMediaData` header (which was dropped too). A `String` body is the prompt only when the
`CamelGoogleVertexAIPrompt` header is not set: with that header, it is media as well, and needs the MIME type header. An
empty or blank `String`, and an empty byte array, are not sent: the request then has the prompt only. A route that sets the
`CamelGoogleVertexAIPrompt` header while the body holds something else it does not mean to send must now remove the body.

=== camel-google-vertexai - headers that were declared but not used

The `CamelGoogleVertexAISystemInstruction`, `CamelGoogleVertexAISafetySettings` and `CamelGoogleVertexAIAnthropicVersion`
headers were documented but not read: they are now applied. `CamelGoogleVertexAISafetySettings` takes a `List` of
`com.google.genai.types.SafetySetting`, the type of the client that the generate operations use (the docs named
`com.google.cloud.aiplatform.v1.SafetySetting`), and another type fails the exchange. `CamelGoogleVertexAIAnthropicVersion`
takes the place of the `anthropicVersion` option for a `rawPredict` or `streamRawPredict` request of publisher `anthropic`
that has no `anthropic_version`.

The generate operations now set the `CamelGoogleVertexAISafetyRatings` header, a `List` of
`com.google.genai.types.SafetyRating` (the docs named `com.google.cloud.aiplatform.v1.SafetyRating`), and the
`CamelGoogleVertexAIContentBlocked` header, which is `true` when the safety filters blocked the prompt (there is then no
text).

The `CamelGoogleVertexAIChatMessages` header is deprecated: the producer never read it, and `generateChat` sends the prompt
only, without a conversation history.

=== camel-xmpp - one endpoint per participant and user, and one connection per account

XMPP URIs that differ only in the participant or the user are now separate endpoints. Before, the component could
return an endpoint that was created for another participant or user, and messages went to the wrong participant.

All the consumers and producers of one account (the same host, port, service name, user, password, resource and login
settings) now share one XMPP connection, also across endpoints. So an account has one session on the server instead
of one per endpoint, and the server no longer closes one of them with a `conflict` error. The connection is closed when
the last route that uses the account stops. With the `connectionConfig` option, endpoints share a connection only when
they refer to the same `ConnectionConfiguration` instance (for example the same bean in the registry).

`XmppEndpoint.createConnection()` now creates a new connection on every call, instead of returning the connection of
the endpoint, and an endpoint no longer disconnects a connection when it stops.

=== camel-xmpp - a consumer with a participant routes only the chat messages from that participant

A consumer with a participant (for example `xmpp://superman@jabber.org/joker@jabber.org`) and no `room` now routes only
the chat messages from that participant. Before, it routed the chat messages of every sender to the account, and every
consumer of the account routed each of them. To route the chat messages of every sender, leave out the participant
(`xmpp://superman@jabber.org`), and read the sender from the `CamelXmppFrom` header.

=== camel-google-bigquery - a grouped exchange with a body that is not a row fails as a whole

When one exchange of a grouped exchange (`Exchange.GROUPED_EXCHANGE`) sent to `google-bigquery` has a body that is not a
`Map` or a `List` of `Map` objects, the grouped exchange now fails with an `IllegalArgumentException`, and none of its rows
is inserted. Before, the exception was set on that one exchange of the group, which nothing looked at: its row was dropped
without notice, the other rows were inserted, and the grouped exchange completed as a success. Filter or convert such
bodies before the aggregation.

A `List` body with an element that is not a `Map` now fails the same way, with an `IllegalArgumentException` instead of a
`ClassCastException`. That case, and an exchange of the group with an empty `CamelGoogleBigQueryTableId` header, now fail
the grouped exchange before any row is sent: before, the rows of the tables that came earlier in the group were already
inserted.

=== camel-google-bigquery - the connectionFactory option of the component is used

The `connectionFactory` option of the `google-bigquery` and `google-bigquery-sql` components is now used by the endpoints
that do not set their own. Before, it was ignored, and each endpoint built a default client from its own options, such as
`serviceAccountKey`.

=== camel-pulsar - a consumer that cannot subscribe fails the start of the route

With `subscriptionType` `Shared`, `Failover` or `Key_Shared`, a consumer that could not subscribe when the route
started (for example a broker that could not be reached within the operation timeout of the Pulsar client) was only
logged at `ERROR`, and the route started with fewer consumers than `numberOfConsumers`, or with none, and never
subscribed them again. Now the consumers that did subscribe are closed and the route fails to start with the error of
the Pulsar client, as it already did with `Exclusive`. When the route starts with the CamelContext, the CamelContext
then fails to start, unless the supervising route controller (`camel.routecontroller.enabled=true`) is used, which
starts such a route again. With `messageListener=true` (the default) the consumers that did subscribe may already have
received messages before they are closed, and messages they received but did not acknowledge can be delivered again
once the route has started.

=== camel-google-functions - callFunction fails the exchange when the function fails

The `callFunction` operation of `google-functions` now fails the exchange with a `CamelExchangeException` when the
`CallFunctionResponse` has an error, which Google Cloud Functions sets when the execution of the function was not
successful. Before, the exchange went on as a success, with an empty body. The `CallFunctionResponse` is still in the
`CamelGoogleCloudFunctionsResponseObject` header, so an error handler (`onException`, or `doTry` and `doCatch`) can read
its error and execution id. A route that read the error from that header after the call must handle the exception
instead.

The body of `callFunction` is now marshalled to JSON when it is a `Map`, a `List` or another Java object, instead of
being sent as its `toString()`. Text, bytes, a stream, a file, XML (a DOM `Node` or a `Source`), a `ByteBuffer`, an enum
or a character are still converted to text, as before. The JSON comes from a Jackson mapper with the Jackson 2 defaults (a
`java.time` value is written as numbers), so a POJO that needs custom JSON must be marshalled in the route first. A message
without a body fails with an `InvalidPayloadException` instead of a `NullPointerException`.

=== camel-vertx-http - a header with several values is sent as several headers

A message header whose value is a `java.util.Collection` or an object array (as the HTTP consumers map a request
header that occurs more than once) was sent as one HTTP header with the `toString()` of the value, for example
`[a, b]`. The producer now sends one HTTP header per value. A collection or an object array that is empty, or that
holds only null values, now sends no header (an empty List was sent as the header value `[]`).

Every other value is converted to one String and sent as one header as before: a String (also when it contains
commas), a `byte[]` and the other primitive arrays, a `Map`, and a Vert.x `JsonObject` or `JsonArray` (sent as JSON).
A route that needs the old single header can set the header to a String, for example with `String.valueOf(list)`.

=== camel-google-mail - messages/attachments removed

The `messages/attachments` method is removed. It was the accessor of the attachments of a message and not a request, so every call failed with a `ClassCastException`; a route with a `google-mail:messages/attachments` endpoint now fails when it starts. Read attachments with the `attachments` API, such as `attachments/get`.

=== camel-vertx - standalone TrustManager on SSLContextParameters is applied

When configuring TLS through `SSLContextParameters` with a standalone `TrustManager` instance (via `TrustManagersParameters.setTrustManager(...)`), Vert.x components now wrap and apply the configured `TrustManager`. Previously, only `TrustAllTrustManager.INSTANCE` and KeyStore-backed configurations were applied, and other standalone `TrustManager` instances were not set on the underlying Vert.x options.

When both a `TrustManager` and a keystore are configured on `TrustManagersParameters`, the `TrustManager` is now used,
as with the other components that use `SSLContextParameters`; before, the keystore-based trust options were used.

=== camel-vertx - a stream body is sent as a Buffer, and the consumer replies with a failure when the body has no codec

When a `vertx` consumer receives a message that expects a reply and the route ends with a stream body (such as a
received `Buffer`, which is a stream with stream caching enabled, that the route did not change), the consumer now
replies with its content as a `Buffer`. When the reply body has no codec on the event bus (such as a POJO that is not
`Serializable`), the consumer now logs the error and sends a failure reply (a `ReplyException` with failure type
`RECIPIENT_FAILURE`, failure code 500 and the message `Exchange processing failed`). Before, both sent no reply, and
the sender waited until its reply timeout (30 seconds by default).

The `vertx` producer now sends a stream body as a `Buffer` too, so a route from one `vertx` endpoint to another works
with stream caching enabled. Before, it failed with `No message codec for type`.

=== camel-knative-http - a header with several values is sent as several headers

A message header whose value is a `java.util.Collection` or an object array (as the consumer maps a request header
that occurs more than once) is now sent as one HTTP header per value. The Knative HTTP producer sent it as one HTTP
header with the `toString()` of the value, for example `[a, b]`, and the reply of the consumer sent only its last
value. A collection or an object array that is empty, or that holds only null values, sends no header (the producer
sent an empty List as the header value `[]`).

Every other value is one header. A String is sent as before, also when it contains commas, and the reply still sends
no header for a blank String. A `byte[]` is now converted to a String with the type converter (the producer sent its
`toString()`, such as `[B@1dc9f5ad`, and the reply only its last byte as a number). The producer sends a `Map` and a
Vert.x `JsonObject` or `JsonArray` as before, as their `toString()` (JSON for the Vert.x types). The reply now does
the same: it sent only the last entry of a `Map` or `JsonObject` in the form `key=value`, and only the last element
of a `JsonArray`.

A route that needs the old single header of the producer can set the header to a String, for example with
`String.valueOf(list)`.

=== camel-google-sheets - spreadsheets/developerMetadata, sheets and values removed

The `developerMetadata`, `sheets` and `values` methods of the `spreadsheets` API are removed. They were accessors of sub-resources and not requests, so every call failed with a `ClassCastException`; a route with one of these endpoints now fails when it starts. Read and write the values of a spreadsheet with the `data` API, such as `data/get` and `data/update`.

=== camel-http - a header with several values is sent as several headers

A message header whose value is a `java.util.Collection` or an object array (as the HTTP consumers map a request
header that occurs more than once) was sent as one HTTP header with the values in the form `[a, b]`. The producer now
sends one HTTP header per value, as camel-vertx-http and camel-knative-http do. Null values are skipped, and a
collection with one value is sent as one header with that value, as before.

Every other value is sent as one header. A String is sent as before, also when it contains commas. A `byte[]` or a
`char[]` is now converted to a String with the type converter, for example `abc` (it was sent as `[97, 98, 99]`), and
a `Map` is sent as its `toString()`, such as `{k1=v1, k2=v2}` (it was `[k1=v1, k2=v2]`). The other primitive arrays,
an `Iterator`, a `java.util.stream.Stream` and an `Iterable` that is not a collection are sent in the form `[a, b]` as before. A route that needs
the old single header can set the header to a String, for example with `String.valueOf(list)`.

This also applies to camel-graphql, which sends its requests with camel-http.

=== camel-core - Switch case values and the Dynamic Router uriDelimiter resolve property placeholders

The `value` of a Switch case and the `uriDelimiter` of the Dynamic Router EIP were used as written, so a property
placeholder or a route template parameter in them never matched (Switch) or did not split the slip (Dynamic Router).
They are now resolved at startup like the other EIP options; a placeholder of a property that does not exist now
fails the route at startup.

=== camel-kamelet - the route of a Kamelet endpoint is removed with the endpoint

A Kamelet endpoint creates a route from the Kamelet's template. When the endpoint is created while the CamelContext
runs and is shut down later, its route is now stopped and removed as well; before, it stayed behind and kept running.
This applies to an endpoint that is used once and discarded (`toD`, `recipientList` or `routingSlip` with
`cacheSize=-1`), which kept one more route for every message. It also applies to a route added at runtime (such as a
dev-mode reload or `CamelContext.addRoutes`) that consumes from a Kamelet (`from("kamelet:my-source")`): removing that
route now also removes the route of the Kamelet. A route id given in the uri (`kamelet:my-source/myRoute` or
`routeId=`) is shared by the endpoints that use it, so such a route is not removed. Routes created when the
CamelContext starts are not changed. The removal of the route of a discarded endpoint waits until the thread pools of
the route are idle, as the calling route can continue on them, but at most the timeout of the shutdown strategy; then
it logs a WARN and removes the route anyway. The route of a Kamelet that a removed route consumed from is removed
without that wait.

=== camel-undertow - the producer refuses a request target or header value with control characters

The undertow producer writes the path, query and header values of the outgoing request to the socket one character at
a time, without validating them. A carriage return or line feed therefore broke the request into further lines, and a
character above `0xFF` was written as a single, different byte (for example `U+010A` became a line feed). The producer
now refuses to send a request path, query or header value that contains a control character or a character above
`0xFF`, and fails the exchange with an `IllegalArgumentException` instead. A space and a tab, which do not change the
framing, are still allowed. Routes that put a percent-encoded control character (such as `%0D%0A`) into the
`CamelHttpPath` or `CamelHttpQuery` header, which the producer decodes before building the request, are the ones most
likely to be affected.

=== camel-undertow - a WebSocket connection opened before a consumer with custom handlers started is no longer served

On a WebSocket path whose producer applies a security provider or allowed roles, a connection could be accepted while
only the producer served the path, before its consumer was started for the first time. Such a connection ran the
producer checks but not the custom `handlers` the consumer configures, as those handlers run only for consumers. Once
the consumer was started, the connection was still treated as having passed the consumer checks, so its messages
reached the route and it kept receiving what the producer sent.

Once a consumer that configures `handlers` is started on a path, a connection opened on that path before the consumer
first ran is no longer served: its messages no longer reach the route and it no longer receives what the producer
sends, and the connection is closed. Clients that open a connection in this window must reconnect after the consumer
has started.

=== camel-undertow, camel-vertx-http - CamelHttpPath with a query in the endpoint uri

When the endpoint uri of an undertow or vertx-http producer has a query, the `CamelHttpPath` header now goes before
that query, as in camel-http. Before, camel-undertow dropped the header path, and camel-vertx-http put it into the
query (or dropped it when `CamelHttpQuery` was set). The query is chosen as before: a `CamelHttpQuery` header wins over
the query of the endpoint.

This changes the target of routes that bridge an HTTP consumer to such a producer, as the HTTP consumers (undertow,
platform-http-vertx, servlet) set `CamelHttpPath` from the path of the request. For example the route
`from("undertow:http://0.0.0.0:8080/api?matchOnUriPrefix=true").to("undertow:http://backend/svc?key=1")`, or the same
with a `vertx-http:` producer and `bridgeEndpoint=true`, now sends a request for `/api/orders/123` to
`/svc/orders/123` instead of `/svc`. To keep the old target, remove the header before the producer, for example with
`removeHeader(Exchange.HTTP_PATH)`. The path comes from the client request, and the producer sends it with the query
of the endpoint (`key=1` in the example): a proxy route should validate or restrict the path, or remove the header.

=== camel-undertow - the bodies of requests and responses

The undertow consumer and producer change how they send and read a message body:

* A number, a boolean, a character or an enum is sent as its text: `setBody(constant(42))` sends `42` and no longer
  the 4 bytes of the binary form of the number. A boolean or a character was not sent at all before.
* A Map, a List or an object that does not convert to bytes is no longer sent as an empty body. The consumer answers
  `500` instead of an empty `200`, and logs what the body is and the marshal that writes it. The producer fails the
  exchange with an `InvalidPayloadException` for a POST, PUT or PATCH instead of sending an empty request; for the
  other methods, such as a GET, it sends no body as before. Marshal the body first, for example with `marshal().json()`.
* The consumer reads the body of a request of any method that has one (a Content-Length above 0 or a chunked body),
  such as a DELETE. Before, only the body of a POST, PUT or PATCH was read, and the body was null for the others.
* The producer gives a null body for a `204` or `304` response and for the response to a HEAD request, instead of an
  empty `byte[]`, as camel-http does. A `200` response with an empty body is still an empty `byte[]`.

=== camel-dapr - the saveBulk and executeTransaction state operations are sent

The `saveBulk` and `executeTransaction` operations of `dapr:state` built their request but never sent it to Dapr, so
nothing was stored and the exchange completed without an error. The requests are now sent, and the producer waits for
the answer like the other state operations: the states are stored, and a request that Dapr rejects (or a sidecar that
cannot be reached) fails the exchange.

=== camel-ibm-secrets-manager - a Map or a POJO body is stored as JSON in an arbitrary secret

The `createArbitrarySecret` operation, and the `updateSecret` operation for an arbitrary secret, now store a `Map`, a
`List` or another object (POJO) in the message body as JSON. Before, the payload was the `toString()` of the body, such
as `{username=admin}`. Text, bytes, a stream, a file, XML (a DOM node or a `Source`), a `ByteBuffer`, a `UUID`, a number,
a boolean, a character or an enum is stored as text, as before. A route that relied on the `toString()` form must convert the body to text
before the call.

=== camel-pubnub - the producer uses its configured channel

The pubnub producer no longer reads the `CamelPubNubChannel` header. This header is set by the pubnub consumer to the
channel a message was received on, and the producer used it instead of the channel configured on its endpoint, so a
route from one pubnub endpoint to another sent each message back to the channel it came from. The producer now uses the
channel of its endpoint, for every operation. To choose a different channel for a message, set the new
`CamelPubNubOverrideChannel` header (`PubNubConstants.OVERRIDE_CHANNEL`). Routes that set `CamelPubNubChannel` to pick
the channel of a pubnub producer must set `CamelPubNubOverrideChannel` instead.

=== camel-dhis2 - the delete body, and one message per item of the collection consumer

The `delete/resource` producer no longer sends the message body as the resource of the request. Previously any body,
also one left by an earlier step, was sent as the payload of the DELETE. To send a resource with the delete, set
`inBody=resource` or the `CamelDhis2.resource` header.

The `post/resource` and `put/resource` producers now send a body of bytes, a stream or a file as the JSON it holds.
Previously bytes were sent as a base64 JSON string, a file as its path and a stream failed.

The `get/collection` consumer now sends one message per item of the collection, a `Dhis2Resource`, as the other API
components do for a list. Previously it sent one message per poll with an `Iterator` of the items as the body: remove
a `split` of the body after the consumer.

=== camel-docker - the events consumer subscribes again when its event stream ends

The `docker:events` consumer subscribes again when its event stream fails or ends (for example when the Docker daemon
restarts): after 1 second, then with a delay that doubles up to 30 seconds while the daemon cannot be reached. Before,
it stayed started without receiving any event. The events of the time without a stream are not received. Stopping a
`docker:events` or `docker:stats` consumer now closes its stream: before, the stream stayed open, and a route that was
stopped and started again received every event once more for each restart.

=== camel-pinecone - the pinecone:embeddings transformer stores the text

The `pinecone:embeddings` data type transformer now reads the message body. The text of a LangChain4j `TextSegment`,
or of a `String` that camel-langchain4j-embeddings converts to one, goes in the `text_segment` field of the new
`CamelPineconeMetadata` header, which UPSERT stores as the metadata of the vector and whose fields UPDATE sets, so the
`pinecone:rag` transformer now gives the text back. Before, the text was not stored, and `pinecone:rag` gave an empty
String for the match of such a vector.

The `pinecone:embeddings` transformer now fails with an `IllegalArgumentException` that names the
`CamelLangChain4jEmbeddingsVector` header when the message does not have it, as `pgvector:embeddings` does. Before, it
failed with a `NullPointerException`.

=== camel-docker - the documented default of the socket option is false

The `socket` option, and the `CamelDockerSocketEnabled` header, were documented with the default `true`, but their
default is `false`: the component connects over TCP to `host` and `port` unless `socket=true` is set. The documented
default is now `false`, its real default.

Up to Camel 4.22, Camel Spring Boot applied the documented default `true` to the component, so a Spring Boot
application that did not set `camel.component.docker.socket` used the unix socket of the Docker daemon, with `host` as
the path of the socket. Such an application now connects over TCP instead (to `tcp:///var/run/docker.sock:2375` when it
set `camel.component.docker.host=/var/run/docker.sock`). A Spring Boot application that relied on the socket must set
`camel.component.docker.socket=true`, or set the host as a URL with the `unix://` scheme, such as
`camel.component.docker.host=unix:///var/run/docker.sock`. In socket mode, the default host `localhost` now means
`/var/run/docker.sock`.

=== camel-keycloak - the consumer reads every event since the previous poll

The `keycloak` consumer (`eventType=events` and `eventType=admin-events`) reads, after its first poll, all the events
since the previous poll, page by page, with `maxResults` as the page size. Before, it read only the latest `maxResults`
events and moved past the older ones, so when more than `maxResults` events happened between two polls the older ones
were never consumed. A poll can now route more than `maxResults` events. The first poll still reads the latest
`maxResults` events.

=== camel-influxdb2 - a write is synchronous and a rejected write fails the exchange

The producer wrote with the batching write API of the InfluxDB client: the point was put into a buffer of the client
and sent later by a background thread, so the exchange completed before anything was written, and a write that
InfluxDB rejected (a line it cannot parse, an unknown bucket, a token that is not authorized) or could not receive was
only logged by the client while the route continued as if the data had been stored. The producer now writes with the
synchronous write API: the exchange continues when InfluxDB has answered, and a failed write fails the exchange with a
`CamelInfluxDb2Exception` (cause: the `InfluxException` of the client), which the error handler can redeliver.

Each exchange is now one write request, where the client used to collect the points of up to one second into one
request, and the client no longer retries a write in the background. Routes with a high message rate can send a
`Points`, `Measurements` or `Records` body (for example from an aggregator) to write many points with one request.

=== camel-elasticsearch-rest-client - the credentials are only sent to the nodes of the cluster

The `user` and `password` are now offered only to the nodes the client talks to: the hosts of `hostAddressesList` and,
when `enableSniffer` is set, the nodes the sniffer finds, matching the scheme, host and port. Previously they were
registered for any host. A request to another host, including the host of a node with another scheme or port, is now
sent without them. A client set with the `restClient` option is not changed.

=== camel-jcr - the insert operation keeps the headers of the message

The `CamelJcrInsert` operation of the producer set the identifier of the new node on a new out message, so every
header of the incoming message was gone after the producer. The headers are now kept, including `CamelJcrOperation`,
`CamelJcrNodeName` and `CamelJcrNodeType`. Remove these headers before a later `jcr` producer in the same route
if it must not use them.

=== camel-jgroups - a stream or a file body is sent as its content

The `jgroups` producer sent the body as is, so a body that is not `Serializable` failed with a
`RuntimeException` caused by a `java.io.NotSerializableException`. A stream body (an `InputStream`, a `Reader` or a
stream cache), a file from the `file` component and a `java.nio.file.Path` are now sent as a `byte[]` of their
content, so the members of the cluster receive a `byte[]`. Any other body that is not `Serializable` now fails with an
`InvalidPayloadException` that says to convert it first, before it reaches JGroups. An `onException` for
`NotSerializableException` must handle `InvalidPayloadException` instead.

=== camel-jgroups - a channel given by the user is not closed when Camel stops

When the CamelContext stops, the `jgroups` component no longer closes the channel of the component (its `channel`
option) and the channels given to an endpoint (`channel=#myChannel`). It disconnects them, so the node leaves the
cluster, and leaves them open, as a closed `JChannel` cannot be connected again: the routes can now use them again when
the CamelContext is started again. The channels that the endpoints create (with `channelProperties` or the default
configuration) are closed as before. Close a channel you give to Camel yourself when the application no longer needs
it, for example as a bean with a destroy method (`close`).

=== camel-jira - the producers wait for the answer of Jira

The `addComment`, `addIssueLink`, `addWorkLog`, `attach`, `deleteIssue`, `transitionIssue` and `watchers` producers
(and `addIssue` for its watchers) sent their request without waiting for the answer, so the exchange continued before
Jira had done the work, and a request that Jira rejected (for example no permission, or a transition that is not
allowed) was not noticed. They now wait for the answer, as `addIssue` and `updateIssue` already did, and a rejected
request fails the exchange with the `RestClientException` of the Jira client. Routes that relied on such failures
being ignored must handle the exception, for example with `onException(RestClientException.class).handled(true)`.

A producer that sends several requests can fail after some of them were done: `transitionIssue` can fail on its
comment after the transition was made, `watchers` and `addIssue` can fail after some of the watchers were changed
(`addIssue` sets the created issue as the message body before it adds the watchers), and a retry or redelivery
repeats the earlier requests.

=== camel-groovy - a script that returns a GString gives a String

A Groovy expression or script that returns a GString (a double-quoted string with placeholders, such as
`+"Hello ${body}"+`) now gives a `String`. Before, the body, header or variable was set to the GString
(`org.codehaus.groovy.runtime.GStringImpl`). A GString is not equal to a `String`, and components that check for a
`String` did not take it as text: for example camel-jms sent it as a `jakarta.jms.ObjectMessage`, which fails because
`objectMessageEnabled` is `false` by default. A Groovy predicate that returns a GString now matches as a `String` does:
empty text and the text `false` do not match. Before, every GString matched.

=== camel-jms - JmsConsumer starts and stops its listener container through private methods

`JmsConsumer.doStart` and `doStop` no longer call the protected methods `prepareAndStartListenerContainer()`,
`stopAndDestroyListenerContainer()` and `testConnectionOnStartup()`, so that a consumer stopped and started again
(with `asyncStopListener=true` or `asyncStartListener=true`) always works on its own listener container. The methods
are still there. A subclass of `JmsConsumer` (created by a custom `JmsEndpoint.createConsumer`) that overrides them
to change how the listener container is started or stopped should override `doStart` and `doStop` instead.

=== camel-jms - preserveMessageQos uses the time to live of the endpoint for a message without expiration

With `preserveMessageQos=true`, a message without a `JMSExpiration` (header missing or 0) was sent with no time to
live, so it never expired, even when the endpoint has a `timeToLive` and, for request/reply, although Camel sets the
`requestTimeout` as time to live otherwise. Such a message now gets the time to live of the endpoint, as the option
documents. Set `disableTimeToLive=true` to send it without time to live.

=== camel-sjms - preserveMessageQos also applies to InOnly sends

With `preserveMessageQos=true`, an InOnly send ignored the `JMSPriority`, `JMSDeliveryMode` and `JMSExpiration`
headers, and used the QoS of the endpoint (or of the JMS client); only request/reply used them. An InOnly send now
uses these headers, as camel-jms does. A message whose `JMSExpiration` is in the past expires at once, and a message
consumed from JMS and sent on keeps the priority, delivery mode and expiration of the consumed message. The replies of
a consumer endpoint with `preserveMessageQos=true` use the headers of the reply message too; these are still the
headers of the request unless the route changes or removes them. An exception reply (`transferException=true`) has
no headers and uses the QoS of the endpoint. `camel-sjms2` behaves the same.

A header that is not set falls back to the endpoint, as the option documents, also with `explicitQosEnabled=false`: to
the `priority` (a `priority` of 0 is not applied, as with `explicitQosEnabled`) and the `deliveryPersistent` (or
`deliveryMode`) of the endpoint, and, for a message without `JMSExpiration` (header missing or
0), to the time to live of the endpoint (`timeToLive`, or the `requestTimeout` for request/reply). This also changes
request/reply: before, a request without `JMSPriority` was sent with the priority of the new JMS message (the default
of the JMS client, 0 with ActiveMQ Classic), a request without `JMSDeliveryMode` was persistent whatever the endpoint
said, and a request without `JMSExpiration` never expired. To send a request without time to live, set
`disableTimeToLive=true` and no `timeToLive`. Remove `preserveMessageQos=true` to keep using only the QoS of the
endpoint for InOnly sends.

=== camel-sjms - an InOut reply that cannot be sent fails the message, and jmsMessageType=Stream

When an InOut consumer could not send its reply (for example, a reply body that cannot be mapped to a JMS message),
the error was set on the exchange but ignored: the incoming message was committed or acknowledged, no reply was sent
and nothing was logged. The incoming message now fails as when the route fails: it is rolled back when
`transacted=true` (or recovered with `CLIENT_ACKNOWLEDGE`), so the broker delivers it again, and the error is logged.

With `jmsMessageType=Stream`, sending failed with a `NullPointerException`. The body is now written to a
`StreamMessage`. A `jmsMessageType` that cannot be sent (`Message`) fails with a `JMSException` that names the type.
`camel-sjms2` behaves the same.

=== camel-jms, camel-sjms - a body that maps to no JMS message type fails the exchange

When the body could not be mapped to a JMS message type (for example a POJO that is not `Serializable` and has no type
converter to a `File` or an `InputStream`), the producer logged a WARN and sent an empty `jakarta.jms.Message`, so the
body was lost and the exchange succeeded. The same happened to the reply of an InOut consumer, and the caller got a
`null` body. Sending now fails with a `jakarta.jms.MessageFormatException` that names the class of the body: the
exchange of the producer fails, and for a reply the incoming message fails as when the route fails (rolled back when
`transacted=true`) and the caller gets no reply. A `null` body is still sent as an empty `jakarta.jms.Message`.

To send such a body, implement `java.io.Serializable` (and enable `objectMessageEnabled`), force a type with the
`jmsMessageType` option, or marshal the body (for example to JSON) before sending it. This applies to the components
that build on camel-jms (`camel-amqp`, `camel-activemq`, `camel-activemq6`) and to `camel-sjms2`.

=== camel-jms - a message sent from a transacted route with a username and password is rolled back with it

When `username` and `password` are set, on the component or on the endpoints, the endpoints that use the connection
factory of the component with the same username and password now share one connection factory. A message sent from a
transacted route (`transacted=true` on the consumer) is now part of the transaction of the consumed message, as it is
without credentials: when the route fails, the message is rolled back with the consumed message. Before, each endpoint
wrapped the connection factory in a `UserCredentialsConnectionFactoryAdapter` of its own, so the message was committed
at once, and sent again when the broker delivered the consumed message again.

In a route that is not transacted, a message sent from the thread of the consumer now uses the session of the consumer
(see `exposeListenerSession`), as it does without credentials, instead of a connection of its own.

An endpoint with its own `connectionFactory` or other credentials keeps a connection factory of its own, as before. This
applies to the components that build on camel-jms (`camel-amqp`, `camel-activemq`, `camel-activemq6`).

=== camel-jms, camel-sjms - text that is not a String is sent as a TextMessage

A body that is text but not a `String` (a `CharSequence` such as a `StringBuilder` or a Groovy GString) is now sent as
a `jakarta.jms.TextMessage`, as a `String` is, and a consumer gets it as a `String`. Before, it was sent as a
`jakarta.jms.ObjectMessage`, as it is `Serializable`. That failed, as `objectMessageEnabled` is `false` by default.
With `objectMessageEnabled=true` the consumer got the serialized object, or failed on the deserialization filter when
the class is not allowed (a GString is not). To send such a body as an `ObjectMessage`, set `jmsMessageType=Object`.

When a body would be sent as an `ObjectMessage` and `objectMessageEnabled` is `false`, the error now names the class
of the body. It also says how to send the body as text: convert it to a `String` first, or marshal it (for example to
JSON). This applies to the components that build on camel-jms (`camel-amqp`, `camel-activemq`, `camel-activemq6`) and
to `camel-sjms2`.

=== camel-amqp - the producers share one connection when the component creates the connection factory

When the `amqp` component creates the connection factory, from `brokerUrl`, from the `host`, `port`, `username`,
`password` and `useSsl` options, or with the `AMQPComponent.amqpComponent` factory methods, its producers now send
over one shared connection, which the component closes when it stops. Before, every message sent opened and closed a
connection to the broker. This also applies to request/reply and to the polling consumer (for example `pollEnrich`).
The consumers keep their own connections, and a message sent in a transacted route takes part in the transaction as
before. As the messages go over one connection, they all go to the same broker when the URL leads to several brokers,
for example behind a load balancer.

A `connectionFactory` set on the component or on an endpoint is used as before, and so is the connection factory of an
endpoint with its own `username` and `password`. To open a connection for every message sent, as before, set the new
`cacheConnection` option to `false` (`camel.component.amqp.cache-connection = false`).

=== camel-pulsar - the producer continues the sequence ids of the broker

The `pulsar` producer no longer passes the default `initialSequenceId=-1` to the Pulsar client, so the client
continues after the last sequence id the broker has for the `producerName`. Before, every start began again at
sequence id 0, and with deduplication enabled on the broker the messages sent after a restart were dropped as
duplicates (the send succeeded) until the sequence ids passed the ones of the previous run. A configured
`initialSequenceId` of 0 or more is still used as before.

=== camel-pulsar - the deadLetterTopic option applies in retry mode

With `enableRetry=true`, the consumer did not pass `deadLetterTopic` to the Pulsar client, so a message that failed
`maxRedeliverCount` times was sent to the client's default dead letter topic (`<topic>-<subscription>-DLQ`, or
`<subscription>-DLQ` when that topic already exists) instead of the configured one. It is now sent to
`deadLetterTopic`. Messages already in the default topic stay there.

With `enableRetry=true` and no `maxRedeliverCount`, the consumer failed to start with a `NullPointerException`. It now
starts without a dead letter policy, as the `maxRedeliverCount` option documents, and the Pulsar client applies its own
in retry mode: 16 redeliveries and its default retry letter and dead letter topics. As without retry mode,
`retryLetterTopic` and `deadLetterTopic` only apply when `maxRedeliverCount` is set. The consumer logs a WARN when it
starts with one of these topics but no `maxRedeliverCount` (and for `retryLetterTopic` without `enableRetry`).

=== camel-pulsar - the producer no longer sends a body with Java serialization

The `pulsar` producer sends the body as a `byte[]`. A body that does not convert to one, such as a `Map`, a `List`, a
number or a POJO, was sent with Java serialization, which only a Java client that deserializes it can read, and no body
was sent as the bytes of a serialized `null`. Such a body, and no body, now fail with an `InvalidPayloadException`.
Text, bytes, a stream and a file are sent as their content, as before.

Marshal the body before the producer, for example with `marshal().json()`. To keep the old behaviour for now, set the
new option `allowJavaSerializedObject=true` on the endpoint or the component. The option is deprecated from the start
and will be removed in a future release.

=== camel-twitter - twitter-search applies its query options again

Since Camel 4.0 (the upgrade to Twitter4J 4.1, whose `Query` is immutable) the `twitter-search` endpoint sent only the
keywords. The consumer ignored `filterOld` (default `true`), `lang`, `count` and the geolocation options, so it routed
the same tweets again at every poll. The producer ignored `sinceId`, `lang`, `count` and the `CamelTwitterSinceId`,
`CamelTwitterMaxId`, `CamelTwitterSearchLanguage` and `CamelTwitterCount` headers. The options are applied again: a
poll returns only the tweets newer than the last one consumed, and a page has `count` tweets (default 5; Twitter's own
default of 15 applied before). Raise `count` and `numberOfPages` if a search can match more tweets than that between
two polls. A consumer with `type=direct` returned nothing when keywords were given, and now runs the search at startup.

=== camel-kudu - a row error of Kudu fails the exchange

The `insert`, `delete`, `update` and `upsert` operations of the producer ignored the answer of Kudu. The Kudu client
does not throw for a row that Kudu rejected; it returns a row error, for example for an insert of a key that is already
present, or for an update or a delete of a row that is not found. Such an exchange completed as if the row had been
written. A write that timed out or could not reach a tablet server was reported the same way, and completed the
exchange too; for such a write it is not known whether the row was written.
The producer now fails the exchange in both cases with a `KuduRowErrorException` (`getRowError()` returns the row
error of the Kudu client, whose status tells the kind of error). Routes that relied on rejected rows being skipped
silently can use the `upsert` operation, or handle the exception, for example with
`onException(KuduRowErrorException.class).handled(true)`.

The producer also closes the Kudu session of each operation. The sessions stayed registered with the Kudu client until
the client was closed, one more for every exchange.

=== camel-ldif - the connection is bound for every message, and the status of a failed change

The producer closes the `LdapConnection` after each message. A connection bean that is not a prototype then
reconnected without a bind for the next message, so its changes failed with `modifications require authentication`.
The producer now binds such a connection again, with the name and credentials of its `LdapConnectionConfig`.
A route that called `bind()` on the connection before each message can drop that step.

The status of a failed change is now the LDAP result code, the DN of the entry and the message from the server, such as
`entryAlreadyExists (68) on uid=test1,ou=test,dc=example,dc=org`, instead of only the message (which OpenLDAP leaves
empty for some failures). A successful change is still `success`.

An empty or null body now gives an empty String as the result, instead of `null`. A missing connection bean fails with
a `NoSuchBeanException` instead of a `NullPointerException`.

=== camel-mllp - the producer verifies the host name of the server over TLS

When `sslContextParameters` is set (on the endpoint, or with `useGlobalSslContextParameters` on the component), the
MLLP producer now checks that the certificate of the server matches the host it connects to, with the same rules as an
HTTPS client. Before, it accepted any certificate trusted by the trust store, whatever host the certificate was issued
for.

A producer that connects with a host name or an IP address that the certificate does not list now fails the TLS
handshake; an IP address must be listed as a subject alternative name of the certificate, and a producer URI without a
host name (such as `mllp://8888`) cannot pass the check. Use a certificate that matches the host of the endpoint, or set
the new `hostnameVerification` option to `false` to keep the previous behaviour.

=== camel-mybatis - the body is kept with InOut, and an Insert, Update or Delete without a body fails when the statement has parameters

With an `InOut` exchange, the `Insert`, `InsertList`, `Update`, `UpdateList`, `Delete` and `DeleteList` statement
types now keep the message body, as they already did with `InOnly`. Before, the body was `null` after the call unless
the `outputHeader` option was set. The result is still in the `CamelMyBatisResult` header (or the `outputHeader`).

An `Insert`, `Update` or `Delete` with a `null` body (or a missing `inputHeader`) now fails with an
`IllegalArgumentException` when the statement has parameters. Before, MyBatis ran the statement with `NULL` for every
parameter: an `Insert` stored a row of nulls, and an `Update` without a `WHERE` set columns to null in every row. A
statement without parameters still runs with a `null` body.

=== camel-aws2-s3 - streaming upload with the random or timestamp naming strategy names the first file too

With `streamingUploadMode=true` and `namingStrategy=random` (or `timestamp`), the first file uploaded by a producer was
named after `keyName` alone (for example `camel.txt`), and only the later files got a UUID (or a timestamp) after
`keyName`. A producer that was started again (a restart of the route or of the application), or another producer with
the same `keyName`, wrote its first file to the same key and replaced the first file written before. Every file now
gets the UUID (or the timestamp), the first one too: `camel-<uuid>.txt`. The progressive naming strategy is unchanged.

=== camel-aws2-s3 - streaming upload writes concurrent exchanges one at a time

With `streamingUploadMode=true` (without `timestampGroupingEnabled`), the producer now writes one exchange at a time,
so exchanges sent concurrently (concurrent consumers, parallel splits, `threads()`) no longer lose objects or parts.
The body of an exchange is read while the other exchanges of the same producer wait, and bodies that complete an
upload of their own (`batchSize` or more, or `batchMessageNumber=1`) are no longer uploaded in parallel. To upload
large bodies in parallel, use the producer without `streamingUploadMode`.

=== camel-atom - sortEntries sorts by the updated date and routes the oldest entry first

With `sortEntries=true` the `atom` consumer sorted the entries of a feed by their published date and routed them from
the newest to the oldest one. With the default idempotent strategy (which skips an entry that is not newer than the
one routed before it) only some entries of a feed were routed, and the others were skipped.

This is fixed. The entries are now sorted by their updated date (by the published date for an entry without an
updated date), which is the date the idempotent strategy compares, and they are routed from the oldest to the newest
one, as camel-rss does and as Camel 3.x did. Entries are no longer skipped because of the routing order, also when an
older entry was updated after newer ones were published.

Routes using `sortEntries=true` with `idempotent=false` get the entries in this order too: the entry that was updated
first is routed first, where the entry that was published last was routed first before.

=== camel-opensearch - the credentials are only sent to the nodes of the cluster

The `user` and `password` are now offered only to the nodes the client talks to: the hosts of `hostAddresses` and,
when `enableSniffer` is set, the nodes the sniffer finds, matching the scheme, host and port. Every node of a
multi-host cluster still receives them. Previously they were registered for any host. A request to another host,
including the host of a node with another scheme or port, is now sent without them. A client set with the `client` or
`openSearchClient` option is not changed.

=== camel-aws-cloudtrail - the consumer looks again for events that CloudTrail shows late

The consumer polls from the newest event time it has seen, so an event that CloudTrail made visible after a newer one
was never routed. Now, every `lookBackInterval` (default 1 minute), the consumer also looks up the events from
`lookBackWindow` (default 10 minutes) before now up to that newest event time, and routes those it has not routed yet.
The normal polls send the same `LookupEvents` requests as before. The late-event lookup adds `ceil(N / maxResults)`
requests per `lookBackInterval`, at most one per poll, where N is the number of events in that range: with the
defaults one request per minute up to 50 events in 10 minutes, 20 per minute for 1000 events, and none once no event
was seen for 10 minutes. AWS allows two `LookupEvents` requests per second per account and Region. Set
`lookBackWindow=0` to disable the late-event lookup.

=== camel-aws2-s3 - a listed object that cannot be fetched is skipped

When the consumer lists the bucket, a failure to get one of the listed objects (or to download its content) no longer
fails the poll, as long as another object of that listing could be got: the object is skipped, the other listed
objects are consumed in that poll, and the skipped object is tried again by a later poll. An object that no longer
exists (for example deleted by another consumer after the listing) is skipped with a log line at `DEBUG`. Any other
failure is given to the exception handler of the consumer, with the key in the message: by default that is a `WARN`
with the stack trace, and with `bridgeErrorHandler=true` the route's error handler gets it. This happens at every poll
that lists an object that can never be read (for example archived, or access denied), until it can be read or is
removed from the bucket. Such a poll is a successful poll, so it does not count for `backoffErrorThreshold`.

The poll still fails, as before, when none of the listed objects could be got and at least one get failed with an
error other than that the object no longer exists (for example expired credentials, no permission to get objects,
throttling): the failure of the first object is then the failure of the poll, and `backoffErrorThreshold` applies.
In both cases the objects are no longer kept in the `inProgressRepository`; before, the object (or a new object with
the same key) was skipped by all later polls until the endpoint was restarted. With the `fileName` option, a failed
get still fails the poll.

=== camel-aws2-s3 - the producer stores the whole body when the content-length header is stale

When the length of the body is known without reading it (a `byte[]`, `String`, file, `Path` or cached stream), the
producer now uploads it with that length, and the `CamelAwsS3ContentLength` header is only used for a stream whose
length is not known. Previously the header took precedence: after a route changed the body of an object read by the
aws2-s3 consumer (which sets the header), a longer body was stored cut to the old length and a shorter one failed.

=== camel-aws2-s3 - the consumer gets each object right before routing it, and ignoreBody no longer downloads objects

The consumer now gets each listed object right before its exchange is routed. Before, it got every object of the poll
before it routed the first one: with the default `includeBody=true` the content of every object of the poll was in
memory at the same time, and with `includeBody=false` the streams of all the objects of the poll were open at the
same time. A poll of more than 50 objects (`maxMessagesPerPoll`) with `includeBody=false` then ran out of the 50
connections that the HTTP client of the AWS SDK has by default, and the AWS SDK aborted the streams that were not read
within 60 seconds, so most of the exchanges of the poll failed.

This changes the following:

* The batch properties count the listed objects of the poll. An object that is skipped when it is got (it was deleted
after the listing; it cannot be got; or it is a folder and `includeFolders` is `false`) is still counted:
`CamelBatchSize` can be higher than the number of exchanges routed for the poll, `CamelBatchIndex` has a gap, and when
the skipped object is the last listed one, no exchange of the poll has `CamelBatchComplete` set to `true`. Before,
they counted only the exchanges that were routed. The number of messages of the poll (used by `greedy` and
`sendEmptyMessageWhenIdle`) still counts the exchanges routed. An aggregator with `completionFromBatchConsumer` then
does not complete the group of a poll in which an object is skipped: the group waits for the exchanges of the next poll.
This happens with `includeFolders=false` and folders in the bucket, or with several consumers of the same bucket (such
as several pods of the same application). Such a route can use `completionTimeout` instead of
`completionFromBatchConsumer` (not both), with a timeout longer than the time between two exchanges of a poll, and
shorter than the `delay` of the consumer minus the `completionTimeoutCheckerInterval` (1 second by default).
* An object that cannot be got is given to the exception handler of the consumer once the other exchanges of the poll
were routed, instead of before the first one.
* A consumer that stops during a poll no longer gets the objects that it does not route.
* The protected method `AWS2S3Consumer.createExchanges(List<S3Object>)` is removed.

With `ignoreBody=true`, the consumer, and the `getObject` operation of the producer, now send a HEAD request
(HeadObject) instead of a GET request, so the content of the object is not downloaded. Before, the whole object was
transferred and discarded. The headers are the same, and HeadObject needs the same `s3:GetObject` permission. However:

* An object whose content cannot be got but whose metadata can is now consumed (or returned by `getObject`) instead of
failing, and with `deleteAfterRead` (the default) deleted. For example an object archived in S3 Glacier that is not
restored, or an object encrypted with SSE-KMS whose KMS key the role cannot use to decrypt (a GET needs
`kms:Decrypt`, a HEAD does not).
* The response to a failed HEAD request has no body: the `S3Exception` has the HTTP status code, but no S3 error code,
except `NoSuchKey` for an object that does not exist.
* The consumer no longer calls `includeS3Object(ResponseInputStream)` or `createExchange(ResponseInputStream, String)`
for these objects, as there is no GET response: a subclass of `AWS2S3Consumer` that overrides them is not called with
`ignoreBody=true`.

The `getObject` operation of the producer now also sets the `CamelAwsS3LastModified` and `CamelMessageTimestamp`
headers, with or without `ignoreBody`, as the consumer does. A `CamelMessageTimestamp` header that the message had
before the operation is replaced.

=== camel-minio - the producer stores the whole body when the content-length header is stale

When the length of the body is known without reading it (a file, `byte[]`, `String`, `Path` or cached stream), the
producer now uploads it with that length, and the `CamelMinioContentLength` header is only used for a stream whose
length is not known. Previously the header took precedence, also over the length of a `java.io.File` body: after a
route changed the body of an object read by the minio consumer (which sets the header), a longer body was stored cut
to the old length and a shorter one failed.

=== camel-mina - the host name of the server is verified over SSL

When `sslContextParameters` is set (on the endpoint, or with `useGlobalSslContextParameters` on the component), the
producer, and the consumer with `clientMode=true`, now check that the certificate of the server matches the `host` of
the endpoint, with the same rules as an HTTPS client. Before, any certificate trusted by the trust store was accepted,
whatever host the certificate was issued for.

A connection to a host name or an IP address that the certificate does not list now fails the SSL handshake; an IP
address must be listed as a subject alternative name of the certificate. Use a certificate that matches the host of the
endpoint, or set the new `hostnameVerification` option to `false` to keep the previous behaviour.

MINA does not pass a failed handshake on as an error: the producer fails with a timeout (after `writeTimeout`, or
`timeout` while waiting for a reply), and the handshake error is logged at `ERROR` level by
`org.apache.mina.filter.ssl.SslHandler`. A producer that starts timing out after the upgrade should be checked for a
certificate that does not list the host.

=== camel-mina - the producer closes its session when a request times out

A synchronous `mina` producer (`sync=true`) now always closes its session when a request times out while waiting for a
reply. Because a synchronous producer is pooled and reused for later exchanges, a session kept open after a timeout
could receive the late reply to the timed-out exchange and hand it to the next exchange that reused the same producer.
The producer now discards that session, so the next exchange opens a fresh one.

The default behaviour is unchanged: `disconnectOnNoReply` defaults to `true`, which already closed the session on a
timeout. Setting `disconnectOnNoReply=false` no longer keeps the producer session open after a timeout. The option
now has no effect: the consumer never used it, and always closes the session when it has no reply to send.

=== camel-ftp - the host name of the FTPS server is verified

The `ftps` endpoint now checks, on the control connection, that the certificate of the server matches the host of the
endpoint, with the same rules as an HTTPS client, with `sslContextParameters` as well as with the
`ftpClient.trustStore.*` options. Before, any certificate accepted by the trust manager was accepted, whatever host the
certificate was issued for. Data connections are not checked by host name.

A connection to a host name or an IP address that the certificate does not list now fails the TLS handshake, before
login; an IP address must be listed as a subject alternative name of the certificate. Use a certificate that matches the
host of the endpoint, or set the new `hostnameVerification` option to `false` to keep the previous behaviour. A custom
`ftpClient` set on the endpoint is not changed.

=== camel-ftp, camel-sftp, camel-ftps, camel-mina-sftp, camel-smb - `${file:name}` in moveExisting is the name of the file

With `fileExist=Move`, the `${file:name}` token of `moveExisting` is now the name of the existing file in its directory,
as the producer of `ftp`, `ftps` and `sftp` already used it with the default `stepwise=true`. So with `stepwise=false`,
with a `tempFileName`, in `mina-sftp` and in `smb`, a relative `moveExisting` built from `${file:name}`,
`${file:onlyname}` or literal text now gives the same result as with `stepwise=true`. Before, `${file:name}` was the
path of the file from the login directory (or the share), and since a relative `moveExisting` is put below the directory
of the file, `moveExisting=backup/${file:name}` for `hello.txt` in the directory `inbox/orders` moved the existing file
to `inbox/orders/backup/inbox/orders/hello.txt`; it is now moved to `inbox/orders/backup/hello.txt`. The tokens
`${file:name.noext}` and `${file:name.noext.single}` read the same name and change the same way: `inbox/orders/hello` is
now `hello`. A backup kept in the nested directory is no longer found there, for example by the check for an existing
backup when `eagerDeleteTargetFile=false`, or by a job that reads the backups. `${file:parent}`, `${file:onlyname}` and
`${file:ext}` are unchanged, and so is a `moveExisting` without `${...}` such as `moveExisting=backup`.

In the same cases, a relative result is now left as it is only when it starts with the directory of the file as whole
path segments (`inbox/orders/backup/hello.txt`); any other relative result is put below that directory. Before, a result
that only started with the same characters was left as it was. So for a file in `inbox/orders`,
`moveExisting=${file:parent}-old/${file:onlyname}` and `moveExisting=inbox/orders-old/${file:onlyname}` now move it to
`inbox/orders/inbox/orders-old/hello.txt` instead of `inbox/orders-old/hello.txt`; to keep a directory next to the
directory of the file, use a path relative to it, such as `moveExisting=../orders-old/${file:onlyname}` (camel-smb
resolves the `..` itself, as SMB servers do not accept it in a path). Such a path can lead out of the directory of the
endpoint: `jailStartingDirectory` does not apply to `moveExisting`. The `file` component is not changed: there
`${file:name}` is still the path of the file, and the check is as before.

=== camel-aws-secrets-manager - the CloudTrail refresh task reads every event since the last check

With `camel.vault.aws.refreshEnabled=true` and without SQS notification, each check now reads every page of the
`PutSecretValue` and `UpdateSecret` events since the previous check, including the events in the same second as the
newest event seen before, so an update is no longer missed when more events than one page happened in between. The
first check looks for the updates made after the task started: an update made before the start no longer reloads the
context. A check sends two `LookupEvents` requests, one per event name, and one more per further 50 updates (before:
always one request, for all Secrets Manager events); reads of secrets no longer add requests. LookupEvents allows two
requests per second per account and Region.

Each check looks up these events from 10 minutes before the newest event seen (but not before the task started), and
skips the events seen by an earlier check: CloudTrail can make an event visible after a newer one, and such an update was
never read. The number of requests is as above, counted over that range.

=== camel-aws2-s3 - a timestamp window uploaded more than once is stored in more than one object

With `streamingUploadMode=true` and `timestampGroupingEnabled=true`, the upload of a time window is completed when it
reaches `batchMessageNumber` messages (10 by default) or `batchSize` bytes, or when `streamingUploadTimeout` runs. A
later message of the same window started a new upload with the same key, and S3 replaced the object of the first upload
