with it, so the messages of the earlier uploads were lost (unless the bucket is versioned). A later upload of a window
now gets the number of the uploads of that window before it after the time range: for example
`grouped-messages_20240101_0800_0800-0805.txt`, then `grouped-messages_20240101_0800_0800-0805-1.txt`,
`grouped-messages_20240101_0800_0800-0805-2.txt`, and so on. The first upload of a window keeps the name it had. The
first time a producer uploads a window, it lists the objects of the window in the bucket (one `ListObjectsV2` request
per window), so after a restart it continues after the highest number stored; this needs the `s3:ListBucket`
permission, and without it the producer logs a WARN and numbers the uploads of the window from the first one. These
keys do not sort in upload order (`-1` sorts before the first upload's name, `-10` before `-2`). Producers running at
the same time with the same `keyName` still need distinct key names.

=== camel-aws2-kinesis - the consumer continues after the last record when a shard iterator expired

When a cached shard iterator has expired (an iterator is valid for 5 minutes, so after a route was suspended for
longer, or with a `delay` or back-off above 5 minutes), the consumer now continues after the last record it routed
from that shard. Previously it requested a new iterator of the configured `iteratorType`: with `TRIM_HORIZON` (the
default) it routed all the records of the shard again, with `LATEST` it skipped the records stored meanwhile.

A shard created by a split or a merge while the consumer is running is now read from its first record, whatever the
`iteratorType`. Previously, with `LATEST`, the records stored in the new shard before the consumer listed it were
skipped. The shards that exist when the consumer starts are read from the configured `iteratorType`, as before.

=== camel-aws2-kinesis - the consumer fetches up to 1000 records per shard in each poll

The default of `maxResultsPerRequest` is now `1000` (it was `1`). In each poll the consumer asks each shard for up to
that many records, so with the default `delay` of 500 ms it can read up to about 2000 records per second from a shard
(within the Kinesis quota of 2 MiB per second), instead of about 2. A poll now routes more records, and holds up to
1000 records per shard in memory while they are routed. The KCL consumer (`useKclConsumers=true`) does not use this
option.

A graceful shutdown waits until the consumer has routed all the records of the poll that is running. With 1000 records
per shard and a route that takes 50 ms per record, a poll takes about 50 seconds, which is more than the default
xref:graceful-shutdown.adoc[shutdown timeout] of 45 seconds. The shutdown is then forced, and the records of the poll
that are not routed yet fail with a `RouteStoppingException`. Before, a poll held one record per shard. Lower
`maxResultsPerRequest`, or raise the shutdown timeout, so that the records of a poll are routed within the timeout.

To keep the previous behaviour, set `maxResultsPerRequest=1`. See the
xref:components::aws2-kinesis-component.adoc[AWS Kinesis component] for how `maxResultsPerRequest`, `delay` and
`greedy` relate to the Kinesis read quotas of a shard.

=== camel-graphql - the credentials are only sent to the authority of httpUri

The `accessToken` (with its `jwtAuthorizationType`) is now added to a request only when the request goes to the
scheme, host and port of `httpUri`, and the `username` and `password` are registered for that authority only.
Previously neither was bound to the authority of `httpUri`. A request to another authority, including the same host
with another scheme or port, is now sent without them. A route that needs to authenticate to another authority must
address it in `httpUri`. When `proxyHost` is set, the `username` and `password` are no longer offered to the proxy when
it asks for authentication.

The producer also no longer takes the request URL from the `CamelHttpUri`, `CamelHttpPath`, `CamelHttpQuery` and other
camel-http control headers of the message. It did so since Camel 4.19, when the component moved onto camel-http; the
request now always goes to `httpUri`, as it did before. For the same reason the `CamelHttpMethod` and
`CamelHttpProtocolVersion` headers no longer apply, and the request is always a POST.

=== camel-elasticsearch - the credentials are only sent to the nodes of the cluster

The `user` and `password` are now offered only to the nodes the client talks to: the hosts of `hostAddresses` and,
when `enableSniffer` is set, the nodes the sniffer finds, matching the scheme, host and port. Previously they were
registered for any host. A request to another host, including the host of a node with another scheme or port, is now
sent without them. A client set with the `client` option is not changed.

=== camel-elasticsearch, camel-opensearch - the client set on the component is not closed by Camel

A `RestClient` set with the `client` option of the component, or autowired from the registry, is shared by all the
endpoints of the component. A producer that stopped (its route stopped or removed, or Camel stopped), and with
`disconnect=true` every request, closed it, and from then on every endpoint of the component failed its requests
until the application was restarted. Camel now closes only the clients its producers create from `hostAddresses`. Close a client you set on
the component yourself when the application no longer needs it (a client that is a Spring bean is closed by Spring).

=== camel-jetty - a multipart text field that occurs several times

A text field of a `multipart/form-data` request that occurs several times (for example a group of checkboxes) is now
mapped to a header whose value is the list of all its values, as a repeated query or form-urlencoded parameter already
is. Before, the header had only the first value and the others were lost. Jetty also returns the query string values
of the same name, so a name that is in both the query string and a multipart text field is mapped to a list of all
these values too, as it already is for a form-urlencoded request.

=== camel-servlet and camel-jetty - gzip request body that arrives after the headers

The servlet and Jetty consumers uncompressed a request body with `Content-Encoding: gzip` only when some of its bytes
had already arrived when the consumer started to read it. When the client sent the body later than the headers (for
example a client that sends `Expect: 100-continue`), the route got the compressed bytes. Such a body is now
uncompressed like any other gzip request body. A route that uncompressed the body itself in that case must no longer
do so. An empty body is passed on as before, and so is the body of an exchange with the `CamelSkipGzipEncoding`
property.
A request with `Content-Encoding: gzip` whose body is not gzip data now fails with a `ZipException` before the route
is called (Jetty answers 500) also when the body arrives after the headers, where the route got the bytes as they
were sent; it already failed in this way when the body had arrived.
With `disableStreamCache=true` the consumer now waits for the first bytes (the GZIP header) of a gzip request body
before it calls the route; with stream caching, the default, it already read the whole body before that.
The same applies to every consumer that reads the request body with `DefaultHttpBinding.parseBody` of
`camel-http-common`, such as the Spring Boot `platform-http` consumer with `useStreaming=true`.

=== camel-undertow - form fields are decoded as UTF-8

The consumer now decodes the fields of an `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` or `multipart/form-data` request that
declares no charset as UTF-8, the encoding browsers use. Before, Undertow's default ISO-8859-1 was used, so `café`
became `cafÃ©` in the headers and the body map. A charset that the request or a part declares is still used: a client
that sends ISO-8859-1 form data without declaring a charset now gets U+FFFD replacement characters instead of its
text, and must declare its charset (for example `application/x-www-form-urlencoded; charset=ISO-8859-1`).

=== camel-milvus, camel-neo4j, camel-qdrant, camel-weaviate - the embeddings transformers without the embedding header, and the text of neo4j:rag

The `milvus:embeddings`, `neo4j:embeddings`, `qdrant:embeddings` and `weaviate:embeddings` data type transformers now
fail with an `IllegalArgumentException` that names the `CamelLangChain4jEmbeddingsVector` header when the message does
not have it, as `pgvector:embeddings` does. Before, they failed with a `NullPointerException`.

The `neo4j:rag` data type transformer now converts a `text` value that is not a `String`, such as a number, to a
`String`, and gives an empty String for a `null` text, as for a row without text. Before, a value of another type
failed with a `ClassCastException`, and a `null` text gave a `null` element.

=== camel-undertow - rest-dsl and plain consumers on the same port are both served

When rest-dsl consumers and plain `undertow:` consumers use the same port, both kinds are now served. Before, only the
kind that registered first on the port was served, the other answered 404, and stopping the last consumer of one kind
stopped the server for the other. A request now goes to the rest-dsl consumers first, and to the plain endpoints when
no rest-dsl consumer matches it, so a plain catch-all (for example `matchOnUriPrefix=true` on `/`, for static files)
and a REST API can share a port. When a rest-dsl path and a plain path both match a request, the rest-dsl consumer
gets it, even when the plain path is more specific (for example a plain `/users/me` next to a rest-dsl
`+/users/{id}+`): give the plain endpoint another path or port. A request under the static part of a plain endpoint
path with a template (such as `/api/x/y` for `+/api/{name}+`) that no template matches now gets 404 (it got 405
before).

=== camel-undertow - the static route of a base path answers it next to a path template

When one route consumes a path template, such as `+/orders/{id}+`, and another route the static path of its base
path (`/orders`, exact or with `matchOnUriPrefix=true`), a request for `/orders` itself (also `/orders/`, and `/`
for templates of the root path) now goes to the static route. Before, the template route answered it with an empty
`id` when it had started first, or when the static route used `matchOnUriPrefix`. When the static route restricts
the HTTP methods (`httpMethodRestrict`), such a request with another method now gets 405 instead of reaching the
template route. A `matchOnUriPrefix` route of the base path now also starts when the template route started first
(it failed with `Duplicate handlers on a path`). An exact static route (without `matchOnUriPrefix`) now answers
only its own path: a deeper path that no template matches, such as `/orders/a/b`, gets 404, as without the template
route (before, the static route answered it, or it got 405 when the static route restricted the methods). To keep
the old behaviour, where the template route answers `/orders` with an empty `id`, drop the static route of the base
path: a template route alone still serves the base path.

=== camel-huaweicloud-obs - a listed object that cannot be got is skipped

When the consumer lists the bucket, a failure to get one of the listed objects (for example access denied, or an
archived object that is not restored) no longer fails the poll, as long as another object of that listing could be
got: the object is skipped, the other listed objects are consumed, and the skipped object is tried again by a later
poll. The failure is given to the exception handler of the consumer, with the key in the message (by default a `WARN`
with the stack trace, at every poll that lists the object). Before, the poll failed, so the other objects listed with
it were not consumed either, and when the bucket fitted in one listing (`maxMessagesPerPoll`, default 10) the consumer
consumed no object at all. The poll still fails when none of the listed objects could be got. With the `fileName`
option, a failed get still fails the poll.

=== camel-huaweicloud-dms - createInstance fails on a body that is not the request

The `createInstance` operation of `hwcloud-dms` now reads the JSON of a `CreateInstanceRequestBody` from text, bytes, a
stream or a file, such as the body of an HTTP request or the file of a file consumer, and from a `Map` with the same
field names, such as the JSON unmarshalled by an earlier step. Before, only a `String` body was read: JSON given as
bytes, a stream, a file or a `Map` was ignored without a warning, and the instance was created from the endpoint options
and the exchange properties.

A body that is not a `CreateInstanceRequestBody` or a valid JSON representation of it now fails the exchange with an
`IllegalArgumentException`. Before, such a body was ignored (a `String` with a WARN log) and the instance was created
from the options. The instance is now created from the options only when the exchange has no body, or an empty one. A
route that creates the instance from the options while it carries another body, such as a text from an earlier step,
must clear the body first, for example with `setBody` and an empty `constant`.

`updateInstance` reads the JSON of an `UpdateInstanceRequestBody` from bytes, a stream, a file or a `Map` too. A body
that is not a valid JSON of it now fails with an `IllegalArgumentException`, where it failed with the exception of
Jackson.

=== camel-github2 - consumers and items of a poll that were not routed

The `github2` consumers (`commit`, `event`, `pullRequest`, `pullRequestComment`, `tag`) remembered every item of a
poll as consumed before routing it. Items that were not routed because the route was suspended during the poll were
never routed. They are now routed by the first poll after the route is resumed.

The `commit` consumer also lost the new commits of a poll in which the GitHub lookup of an author or a committer
failed (for example a 404 for a deleted account, or a server error). Such a commit is now routed, and a WARN is logged
for it. Its `CamelGitHubCommitAuthor` and `CamelGitHubCommitCommitter` headers then have the name that git recorded in
the commit instead of the name of the GitHub profile, and are not set when the commit has no name either.

=== camel-huaweicloud-functiongraph - the whole result of a function without a body field

The `invokeFunction` operation of `hwcloud-functiongraph` now gives the whole result of the function as the message
body when the result is not a JSON object with a top-level `body` field. Before, the body was null when the result was
a JSON object without a `body` field, and the call failed with a Gson `JsonSyntaxException` when the result was a JSON
array, another JSON value or plain text. A result with a `body` field, such as the result of a function behind an API
Gateway trigger, still gives that field, and no body when the field is null. A route that tested for a null body after
the call must now test the result instead.

=== camel-slack - consumer and a suspended route

When a route is suspended (`RouteController.suspendRoute`, also from a route policy that suspends the route such as
`DurationRoutePolicy`, or `CamelContext.suspend()`) while the `slack` consumer routes the messages of a poll, the
consumer stops after the message that is being routed. The other messages of that poll were skipped when the route
was resumed. They are now kept and routed by the first poll after the resume, together with the messages posted in
the meantime. They are kept in memory only: a route that is stopped and started again does not route them.

=== camel-huaweicloud-iam - the JSON results use the field names of the IAM API

The operations of `hwcloud-iam` now write their results (the users or the groups of `listUsers`, `listGroups` and
`getGroupUsers`, and the user or the group of `getUser`, `updateUser` and `updateGroup`) as JSON with the field names of
the IAM API. These are the names that the JSON requests of `updateUser` and `updateGroup` already used, and that
`hwcloud-dms` writes too. Before, the results used the Java field names of the Huawei Cloud SDK. For example,
`domainId` is now `domain_id`, `pwdStatus` is `pwd_status`, `lastProjectId` is `last_project_id`, `passwordExpiresAt`
is `password_expires_at`, `createTime` is `create_time` and `isDomainOwner` is `is_domain_owner`. A route that reads
these fields, for example with the JSONPath `$.domainId`, must use the new names.

The component no longer depends on Gson.

=== camel-mail - mails of a poll that were not routed, with an idempotentRepository

With an `idempotentRepository`, the mail consumer now removes the keys of the mails that a poll retrieved but did not
route (the consumer was stopped in the middle of the batch, or the poll failed on a later mail), so that a later poll
routes them. Before, they were skipped as duplicates by every later poll. As a consequence, a mail that the consumer
cannot read (with the default `skipFailedMessage=false` and `handleFailedMessage=false`) now fails every poll until it
is removed, as it does without an `idempotentRepository`. Before, it failed only the first poll and was then skipped,
together with the mails retrieved before it. Set `skipFailedMessage` or `handleFailedMessage` to skip such a mail.

=== camel-huaweicloud-smn - a Map, a List or a POJO is published as JSON, and the CamelHwCloudSmnMessageId property

The `hwcloud-smn` producer now publishes a `Map`, a `List` or a POJO in the message body as JSON. Before, it published
the `toString()` of the body, such as `{orderId=1, status=shipped}` for a Map. A Map or a List that cannot be written as
JSON now fails with an `IllegalArgumentException`. Text, bytes, a stream, a file, an XML `Source` or DOM node, and a
single value such as a number are published as text, as before.

The id of the published message is now set in the `CamelHwCloudSmnMessageId` exchange property
(`SmnProperties.MESSAGE_ID`). The `CamelHwCloudSmnMesssageId` exchange property (`SmnProperties.SERVICE_MESSAGE_ID`),
whose name has a typo, is still set to the same id, but it is deprecated and will be removed in a future release:
read `CamelHwCloudSmnMessageId` instead.

=== camel-smpp - the host name of the SMSC is verified over SSL

With `smpps` (or `usingSSL=true`), directly or through an HTTP proxy, the connection now checks that the certificate of
the SMSC matches the host it connects to, with the same rules as an HTTPS client. Before, any certificate trusted by the
JVM was accepted, whatever host it was issued for.

A connection to a host name or an IP address that the certificate does not list now fails the SSL handshake; an IP
address must be listed as a subject alternative name of the certificate. Use a certificate that matches the host of the
endpoint, or set the new `hostnameVerification` option to `false` to keep the previous behaviour.

A mismatch shows as the producer or consumer failing to start, with an `IOException` whose cause names the host that
was checked:

[source]
----
java.io.IOException: No subject alternative names matching IP address 127.0.0.1 found
    Caused by: javax.net.ssl.SSLHandshakeException: No subject alternative names matching IP address 127.0.0.1 found
----

=== camel-netty-http - the request body read as an InputStream

The body of a request received by the consumer is a `NettyChannelBufferStreamCache`. Its `read()` method now returns
a byte as a value from 0 to 255, and -1 at the end of the body, as `java.io.InputStream` requires. Before, a byte
from 0x80 was returned as a negative value (0xFF as -1, which a reader takes for the end of the body), and the method
threw an `IndexOutOfBoundsException` at the end. `markSupported()` now returns `false` (`mark(int)` never did
anything): a reader that needs mark support has to wrap the stream, for example in a `BufferedInputStream`.
`reset()` goes back to the start of the body, as before.

=== camel-netty-http - securityConfiguration is copied per endpoint

When `securityConfiguration` is set on the `NettyHttpComponent`, each endpoint now receives an independent copy of the configuration. Modifying `securityConfiguration.*` options on an endpoint URI no longer alters the component-level configuration or other endpoints.

=== camel-netty-http - the endpoint URI includes the endpoint options

The endpoint URI of a `netty-http` endpoint (`Endpoint.getEndpointUri()`) is now its full URI, with the component
scheme and the endpoint options, as for `netty` and most other components. For example, the endpoint
`netty-http:http://localhost:8080/foo?throwExceptionOnFailure=false` now has the endpoint URI
`netty-http://http://localhost:8080/foo?throwExceptionOnFailure=false`. Before, it was `http://localhost:8080/foo`.

Endpoints whose URIs differ only in their options are therefore no longer equal, and each of them gets its own
producer when a `ProducerTemplate`, `toD` or another EIP with a dynamic URI sends to them. Before, these endpoints used
the producer of the endpoint that was used first, with the options of that endpoint.

The request is still sent to the same HTTP address, which `NettyHttpEndpoint.getAddressUri()` returns. The new endpoint
URI shows in:

* the name of an auto-mocked endpoint, for example `mock:netty-http:http://localhost:8080/foo` instead of
  `mock:http:localhost:8080/foo`. A `mockEndpoints` pattern such as `http*` no longer matches a `netty-http` endpoint,
  and `netty-http*` does.
* the name of the endpoint in JMX, for example `netty-http://http://0.0.0.0:8080/foo`.
* the `CamelToEndpoint` exchange property.
* tracing: a `netty-http` endpoint now gets the span decorator of `netty-http` instead of the one of `http` or
  `https`, and the include and exclude patterns are matched against the new endpoint URI.
* the `endpointName` tag of the Micrometer exchange event metrics.

=== camel-netty-http - endpoint options are no longer sent to the server as query parameters

The `netty-http` producer no longer sends some of its endpoint options, such as `cookieHandler`, `httpMethodRestrict`,
`traceEnabled` and `exchangePattern`, to the HTTP server as query parameters. For example,
`netty-http:http://localhost:8080/foo?bar=1&cookieHandler=#myCookieHandler` now calls
`http://localhost:8080/foo?bar=1`. Before, the server also received the `cookieHandler` parameter. Only the
query parameters that are not endpoint options are sent, as the documentation describes. To send a query parameter
that has the same name as an endpoint option, set the query in the `CamelHttpQuery` header.

=== camel-netty - hostnameVerification verifies the host instead of failing

`hostnameVerification=true` on a `netty` producer, or on a consumer with `clientMode=true`, now checks that the
certificate of the server matches the host the endpoint connects to. Before, it made the TLS handshake fail for every
certificate, matching or not, with `CertificateException: Hostname or IP address is undefined` - the engine was
created without a peer host, so the JDK had no name to compare against.

A route that had the option set was therefore not connecting at all, and needs no change. A route that sets it while
connecting to a host the certificate does not list - most commonly an IP address, which has to appear as a subject
alternative name of its own - now fails the handshake with the mismatch as the reason. The default is unchanged:
`hostnameVerification` is `false`, and the host is then not checked.

Enabling the option also makes the JDK send the host as an SNI server name, as `camel-netty-http` already did. The
default path still creates the engine without a peer and sends no SNI.

On a consumer that acts as a server the option is now ignored instead of being passed to the engine. It never verified
anything there - the JDK does not support host name verification on the server side of a handshake, and rejected the
client certificate with `Endpoint Identification Algorithm HTTPS is not supported on the server side` - so a consumer
that asks for client certificates and had the option set now completes the handshake instead of failing it.

=== camel-netty-http - hostnameVerification is ignored on a consumer

A `netty-http` consumer no longer passes `hostnameVerification=true` to its TLS engine, as `netty` consumers already
do not (see above). The JDK does not support host name verification on the server side of a handshake: a client that
presented a certificate failed the handshake with
`Endpoint Identification Algorithm HTTPS is not supported on the server side`, and a client that presented none was not
checked at all. A consumer with `needClientAuth=true` and the option set now accepts trusted client certificates
instead of rejecting all of them. Consumers that share a server through `nettySharedHttpServer` never applied the
option, and are unchanged. The option still verifies the host on a `netty-http` producer.

=== camel-alibaba-oss - a listed object that cannot be got is skipped

When the consumer lists the bucket, a failure to get one of the listed objects (for example access denied, or an
archived object that is not restored) no longer fails the poll, as long as another object of that listing could be
got: the object is skipped, the other listed objects are consumed, and the skipped object is tried again by a later
poll. The failure is given to the exception handler of the consumer, with the key in the message (by default a `WARN`
with the stack trace, at every poll that lists the object). Before, the poll failed and the next poll listed the same
page again, so one such object stopped the consumer from consuming any object. The poll still fails when none of the
listed objects could be got, and the next poll then reads the next page of the listing.

=== camel-alibaba-fc - invokeFunction sends a stream or a file as its bytes

The `invokeFunction` operation of `alibaba-fc` now sends a stream or a file body (an `InputStream`, a `java.io.File`,
or a `GenericFile` from a file consumer) as its bytes, as they are. Before, it read such a body as text and sent that
text in UTF-8, which changed binary data. A `String` is still sent in UTF-8, a `byte[]` as it is, and no body as an
empty payload.

A body that does not convert to bytes, such as a `Map` or a POJO, is no longer sent as its `toString()`: it now fails
with an `InvalidPayloadException`. Marshal such a body first, for example to JSON with `marshal().json()`.

=== camel-dropbox - search and get read every page of the result

The `search` operation (producer and consumer) now returns every match that the service gives. Previously it returned
only the first page of the matches (the Dropbox SDK asks for 100 matches per page), and the rest was dropped without
any sign. A search with many matches now sends one request per page, for the consumer at each poll. When one of these
requests fails, the operation fails and no match is returned, as before.

The `get` operation on a folder (producer and consumer) now downloads every file of the folder. Previously it
downloaded only the files of the first page of the folder listing. As before, the content of all the files is in the
message body, so a large folder needs more memory than it did when only the first page was read.

=== camel-alibaba-kms - encrypt encrypts a stream or a file as its bytes

The `encrypt` operation of `alibaba-kms` now encrypts a stream or a file body (an `InputStream`, a `java.io.File`, or
a `GenericFile` from a file consumer) as its bytes, as they are. Before, it read such a body as text and encrypted that
text in UTF-8, which changed binary data, so `decrypt` did not give the same bytes back. A `String` is still encrypted
in UTF-8, a `byte[]` as it is, and the `plaintext` option or the `CamelAlibabaKmsPlaintext` header still win over the
body.

A body that does not convert to bytes, such as a `Map` or a POJO, is no longer encrypted as its `toString()`: it now
fails with an `InvalidPayloadException`. Marshal such a body first, for example to JSON with `marshal().json()`.

=== camel-pqc - a configured KeyGenerator or KeyAgreement is used by one exchange at a time

When Camel creates the instance itself, the KEM operations of the `pqc` component (including the hybrid KEM
operations) and the PQC data format now use a new `KeyGenerator`, and for the hybrid operations a new `KeyAgreement`,
for every operation. A configured instance is still shared: a registry bean, which is autowired into every `pqc`
endpoint, a `KeyGenerator` set on the data format, or the default material used when no key material is configured.
Each operation now holds its lock from `init` until the result is returned, so concurrent exchanges that use it run
one at a time.

A started PQC data format no longer keeps a `KeyGenerator` of its own: `getKeyGenerator()` returns only a configured
instance and, unless one is configured, the `CamelPQCKemAlgorithm` header now selects the KEM algorithm of each
operation.

=== camel-pqc - message bodies are signed and verified over their bytes

The `sign`, `verify`, `hybridSign` and `hybridVerify` operations now read a `java.io.File` or `java.nio.file.Path`
body, and a file from a file consumer (a `GenericFile` body), as bytes from the file, the same way as a `byte[]` or
`InputStream` body. A `String` (or any `CharSequence`) body is encoded as UTF-8, and any other body that has a binary
type converter (for example a `java.nio.ByteBuffer`, a `java.io.ByteArrayOutputStream`, a Netty `ByteBuf` or a Vert.x
`Buffer`) is signed over those bytes.

A body with no binary representation (for example a `Map` or a POJO) is no longer signed over its `toString()`: it is
now rejected with an `InvalidPayloadException`. Convert such a body to a `String`, `byte[]` or other binary type
earlier in the route.

Signatures created by earlier versions over a `File`, `Path`, `GenericFile` or other non-`byte[]`/`InputStream` body
may not verify and need to be created again.

=== camel-alibaba-mns - binary bodies are kept, the message id and MD5 are headers, and receiveMessage is not an operation

The `sendMessage` and `publishMessage` operations of `alibaba-mns` now send a `byte[]`, a stream or a file body (an
`InputStream`, a `java.io.File`, or a `GenericFile` from a file consumer) as its bytes, as they are. Before, they read
such a body as text and sent that text in UTF-8, which changed binary data, and a binary file from a file consumer
failed to convert. A `String` is still sent as it is, and any other body, such as a `Map`, a `List` or a POJO, is
still sent as its `toString()`.

The consumer now gives the body of a message as a `byte[]` when its bytes are not UTF-8 text, such as the bytes of a
binary file. Before, it gave a `String` in which such bytes were replaced. A message of UTF-8 text is still a `String`.

`sendMessage` and `publishMessage` now set the `CamelAlibabaMnsMessageId` and `CamelAlibabaMnsMessageBodyMd5` headers,
as the docs say, so after a send these headers hold the id and the MD5 of the message that was sent, not of a message
consumed earlier in the route. They are still set as exchange properties too, as before.

`receiveMessage` is no longer a value of the `operation` option: the producer never supported it, and the consumer,
which receives the messages of a queue, does not use the option. A producer with `operation=receiveMessage` still
fails, now with a message that says so. Remove `operation=receiveMessage` from consumer endpoints, which tooling that
validates endpoint options now reports.

=== camel-minio - a listed object that cannot be got is skipped

When the consumer lists the bucket, a failure to stat or get one of the listed objects (for example access denied, or
an object encrypted with a customer key the consumer does not have) no longer fails the poll, as long as another listed
object could be got: the object is skipped, the other listed objects are consumed, and the skipped object is tried
again by a later poll. The failure is given to the exception handler of the consumer, with the object name in the
message (by default a `WARN` with the stack trace, at every poll that lists the object). Before, the objects listed
after it in the same poll were not consumed, so one such object could keep the consumer from consuming the rest of the
bucket. The poll still fails when none of the listed objects could be got, and when `objectName` is set. When a poll
lists `maxMessagesPerPoll` objects, the next poll now continues after the last listed object (before, after the last
object that was consumed), so an object that cannot be got at the end of a page does not hold the listing back.

=== camel-minio - maxMessagesPerPoll now limits a poll to one page

The consumer used `maxMessagesPerPoll` only as the page size of the bucket listing, so one poll listed every page of
the bucket, got the metadata of every object, and routed all of them. A poll now lists at most `maxMessagesPerPoll`
objects (one page, as camel-aws2-s3 does), and the next poll continues after the last object the previous poll
listed. Draining a large bucket therefore takes more polls than before. Setting `maxMessagesPerPoll` to 0 or a
negative number keeps the previous behaviour of listing the whole bucket in one poll. Some listed objects are not
routed (folders when `includeFolders` is false, objects still being uploaded, objects that cannot be got), so a poll
can route fewer objects than the limit, and the next poll continues after them. The `startAfter` option now marks
where each pass through the bucket begins: the polls of a pass continue after the last listed object, and a new pass
starts after `startAfter` again once the listing has been read to its end.

=== camel-minio - Server-side encryption options now apply to MinioProducer

The `serverSideEncryption` and `serverSideEncryptionCustomerKey` endpoint options now apply to producer operations:
- Put operations apply the configured server-side encryption or customer-supplied key.
- Copy operations apply the configured encryption to the destination object (and `serverSideEncryptionCustomerKey` to the
  source object when configured).
- Get operations (`getObject` and `getObjectRange`) pass the customer key when configured.
- Only one of `serverSideEncryption` or `serverSideEncryptionCustomerKey` can be configured on an endpoint; configuring
  both throws `IllegalArgumentException` on producer startup.

=== camel-box - getEnterpriseEvents returns the events of every chunk

The `getEnterpriseEvents` operation of the `event-logs` API now returns all the events between `after` and `before`.
Previously it returned only the first chunk of the event log (at most 500 events), and the rest was dropped without
any sign. A wide `after`/`before` window now returns every event in one list: that is one request per 500 events plus
one that finds the end of the log, and more memory. Narrow the window when the event log is large.

=== camel-aws2-eventbridge - the consumer keeps one target for a queue given with queueUrl

A consumer that uses its own queue (`queueUrl`) added a target with a new random id to the EventBridge rule at every
start, and removed it on stop. Two consumers that share the queue, or a restart after a consumer was not stopped
cleanly, left two targets for the queue, and EventBridge put every event on the queue twice. The target now has an id
derived from the queue ARN, and stays on the rule when the consumer stops, so the queue also collects the events of the
rule while no consumer runs. Remove the target from the rule yourself when you no longer use the queue, and remove
targets with ids `camel-eb-` plus 8 characters that an earlier version left for the queue. An auto-created queue is
not affected. During a rolling upgrade from an earlier version, events can be put on the queue twice until the last
instance on the earlier version stops.

=== camel-chunk - message data printed with a direct tag is rendered literally

Previously the `chunk` component bound the message body, headers and variables into the Chunk engine so that
Chunk re-parsed those values as template source: a value that itself contained Chunk tags (such as a body of
`{$headers.name}`) had those tags expanded instead of being printed verbatim.

Message data bound to the engine is now neutralised so that a direct `{$tag}` printing the body, a header, a
variable, or an element of a nested map, list or array renders the value literally instead of evaluating the
tags inside it. Tag-free data is unchanged and Chunk's string filters (for example `slice`, `get`, `join`)
keep their existing behaviour. Routes that relied on message data being evaluated as Chunk template code must
move that markup into the template itself.

As part of this, a non-`List` `Collection` value (such as a `Set`) is now bound as a `List`, so a direct
`{$body}` prints Chunk's list placeholder instead of the collection's `toString()`, and a filter such as
`|join` operates on its elements.

This does not cover every path: Chunk re-parses any string it produces while rendering, so the output of a
filter (`{$body|trim}`), a string property of a bean body (`{$body.name}`, `{$exception.message}`) and string
items bound by a `{% loop %}` are still parsed as template code. Keep untrusted data to direct `{$tag}`
output; do not pass untrusted values through Chunk filters, bean properties or loops. If untrusted data must
go through one of those paths, remove the `{` and `}` characters from it in the route before the `chunk`
endpoint (Chunk has no escape for tags inside data), for example with a processor that rewrites the body or
header.

=== camel-azure-storage-blob - a listed blob that cannot be got is skipped

When the consumer lists the container, a failure to get one of the listed blobs (for example a blob in the archive
tier, or access denied) no longer fails the poll, as long as another listed blob could be got: the blob is skipped, the
other listed blobs are consumed, and the skipped blob is tried again by a later poll. The failure is given to the
exception handler of the consumer, with the blob name in the message (by default a `WARN` with the stack trace, at every
poll that lists the blob). Before, the poll failed, and as every poll lists the container again, one such blob stopped
the consumer from consuming any blob. The poll still fails when none of the listed blobs could be got.

=== camel-azure-eventhubs - an event that completes when the checkpoint timer of its window has run is checkpointed

With `checkpointBatchTimeout`, the consumer now starts a new checkpoint window when the timer of the current window
has run, and no longer when the wall clock passes the time the timer was scheduled for. Before, an event that
completed in the millisecond in which the timer ran (or while the timer was taking the count) was counted in the
window whose count had already been taken: it was checkpointed only with the next event, and when the partition stayed
quiet it was delivered again after a restart.

The `CamelAzureEventHubsCheckpointUpdatedBy` header can now be `timeout`. An event that completes after the
scheduled time of its window, but before the (late) timer has run, joins that window and gets `timeout`; its offset is
checkpointed by that timer. Before, such an event always started a new window, so `timeout` was practically never
set. Routes that read this header can now see `timeout` as well as `size`.

=== camel-azure-functions - invokeFunction fails on an HTTP error status, and sends the body as bytes

The `invokeFunction` operation now fails the exchange with an `org.apache.camel.http.base.HttpOperationFailedException`
when the function answers with an HTTP status of 400 or above, as the HTTP components do. The exception holds the
status, the response body and the response headers. Previously the exchange succeeded, with the error response as the
body and the status in the `CamelAzureFunctionsStatusCode` header. Set the new `throwExceptionOnFailure` option to
`false` to keep the previous behaviour.

The request body is now sent as bytes: a stream or a file is sent as its content, and bytes are sent as they are.
Previously a body other than text or bytes was sent as its `toString()`, and bytes were decoded as UTF-8. Text, a number
or a boolean is still sent as its text. Another body that does not convert to bytes, such as a `Map` or a POJO, now
fails the exchange: marshal it to JSON first. A request with a
body now always has `Content-Type: application/json` unless the `CamelAzureFunctionsHttpHeaders` header sets a
`Content-Type`; previously it had no `Content-Type` when that header was not set.

=== camel-azure-servicebus - the dead-letter reason and description are bounded

When the consumer dead-letters a message after a failed route with `enableDeadLettering=true`, the reason and the
error description it writes are now capped at 4096 characters, the limit the Azure SDKs apply to both fields. A value
that was cut ends with `...`. The description holds the stack trace, so a deep one is now truncated rather than sent
in full and rejected by the broker.

A settlement request the broker rejects is now reported through the consumer's exception handler.

=== camel-azure-key-vault - the body after deleteSecret, and a Map or a POJO given to createSecret

The `deleteSecret` operation now sets the `DeletedSecret` of the Azure SDK as the message body, with the recovery id and
the dates of the deletion and of the scheduled purge. Before, the message body after `deleteSecret` was always `null`.
A route that relied on an empty body after `deleteSecret` must now clear the body itself.

The `createSecret` operation now stores a `Map`, a `List` or a POJO body as JSON, such as
`{"username":"admin","password":"secret"}`, with dates and times as ISO-8601 text. Before, it stored the text the type
converter gives (usually `toString()`), such as `{username=admin, password=secret}`. A POJO that has its own type
converter to `String`, or a JAXB object, is now stored as the JSON of its properties as well: to store the text of the
converter, convert the body to a `String` first, for example with `convertBodyTo(String.class)`. A `Map` or a `List`
that Jackson cannot serialize now fails with an `IllegalArgumentException`. Text, bytes, a stream or a file is stored
as its text, as before.

=== camel-nats - the JetStream pull consumer subscribes again when its ephemeral consumer was deleted

With `jetstreamEnabled=true` and a pull subscription (the default), the `nats` consumer kept fetching from a JetStream
consumer that the server had deleted, and consumed nothing more until the route was restarted. It now subscribes again
when the server no longer has its ephemeral consumer (no `durableName`, and no durable name in the
`consumerConfiguration`), which the server deletes after about 5 seconds without a pull request, for example while the
client is disconnected, or loses on a restart. The new consumer starts at the first message the route did not
acknowledge, so the route can see again the messages after that one (with `ackPolicy=None`, after the last message
fetched). On a stream with `WorkQueue` retention, and on a stream that was created again since the route subscribed, the
new consumer uses the consumer configuration as it is. The consumer asks the server for its consumer only after a fetch
that ended empty well before `pullFetchTimeout`, at most every 5 seconds. A durable consumer is not created again: when
it is gone (deleted by an administrator, or after its `inactiveThreshold`), the consumer logs a WARN and keeps fetching,
and the fetches work again when the durable is created again with the same name.

=== camel-azure-cosmosdb - a client set on the component or autowired is not closed by Camel

A `CosmosAsyncClient` set with the `cosmosAsyncClient` option, or autowired from the registry, is shared by all the
azure-cosmosdb endpoints. An endpoint that stopped (for example because its route was removed or reloaded) closed it,
and from then on the other endpoints, and the application, failed every request until the application was restarted.
Camel now closes only the clients that its endpoints create. Close a client you set yourself when the application no
longer needs it (a client that is a Spring bean is closed by Spring).

=== camel-azure-cosmosdb - the JSON of an item is parsed into a Map

The `createItem`, `upsertItem` and `replaceItem` operations now parse a message body that holds the JSON of the item
(a `String`, a `byte[]`, a stream or a file) into a `Map` before they write it. Before, a JSON `String` or a file was
written as a JSON string and failed, and a stream could not be serialized. A `byte[]` body was sent as it is, and the
message body after the operation was the written item as a `byte[]`: it is now a `Map`, as for a `Map` body. If the
next steps need the JSON as bytes, marshal the body, for example with `marshal().json()`.

A `List` body, or JSON that is not one object (such as a JSON array), now fails with an `IllegalArgumentException`
before Cosmos DB is called; before, Cosmos DB rejected it with an error 400. These operations write one item: send each
item in its own exchange, for example with the Split EIP.

=== camel-telegram - a failed send fails the exchange once

When Telegram answers a message with an error status (for example `400` for an unknown chat), or with a body that
cannot be read, the producer now fails the exchange once and the route stops there. Previously the route first
continued after the producer as if the message had been sent, and then the exchange failed as well.

The exception of the exchange is the one that `process()` of the producer threw before: a `RuntimeException` that
wraps an `ExecutionException`, with the `RuntimeCamelException` (status and body of the answer) as its cause, or the
`IOException` when the request got no answer. The producer still waits for the answer before it returns, so the
order of the messages is the same. The steps after the producer now run on the calling thread, and no longer on a
thread of the HTTP client.

=== camel-nats - the JetStream push consumer subscribes again when its ephemeral consumer was deleted

With `jetstreamEnabled=true` and `pullSubscription=false`, the consumer now creates its ephemeral JetStream consumer
again after a reconnect when the server deleted it, starting at the first message the route did not acknowledge, so
the route can see again the messages after that one. A durable consumer is not created again (a WARN is logged), and a
consumer deleted while the connection stays up is not detected. See the
xref:components::nats-component.adoc[NATS component] page for the details.

=== camel-vertx-websocket - the client consumer makes one reconnect attempt at a time

When the WebSocket of a consumer with `consumeAsClient=true` is closed, the consumer now makes one reconnect attempt
at a time: while an attempt waits for the server (up to the connect timeout), the next runs of `reconnectInterval`
are skipped, and `maxReconnectAttempts` counts these attempts one after the other. Previously every interval started
another attempt, and when the server answered late each of them opened a WebSocket, so that every message was routed
once per WebSocket. A consumer that is stopped no longer reconnects.

=== camel-core - a route from a route template has the template's resource as its source

A route created from a route template or a Kamelet now returns the resource with the route template from
`Route.getSourceResource()` (it returned null). This is the resource its source location (`Route.getSourceLocation()`)
was already in, so the debugger and message history consoles show the source code of these routes too.

A route created from a `templatedRoute` also remembers the resource that declares the `templatedRoute`
(`Route.getTemplatedRouteResource()`, the `TemplatedRouteSource` JMX attribute, and `templatedRouteSource` in the
route dev console), and the route template id is available from `Route.getRouteTemplateId()`, the `RouteTemplateId`
JMX attribute, and `routeTemplateId` in the route dev console.

Code that took a null source resource to mean that a route was created from a route template should use
`Route.isCreatedByRouteTemplate()` instead.

=== camel-aws2-ddb - aws2-ddbstream consumer and a suspended route

When a route is suspended (`RouteController.suspendRoute`, also when a route policy calls it, or
`CamelContext.suspend()`) while the `aws2-ddbstream` consumer routes the records of a poll, the consumer stops after
the record that is being routed. The other records of that poll (up to `maxResultsPerRequest`, default 100) were
skipped when the route was resumed. They are now routed after the resume. A consumer that is suspended directly
(`consumer.suspend()`) routes all the records of its poll and was not affected.

=== camel-jackson and camel-jackson3 - the bodies of the data type transformers

When the `X-Content-Class` exchange property is not set, the `application-json` data type transformer now writes a
`Map` body as a JSON object and a `List` (or another `Collection`) body as a JSON array, and reads text, bytes or a
stream (an `InputStream`, a `Reader` or the stream cache) as text: a JSON object or array is kept as it is, and any
other text becomes a JSON string, so `Hello` becomes `"Hello"`. This includes text that is a JSON number, boolean, null
or quoted string: `42` becomes `"42"`, `true` becomes `"true"`, and `"Hello"` with its quotes becomes `"\"Hello\""`.
These bodies failed before. With the `X-Content-Class` exchange property set, the transformer still writes only a body
of that class, or JSON object or array text. A `Map` entry with a null or empty value is left out, as the transformer
already did for the properties of a POJO.

Bytes and a stream are read as text with the charset of the exchange (the `CamelCharsetName` header or exchange
property, else UTF-8), and the JSON is written in UTF-8. A payload that is not text, such as Avro binary or an image,
therefore becomes a JSON string with replacement characters. Encode such a payload first, for example in Base64, or
give it a content class: a `byte[]` body with the `X-Content-Class` exchange property set to `byte[]` is written as a
Base64 JSON string.

The `avro-x-struct` data type transformer now reads a JSON object or array given as text as JSON, as
`protobuf-x-struct` does. Before, it read the text as Avro binary, which failed or gave a wrong `JsonNode`.

The `avro-x-java-object` and `protobuf-x-java-object` data type transformers of camel-jackson-avro and
camel-jackson-protobuf now read a `JsonNode` body into the class of the `X-Content-Class` exchange property, as the
camel-jackson3 ones do. Before, they failed with an `UnsupportedOperationException`.

The `application-x-java-object`, `avro-x-java-object` and `protobuf-x-java-object` data type transformers now fail on a
null body with a `CamelExecutionException`, whose cause is an `InvalidPayloadException`, instead of a
`NullPointerException`, when the `X-Content-Class` exchange property is set.

The `avro-binary`, `avro-x-struct`, `protobuf-binary` and `protobuf-x-struct` data type transformers of
camel-jackson3-avro and camel-jackson3-protobuf now wrap a `JacksonException` in a `CamelExecutionException`, as their
camel-jackson-avro and camel-jackson-protobuf counterparts and the camel-jackson3 JSON transformers do. The
`JacksonException` is the cause of the `CamelExecutionException`.

=== camel-core - the route structure leaves out onException clauses that never run

A route with `noErrorHandler` runs none of its `onException` clauses, and a global `onException` is copied into every
route of its route builder or file. The route structure now leaves these clauses out of such routes: the
`route-structure` dev console, `camel cmd route-structure`, and the route diagrams drawn from it. Before, they were
listed among the steps of the route.

=== camel-core - simple `==` compares a number and a numeric String by their values

The `==` and `!=` operators of the simple language compare a number with a `String` that holds a number by their
values, as `<`, `<=`, `>` and `>=` (and `in` when the left value is a number) do since Camel 4.23. Before, when the two
were written differently, the `String` was converted to the type of the number and compared with `equals` (which also
compares the scale of a `BigDecimal`), or the two were compared as text. So `${header.amount} == '10'` is now `true`
for a `BigDecimal` of `10.00` (it was `false`, while `${header.amount} <= '10'` and `>= '10'` were `true`), and so are
`${header.count} == '3.0'` for an `Integer` of `3` and `${header.text} == 10` for a `String` of `10.00`. Spaces around
the number and an exponent (such as `' 10'` and `'1e1'`) are allowed, as in the `in` operator, and `<` and `>` compare
such a `String` by its exact value too (it was compared as a double). The same applies to `=~`, to `contains` on a
collection, and to the Java DSL and mock predicates such as `header("amount").isEqualTo("10")`. Two `String` values,
and a `Double` or `Float` compared with a `String`, compare as before. Use `equals` to compare the two values as text.

=== camel-diagram - a route diagram draws the steps of a route

A route diagram drew the `onException` clauses of a route among its steps, though they are not steps: a global
`onException` is copied into every route, and runs only while a failure is handled. A route diagram now draws only the
steps of the route: `camel cmd route-diagram`, the `route-diagram` dev console and its web component, and the route
diagrams dumped by `DumpRoutesStrategy`. Use `--errors` (`?errors=true` in the dev console, the `errors` attribute of
the `<camel-route-diagram>` web component, `errors` of `DumpRoutesStrategy` and `@EnableRouteDiagramDump`, and
`dumpRouteDiagramErrors` of `@CamelMainTest`) to draw them in a frame of their own below the steps, with where the
failures of the route go. A highlighted path that steps into an `onException` clause draws the frame as well.

The `<camel-topology-diagram>` web component no longer draws the error paths of the route topology as calls between
routes. With its new `errors` attribute it draws the routes reached only on error in a frame below the other routes.

=== camel-jbang - the default name of a run of a whole directory

`camel run --source-dir`, and `camel run` of a directory, without `--name` name the integration after the directory.
A folder of a project layout (`src`, `main`, `resources`, `java`, `camel` or `routes`) gives way to the first parent
with another name, and `.` is the working directory. Before, the name was the one of the first file, in the order that
the file system lists them, which could be any file, such as a Java class that a route uses. `--name`, and
`camel.main.name` in application.properties, still win.

=== camel-jbang - route-diagram, route-structure and route-topology read Java sources without compiling them

Given Java source files, `camel cmd route-diagram`, `camel cmd route-structure` and `camel cmd route-topology` (and the
`camel_render_route_diagram` MCP tool) now read the routes with the Java DSL parser, as `camel transform route` and
`camel overview` do, instead of compiling the sources and loading their routes in a short-lived Camel. A step the parser
cannot work out, such as a lambda or a processor, is shown as a placeholder, and the parts it could not read are
listed (with `--json`, as an `unread` array in the JSON). Use the new `--compile` option to compile the sources and
load their routes as before.

=== camel-diagram - a route diagram image draws the steps as cards

The PNG image of a route diagram draws each step as a card: an icon for the kind of step, in a circle of the color of
its kind, the kind in bold (such as `setBody`) and its detail below (such as `groovy: body.items`). Before, a step was
a box filled with the color of its kind, with its code (`setBody[groovy{body.items}]`) in one line. This applies to
the `dark`, `light` and `transparent` themes, in `camel cmd route-diagram`, `camel get history --diagram`,
`camel get error --diagram`, the `camel_render_route_diagram` MCP tool, the `route-diagram` developer console
(`format=png`) and the `RouteDiagramDumper`. A card is taller than a box, so the image of the same route is taller.
The ASCII and Unicode diagrams, the topology diagram and the web component do not change.

A custom color spec sets the colors of the cards with `card`, `cardBorder`, `cardText` and `cardDetail`. The `text`
color no longer applies to the steps of a route diagram (it still does to the topology diagram).

The `RouteDiagramRenderer(nodeWidth, fontSizeScaled, nodeTextPadding, metrics)` constructor is deprecated, as the text
of a card starts after its icon and `nodeTextPadding` is not used. Use `RouteDiagramRenderer(nodeWidth, fontSizeScaled,
metrics)` instead.

A program that lays out routes with `RouteDiagramLayoutEngine` and draws them with `RouteDiagramRenderer` must call
`engine.setCardStyle(true)` before `layoutRoute`. Without it, the cards get the height and text of the old boxes.

`camel cmd route-diagram` of a running application whose message history recorded the body types (such as in the dev
profile) shows them on the arrows, with longer arrows to hold them. Use `--body-types=false` for the diagram without
them.

`camel cmd route-diagram` of source files, and the `camel_render_route_diagram` MCP tool, check the routes as
`camel validate` does, and mark the steps with a problem: a yellow border and a callout beside the route (a numbered
list below a route with branches). An image of routes with problems is wider or taller. Use `--problems=false` for the
diagram without them.

`camel cmd route-diagram --output=diagram.png` without `--theme` now saves the PNG image in the `light` theme. Before,
the default theme was `unicode` also for a file, so the command saved the text diagram in `diagram.txt` instead. A
diagram in the terminal is still text (`unicode`) by default, and a file that does not end with `.png` still gets the
text diagram. Use `--theme=unicode` to save the text diagram.

The `camel_render_route_diagram` MCP tool also draws in the `light` theme when no theme is given (before, it failed
without a theme).

=== camel-jbang - export to Quarkus keeps the names in `quarkus.*` keys

`camel export --runtime=quarkus` wrote every `quarkus.*` key except `quarkus.hawtio.*` in dash case: an upper-case
letter became a dash and the lower-case letter, and an underscore became a dash. That also changed the names that a
key holds, such as a log category, a datasource name or a map key. Now only option names in camel case are written in
dash case, such as `quarkus.http.rootPath` as `quarkus.http.root-path`. A segment of the key that is quoted, that
starts with an upper-case letter, or that has no upper-case letter is kept as it is. So
`quarkus.log.category."com.example.MyRoute".level`, `quarkus.datasource.Orders.db-kind` and
`quarkus.datasource.order_history.db-kind` are no longer exported as
`quarkus.log.category."com.example.-my-route".level`, `quarkus.datasource.-orders.db-kind` and
`quarkus.datasource.order-history.db-kind`. The same applies to `camel run --runtime=quarkus`, which exports the
project in the same way.

A name in camel case that starts with a lower-case letter, such as the datasource `myOrders` or `my_DataSource`, still
looks like an option name and is written in dash case as a whole, underscores included (`my-orders`,
`my-data-source`). Quote it to keep it: `quarkus.datasource."myOrders".db-kind`.

=== camel-jbang - camel dirty removes only the files of processes that are gone

`camel dirty` counts, and `camel dirty --clean` removes, only the files named after the pid of a process that is gone,
such as `<pid>-status.json`. Before, it counted every other file in `~/.camel` too, and `--clean` removed them: the log of
a Spring Boot or Quarkus app that `camel run` runs (`<name>.log`), also while the app ran, the console output that the AI
tools keep, and the history of the AI prompt. Directories are not counted, and `--clean` counts only the files it
removed.

=== camel-huggingface - bounded chat histories, outputs that contain the word error, and no Content-Type for text-to-speech

The chat task keeps the history of each conversation in memory, one per memory ID (the `CamelChatMemoryId` header).
It used to keep every history for the life of the endpoint. It now keeps at most 1000 of them: once there are more
than 1000 memory IDs, the oldest conversations are evicted (the history that was updated least recently first), and a
message for an evicted memory ID starts a new conversation without its earlier messages. Set the new
`maxChatHistories` option to change the limit, or to `0` to keep every history as before.

Each history used to keep every message of its conversation. The new `maxHistoryMessages` option keeps the last 50
messages by default, besides the system prompt: the oldest messages are dropped first, so a long conversation no longer
sends its earliest turns to the model. Set it to `0` to keep every message as before. Its value must be `0` or at least
`2`: other values fail when the route starts.

A task no longer fails when its output has the text `"error"` in it. Only the error payload of the Python handler, a
JSON object with the single field `error`, fails the task. The message of the `RuntimeCamelException` is now
`Python inference failed:` followed by the error text, instead of the whole payload (automatic-speech-recognition used
`Python inference error:`).

The text-to-speech task no longer sets the `Content-Type` header to `application/octet-stream`, as its body is a DJL
`Audio` and not encoded audio bytes: it now removes the `Content-Type` header from the message, so the content type of
the request does not stay on the result. A route that relied on this header, for example to answer an HTTP request,
must set it itself after encoding the audio.

=== camel-djl - the model and translator of a custom model are looked up once

With a custom model (the `model` and `translator` options), the model and translator beans are looked up in the
registry at the first message and kept until the route is stopped. Before, they were looked up for every message. A
bean bound again under the same name is used after the route is restarted. A missing bean fails the message with a
`NoSuchBeanException` that names the bean, instead of a `NullPointerException`, and an endpoint without the `model`
or `translator` option fails to start.

The nlp applications take a `byte[]` or `InputStream` body as text, as well as a `String`.

=== camel-google-storage - a listed object that cannot be got is skipped

When the consumer lists the bucket, a failure to download one of the listed objects (for example access denied, or an
object whose encryption key the consumer cannot use) no longer fails the poll, as long as another listed object could
be got: the object is skipped, the other listed objects are consumed, and the skipped object is tried again by a later
poll. The failure is given to the exception handler of the consumer, with the object name in the message (by default a
`WARN` with the stack trace, at every poll that lists the object). Before, the poll failed, and as every poll lists the
whole bucket, one such object stopped the consumer from consuming any object. The poll still fails when none of the
listed objects could be got.

=== camel-mongodb-gridfs - the consumer routes a file once, offers the file of a failed exchange again and sets CamelFileName, and an unknown operation fails

With the `TimeStamp` (default) and `PersistentTimestamp` query strategies, the `mongodb-gridfs` consumer routed
every file uploaded since the consumer started again at each poll (every `delay`, 500 ms by default). The upload
date of the newest routed file is now kept between the polls, so a file is routed once. A route that relied on
receiving the same file repeatedly now receives it once.
These two strategies now skip a file whose upload date is older than that of a file already routed (as they did
before Camel 3.13), for example a file from a second uploader, or from a client with another clock, that becomes
visible later: GridFS sets the upload date on the client. Use `TimeStampAndFileAttribute` or
`PersistentTimestampAndFileAttribute` if such files must be routed: these strategies keep the start date in their
query, as before, and mark each file so that it is routed once.

When the exchange of a file fails, the consumer no longer marks the file as processed or moves its timestamp past the
file: it reports the failure to its exception handler (which logs it by default), and the next poll offers the file
again. Before, the failure was ignored and the file was never offered again. A failure that the route handles, such as
with a dead letter channel, still completes the file. With the `TimeStamp` and `PersistentTimestamp` query strategies,
the files of a poll are now routed in the order of their upload date, and the poll stops at a file whose exchange
fails, so a file that always fails holds back the files uploaded after it: handle such failures in the route, or use a
strategy with a file attribute, which goes on with the other files. Only the first failure of a file is reported to
the exception handler: when the file fails again at the next poll, the consumer logs the failure at `DEBUG`, so a file
that always fails is reported once (it is reported again when it fails after a poll that routed it or did not offer
it). The `exceptionHandler` and `bridgeErrorHandler` options now apply to the consumer; before, they had no effect.

The consumer now sets the `CamelFileName` header to the name of the file. A route that sends the message on to an
endpoint that uses this header, such as a `file` endpoint, now writes the file under that name instead of a generated
one. Remove the header before that endpoint to keep the generated name.

The consumer now closes the stream of the content of the file when the exchange is done. With stream caching turned off
(it is on by default), read the stream before the exchange is done.

The producer now fails with an `IllegalArgumentException` that lists the supported operations when the `operation`
option or the `CamelGridFsOperation` header is not `create`, `remove`, `findOne`, `listAll` or `count`. Before, a
misspelled operation, such as `findone`, did nothing and the exchange succeeded.

`listAll` now gives the id of each file as the hex string of its ObjectId, such as `665f1c2e8b3e4a5d6c7b8a90`. Before,
each line held `BsonObjectId{value=665f1c2e8b3e4a5d6c7b8a90}`.

=== camel-paho-mqtt5, camel-paho - consumers that share a client get the messages of their own topic

A client has a single callback, and each consumer set it on its client. The consumers of several endpoints that use
the same client (the `client` option) therefore all routed their messages through the consumer that started last, and
only that consumer subscribed again after the client reconnected. The consumers of a client now share one callback:
a message goes to the consumers whose topic filter matches it, also when a single consumer of the client is left
(only a client that the consumer created for itself still gives it every message), and each consumer subscribes again
after a reconnect.
When a consumer that uses a client set with the `client` option stops, it now unsubscribes its topic from the client
(unless `cleanStart=false`, or `cleanSession=false` for `camel-paho`, or another running consumer of the client uses
the same topic filter), as the client stays connected, and it uses the same client again when it starts again (it
created a client of its own before). This depends on the endpoint option, not on how the client was connected: set
`cleanStart=false` on the endpoints to keep the subscriptions of a client connected with `cleanStart=false`. With
`cleanStart=false` the stopped consumer's subscription stays on the connected client, so the broker keeps sending its
messages, which are dropped (with one WARN per topic, then at DEBUG) until the consumer starts again. With
`manualAcksEnabled=true` such a dropped message is also acknowledged, so it is gone from a session that the broker
keeps: left unacknowledged, it would count against `receiveMaximum` (for `camel-paho`, the broker's limit of
unacknowledged messages per client), and the broker would then send nothing more to any consumer of the client.
Consumers of one client whose topic filters overlap (such as `orders/#` and `orders/eu`, or the same filter) each get
a message that matches several filters, and a broker that sends one copy per matching subscription (MQTT allows it,
Apache ActiveMQ Artemis does it) makes each of them get every copy. With `manualAcksEnabled=true` each of them
acknowledges the message, so a message that failed in one of these routes is still acknowledged when another of them
processes it. Each subscribe of a filter makes the broker send the retained message of a matching topic again, so
consumers of the same filter get it again when another of them starts and each time they subscribe again after a
reconnect. Only the running consumers count when a consumer stops: with consumers of the same filter on one client,
one with `cleanStart=true` and one with `cleanStart=false` (`cleanSession` for `camel-paho`), stopping the durable one
and then the other unsubscribes the filter, which also removes the subscription that the durable consumer kept. Give
such consumers a client of their own.

=== Semantic image input and audit output

Semantic evaluations now recognize a selected `$\{body}` with an image `Content-Type`, and
self-describing `data:image/...` strings, as image input. Text-only experts reject these inputs
instead of treating encoded images as text. For binary image streams, Camel preserves the bytes
using a replayable stream cache; downstream code must not rely on the original stream class.
For the exact `$\{body}` selector, `audio/*` and `video/*` content types now fail before inference,
even for a `String` body. Select an explicitly textual field to evaluate text accompanying audio
or video. Content-Type normalization does not apply to other selectors such as `$\{bodyAs(byte[])}`;
use `$\{body}` for image bytes or select a media envelope or image data URL explicitly.
Ordinary strings without a media content type remain text.
The `$semanticInput` key is reserved for explicit media envelopes. TypeSafe AI rejects that
key in structured state, including nested objects; rename an application-state key that conflicts with it.

`SemanticAdapter` has an additive default `validateState(Object)` hook for validation shared by
all questions for one expert. Existing per-evaluation `validateInput` hooks still run; both finish
for every expert before any provider performs inference. A shared validation failure marks that
expert's group as invalid input in the audit.

The additive `SemanticAdapter.getMaxImageBytes()` method exposes an expert's configured per-image
byte limit, with zero meaning no advertised limit. Image-capable adapters should advertise a
positive limit and enforce it before inference. CLI and TUI local image loading now requires
that metadata; the configured value appears as `maxImageBytes` in each image operation's contract.

Semantic audit backends can opt into the additive `inputType` extension. Readers that do not
support it keep their existing projection. The `camel semantic audit --json` display omits image
base64 and reports metadata instead. Use `--output=<new-file>` to export the full returned JSON,
with `--include-input` for case/bulk requests that need captured input. Existing files are not overwritten.

=== camel-azure-storage-datalake - flush position is the final file length

The `flushToFile` operation now uses the `position` option or `CamelAzureStorageDataLakePosition` header
as the absolute final file length, as documented. Previously, it added this value to the current file length.
Routes that supplied only the number of appended bytes must now supply the total final length.
For example, after appending 20 bytes to a 100-byte file, use `120` instead of `20`.
When no position is supplied, the operation still uses the current committed file length.

=== camel-azure-storage-datalake - a listed file that cannot be got is skipped

When the consumer lists the file system, a failure to get one of the listed files (for example access denied by the ACL
of the file) no longer fails the poll, as long as another listed file could be got: the file is skipped, the other
listed files are consumed, and the skipped file is tried again by a later poll. The failure is given to the exception
handler of the consumer, with the file name in the message (by default a `WARN` with the stack trace, at every poll that
lists the file). A file deleted after the listing is skipped as well; before, it made the whole poll return no file.
Before, any other failure failed the poll, and as every poll lists the file system again, one such file stopped the
consumer from consuming any file. The poll still fails when none of the listed files could be got.

=== camel-azure-storage-datalake - streaming downloads honor ranges and request conditions

The `getFile` operation now honors the `CamelAzureStorageDataLakeFileRange` header, or the endpoint
`fileOffset` and `dataCount` options, when it returns an `InputStream`. Previously, these settings
only applied when the message body supplied an `OutputStream`; streaming reads returned the entire file.
This also affects consumers without `fileDir`: they now deliver the configured byte range instead of the whole file.
Remove the range header and options if your route needs the entire file.

The streaming path also honors the `CamelAzureStorageDataLakeRequestCondition` header, whether or not a range
is set. An ETag mismatch or an invalid lease can now fail a streaming read that previously ignored the condition.

=== camel-azure-storage-datalake - getFile writes only into an OutputStream body

The getFile operation now writes the file into the message body only when the body is an `OutputStream`. A body that
only converts to one, such as a `java.io.File`, a `java.nio.file.Path` or the file of a file consumer, is replaced by an
`InputStream` of the file, as any other body is. Previously such a file was overwritten with the remote file, also with
`noop=true` on the file consumer. To save a file locally, use the downloadToFile operation, or a `file` endpoint after
getFile.

=== camel-chatscript - the first message of a conversation is sent, and the headers are kept

The first message of a ChatScript endpoint, and every message with `resetChat=true`, used to start the conversation
with the text `null` and return the greeting of the bot as the reply, so the message itself was never sent. The
conversation now starts with an empty message, as the ChatScript protocol requires, and the message is then sent, so
the reply is the answer of the bot to it. A first message with an empty text only starts the conversation and still
returns the greeting.

With `resetChat=true` the `:reset` command is now sent for the user of the message (or for the `chatUserName` option
when the message has no user), not for the `chatUserName` option alone.

The producer now keeps the headers of the message, which it used to drop.

=== camel-milo - a Bad or Uncertain status is delivered by default

A `milo-client` consumer now delivers a data change with a Bad or Uncertain status by default, even when its value is
null; previously the default `omitNullValues=true` dropped it. `omitNullValues` now only omits a null value with a Good
status. A route may therefore receive a Bad status with a null value, also as the first notification of a monitored
item at startup and after a reconnect, for example `Bad_WaitingForInitialData` or the status of a `milo-server` item
that was not written yet. A route that uses the value must check `getStatusCode()` of the `DataValue` body first:
Camel's type converter returns `0` or `false` for a null value that is converted to a primitive type such as `double`
or `boolean`. To skip the startup notification, filter on its status code; a filter on `isGood()` would also drop the
Bad status of a failed sensor.

=== camel-milo - creating and deleting the monitored items of a consumer

Since 4.16, a `milo-client` consumer whose monitored item could not be created, for example because the node does not
exist, received nothing. Now it receives one `DataValue` with the bad status code. The
monitored item is then created again every `reconnectTimeout` (10 seconds by default) on the same connection, without
delivering anything for a failed attempt, until the server accepts it; its first value is the current value of the node.
A route restart or a connection that Camel rebuilds creates it again too.

A node ID with a namespace URI (`nsu=`) that is not in the namespace table of the server no longer takes the whole
client connection down, including the consumers of other nodes. Its consumer receives one `DataValue` with
`Bad_NodeIdUnknown`, like a node that does not exist.

The consumer now sets the `CamelMiloNodeId` header on every exchange, including a consumer with a single `node`.

Stopping a consumer now deletes its monitored items. Previously they stayed on the server while another endpoint kept
the shared connection open, so a restarted consumer received each value twice. While the server is unreachable, the
stop waits until the client's next attempt to reconnect has failed.

When the server removes the subscription that the consumers of a connection share, or the subscription times out on the
server, it is now created again in the existing session; previously the consumers stopped receiving values until the
connection was rebuilt for another reason. The monitored items deliver their current values again after the
subscription is recreated.

=== camel-milo - a producer request for a node with an unknown namespace URI

A `milo-client` write, method call or read, or a `milo-browse` browse, for a node ID with a namespace URI (`nsu=`) that
is not in the namespace table of the server no longer makes the client reconnect, which also recreated the
subscriptions of the consumers that share the connection. A write, read or browse answers the node like a node that
the server does not have, with the status code `Bad_NodeIdUnknown`, and the other nodes of a read or browse are not
affected. Previously an awaited write or read completed without an error and with a `null` body, and a browse failed
with a `NullPointerException`.

=== camel-milo - the consumer reports a lost connection and refreshes its values after it

The OPC UA client consumer delivers `Bad_NoCommunication` with a null value for every monitored node when
communication with the server is lost, and the current value of every node once communication is restored. Before,
the consumer delivered nothing during an outage, and values that did not change were not delivered again after it.

A consumer that starts while the server cannot be reached, or whose client cannot connect at all, for example because
of wrong credentials or an untrusted server certificate, delivers the status for its nodes too; the cause is in the
log. A connection that Camel rebuilds delivers it for every node of the connection. Each loss and each recovery
delivers one exchange per node.

A route that uses only the value of the `DataValue` body receives a null for these exchanges, which the type converters
