turn into `0` or `false`; a route that stores values checks the status code of the body first, for example with
`${body.statusCode.bad}`. A value can now arrive again after an outage, so a route that counts values, computes
differences or detects changes must tolerate repeats. The consumer's route also runs during an outage: an OPC UA write
that it makes while the client reconnects waits for the next reconnect attempt and can take effect after communication
is restored.

To keep the previous behaviour, filter the exchanges with a bad status out right after the consumer, for example with
`filter(simple("${body.statusCode.bad} == false"))`. This also drops the other bad statuses, such as the refusal of a
node, and the current values delivered after an outage still arrive.

=== camel-tensorflow-serving - a body that is not the request of the api fails

The `tensorflow-serving` producer converted the body to the protobuf request of the api, and dropped a body that
did not convert, such as text, JSON, bytes or a `Map`, without an error. The request was then sent without it, so
`predict`, `classify` and `regress` failed on the server with an error about the missing inputs, and `model-status`
and `model-metadata` ignored the body. Such a body, when it is not empty, now fails the exchange with an
`InvalidPayloadException` that names the request type of the api, before anything is sent. Build the protobuf request
before the endpoint, or remove a body that an earlier step left before calling `model-status` or `model-metadata`.

No body, a blank `String` or an empty `byte[]` still sends a request built from the options and headers, and a body
that is the request or its `Builder`, or an `Input` for `classify` and `regress`, is sent as before.

`model-metadata` asked for `signature_def` in addition to the metadata fields of a `GetModelMetadataRequest` body, so
a request that named `signature_def` asked for it twice. It now asks for `signature_def` only when the request names
no metadata field.

=== camel-milo - an awaited write, call or read that fails now fails the exchange

An awaited `milo-client` write, method call or read that fails at the service or transport level, for example a
request that times out (`Bad_Timeout`) or a lost connection, now fails the exchange and lets the route's error
handler run. Before, the exchange completed without an error and with a `null` body, silently discarding the failure.
A bad status for the node itself, such as `Bad_TypeMismatch` on a write, is not a failure of the operation and is
still returned as the body.

An awaited `call` that succeeds now returns the `CallMethodResult` as the exchange body. Before, the body was
always `null` even on success. A route that reads the result of an awaited call must be updated to read the
`CallMethodResult` from the body instead of expecting `null`.

=== camel-milo - a failed producer request keeps the connection

A `milo-client` write, method call or read, or a `milo-browse` browse, that fails (for example with `Bad_Timeout`, or
because the session was lost) no longer disconnects the client connection and rebuilds the subscriptions of the
consumers that share it. The failure is still set on the exchange of an awaited request. A lost session is recovered by
the client itself, and a session that the server no longer has rebuilds the connection, as before. The client recovers
a lost session with the endpoint and the server certificate it found when it connected, so a server that changes either
while it cannot be reached is reached again only when Camel rebuilds the connection, for example when the Camel context
restarts.

Producer requests and `MiloClientConnection.getOpcUaClient()` no longer wait for the subscription work on the same
connection to finish, such as a consumer that starts or stops, or the periodic attempt to create the monitored items
that the server refused. Before, they waited until the server answered that work, and while the network was down
silently, until the client noticed the lost session. While Camel rebuilds the connection, a producer request now fails
at once with an `IllegalStateException` ("No connected", caused by the failure that ended the connection) instead of
waiting for the rebuild to finish. The attempts to create the refused monitored items again now run on the
`MiloClientReconnect` thread instead of the `MiloClient` pool, so that a slow attempt no longer holds up the outcome of
the requests, which that pool completes; on a machine with one processor the pool has a single thread.

=== camel-milo - the reconnectTimeout of the components sets the delay between connection attempts

`setReconnectTimeout` on the `milo-client` and `milo-browse` components set the request timeout of every service call,
and the delay between two connection attempts stayed 10 seconds. It now sets that delay, which the new
`reconnectTimeout` option of both endpoints and components sets too; its default of 10000 milliseconds is the previous
delay, and a value of 0 or less is rejected. The option also sets how often the monitored items that the server
refused are created again: a lower value repeats the `CreateMonitoredItems` request for them, for example for a node ID
with a typo, more often while the connection lasts. To set the request timeout, use the `requestTimeout` option instead
(for example `camel.component.milo-client.requestTimeout`, or `getConfiguration().setRequestTimeout(...)` in Java).

=== camel-milo - the sessionTimeout of the components reaches their endpoints

A `sessionTimeout` configured on the `milo-client` or `milo-browse` component (for example
`camel.component.milo-client.sessionTimeout`) did not reach its endpoints, so their sessions used the default of Milo,
120000 milliseconds. The endpoints now use it, unless they set `sessionTimeout` themselves.

=== camel-kserve - a body that is not the request of the api fails

The `kserve` producer converted the body of `infer`, `model/ready` and `model/metadata` to the protobuf request of
the api, and dropped a body that did not convert, such as text, JSON, bytes or a `Map`, without an error. The request
was then sent without it, so `infer` sent a request with no inputs, and `model/ready` and `model/metadata` ignored the
body. Such a body, when it is not empty, now fails the exchange with an `InvalidPayloadException` that names the
request type of the api, before anything is sent. Build the protobuf request before the endpoint, or remove a body
that an earlier step left before calling `model/ready` or `model/metadata`.

No body, a blank `String` or an empty `byte[]` still sends a request built from the options and headers, a body that
is the request or its `Builder` is sent as before, and the `server/ready`, `server/live` and `server/metadata` apis
still do not use the body.

=== camel-milo - a write of a null body fails the exchange

A `milo-client` producer no longer writes a `null` body; the exchange now fails with an `InvalidPayloadException`,
whether or not the write is awaited. Before, it wrote a null value, which a server typically refused with
`Bad_TypeMismatch`, and an awaited write completed without an error and with that status as the body. To write a null
value, set the body to `Variant.NULL_VALUE` or to a `DataValue` that carries it. A method call with a `null` body still
calls the method without input arguments.

=== camel-infinispan - the key option of the producer takes precedence over the key header

The `infinispan` and `infinispan-embedded` producers now use the `key` option of the endpoint when it is set, and use the
`CamelInfinispanKey` header only when the endpoint does not set `key`. Previously the header took precedence over the
option, so a route that sent the events of an Infinispan consumer, which set `CamelInfinispanKey` to the key of the event,
to a producer with a fixed `key` worked on the key of the event.

Routes that set `key` on the endpoint and override it per message with the `CamelInfinispanKey` header must remove `key`
from the endpoint.

The `CamelInfinispanCacheName` header still takes precedence over the cache of the endpoint, so a route that sends the
events of an Infinispan consumer to a producer must still remove the `CamelInfinispan*` headers first, see _Key and cache
name headers_ in the component docs.

=== camel-aws2-athena - the SQL is taken from the message body only with useMessageBodyForSql

The `startQueryExecution` operation of the `aws2-athena` producer, which is its default operation, now takes the SQL to
run from the `CamelAwsAthenaQueryString` header, and when the header is not set, from the `queryString` option. It uses
the message body as the SQL only when the new `useMessageBodyForSql` option is set to `true`, like the option of the
same name in `camel-sql`. A non-empty body then takes precedence over the header and the option, as before. Previously
the message body always came first, so an endpoint configured with `queryString` used the message body as the SQL
whenever the message had one.

Routes that put the SQL in the message body, as the examples in the component documentation did, must set
`useMessageBodyForSql=true`, or set the SQL in the `CamelAwsAthenaQueryString` header or the `queryString` option
instead. Without SQL, the exchange fails with an `IllegalArgumentException` that names these options.

With `includeTrace=true`, the URI of the from endpoint in the SQL comment is now sanitized, as Camel does when it logs
an endpoint URI, and it is left out when the exchange has no from endpoint, which failed with a `NullPointerException`
before.

=== camel-aws2-athena - result headers are not taken as the input of a later operation

* The `CamelAwsAthenaOutputLocation` header set by getQueryExecution, by getQueryResults with `S3Pointer`, and by
  startQueryExecution with a `waitTimeout` is the file with the results of that query. A later startQueryExecution on
  the same message no longer uses it, and puts its results under its `outputLocation` option. Previously it put them
  under the result file of the earlier query.
* The `CamelAwsAthenaNextToken` header set by listQueryExecutions is now used only by a later listQueryExecutions, and
  the one set by getQueryResults only by a later getQueryResults for the same query. Previously the token was sent to
  the other operation, or for another query, which Athena rejects.
* A header that the route sets itself is used as before.
* A query execution without a result configuration, as with managed query results, no longer fails with a
  `NullPointerException`: the `CamelAwsAthenaOutputLocation` header is null.

=== camel-couchdb - the producer saves for the UPDATE method, and fails for an unknown method or a change of the consumer

The couchdb producer now saves the document of the body when the `CamelCouchDbMethod` header is `UPDATE`, the method
that the couchdb consumer sets for an insert or an update; before, it did nothing for `UPDATE`. A method other than
`GET`, `DELETE` and `UPDATE` now fails with a `CouchDbException` naming the method, where it did nothing before.

The body of the couchdb consumer is the change (its `seq`, `id` and `changes`), not the document, and the consumer
sets the `CamelCouchDbMethod` header. When the consumer's header and its change reach the producer, the producer now
fails with a `CouchDbException` that says the body is a change and not a document, instead of saving or deleting it.
A route from a couchdb consumer straight to a couchdb producer therefore fails, where it did nothing before. To copy
the document, get it first, with the `CamelCouchDbMethod` header set to `GET` and the `CamelCouchDbId` header that the
consumer sets, or set the body to the document; once the body is a document, remove the `CamelCouchDbMethod` header,
or set it to the method the producer must use. To store the change itself, remove the `CamelCouchDbMethod` header: the
producer then saves the change as a new document with a generated id, as before. In a route back to the database that
the consumer reads, each such save is a new change in turn, so that route writes without end.

With the `GET` method the producer no longer reads the body, so an empty body or a body that is not JSON no longer
fails it. A body that the producer cannot save or delete now fails with a message that says why: JSON text that is not
valid fails with an `InvalidPayloadException` whose cause is the parse error, a JSON array fails with a
`CouchDbException` that says the body must be one JSON object (before, `Could not save document [unknown reason]`),
and a `DELETE` of a body without `_id` and `_rev` fails with a `CouchDbException` instead of a `NullPointerException`.

=== camel-protobuf - a bytes field in a Map

When a protobuf message is converted to a `Map` (for example `getBody(Map.class)` after unmarshalling), the value of
a `bytes` field is now a `byte[]` with the content of the field. Previously it was the `String` that
`ByteString.toString()` returns, a description such as `<ByteString@1b6d3586 size=4 contents="...">`, which does not
contain the whole value. When a `Map` is marshalled, a `byte[]` value is accepted for a `bytes` field.

=== camel-jbang - where the CLI writes its log files

* The `log4j2.properties` of camel-jbang-core logs to the console only. It is the configuration that log4j finds on the
  classpath when nothing configures logging. It also wrote a log file into `~/.camel`, named after the `pid` system
  property, which only the CLI sets: a JVM that uses camel-jbang-core without the CLI (such as a test) wrote
  `~/.camel/${sys:pid}.log`, and `camel test run` left a `<pid>.log` there. The configuration that `camel run` uses with
  color is now `log4j2-color.properties`, next to `log4j2-no-color.properties`.
* The log files of the CLI (`<pid>.log`, `camel-export.log` and `camel-script.log`) are written into the Camel directory
  of the CLI, where `camel log` reads them, instead of `~/.camel` under `user.home`. The two differ when `user.home` is
  `?` (a user without a home, such as in a container), as the CLI then uses `$HOME`, and when a test gives the CLI a
  home of its own. The CLI sets the directory as the system property `camel.jbang.logDir`. A Quarkus app that
  `camel run` exports and runs also logs there.
* `camel run --logging-json` writes `<pid>.log` too, as `camel run` does with and without color. The JSON is for the
  console only: the file has the pattern that `camel log`, the TUI and the AI tools read, the same as without color.
  Before, the file stayed empty with JSON logging, so they showed no log.

=== camel-jbang-mcp - runtime tools that changed behaviour

Some runtime tools of the Camel CLI MCP server now do what their description says, which changes what a client sees:

* `camel_runtime_receive` receives a message in one call: it starts a consumer on the endpoint, waits up to
  `timeoutSeconds` (default 10, at most 60) for a message, returns it and stops the consumer. It returns one message
  by default; `limit` returns up to that many, and `received` counts every message the consumer took, returned or
  not. Before, it started a consumer that kept running, took the messages from the routes, and returned none of
  them. It is now marked destructive, so the `read-write` access level (`camel.mcp.security.access-level`) no longer
  offers it, only `admin` does. It runs one call at a time per process, and refuses to run while `camel cmd receive`
  is receiving from the same process.
* `camel_runtime_memory_leak` with `command=start` and no `mode` makes one recording (60 seconds by default) and
  returns at once; get the results with `query` once it is done. Before, it made two recordings and blocked for about
  90 seconds, longer than many MCP clients wait. Pass `mode=dual` for the previous behaviour, or make the two
  recordings yourself and use the new `compare` command. A `duration` that is not a whole number of seconds is
  refused.
* `camel_runtime_route_dump`, `camel_runtime_route_structure` and `camel_runtime_route_analysis` return the routes
  that `routeId` names; they returned every route whatever `routeId` was.

=== camel-jbang - the AI write and edit tools repair YAML shapes that have one reading

The `camel_write_file` and `camel_edit_file` tools used to refuse content with a YAML shape that a model often gets
wrong but that has only one reading, such as `steps:` written at the column of its EIP, or an option at the column of
the EIP it belongs to. Such content is now repaired and written, and `repaired` in the answer lists the repairs.
Content with more than one reading is refused as before.

`camel_edit_file` now matches lines to find that are not in the file as given, as when a model writes them from memory,
to the closest lines of the file when exactly one place matches well. It changes there the lines the model changed and
keeps the file's version of the other lines, and the lines of the file the model did not have. The edit stands only
when its result is valid; otherwise the answer is the one of a miss, with the lines the file has there. `matchedLines`
in the answer names the lines it matched.

A write of the content the file already has, and an edit whose replacement gives the text the file has, answer with
the status `unchanged` instead of `overwritten` or `edited`. Nothing is written, so a running integration does not
reload. When one of several edits misses, the answer shows the file as it is in `fileWindow` and says that none of the
edits was written, also not the ones before it that matched.

`camel_get_log` marks the records from before the last reload of the routes with `beforeLastReload`, with a
`reloadNote` first in the answer. The note of `camel_get_errors` on errors from before the last reload is now the first
key of its answer, and says how many exchanges failed since.

=== camel-jbang - the console output of an integration that the AI tools start

`camel_run` and `camel_control restart` send the console output of the integration they start to
`~/.camel/<pid>-run.log`, as `camel run --background` does, and remove it when the integration ends. It went to
`~/.camel/camel-launch-<number>.log` or `camel-restart-<number>.log`, which stayed until the MCP server exited. An
integration that still runs when the server exits keeps the file while it runs: `camel stop` removes it, and
`camel dirty --clean` once the integration has ended.

=== camel-main - a properties file that changed while reloading applies its beans and components

With live reloading (such as `camel run --dev`), a change to application.properties now applies its configuration:

* The file is matched to the properties file the application loaded, also when its location is written another way:
  `camel run --source-dir=.` loads `file:./application.properties`, and the file watcher reports
  `file:application.properties`. Before, the two did not match, so the file was added once more for its property
  placeholders, but its `camel.component.*`, `camel.dataformat.*` and `camel.language.*` options were not applied.
* A new bean (`camel.beans.*`) is created and bound, before the components are configured again, so a component can
  refer to it (`#bean:name`), and a component that is created later can autowire it. With camel-jbang, the class of
  the bean is downloaded when camel-jbang knows it, such as the Artemis client. A dependency that is added to
  `camel.jbang.dependencies` while the application runs is not downloaded until a restart (CAMEL-25833).
* A bean that exists is left as it is, as components may hold it. When its properties change, a `WARN` says that a
  restart is needed.
* A properties file that is created while the application runs is applied as well.

`PropertiesReload` has a new default method, `onChanged`, which a properties file that changed calls with the properties
that changed. A context reload (such as for secret rotation) still calls `onReload`.

=== camel-jbang - camel run keeps the log of the previous run

When `camel run` ends, its log is no longer deleted. It is kept as `~/.camel/<name>-previous.log`, by the name of the
integration, with `~/.camel/<name>-previous.json`, which says when and how the run ended. The next run of that name that
ends replaces both, and at most the last 10 MB of a log are kept. `camel log <name> --previous` prints the log, like
`kubectl logs --previous` in Kubernetes. The log of an integration that was killed is kept the same way, the next time a
previous run is looked up. `camel dirty --clean` cleans these files too.

=== camel-jbang - the AI tools wait for Camel to start, and read the log of the previous run

* `camel_run` and `camel_control restart` answer that the integration started once its CamelContext has started. They
  answered as soon as the status file of the integration appeared, which happens while Camel starts and before the
  routes start. An integration whose route then failed to start was answered as started, and stopped right after.
  Now the answer is the failure, with its cause.
* `camel_get_log` of an integration that is not running answers with the log of its previous run (see
  `camel log --previous`), and its message says how that run ended. `previous=true` asks for that log also when the
  integration runs again. `camel_get_errors` of an integration that is not running says how its previous run ended.

=== camel-toon - the lengthMarker option is removed (Breaking change)

`camel-toon` now uses JToon 2.0.7, which follows a newer version of the TOON specification.

The `lengthMarker` option of the TOON data format is removed, because the TOON specification no longer has the `[#N]`
array length marker. Remove the option from the data format in Java, XML and YAML routes. TOON text with a `[#N]` array
header, such as the output of a data format with `lengthMarker=true`, now fails to unmarshal in both strict and
non-strict mode; in Camel 4.23 it already failed in strict mode (the default). To read such data, remove the `#` from
its array headers (`tags[#2]:` becomes `tags[2]:`).

With `strict=false`, unmarshalling no longer returns `null` or a partial result for malformed TOON. Only the non-strict
recoveries of the TOON specification apply, such as accepting an array whose length differs from the declared length;
other malformed TOON fails with `IllegalArgumentException`, as in strict mode.

=== camel-as2 - consumers sharing a port use their own asynchronous MDN settings

`as2://server/listen` consumers that share a `serverPortNumber` now deliver asynchronous MDNs with their own
`mdnUserName`, `mdnPassword`, `mdnAccessToken` and `asyncMdnAllowedHosts`, selected by the matched `requestUriPattern`
in the same way as the signing material. Previously the values of the first consumer configured on the port were used
for every consumer on it, and the values set on the other consumers were ignored. A consumer that relied on inheriting
them now has to set them itself.

This applies to `asyncMdnAllowedHosts` as well. A consumer without its own list used to inherit the first consumer's
list, so asynchronous MDNs to other hosts were refused; it now delivers to the address the sender gives, without
credentials. A deployment that counted on the inherited list as a restriction has to set it on each consumer.

A consumer whose `requestUriPattern` is `/` now also uses its own keys, certificates and asynchronous MDN settings when
another consumer created the shared server connection; before, it used the first consumer's.

=== camel-arangodb - the operation option is required, and a Collection body is saved as several documents

A route that sends to an `arangodb` endpoint without the `operation` option, set on the endpoint or on the component
configuration, now fails to start with a message that names the option. Before, the route started and each message
failed with `Operation not supported. Value: null`.

The `SAVE_DOCUMENT` operation now inserts a `Collection` body, such as a `List` of documents, as several documents, as
it does with the `CamelArangoDbMultiInsert` header, and the body after it is a `MultiDocumentEntity`. Before, without
the header, ArangoDB inserted the documents and the exchange then failed, so a redelivery inserted them again.

=== camel-aws2-sqs - the CloudEvent source is the queue name

The `aws2-sqs:application-cloudevents` data type transformer now sets the `CamelCloudEventSource` header to
`aws.sqs.queue.` followed by the name of the queue the message was received from. Before, it used the receipt handle
of the message, which changes each time a message is received, so every event had a source of its own.

The queue name comes from the new `CamelAwsSqsQueueName` header, which the `aws2-sqs` consumer now sets on each
message. When the endpoint names the queue by its ARN, the header holds the queue name part of the ARN, and when the
`queueUrl` option is set, the last part of its path, as that is the queue the consumer reads. A message without this
header, such as one that does not come from the `aws2-sqs` consumer, gets no CloudEvent source from the transformer,
also when it has a `CamelAwsSqsReceiptHandle` header. A route that relies on the old source can set the
`CamelCloudEventSource` header itself after the transformer.

=== camel-aws2-sqs - the consumer receives up to 10 messages per poll

The `maxMessagesPerPoll` option of the `aws2-sqs` consumer now defaults to 10, the most that SQS returns to one receive
request, so each poll now takes up to 10 messages. Before, it defaulted to 0, documented as unlimited, which the
consumer turned into a receive request for one message, so each poll took one message. As before, a value above 10 is
split into receive requests of at most 10 messages, and 0 or a negative value receives one message per poll.

The messages of a poll are routed one after the other, and the visibility timeout of each one runs from its receive.
With a slow route and without `extendMessageVisibility`, the last messages of a poll can become visible again before
their turn comes, and SQS then delivers them a second time. Enable `extendMessageVisibility` together with
`visibilityTimeout`, which keeps every message of the poll invisible until it is processed, or use a visibility timeout
that covers the processing of 10 messages.

With a FIFO queue, a poll can now receive several messages of the same message group: when one of them fails, the next
ones of its group in the same poll are still routed and deleted, and the failed message is delivered again after them.

Set `maxMessagesPerPoll=1` to keep the previous behaviour.

=== camel-aws2-ddb - the x-struct transformer reads bytes as JSON text

The `aws2-ddb:application-x-struct` data type transformer now reads a `byte[]` body as JSON text, with the charset of
the exchange, as it reads a stream or a file and takes a String as it is, so the same JSON gives the same String
whatever the type of the body. Before, Gson wrote the bytes as a JSON array of numbers. A stream or a file body, which
failed before, is now read as text as well.

=== camel-ftp, camel-sftp, camel-ftps, camel-mina-sftp - sendNoop also applies to the consumer

The `sendNoop` option now also applies to the check a consumer makes before each poll, not only to the pre-write check
of a producer, and its label is now `common,advanced` instead of `producer,advanced`. The default is still `true`, so a
consumer sends a noop before each poll as before.

A consumer endpoint that already sets `sendNoop=false`, which was ignored before (for example a URI shared by `from`
and `to`), now skips that noop and only checks whether the client still regards itself as connected. Remove
`sendNoop=false` from the consumer endpoint to keep the noop before each poll.

For ftp and ftps the client does not notice a connection that the server has closed, so such a consumer finds it only
when the next operation fails, during the poll or while processing a file. After a failure caused by an I/O error, the
consumer sends a noop before the next poll, and connects again if the noop fails. If the FTP server closes idle
connections and the `delay` between polls is longer than its idle timeout, every other poll fails and is logged before
the consumer connects again. Keep `sendNoop=true` for such a consumer. Whatever `sendNoop` is, the ftp and ftps
consumer still sends a noop before it renames or deletes a file on the server, as it does for each processed file
unless `noop=true`.

For sftp and mina-sftp the client notices a closed connection, but not one that dropped silently. For sftp the noop is
a keep-alive message that does not wait for a reply, so it does not find such a connection either. For mina-sftp the
noop is a request that waits for the reply, so with `sendNoop=false` such a connection is found by the next poll,
which fails and is logged before the consumer connects again. For sftp and mina-sftp, set `serverAliveInterval` to let
the client close such a connection itself, so that the consumer connects again before the poll.

=== camel-ftp, camel-mina-sftp, camel-azure-files, camel-smb - a readLock the component does not implement fails the consumer

The consumers of `ftp`, `ftps` and `sftp` (all three in `camel-ftp`), `mina-sftp` and `azure-files` support the read
locks `none`, `rename` and `changed`, and `readLock=false` and `readLock=off` still mean `none`. Any other `readLock`
value now fails when the consumer is created, with
`IllegalArgumentException: ReadLock invalid: <value>, must be one of: none,rename,changed`, so the route does not start.
With `poll` or `pollEnrich`, the exchange fails with a `FailedToCreateConsumerException` caused by this
`IllegalArgumentException` when it polls the endpoint. Before, such a value was accepted and the consumer ran without
any read lock, so it could pick up a file that was still being written. This concerns the read locks that only the
`file` component implements (`markerFile`, `fileLock`, `idempotent`, `idempotent-changed` and `idempotent-rename`), and
misspelled values. The `readLock` option is not checked when a custom `exclusiveReadLockStrategy` or `processStrategy`
is set, as it does not apply then. The `smb` consumer already failed on these values, and on `false` and `off`, and is
unchanged.

To keep the previous behavior, remove the `readLock` option, or set `readLock=none`. To keep a file from being picked
up while it is being written, use `readLock=changed` or `readLock=rename`.

The `readLock` option of these endpoints, and of `smb`, now lists only `none`, `rename` and `changed` in the component
documentation, the catalog and the Java Endpoint DSL, instead of all the read locks of the `file` component. The
endpoint validation of the catalog, as used by `camel-report-maven-plugin:validate`, now reports `markerFile`,
`fileLock`, `idempotent`, `idempotent-changed` and `idempotent-rename` as invalid values of `readLock` on these
endpoints, as it already did for `false` and `off`.

The following options only apply to read locks of the `file` component, did nothing on these endpoints, and are no
longer listed in the component documentation, the catalog and the Java Endpoint DSL:

* `ftp`, `ftps`, `azure-files` and `smb`: `readLockDeleteOrphanLockFiles`, `readLockRemoveOnCommit` and
  `readLockRemoveOnRollback`.
* `sftp` and `mina-sftp`: the same three options, and `readLockIdempotentReleaseAsync`,
  `readLockIdempotentReleaseAsyncPoolSize`, `readLockIdempotentReleaseDelay` and
  `readLockIdempotentReleaseExecutorService`, which `ftp`, `ftps`, `azure-files` and `smb` already did not list.

Java Endpoint DSL code that calls the builder methods of these options, such as
`sftp(...).readLockIdempotentReleaseDelay(1000)`, no longer compiles: remove the calls. An endpoint URI that sets
these options still works: the value is still set on the endpoint, and still has no effect. The endpoint validation of
the catalog, as used by `camel-report-maven-plugin:validate`, now reports them as unknown options.

=== camel-cometd - the CORS filter allows credentials only for listed origins

With `crossOriginFilterOn=true`, the CORS responses now include `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true` only when
`allowedOrigins` lists the origins explicitly, without any `*` or `null`. When `allowedOrigins` is not set, is `*`, or
has an entry with a wildcard such as `https://*` or an entry `null`, the origin is still allowed, but without credentials. Previously credentials
were allowed for every origin. A cross-origin client that sends cookies or other credentials must have its origin
listed in `allowedOrigins`.

=== camel-neo4j - the similarity search gives the score, and the CamelNeo4jLabel header and query option are deprecated

`VECTOR_SIMILARITY_SEARCH` now adds the similarity score of each node to its `Map` in the body, as a `Double` under the
`score` key. It replaces a property of the node named `score`; the records of the Neo4j driver in the
`CamelNeo4jQueryResultListNeo4jNodes` header keep both. The body of the search can now also be a `List` or an array of
any numbers, such as a `List<Double>`. A body that is not a vector, such as text, now fails with an
`InvalidPayloadException` instead of a `NullPointerException` or a type conversion error.

`RETRIEVE_NODES_AND_UPDATE_WITH_CYPHER_QUERY` with a query whose first column is not a node, such as a property or a
count, now gives each record as a `Map` of its columns. Before, it failed with `Cannot coerce ... to Node`.

`CREATE_NODE` without a body now fails with an `InvalidPayloadException` instead of a `NullPointerException`.

The `CamelNeo4jLabel` header and the `query` option are deprecated: the component never used them. `CREATE_VECTOR` takes
the label of the node from the `label` option (`Embedding` when it is not set), and the Cypher operations take the query
from the message body.

The catalog now declares the `CamelNeo4jQueryResultRetrieveSize` header and the counters of the write operations (such
as `CamelNeo4jQueryResultNodesCreated`) as `Integer`, the type the producer always set, and
`CamelNeo4jQueryResultListNeo4jNodes` as a `List` of the `Record` of the Neo4j driver.

=== camel-tahu - a Device producer uses the client of its started Edge Node producer

A Device producer (`tahu-edge:group/edgeNode/device`) no longer creates the client of its Edge Node: only the Edge
Node producer creates it, from the options of the Edge Node endpoint (`deviceIds`, `primaryHostId`, `servers`). A
Device route that starts before the Edge Node route, or while it is stopped, therefore no longer fails with a
`NullPointerException` when the Device URI has no `deviceIds`. Sending to a Device endpoint while no producer of its
Edge Node endpoint is started now fails the exchange with an `IllegalStateException`; before, the data was dropped.

=== camel-tahu - one exchange for each Sparkplug message, and the payload body of the Edge Node producer

The `tahu-host` consumer gave two exchanges for each Sparkplug message (three for an `NDATA`). It now gives one. The
exchange of a message with metrics now keeps the `CamelTahuMessageType`, `CamelTahuEdgeNodeDescriptor`,
`CamelTahuMessageTimestamp`, `CamelTahuMessageSequenceNumber` and `CamelTahuMessageUUID` headers next to the
`CamelTahuMetric.<name>` headers; before, the metric headers replaced them. The exchange of each metric is unchanged:
it has no `CamelTahuMessageType` header.

The `tahu-edge` producer publishes a message body that is not a `SparkplugBPayload`, such as a text or a byte array, as
the body of the payload, whose metrics are made from the `CamelTahuMetric.<name>` headers. Before, such a body failed
with an `InvalidPayloadException`. A body that does not convert to a byte array still fails.

=== camel-aws - producers without an operation, and headers of the wrong type

The producers of `aws2-ec2`, `aws2-ecs`, `aws2-eks`, `aws2-mq`, `aws2-msk`, `aws2-rekognition`, `aws2-step-functions`,
`aws2-transcribe`, `aws-bedrock-agent` and `aws-config` now fail with an `IllegalArgumentException` that says to set
the `operation` option or the operation header, when neither is set. Before, they failed with a `NullPointerException`.

A header that holds a value of the wrong type is no longer dropped: the request is not sent, and the exchange fails
with an `IllegalArgumentException` that names the header. This applies to `CamelAwsEKSVPCConfig`,
`CamelAwsMSKBrokerNodesGroupInfo`, `CamelAwsMQBrokerUsers` and `CamelAwsMQConfigurationID`. `CamelAwsMQConfigurationID`
also accepts the id of the configuration as a String. In `aws2-ec2`, `CamelAwsEC2InstanceType` also accepts the AWS
value of the instance type (such as `t2.micro`), and `CamelAwsEC2InstancesIds` a String of ids separated by commas.
In `aws2-rekognition`, `CamelAwsRekognitionFacialAttributes` and `CamelAwsRekognitionDetectionAttributes` also accept
the names of the attributes as Strings.

In `aws2-transcribe`, a List in the `CamelAwsTranscribeVocabularyPhrases` header is now sent as one phrase per element
by createVocabulary, and updateVocabulary now sends the phrases too. deleteVocabulary and deleteTranscriptionJob now
set the response as the message body, as the other delete operations do.

The catalog and the endpoint DSL of `aws-bedrock-agent` listed the headers of `aws-bedrock-agent-runtime`. They now
list the headers of `aws-bedrock-agent`, so the `CamelAwsBedrockAgentRuntime*` header names are no longer in the
header builder of the `aws-bedrock-agent` endpoint DSL.

The `CamelAwsEKSDescription` header of `aws2-eks` is removed, together with the `EKS2Constants.DESCRIPTION` constant and
its method in the header builder of the endpoint DSL. No operation of the component read it.

The `aws2-mq` component, which manages Amazon MQ brokers, is now in the `management` category instead of `messaging`.

In `aws2-step-functions`, the `CamelAwsStepFunctionsExecutionInput` header of startExecution and startSyncExecution
must be JSON text (a String, a byte array or a stream). A value of another type, such as a `Map`, was sent as its
`toString()`, which is not JSON; now the exchange fails. startSyncExecution now sets the
`CamelAwsStepFunctionsExecutionStatus` header to the status of the execution (`SUCCEEDED`, `FAILED` or `TIMED_OUT`),
and the `CamelAwsStepFunctionsExecutionError` and `CamelAwsStepFunctionsExecutionCause` headers when it failed. The
exchange still does not fail when the execution did not succeed.

In `aws-bedrock-agent`, the `CamelAwsBedrockAgentKnowledgeBaseId`, `CamelAwsBedrockAgentDataSourceId` and
`CamelAwsBedrockAgentIngestionJobId` headers now override the `knowledgeBaseId`, `dataSourceId` and `ingestionJobId`
options, as the headers of the other AWS components do. Before, a header was used only when the option was not set.

=== Data formats - bodies they cannot write fail, and stream bodies

Several data formats wrote a body they cannot handle as empty or wrong output, or returned a stream that was closed
by the time the next step read it. They now behave as follows:

* `bindyCsv`, `bindyFixed` and `bindyKvp`: marshal of a body that is not an object of the model class, a List of them,
  or a Map of the model class names to the objects fails with an `InvalidPayloadException`, instead of writing an
  empty line. `bindyFixed` now takes a Map as a record of model class names, as `bindyCsv` and `bindyKvp` do.
* `flatpack`: marshal writes a single Map as one row, and fails with an `InvalidPayloadException` for a body that is
  neither a Map nor a List of Maps. The name of the data format, as shown in JMX and in error messages, is now
  `flatpack` instead of `flatback`.
* `protobufJackson`: a body that is already JSON text (a String with a JSON object or array, bytes, a stream or a
  file) is no longer written as it is and labelled `application/protobuf`. It is marshalled as any other body.
* `toon`: marshal reads a `byte[]`, a stream or a file as JSON text, as it does a String.
* `base64`: unmarshal decodes the data right away and returns a stream cache, instead of a stream that decoded
  while it was read.
* `mimeMultipart`: unmarshal of a message that is not a multipart leaves the body as it was, instead of setting it
  to a stream.
* `tarFile`: with `usingIterator`, each entry is copied into a stream cache, as `zipFile` does, so a split that is
  not streaming gets the content of each entry. Marshal takes the size of the entry from the body, not from the
  `CamelFileLength` header. The messages of the `tarFile` and `zipFile` iterators have the size of their entry in
  `CamelFileLength`, instead of the size of the archive.
* `zipFile` and `tarFile`: unmarshal without `usingIterator` skips the folder entries, so an archive of a folder with
  one file gives that file. An archive with only folder entries now fails.
* `iso8583`: the `configFile` is loaded with the Camel resource loader, so `file:` and `http:` work, and a
  `configFile` that is set but not found fails at startup. Without the `isoType` option and the
  `CamelIso8583IsoType` header, unmarshal fails with a message that says so.
* `xmlSecurity`: the catalog now gives the defaults the data format uses: `secureTagContents` is `true`, and
  `keyCipherAlgorithm` has no default. The behaviour is unchanged.

=== camel-influxdb2 - a point without fields fails the exchange, and InfluxDB is called at startup only to create the organization or bucket

A `Point` without a field that has a value (no field at all, or only fields whose value is `null`, NaN or infinite) now
fails the exchange with a `CamelInfluxDb2Exception`. This also applies to a `Point` converted from a `Map` body and to
each point of a `Points` body. The InfluxDB client skipped such a point, so the exchange completed while nothing was
written.

The endpoint now looks the organization up when it starts only when `autoCreateOrg` is `true` (the default), and the
bucket only when `autoCreateBucket` is `true` (the default). With both options set to `false` it no longer calls
InfluxDB when it starts, so the route starts while InfluxDB is down, and an organization or a bucket that does not exist
fails the first write instead of being looked up. With `autoCreateOrg=false` and `autoCreateBucket=true` the
organization is looked up only when the bucket must be created, and the endpoint fails to start with a message that
names them when that organization does not exist.

=== camel-mail - the text of multipart mails, the charset of sent text, and producers on imap or pop3

A multipart mail without a text part, such as one with only a PDF attachment, now converts to a `String` or an
`InputStream` as `null`. Previously it converted to `jakarta.mail.internet.MimeMultipart@...`, and the type converter
then remembered that conversion, so every later multipart mail in the CamelContext converted to such a text too. A
`Message` whose content is null also converts to `null`.

A text part that Jakarta Mail has no content handler for, such as `text/csv` or `text/calendar`, now converts to its
text in the charset of the part. Previously it converted to the name of a stream.

When the `contentType` of the mail producer is `text/*` and gives no charset (not in the option, the header or the
`CamelCharsetName` exchange property), the Content-Type of the sent mail now declares the charset the text is encoded
in: `mail.mime.charset`, else the default charset of the JVM (UTF-8). The bytes sent are unchanged. Previously the
Content-Type had no charset, so a reader decoded the text as US-ASCII. This also applies when
`ignoreUnsupportedCharset=true` drops an unsupported charset.

An endpoint on `imap`, `imaps`, `pop3` or `pop3s` used as a producer now fails when the producer is created. Previously
every send failed with `NoSuchProviderException: invalid provider`. Use `smtp` or `smtps` to send mails.

A mail read with `mapMailMessage=false` can now be sent with `smtp`: a `MimeMessage` body that comes from a mail store
is sent as a copy, as the messages of a store are read-only. A `MimeMessage` built by the route is sent as it is.

Setting the body of the message of a mail consumer, also to `null`, now sticks. Previously a `null` body gave the
content of the mail again when it was read next.

=== camel-smpp - the body of the producer, InOut, and unknown commands

After an InOut send, the message now keeps its body and the headers set before the send, and the result headers, such
as `CamelSmppId`, are added. Previously the message had only the result headers and no body.

A `CamelSmppCommand` value that is not a command now fails with an `IllegalArgumentException`. Previously the body was
sent as an SMS (SubmitSm). The names are case-sensitive, and a message without the header is still sent with SubmitSm.

SubmitSm, SubmitMulti and ReplaceSm read the body once, so a stream body is no longer sent as an empty short message.
A body that is not a `String` or a `byte[]`, such as a stream or a file, is replaced on the message by the value it is
converted to. A message without a body fails with an `SmppException` caused by an `InvalidPayloadException`, instead of
a `NullPointerException`.

With the `dataCoding` or `alphabet` option set to an 8-bit value, and neither the `CamelSmppDataCoding` nor the
`CamelSmppAlphabet` header set, a `byte[]` body is now sent as it is, as with the headers. Previously its bytes were
decoded as text, so bytes that are not text became `?`. A text body is still encoded with the `encoding` option. A
message whose data coding is 8-bit is now split by its length in bytes.

`SmppSmCommand.getShortMessage(Message)` now declares `throws SmppException`.

=== camel-influxdb - a query error fails the exchange, and the operation is checked at startup

InfluxDB answers a query it cannot run, such as a query on a database that does not exist, with the error inside the
result. The producer used to set that `QueryResult` as the body, and the route continued as if the query had
succeeded. An error in the result of the query, or of one of its statements, now fails the exchange with a
`CamelInfluxDbException` whose message holds the error of the server.

The `operation` option now lists its values (`insert`, `query` and `ping`) and is not case-sensitive, so
`operation=QUERY` runs a query. An endpoint with another operation now fails to start, where it used to start and fail
every message with an `IllegalArgumentException`.

=== camel-paho, camel-paho-mqtt5 - a body that does not convert to bytes

The `paho` and `paho-mqtt5` producers now fail with an `InvalidPayloadException` that names the type of the body for a
body that does not convert to a `byte[]`, such as a `Map`, a POJO or no body. Previously they failed with a
`NullPointerException` with no message.

=== camel-spring-ai-chat - File, Path and stream bodies, media without a user message, and outputFormat

A `java.io.File` or `java.nio.file.Path` body of an image, audio, video or document is now sent as media, like a
`WrappedFile`. An `InputStream` body is sent as media when the `CamelSpringAiChatMediaType`, `CamelFileContentType` or
`Content-Type` header, or the extension in the `CamelFileName` header, names an image, audio, video or document (PDF,
Word, Excel) media type. Previously both were sent as their text. A text file, a file with an unknown extension, and a
stream with another or no media type are still sent as text.

A media body (`byte[]`, `File`, `Path`, `InputStream`, `WrappedFile` or `List<WrappedFile>`) without the `userMessage`
option or the `CamelSpringAiChatUserMessage` header now fails with an `IllegalArgumentException` that names them, before
the media is read. Previously it failed inside Spring AI with "text cannot be null or empty".

With `outputFormat` set to `BEAN`, `MAP` or `LIST`, the format instructions are now appended to the user message, or to
the last user message of `CHAT_MULTIPLE_MESSAGES` and `CHAT_SINGLE_MESSAGE_WITH_PROMPT`. Previously they replaced the
text of the user message, so the model did not get the question, and they were an extra user message after a
conversation or a prompt template.

`CHAT_MULTIPLE_MESSAGES` now takes `String` elements in the list and sends each one as a `UserMessage`. Another element
fails with an `IllegalArgumentException`. Previously both failed with a `ClassCastException`.

=== camel-spring-rabbitmq - the content type and the correlation id of a consumer reply

The reply of a `spring-rabbitmq` consumer used to take the content type of the request whenever the route left the
`CamelSpringRabbitmqContentType` header as the request set it. Now, when the route leaves the header unchanged:

* A `String` reply keeps the content type of the request, as before.
* A `byte[]` or stream reply keeps it too, unless it is a text content type such as `text/plain`. It is then sent as
  `application/octet-stream`, so the requester receives a `byte[]` and does not decode the bytes as text.
* Any other reply body gets the content type that the message converter derives from it.

A content type that the route sets is used as it is. A reply whose body is a Spring AMQP `Message` now gets the
correlation id of the request when it has none, so the requester no longer times out.

The producer is unchanged: a route that changes the type of the body of a consumed message and sends it on should
remove the `CamelSpringRabbitmqContentType` header.

=== camel-elasticsearch - built requests, search bodies, DeleteIndex and file bodies

A request of the Elasticsearch client in the message body (an `IndexRequest`, `UpdateRequest`, `GetRequest`,
`DeleteRequest`, `SearchRequest`, `MsearchRequest`, `MgetRequest`, `BulkRequest` or `DeleteIndexRequest`) is now sent
as it is, like its builder. Before, a built `IndexRequest` was written as the document itself, with a generated id, a
built `UpdateRequest` failed without the id header, and the other built requests failed with "Wrong body type".

The `Search` operation now uses the whole search request of a JSON text or `Map` body: its `size`, `from`, `sort`,
aggregations, `_source` and the other members are no longer dropped, so the hits returned can change. A member that
is not part of a search request now fails the exchange with an error that names it; before, it was dropped like every
member other than `query`. A JSON object with a single member other than `query`, such as `{"match": {...}}`, is
still taken as the query alone. The `size` and `from` headers or options, when set, still override those of the body.

The `DeleteIndex` operation now deletes the index named by a `String` body when neither the
`CamelElasticsearchIndexName` header nor the `indexName` option is set. Before, the call failed.

The `Index` and `Update` operations now fail with an `IllegalArgumentException` when the body is null, instead of
failing with a Jackson error or an error of the server. A `java.io.File`, a `java.nio.file.Path` or the file of the file
component is now read as the JSON of the document, instead of being written as a JSON string of its path.

=== camel-nats - the replies of an InOut consumer

A `nats` consumer with `exchangePattern=InOut` now answers with an empty reply when the route ends with no body.
Previously it sent no reply, and the requester timed out. A body that does not convert to a `byte[]`, such as a `Map` or
a POJO, is still not sent, but the error now goes to the exception handler of the consumer, which logs it at WARN level.

A consumer with `jetstreamEnabled=true` and `exchangePattern=InOut` no longer publishes the reply to the ack subject of
the JetStream message. InOut replies are for core NATS only, and the consumer logs a WARN when it starts.

=== camel-solr - the converter to Map, and bodies that are not sent

With camel-solr on the classpath, converting a value that is not a Solr response to a `java.util.Map` no longer gives an
empty `Map`. It now fails, or gives `null` with `tryConvertTo`, as without camel-solr.

Without a body:

* SEARCH and DELETE now fail with an `InvalidPayloadException`. Previously they searched for, or deleted, the text
  `java.lang.Object@...`.
* SEARCH still works with the `CamelSolrQueryString` header, or a `CamelSolrParam.q` header.
* INSERT builds the document from the `CamelSolrField.*` headers.
* INSERT and DELETE with request parameters, such as the commit of `autoCommit=true`, send only the parameters.

An INSERT of a body that is not a document, such as plain text, a `byte[]`, a stream, a number or a bean without
`@Field`, now fails with an `InvalidPayloadException` also when the request has parameters. Previously it reported
success and stored nothing.

The catalog now says that `deleteByQuery` defaults to `false`, as it always did at runtime. A `null` value in a `Map`
body or in a `CamelSolrField.*` header is left out of the document, instead of failing with a `NullPointerException`.

=== camel-opensearch - built requests, search bodies, DeleteIndex and file bodies

A request of the OpenSearch client in the message body (an `IndexRequest`, `UpdateRequest`, `GetRequest`,
`DeleteRequest`, `SearchRequest`, `MsearchRequest`, `MgetRequest`, `BulkRequest` or `DeleteIndexRequest`) is now sent
as it is, like its builder. Before, a built `IndexRequest` was written as the document itself, with a generated id, a
built `UpdateRequest` failed without the id header, and the other built requests failed with "Wrong body type".

The `Search` operation now uses the whole search request of a JSON text or `Map` body: its `size`, `from`, `sort`,
aggregations, `_source` and the other members are no longer dropped, so the hits returned can change. A JSON object
with a single member other than `query`, such as `{"match": {...}}`, is still taken as the query alone. The `size` and
`from` headers or options, when set, still override those of the body.

The `DeleteIndex` operation now deletes the index named by a `String` body when neither the
`CamelOpensearchIndexName` header nor the `indexName` option is set. Before, the call failed.

The `Index` and `Update` operations now fail with an `IllegalArgumentException` when the body is null, instead of
failing with a Jackson error. A `java.io.File`, a `java.nio.file.Path` or the file of the file
component is now read as the JSON of the document, instead of being written as a JSON string of its path.

=== camel-ssh - the shell channel and sleepForShellPrompt

An `ssh` endpoint with `channelType=shell` and no `shellPrompt` now fails when it starts. Previously each command
failed with a `NullPointerException`.

`sleepForShellPrompt` now defaults to 100 milliseconds, as the documentation said. Previously it was 0, so the shell
channel waited for the prompt in a busy loop.

The deprecated `SshResult.STDERR` is `CamelSshStderr` again. Previously it was `CamelSshExitValue`.

=== camel-slack - the JSON of the webhook mode

In webhook mode the `slack` producer now writes the JSON keys in snake_case, as Slack reads them, such as `icon_emoji`,
`icon_url`, `thread_ts`, `unfurl_links`, `image_url`, `alt_text`, `author_name` and `title_link`, and `short` for the
fields of an attachment. Previously they were written in camelCase, and Slack ignored them.

Other changes:

* The send no longer changes the message body.
* The `channel`, `username`, `iconUrl` and `iconEmoji` of the endpoint are set only when the endpoint sets them, so a
  `SlackMessage` body keeps its own values when the endpoint has none.
* A `com.slack.api.model.Message` body also gets the icons of the endpoint.
* A `RuntimeException` from the send now fails the exchange, instead of being thrown.

In token mode a `SlackMessage` body now sends its attachments too, not only its text.

The Slack CloudEvents data type transformer now sets `CamelCloudEventSource` to `slack.<appId>` and
`CamelCloudEventSubject` to the channel. Previously they were `slack.true` and `true`.

=== camel-elasticsearch-rest-client - SEARCH body and the id of GET_BY_ID and DELETE

The `SEARCH` operation now sends a body that is not a `Map` (JSON text, bytes, a stream or a file) as the JSON query,
when the `CamelElasticsearchSearchQuery` header is not set. Before, such a body was ignored and every document matched.
A route whose `SEARCH` step gets a body left by a previous step, such as the id given by `INDEX_OR_UPDATE`, now sends
that body as the query and fails: remove the body, or set the query in the header, to match every document as before.

The `GET_BY_ID` and `DELETE` operations now take the id of the `CamelElasticsearchId` header first, and the body only
when the header is not set, as `INDEX_OR_UPDATE` does. Before, the body won over the header, so a body left by a
previous step was used as the id.

=== camel-telegram - media bodies, messages without text, and answers that are not ok

The `telegram` producer changes:

* A `byte[]` body with `CamelTelegramMediaType=TEXT` is now sent as a text message, decoded in the charset of the
  exchange. Previously it was sent as a document named `file`.
* A stream, a file or a stream cache body with a media type other than TEXT is now sent as that media. Previously it
  failed with "Unsupported conversion from String".
* An `IncomingMessage` body without text, such as a received photo, is no longer sent, as a `null` body is not.
  Previously its `toString()` was sent as the text.
* An answer of the Bot API with `"ok": false` now fails the exchange with the error code and the description of the
  answer. Previously the body was a `MessageResult` with `ok=false`.

The `bufferSize` option no longer limits the size of a request, so media larger than 1 MB can be sent. It is now the
initial size of the buffer, and its default changed from 1048576 to 8192. The parts of a multipart request that are
numbers or booleans, such as `duration` or `disable_notification`, are now sent; previously they were sent empty. The
text parts are written in UTF-8, as they declare.

=== camel-spring-ai-image - an ImagePrompt body uses the endpoint options, and a URL-only Image fails to convert

With an `ImagePrompt` body, the endpoint options and headers (`n`, `width`, `height`, `model`, `responseFormat` and
`style`) now fill the generic options that the prompt leaves unset; the options the prompt sets still win. Before, an
`ImagePrompt` body was sent as is, and the endpoint options and headers did not apply. The options of a provider, such
as `OpenAiImageOptions`, are still sent as is.

The type converter from an `Image` that has only a URL (no base64 data) to `byte[]` or `InputStream` now fails with a
message that suggests `responseFormat=b64_json`. Before, it gave the bytes of the URL text, so a route such as
`.to("spring-ai-image:gen").to("file:out?fileName=image.png")` wrote the URL into the `.png` file. The conversion to
`String` still gives the URL.

=== camel-hazelcast - getAll copies, map expired events, queue removeIf and clear errors

The `getAll` operation of `hazelcast-list` and `hazelcast-set` now sets a copy of the items as the message body (a
`java.util.ArrayList` and a `java.util.LinkedHashSet`) instead of the distributed list or set itself. A later change of
the body no longer changes the distributed collection, and the body no longer shows the changes made to it afterwards.

The `hazelcast-map` consumer now also routes an event when the time to live of an entry ends: the
`CamelHazelcastListenerAction` header is `expired`, the key is in the `CamelHazelcastObjectId` header, and there is no
body, as for `evicted`. Before, such an entry gave no event. A route that sends an unknown event to an error path must
handle `expired`.

The `removeIf` operation of `hazelcast-queue` now works: it tests the items of the queue with the
`java.util.function.Predicate` of the body, removes the matching ones, and sets the body to `true` when it removed an
item. Before, it always failed with an `UnsupportedOperationException`. The operation is not atomic: the items are read
first, and the matching ones removed in one call afterwards.

The following now fail with an `IllegalArgumentException` that names what is missing, where they failed with a
`NullPointerException` before: a producer of `hazelcast-map`, `hazelcast-multimap`, `hazelcast-replicatedmap`,
`hazelcast-list`, `hazelcast-set`, `hazelcast-ringbuffer` or `hazelcast-pncounter` with neither the
`CamelHazelcastOperationType` header nor the `defaultOperation` option, `get` on `hazelcast-list` without the
`CamelHazelcastObjectIndex` header, `drainTo` on `hazelcast-queue` without a `Collection` in the
`CamelHazelcastDrainToCollection` header (a value that is not a `Collection` failed with a `ClassCastException`),
`removeIf` without a `Predicate` body, `getAndAdd` on `hazelcast-pncounter` without a numeric body, and a
`hazelcast-seda` producer with no body or a body that is neither `Serializable` nor convertible to bytes.

=== camel-spring-redis - commands the producer does not run fail, and a read that finds nothing gives no body

The `spring-redis` producer now fails the exchange for a `CamelRedis.Command` it does not run. Previously these did
nothing, and the body stayed as it was:

* An unknown command, such as a misspelled one, fails with an `IllegalArgumentException`.
* AUTH, BGREWRITEAOF, BGSAVE, CONFIG, DBSIZE, DEBUG, FLUSHALL, FLUSHDB, INFO, LASTSAVE, MONITOR, PUNSUBSCRIBE, RENAMEX,
  SAVE, SELECT, SHUTDOWN, SLAVEOF, SUBSTR, SYNC and UNSUBSCRIBE fail with an `UnsupportedOperationException`.
* SUBSCRIBE and PSUBSCRIBE fail too, as they are for the consumer.

A command in mixed case, such as `Get`, now runs the command. ZSCORE is now implemented and returns a `Double`.

The commands that read one value set the body to `null` when they find none, so a route can tell a miss from a value.
Previously the body stayed as it was. These are GET, GETSET, HGET, LINDEX, LPOP, RPOP, BLPOP, BRPOP, RPOPLPUSH,
BRPOPLPUSH, SPOP, SRANDMEMBER, RANDOMKEY, ZSCORE, ZRANK, ZREVRANK and GEODIST. For BLPOP and BRPOP, this includes when
the timeout passes.

Other changes:

* MSETNX sets its `Boolean` result as the body.
* ECHO without the `CamelRedis.Value` header fails with a `NoSuchHeaderException`, instead of a `NullPointerException`.
* SETBIT and GETBIT serialize the key with the key serializer of the template, as the other commands do. With the
  default JDK serializer they now work on the same Redis key as SET and GET. Bits stored by older versions under the
  raw key are not found.
* The `CamelRedis.Keys` header may be a `String`: one key, or keys separated by commas.

The consumer now gives a message that its serializer cannot deserialize, such as one published by `redis-cli`, to the
route as a `byte[]`. Previously the message was dropped with a WARN. A failed exchange of the consumer is now handed to
its exception handler.

=== camel-redis - Redis Cluster hash slot requirement for optimistic recovery

When using `RedisAggregationRepository` with optimistic removal and recovery enabled
on Redis Cluster, `mapName` and `persistenceMapName` must resolve to the same Redis hash slot.

Use a common Redis hash tag to ensure both keys are assigned to the same slot. For example:

* `mapName`: +orders:{aggregation}+
* `persistenceMapName`: +orders:{aggregation}-completed+

Existing Redis Cluster configurations using different hash slots must update their map names
before upgrading. Existing aggregation data may need to be migrated to the new Redis keys.

The repository now validates this configuration during startup and throws an
`IllegalArgumentException` if the keys belong to different hash slots.

=== camel-spring-ws - the reply of a consumer, and File and stream bodies of the producer

A `spring-ws` consumer route that sets the body to `null` in a processor, such as with
`exchange.getMessage().setBody(null)`, now answers with an empty SOAP body. Previously the message created its body
again from the request, so the request payload was sent back. Likewise, an OUT message that the route creates with
`exchange.getOut()` no longer starts with the request payload as its body.

A reply body that does not convert to a `javax.xml.transform.Source`, such as a `Map`, now gives a SOAP fault with the
conversion error. Previously it gave an empty SOAP body. A reply body that is an `InputStream` is now sent as XML.

The `spring-ws` producer now sends a `File` body, the file of a `file` or `ftp` consumer, and an `InputStream` body
(also when stream caching is off) as XML. Previously these failed with an `InvalidPayloadException`.

=== camel-ignite - cache PUT of a Map, the existing query results, RUN bodies and missing operations

With `fireExistingQueryResults=true`, the `ignite-cache` consumer now sends each entry that the initial query finds
with the value as the body and the key in the `CamelIgniteCacheKey` header, like the events that follow. Before, the
body was the `javax.cache.Cache.Entry` and the key header was not set.

A PUT of a `Map` body on `ignite-cache` with the `CamelIgniteCacheKey` header now stores the `Map` as the value of that
key. Before, the header was ignored and all the entries of the `Map` were put. The same holds with
`treatCollectionsAsCacheObjects=true`, where a PUT of a `Map` without the header now fails, as there is no key to store
it under. A PUT of a `Map` without the header still puts all its entries, and now keeps the body when
`propagateIncomingBodyIfNoReturnValue` is `true` (the default). Before, the body was `null` after such a PUT.

The `RUN` and `AFFINITY_RUN` execution types of `ignite-compute` have no result, and now keep the body (the job) when
`propagateIncomingBodyIfNoReturnValue` is `true` (the default). Before, the body was `null`. Set
`propagateIncomingBodyIfNoReturnValue=false` to keep the previous behaviour.

The `ignite-cache`, `ignite-idgen`, `ignite-queue` and `ignite-set` producers now fail with an
`IllegalArgumentException` that names the `operation` option and the operation header when neither sets the operation.
A missing body now fails with an `InvalidPayloadException` that names what the operation needs: the job for
`ignite-compute` (except `EXECUTE` with the `taskName` option, which now runs the task with the
`CamelIgniteComputeParameters` header), the number to add for `ADD_AND_GET` and `GET_AND_ADD` of `ignite-idgen`, and
the element for `ADD`, `CONTAINS`, `REMOVE` and `RETAIN_ALL` of `ignite-queue` and `ignite-set` and for `OFFER` and
`PUT` of `ignite-queue`. Before, these failed with a `NullPointerException`. The `GET`, `GET_AND_INCREMENT` and
`INCREMENT_AND_GET` operations of `ignite-idgen` no longer fail on a body that is not a number, as they do not use it.

=== camel-couchbase - the document id, the operation option and the bodies of CCB_PUT

The `couchbase` producer now uses the `key` option as the document id when the message has no `CamelCouchbaseId`
header. When neither gives an id, and `autoStartIdForInserts` is not enabled, the call fails. Previously the document
was written under the id of the Camel endpoint, such as `endpoint6`, and the `key` option was not read. The producer
no longer removes the `CamelCouchbaseId` header after the call.

The `operation` option is now an enum of `CCB_PUT`, `CCB_GET` and `CCB_DEL`. Any other value, including one in lower
case, fails when the producer starts. Previously it did nothing.

CCB_PUT now stores the body as follows:

* Text or a `byte[]` that holds a JSON object or array is stored as a JSON document. Previously text was stored as a
  JSON string, and a `byte[]` failed.
* Any other `byte[]` is stored as a binary document. Read it back with `contentAsBytes()` of the `GetResult`.
* A stream is read as bytes, and stored as a JSON document or as a binary document by the same rule. Previously it
  failed.
* Other text, a `Map`, a `List`, a `JsonObject` or a POJO is stored as before.

The SQL++ query that the consumer generates when neither `statement` nor `collection` is set now reads the default
collection of the bucket, ``FROM `bucket`.`_default`.`_default` AS `bucket` ``. Previously it failed on every poll with
"Keyspace not found", as the query runs on the scope. For the same reason, an explicit `statement` that names the bucket
in FROM fails: use the collection, such as ``FROM `_default` ``, or the full path.

In view mode, the rows of a reduce view and the rows with a compound key no longer fail the poll. The body of a reduce
row is its value, and it has no `CamelCouchbaseId` header. A compound key is set in `CamelCouchbaseKey` as its JSON text.

=== camel-jolt, camel-jsonata - JSON text, bytes, streams and files are parsed with the default inputType

With the default `inputType` (`Hydrated` for jolt, `Jackson` for jsonata), a body that is bytes, an `InputStream`
(including a stream cache) or a file is now parsed as JSON, and so is text whose first non-whitespace character is `{`
or `[`. Previously such a body reached the specification or expression unparsed: jolt gave null or the text back, and
jsonata read the text as one JSON string, refused bytes and failed on a stream. The bytes are decoded in the charset of
the exchange when one is set; otherwise the JSON parser detects the encoding. Such text that is not valid JSON now fails
with the parse error. Other text, a `Map`, a `List` and other objects are used as before.

=== camel-jsonata - the endpoint options of an http resource

For a `jsonata:http:` or `jsonata:https:` resource, the options of the endpoint, such as `inputType`, `outputType` and
`prettyPrint`, are now used by the endpoint. Previously they were appended to the URL of the resource and sent to the
server, and the endpoint used its defaults. Only the other query parameters are sent to the server.

=== camel-state-store - a stream body is stored as its bytes, and a Reader as its text

The `put` and `putIfAbsent` operations of the `state-store` component now read a stream body fully into memory and
store its content: an `InputStream` or the stream cache of Camel as its bytes, so a later `get` gives a `byte[]`, and a
`Reader` as its text, a `String`, like a text body. Before, the stream object itself was stored: the first `get` could
read it, later ones got an empty stream, and every exchange that got the key shared the same stream object. A backend
that serializes its values could not store such a stream at all. Other bodies are still stored as they are.

The charset of the stored bytes is not stored with them. A `convertBodyTo(String.class)` after a `get` decodes them in
the charset of that exchange, UTF-8 by default, so use `convertBodyTo(String.class, "<charset>")` when the stored bytes
are not UTF-8.

=== camel-saxon - the xquery endpoint is producer-only

The `xquery` endpoint can only be used as a producer. A route that starts `from("xquery:...")` now fails when it
starts, with a message that says to use the endpoint in `to`. Previously it polled, and every poll failed because the
query had no message to run on. The consumer and scheduler options (such as `delay`, `initialDelay`,
`sendEmptyMessageWhenIdle`, `bridgeErrorHandler` and the `scheduler` and `backoff` options) are removed from the
endpoint, and `bridgeErrorHandler` from the component.

=== camel-duckdb - the CamelDuckDbDatabasePath header needs the allowDatabasePathFromHeader option

The `CamelDuckDbDatabasePath` header made the `duckdb` producer open a short-lived connection to the DuckDB file at
the path that the header named, instead of using the `databasePath`, `jdbcUrl` or `DataSource` of the endpoint, and
DuckDB created that file when it did not exist. The header is now ignored unless the endpoint sets the new
`allowDatabasePathFromHeader=true` option (disabled by default), so a message no longer moves the endpoint to another
database file. A route that relies on the header must set this option on the endpoint.

The `CamelDuckDbOperation`, `CamelDuckDbQuery` and `CamelDuckDbTable` headers still override the operation, the SQL and
the table of the endpoint, as before.

=== camel-schematron - no body fails, and bytes are read in the encoding of the XML declaration

A message without a body now fails with an `InvalidPayloadException` instead of a `NullPointerException`.

A body that is bytes, a stream or a file is given to the XML parser as bytes, so it is read in the encoding of the XML
declaration (UTF-8 when there is none), unless the exchange has a charset set. A `String` body is read as text.
Previously the body was converted to a String and encoded again in the platform charset, so a document whose declared
encoding was not the platform charset could be read wrong and its validation result may now differ.

=== camel-mustache - contentCache=false reads the template for each exchange

With `contentCache=false`, the template is now read and compiled for each exchange, so a changed template is used.
Previously it was compiled once, and only the JMX operation `clearContentCache` read it again. The default,
`contentCache=true`, is unchanged.

=== camel-clickhouse - the database and table headers need the allowDatabaseAndTableFromHeaders option

The `CamelClickHouseDatabase` and `CamelClickHouseTable` headers took the place of the database and the table of the
`clickhouse` endpoint, so a message could insert into, or query, another database or table than the one the endpoint
is configured with. These headers are now ignored unless the endpoint sets the new
`allowDatabaseAndTableFromHeaders=true` option (disabled by default). A route that relies on them must set this option
on the endpoint.

An insert endpoint without a table, which took the table from the `CamelClickHouseTable` header, now fails with an
error that asks for the table, unless it sets `allowDatabaseAndTableFromHeaders=true`.

The `CamelClickHouseOperation` and `CamelClickHouseFormat` headers still override the operation and the format of the
endpoint, as before.

=== camel-stub - a stub endpoint without a consumer keeps its latest messages

A stub endpoint that no route consumes from, such as the endpoints `camel run --stub` stubs, keeps the latest `size`
messages (1000 by default) and drops the oldest one when the queue is full. Previously the producer failed with
`IllegalStateException: Queue full` after `size` messages, or with `blockWhenFull=true` waited for a consumer that
never came. The on-completions of a dropped message run as done. With a consumer, the stub endpoint queues as seda does.

Browsing a stub endpoint with a limit, such as `camel cmd stub --browse --limit`, now shows the latest messages.
Previously the newest message was left out.

=== camel-stream - stream:header without the stream header fails

The `stream:header` producer now fails with a `NoSuchHeaderException` when the `stream` header is missing or is not an
`OutputStream`. Previously it failed with a `NullPointerException`, or did nothing when the body was null.

=== camel-hazelcast - the hazelcast-seda consumer restores a transferred exchange only with transferExchange=true

A `hazelcast-seda` consumer now restores the headers and exchange properties of an exchange that a producer with
`transferExchange=true` put on the queue only when the consumer has `transferExchange=true` as well. A consumer without
the option, the default, now takes only the body of such an exchange, as it does for any other item of the queue, and
logs a warning the first time. Before, the consumer restored the headers and exchange properties whatever its
`transferExchange` option said.

Set `transferExchange=true` on both the producer and the consumer endpoints to keep transferring the headers and
exchange properties.

=== camel-knative - a structured CloudEvent whose Content-Type has parameters

The consumer now finds the content mode of a CloudEvent by the media type of the `Content-Type` header, ignoring its
parameters and its case. A request with `Content-Type: application/cloudevents+json; charset=UTF-8` is now read as a
structured CloudEvent: the body is the `data` field and the attributes are set as the CloudEvent headers. Previously it
was read as binary mode, and the body was the whole JSON envelope. A batch content type with parameters is now refused
as unsupported, as the plain one is.

=== Producers that pick their operation by a header - an unknown value fails

A producer that picks its method by a header or an option (a `HeaderSelectorProducer`, used by camel-caffeine,
camel-consul, camel-dynamic-router, camel-ehcache, camel-infinispan, camel-servicenow, camel-spring-redis and
camel-web3j) now fails the exchange with an `IllegalArgumentException`, such as
`Unsupported value NOSUCH of header CamelCaffeineAction`, when no method handles the value. Previously the exchange
completed with no error and the body unchanged.

The SPI `InvokeOnHeaderStrategy` has a new constant `NO_METHOD`, which the strategies generated by the Camel tooling
now return for a key without a method. A strategy generated by older tooling returns null and keeps the old behaviour.
The strategy of a parent class is now tried only for `NO_METHOD`, so a void method no longer runs the parent's
strategy too; a third-party producer with a parent class that has its own strategy must have its strategies
regenerated with the 4.24 tooling.

=== API components - the inBody body is converted to the type of its parameter

The API components (camel-as2, camel-box, camel-braintree, camel-dhis2, camel-fhir, camel-google-calendar,
camel-google-drive, camel-google-mail, camel-google-sheets, camel-olingo2, camel-olingo4, camel-twilio and
camel-zendesk) now convert the message body to the type of the method parameter that `inBody` names, for example a
`byte[]`, a stream or a file to a `String`. Previously the body was passed as it was, and a body of another type failed
with `argument type mismatch`; a body that cannot be converted now fails before the call, with an error that names the
property.

A header named exactly as the prefix of an API component, such as `CamelFhir` or `CamelFhir.`, is now ignored.
Previously it failed every call with a `StringIndexOutOfBoundsException`.

=== camel-google-sheets - splitResult is true by default

The `splitResult` option of the google-sheets consumer now defaults to `true`, as documented and as the other API
components do: an array or collection result gives one exchange per element. Set `splitResult=false` for one exchange
with the whole result.

=== camel-aws2-sns - string array message attributes

String values in list-valued message headers are now JSON-escaped when mapped to SNS
`String.Array` message attributes. Quotes, backslashes and control characters are preserved
as string data. Routes should supply the original strings rather than pre-escaping them.
Other member types, such as enums, characters, UUIDs and dates, are serialized as JSON strings.
Non-finite floating-point values (`NaN` and infinities) are also serialized as strings; finite numbers,
booleans and null remain JSON primitives.
The JSON utility also escapes forward slashes and some Unicode characters, so raw `String.Array`
attribute text may differ even when its decoded string values are unchanged.

=== camel-aws2-sqs - sendBatchMessage of more than 10 entries

With more than 10 entries, which are sent in requests of 10, the message body is now one `SendMessageBatchResponse`
with the successful and failed entries of all the requests. Previously the body had the entries of the last request
only. An element of the body that is not a String is now converted to a String, instead of failing with a
`ClassCastException`.

=== camel-aws2-kinesis - a Path body is one record

A `java.nio.file.Path` body is now sent as one record with the content of the file, as a `java.io.File` is. Previously
it was sent as a batch of its name elements, which failed. A body that does not convert to a `byte[]` now fails with an
