`InvalidPayloadException` instead of a `NullPointerException`.

=== camel-aws2-s3 - headBucket and headObject

The `headBucket` and `headObject` operations now take the bucket from the endpoint (or the
`CamelAwsS3OverrideBucketName` header) and the key from the `keyName` option (or the `CamelAwsS3Key` header), as the
other operations do. Previously they failed without the headers.

=== camel-aws2-cw - the metric data of putMetricData

* A `Collection` or an array of `MetricDatum` in the body is now sent. Previously only a `List` was, and a Set or an
  array was ignored, so the metric was taken from the headers and options. A collection or an array with an element
  that is not a `MetricDatum` now fails with an `IllegalArgumentException`.
* The `unit` option and the `CamelAwsCwMetricUnit` header now match the unit in any case (`BYTES` is `Bytes`). An
  unknown unit now fails, where it was sent as `null` before.
* A `java.util.Date` in the `CamelAwsCwMetricTimestamp` header is now used. Previously it was ignored. The catalog
  lists the header as an `Instant`.

=== camel-pinecone - default topK, generated UPSERT id and the pinecone:rag transformer

QUERY and QUERY_BY_ID without the `CamelPineconeQueryTopK` header now return 10 matches, as camel-weaviate does. Before, they failed with a `NullPointerException`.

UPSERT without the `CamelPineconeIndexId` header now stores the vector under a random UUID, as the `pinecone:embeddings` data type transformer does, and sets the `CamelPineconeIndexId` header to that id. Before, it failed with a `PineconeValidationException`.

The `pinecone:rag` data type transformer now reads the `QueryResponseWithUnsignedIndices` body that a QUERY or QUERY_BY_ID leaves, and returns the texts of its matches. Before, it returned an empty List for that body. A route that passes the `matchesList` of the response to the transformer gets the same result as before.

=== camel-aws2-lambda - the code of createFunction and updateFunction, and the payload of invokeFunction

* createFunction and updateFunction: the `CamelAwsLambdaZipFile` header, or the S3 location of the
  `CamelAwsLambdaS3Bucket` and `CamelAwsLambdaS3Key` headers, now wins over the message body. The body is the zip of
  the code only when neither is set. Previously the body won over the zip file header, and with the S3 headers both a
  zip and an S3 location were sent. Setting the zip file header and the S3 headers together, or only one of the S3
  bucket and key headers, now fails. A body that is not the zip as bytes, a stream or a file now fails with an
  `IllegalArgumentException` instead of a `NullPointerException`.
* invokeFunction without a body now invokes the function without a payload, instead of failing with a
  `NullPointerException`. A `byte[]` or an `InputStream` body is sent as its bytes, and another body as text in UTF-8
  (previously in the default charset of the JVM).

=== camel-google-calendar - the CloudEvent transformer on a body that is not an Event

The `google-calendar-stream:application-cloudevents` data type transformer now fails with a `CamelExecutionException`
that says the body must be a Google Calendar `Event` when the body is not one, and it sets no CloudEvent header then.
Before, it failed with a `NullPointerException`. The body of the `google-calendar-stream` consumer is always an
`Event`, so routes that use the transformer after that consumer are not affected.

=== camel-aws2-eventbridge - putEvent fails when the event is rejected

When EventBridge rejects the event, for example with `MalformedDetail` for a detail that is not a JSON object, the
putEvent operation now fails the exchange with a `CamelExecutionException` that has the error code and message.
Previously only the `CamelAwsEventbridgeFailedEntryCount` header showed it; the header is still set. Without a body,
putEvent now fails with the `InvalidPayloadException` itself instead of a `RuntimeException`. The ARNs of the
`CamelAwsEventbridgeResourcesArn` header are now trimmed.

=== camel-aws2-ses - raw mails

* A `RawMessage` body is now sent as a raw mail (`sendRawEmail`), so the recipient gets the mail itself. Previously it
  was sent as a plain mail whose text was the MIME source of the raw mail, with the subject of the endpoint.
* A `SendRawEmailRequest` body is now sent as it is. Previously it was sent as its `toString()`.
* For a raw mail (a `jakarta.mail.Message` or a `RawMessage` body), the cc and bcc options and headers are now added
  to the destinations together with the to ones. When none is set, SES sends the mail to the recipients of its own
  headers.
* A plain mail without a body now fails with an `InvalidPayloadException` before it is sent to SES.

=== camel-aws-secrets-manager - binary secrets

* With `binaryPayload=true`, createSecret, updateSecret and putSecretValue now store the bytes of the message body as
  the binary of the secret. Previously they stored the Base64 text of the body, which the AWS SDK Base64-encodes once
  more, so other clients read the Base64 text instead of the value.
* getSecret now gives the value the secret has, whatever `binaryPayload` is: a `byte[]` for a binary secret, a String
  for a string secret. batchGetSecret gives a List with such a value per secret, and the
  `CamelAwsSecretsManagerSecretVersionIds` header lines up with it. batchGetSecret now fails when AWS reports errors
  for some of the secrets, which were dropped before.
* The `aws:` properties function reads a binary secret as the UTF-8 text of its bytes. Previously it decoded it from
  Base64 and failed for a binary secret that another client stored.
* Without an operation, the producer now fails with an `IllegalArgumentException` instead of a `NullPointerException`.

A binary secret that an earlier Camel version stored with `binaryPayload=true` holds the Base64 text of the value, and
now reads back as that text, in getSecret and in the properties function. Store such secrets again, or decode the
value from Base64 in the route.

=== camel-aws2-iam - the access key status in any case

The `CamelAwsIAMAccessKeyStatus` header now matches `Active` and `Inactive` in any case. An unknown status now fails
in Camel, before the request is sent.

=== camel-aws2-ddb - the operation of the JSON data type transformer

The `aws2-ddb:application-json` data type transformer now takes the operation only from the route: the
`CamelAwsDdbOperation` header, else the `operation` exchange property. When the route names neither, the transformer no
longer sets the `CamelAwsDdbOperation` header (and the `CamelAwsDdbReturnValues` header), so the `operation` option of
the endpoint applies. It then sets the item, key and update values headers that PutItem, UpdateItem and DeleteItem
need. Previously the transformer took the operation from a top-level `operation` field of the JSON body, else PutItem,
and always set the header, which won over the operation of the endpoint: `aws2-ddb:orders?operation=DeleteItem` put the
item.

A JSON body with a top-level `operation` field now fails with an `IllegalArgumentException`, unless the route names
that same operation. A route that chose the operation in the body must set the header or the exchange property
instead. The aws-ddb-sink Kamelet sets the exchange property, and is not affected.

=== camel-aws2-ddb - result headers

* DescribeTable and DeleteTable no longer set the `CamelAwsDdbTableName` header. It is also the header that names the
  table of an aws2-ddb step, so the next step went to that table instead of the one of its endpoint.
* UpdateTable now sets the headers of the table description of the response (`CamelAwsDdbProvisionedThroughput`,
  `CamelAwsDdbCreationDate`, `CamelAwsDdbTableItemCount`, `CamelAwsDdbKeySchema`, `CamelAwsDdbTableSize` and
  `CamelAwsDdbTableStatus`), and DescribeTable also sets `CamelAwsDdbProvisionedThroughput`.
* Query and Scan now ask for the consumed capacity, so the `CamelAwsDdbConsumedCapacity` header has a value. It was
  always null before.
* The catalog now gives the types the headers have, such as `TableStatus` for `CamelAwsDdbTableStatus` and `Instant`
  for `CamelAwsDdbCreationDate`, and lists the `CamelAwsDdbNextToken` header and the headers of aws2-ddbstream.

=== camel-azure-storage-blob - getBlob writes only into an OutputStream body

The getBlob operation now writes the blob into the message body only when the body is an `OutputStream`. A body that
only converts to one, such as a `java.io.File`, a `java.nio.file.Path` or the file of a file consumer, is replaced by an
`InputStream` of the blob, as any other body is. Previously such a file was overwritten with the blob, also with
`noop=true` on the file consumer. To save a blob into a file, use the downloadBlobToFile operation, or a `file`
endpoint after getBlob.

uploadBlockBlob, uploadBlockBlobChunked and commitAppendBlob without a body now fail with an `InvalidPayloadException`
instead of a `NullPointerException`, and commitAppendBlob and uploadPageBlob without a body no longer create an empty
blob first.

=== camel-azure-storage-queue - sendMessage without a body

sendMessage without a body now fails with an `InvalidPayloadException`, before the queue is created, instead of a
`NullPointerException` of the Azure SDK.

=== camel-azure-servicebus - the body of the consumer converts to bytes and streams

The body that the consumer gives is a `com.azure.core.util.BinaryData`, which now converts to `byte[]` and `InputStream`
as well as to `String`, so an unmarshal, a file producer or `convertBodyTo` byte[] can follow the consumer directly.

=== camel-google-pubsub - the producer sends the content of streams and files

A body that is not a String or a `byte[]` is now converted to a `byte[]` first, and only a body that does not convert
goes to the serializer (Java serialization by default). So a stream, a stream cache, a `java.nio.file.Path` and the file
of a file consumer are now sent as their content, where they failed with a `NotSerializableException`, and a
`java.io.File` is sent as the content of the file instead of as a serialized File object. A Map or a POJO is serialized
as before, and a custom `serializer` is now called only for such bodies. A message without a body now fails with an
`InvalidPayloadException`, where it published a serialized null.

=== camel-ibm-cos - the content length of putObject

putObject now takes the content length from the body when the length of the body is known (a `byte[]`, a String, a
stream cache, a file), and uses the `CamelIBMCOSContentLength` header only for a stream of unknown length. Previously
the header won, so a route that read an object, changed the body and wrote it failed, as the header still had the
length of the object it read.

=== camel-minio - the getObjectRange operation

The `getObjectRange` operation, as the catalog and the documentation name it, now works for the `operation` option and
the `CamelMinioOperation` header; it failed at startup. The former name `getPartialObject` still works, but is
deprecated.

=== camel-docling - a stream body

An `InputStream` body, such as a stream cache, is now written to a temporary file as a `byte[]` body is, within
`maxFileSize`, instead of failing with an `InvalidPayloadException`.

=== camel-kafka - only a JSON array is sent as one record per element

With `useIterator=true` (the default), the producer now splits a Jackson JSON node only when it is an array, for
Jackson 2 and Jackson 3. An object node is now sent as one record; previously it was sent as one record per field
value, without the field names, as the 4.22 upgrade guide said. A Jackson 3 scalar node is now sent as one record,
where nothing was sent before.

=== camel-kafka - breakOnFirstError while the consumer is paused

With `breakOnFirstError=true`, a failed record now creates a new Kafka consumer also when the consumer is paused in the
same poll, for example by a `ThrottlingExceptionRoutePolicy` that suspends the route on the failed exchange. The new
consumer stays paused until the route is resumed, and then polls each partition from its committed offset, or from
`autoOffsetReset` when it has none, as on a break without a pause. Before, a partition without a committed offset
resumed after the failed record. The new consumer leaves and rejoins the consumer group, so each such break is now a
rebalance of the group, also when the partition has a committed offset. There is no option to keep the previous
behaviour.

=== camel-kafka - the Kafka idempotent and key-value repositories use the acks of the Kafka client

`KafkaIdempotentRepository` and `KafkaKeyValueRepository` no longer set `acks=1` on their producer, so the default of
the Kafka client applies: `acks=all` with idempotence. Every change of a repository is now acknowledged once the
in-sync replicas have it, so it is not lost when the leader of the partition fails over. Each change waits for the
replicas; with fewer in-sync replicas than `min.insync.replicas` it waits up to `delivery.timeout.ms` (2 minutes by
default) and then fails, where it succeeded with `acks=1`. A broker before Kafka 2.8 that uses ACLs needs the
`IdempotentWrite` permission for the repository's producer. A `producerConfig` that sets only
`enable.idempotence=true` no longer fails at start. To keep the previous behaviour, set `acks=1` in the `producerConfig`
of the repository (with `bootstrap.servers`, as a `producerConfig` replaces the `bootstrapServers` of the repository
for the producer).

=== camel-dns - dig without a type, and the wikipedia type

`dns:dig` without the `CamelDnsType` header now asks for type A, and without the `CamelDnsName` header fails with an
`IllegalArgumentException` that names the header; both failed with a `NullPointerException` before. The `wikipedia`
type and the `CamelDnsTerm` header are deprecated, as the service behind them is gone.

=== camel-as2 - client/send

* A partner answer with an HTTP status outside 2xx, such as a 500 when the route of the partner fails, now fails the
  exchange. Previously the exchange succeeded with a null body. The `CamelAs2.interchange` exchange property is still
  set.
* The EDI message can now be a `byte[]`, a `java.io.File`, the file of a file consumer, or anything that converts to
  bytes, as well as text and a stream.
* The `requestUri` option now defaults to `/` for client/send, as the catalog says.
* The `ediMessageType` option is deprecated: use `ediMessageContentType`. When only `ediMessageType` is set,
  client/send uses it as the content type.
* `ediMessageTransferEncoding=quoted-printable` now encodes the message as quoted-printable. Previously it was
  encoded as Base64 but labelled quoted-printable, which the receiver rejected.

=== camel-as2 - server/listen - signature and MIC over the message as received

The signature of a `multipart/signed` message is now verified over the signed part exactly as it was received, its
MIME headers and its body in the transfer encoding the sender used (RFC 1847, section 2.1), and the `Received-Content-MIC`
of the MDN is computed over the same bytes (RFC 4130, section 7.1). Previously both were computed over a
re-serialisation of the parsed part, which matches the sender's bytes only when the part is `binary`, `7bit` or
`8bit` and its headers are formatted the way camel-as2 writes them: a `base64` or `quoted-printable` part from another
AS2 implementation, encoded with a different line length or escaping, or a part with folded headers or no space after
the colon, was refused with `authentication-failed` although correctly signed, and the MDN carried a MIC the sender
could not match. Messages from camel-as2's own client are not affected. `MultipartSignedEntity.getSignedDataRaw()`
returns the part as received, `getSignedDataContent()` the bytes a signature is verified against.

The text of a body part now ends exactly where the boundary delimiter begins: the line break before the delimiter
line is removed as the sender wrote it, CRLF or a bare LF. Before, two characters were always removed, so a part
framed with bare line feeds (as `openssl cms -sign` writes them without `-crlfeol`) lost its last byte.

=== camel-graphql - the HTTP headers of the request

The `Content-Type`, `Accept` and `Accept-Encoding` headers of the request are now always the ones of the producer
(JSON), whatever headers the message has, and a `Content-Encoding` header of the message is no longer sent. The
headers named by the `queryHeader` and `variablesHeader` options are no longer sent as HTTP headers. After the call
the `CamelHttpMethod`, `Accept` and `Accept-Encoding` headers that the producer set no longer stay on the message, so
a later http producer no longer sends a POST because of them. When the body is the query, a body that is not text,
bytes, a stream or a file, such as a Map, now fails instead of being sent as its `toString()`, and a Map body (or a
Map in the variables header) is used as the variables.

=== camel-vertx-http - Path and Reader bodies, and urlencoded forms

A `java.nio.file.Path` body is now sent as a file and a `Reader` body as its text; both failed before. The values of
an `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` String body are now decoded before Vert.x encodes the form, so an escape such as
`%26` is no longer sent encoded twice, and a name that is repeated sends all its values. `RAW(...)` values are no
longer recognized in such a form body.

=== camel-github2 - getCommitFile

getCommitFile now takes the commit from the `CamelGitHubCommitSha` header, which must be a commit id of 40
hexadecimal characters, and the path of the file from the new `CamelGitHubFilePath` header, or else the body, and gives
the content of the file at that commit. Previously the body was taken as the commit when the header was not set, and
the operation could not give the content of a file.

=== camel-pdf - page sizes, extractText and line ends

* The `pageSize` values of the catalog (`LETTER`, `LEGAL`, `A0` to `A6`) now work: `pageSize=A4` gave a Letter page
  before. The former names such as `PAGE_SIZE_A4` still work. An unknown value now fails when the endpoint is
  created.
* extractText now reads a `byte[]`, a `ByteArrayOutputStream` (the output of create and merge), a stream, a file or a
  Path, so create followed by extractText works. Another body, or none, fails with an `InvalidPayloadException`.
* create without a body fails with an `InvalidPayloadException`. With the default `lineTermination`, a CR LF or a CR
  now ends a line, a tab is written as spaces and other control characters are left out; they failed before.
* The `CamelPdfFilesToMerge` header of merge can also hold `java.nio.file.Path` elements.

=== camel-tika - the body and the charset of the output

detect and parse fail with an `InvalidPayloadException` for a body that does not convert to a stream, or no body,
instead of a `NullPointerException`. parse now sets the charset of the exchange to `tikaParseOutputEncoding`, so the
output converts to text in that charset.

=== camel-infinispan-embedded - the configurationUri

The `configurationUri` of the component now reads a `file:` URI, an absolute path and property placeholders, as well
as a name on the class path. The `configurationUri`, `cacheContainer`, `cacheContainerConfiguration` and `flags`
options are read on the component only: set on an endpoint they are not used.

=== camel-snmp - the producer and the poll consumer

* A producer without `type` now sends a GET with the `oids` of the endpoint, where it sent a GET without them, and
  without a port in the URI a producer that is not of type TRAP now uses port 161 instead of 162.
* The trap producer fails with an `InvalidPayloadException` for a body that is not an `org.snmp4j.PDU`.
* The `SnmpMessage` body of the producer converts to the XML text of its PDU, as the body of the consumer is.
* The POLL consumer now waits for the answer of each poll, and a poll without an answer counts as an empty poll, so
  `sendEmptyMessageWhenIdle` and the backoff options work. `SnmpOIDPoller` no longer implements `ResponseListener`.

=== camel-jdbc - generated keys, outputClass and the body

The `CamelGeneratedKeysRows` header is now always a List of Maps of the key columns: the `outputClass` and `readSize`
options no longer apply to it. An `outputClass` that does not resolve fails with an `IllegalArgumentException` that
names the class, and a message without a body fails with an `InvalidPayloadException`. The `transacted` option is
deprecated, as it is not used.

=== camel-jsch - scp without a known hosts file

With `useUserKnownHostsFile=false` and no `knownHostsFile`, scp no longer fails with a `NullPointerException`: no host
is known, and `strictHostKeyChecking` decides whether to connect.

=== camel-aws2-polly - textType, outputFormat and the next token

* The `textType` and `outputFormat` options no longer have a default value, so the `CamelAwsPollyTextType` and
  `CamelAwsPollyOutputFormat` headers are now read when the option is not set. Previously the defaults `TEXT` and `MP3`
  were always set, and the headers were ignored: SSML sent with `CamelAwsPollyTextType=ssml` was spoken as plain text.
  When neither the option nor the header is set, `TEXT` and `MP3` are still sent.
* describeVoices, listLexicons and listSpeechSynthesisTasks now read and set the new `CamelAwsPollyNextToken` header:
  it is sent as the token of the next page, and set to the token of the response, or removed on the last page. A route
  that calls a different list operation on the same message must remove the header first.
* The lexicon names are trimmed, and empty names are dropped.

=== camel-aws2-translate - the languages and the code of Croatian

* Each language now comes from its option, or else from its header. Previously, when one of the `sourceLanguage` and
  `targetLanguage` options was set, both were taken from the options, and the request had no language for the other.
* A missing target language, or a missing source language without `autodetectSourceLanguage`, now fails with an
  `IllegalArgumentException` that names the option and the header.
* `Translate2LanguageEnum.CROATIAN` is now `hr`, the code Amazon Translate uses. It was `cr`, which is Cree.

=== camel-aws2-textract - feature types and the job id

* The `CamelAwsTextractFeatureTypes` header now also takes Strings (in any case), as a list, an array or one String
  with comma separated values. An unknown value, or an element of another type, fails with an
  `IllegalArgumentException` instead of a `ClassCastException` in the AWS SDK. A single String, which was ignored
  before, is now used.
* startDocumentTextDetection, startDocumentAnalysis and startExpenseAnalysis now set the `CamelAwsTextractJobId`
  header to the job id of the response, so a get step that follows uses it. The body is still the response.

=== camel-aws2-timestream - describeScheduledQuery and the operation

* describeScheduledQuery now sends the ARN of the `CamelAwsTimestreamScheduledQueryArn` header. Previously it worked
  with `pojoRequest=true` only.
* Without an operation option and an operation header, the producer fails with an `IllegalArgumentException` instead of
  a `NullPointerException`. An operation of the other client type fails with a message that names the client type to
  use; when it is the `operation` option, the route already fails to start.
* An unknown client type in the URI fails with `Unknown clientType ...: use write or query`.
* The catalog now describes `CamelAwsTimestreamRecord` as what writeRecords sends it as: the common attributes of the
  records of `CamelAwsTimestreamRecordList`. `CamelAwsTimestreamRecordVersion` is listed as a `Long`, the type it is
  read as.

=== camel-aws2-s3-vectors - headers, returned data and metadata filter

* The consumer and the producer no longer set the `CamelAwsS3VectorsVectorBucketName` and
  `CamelAwsS3VectorsVectorIndexName` headers. They only echoed the endpoint, and the next aws2-s3-vectors step of the
  route then used that bucket and index instead of its own: a route from the consumer to putVectors on another index
  overwrote the consumed vectors. The headers still override the endpoint when the route sets them.
* getVectors and deleteVectors take their keys from the message body when it holds keys (a String, or a collection or
  an array of Strings), else from the `CamelAwsS3VectorsVectorId` header. Previously the header won, so a getVectors
  after a putVectors got the key of the putVectors. Keys that are not Strings now fail with an
  `IllegalArgumentException`.
* getVectors now returns the data and the metadata of the vectors, and queryVectors and the consumer return the
  metadata and the distance. Previously they returned the keys only. The consumer sets the new
  `CamelAwsS3VectorsDistance` header.
* The `CamelAwsS3VectorsMetadataFilter` header and the `consumerMetadataFilter` option are now applied. They are JSON
  objects in the filter syntax of S3 Vectors. Invalid JSON fails the exchange, or the consumer does not start.
  Previously both were ignored.
* The `similarityThreshold` option and the `CamelAwsS3VectorsSimilarityThreshold` and
  `CamelAwsS3VectorsSimilarityScore` headers are deprecated. They never did anything: S3 Vectors returns a distance and
  has no threshold.
* The values of `CamelAwsS3VectorsVectorMetadata` may be numbers, booleans and lists, which failed with a
  `ClassCastException` before. A null in a vector fails with an `IllegalArgumentException` that names its position.
* The `dataType` option no longer offers `float16`, and the `distanceMetric` option no longer offers `dot-product`:
  S3 Vectors has neither.

=== camel-aws-security-hub - headers of another type and failed imports

* A `CamelAwsSecurityHubFilters`, `CamelAwsSecurityHubNote`, `CamelAwsSecurityHubSeverity` or
  `CamelAwsSecurityHubWorkflow` header that is not of the AWS SDK type (`AwsSecurityFindingFilters`, `NoteUpdate`,
  `SeverityUpdate`, `WorkflowUpdate`) now fails with an `IllegalArgumentException`. Previously it was ignored: getFindings
  with the filters as JSON text returned every finding.
* batchImportFindings now fails the exchange with an `IllegalStateException` when Security Hub does not import some of
  the findings. The message names their ids and errors. The `CamelAwsSecurityHubFailedCount` and
  `CamelAwsSecurityHubSuccessCount` headers and the body are still set.
* batchImportFindings also takes an array or any collection of `AwsSecurityFinding` in the body. An element of another
  type fails with an `IllegalArgumentException` instead of a `ClassCastException`.

=== camel-aws-cloudtrail - the content type of the CloudEvent data type

The `aws-cloudtrail:application-cloudevents` data type now sets the content type `application/json`, as the body is
the JSON of the event. It was `text/plain`.

=== camel-python - a script gives the Java value of its result

The `python` language (Jython) now gives the Java value of what a script returns, instead of its Python text from
`str()`. `None` is `null`, a string, a number or a boolean is the Java `String`, number or `Boolean`, a dict, list,
tuple or set is a `Map`, `List` or `Set` of Java values, and a Java object bound to the script, such as the body, is
returned as it is. For example `1 + 2` gives the `Integer` 3 instead of the text `3`, and `body` on a byte array gives
the byte array instead of the text `array('b', [...])`. Set `resultType` to `java.lang.String` to get text.

This also changes predicates: `None` was the text `None`, which counted as true, and is now `null`, which is false. An
empty list was the text `[]`, which counted as true, and is now an empty collection, which is false.

An error while a script runs, such as a `NameError`, now fails with `ExpressionEvaluationException` instead of
`ExpressionIllegalSyntaxException`, which is kept for a script that does not compile.

=== camel-openfeature - an expression gives the value of a boolean flag

Used as an expression, for example in `setBody`, `setHeader` or `setProperty`, the `openfeature` language now gives the
`Boolean` value of a boolean flag. Before, it evaluated every flag as a string flag, so a boolean flag gave the
`defaultValue` of the endpoint as text (`false` unless set) and logged a `TYPE_MISMATCH` warning. String flags give
their text as before, and number and object flags still give the `defaultValue`. Predicates are unchanged.

=== camel-datasonnet - a null result and a stream body

An expression whose result is `null` now gives `null` instead of a `Document` without content, unless `resultType` is
`com.datasonnet.document.Document`. A predicate whose result is `null` is false; before, it failed with a
`NullPointerException`.

A stream body without a body media type is now read as text. Before, it failed while the stream was mapped as a Java
object.

=== camel-hl7 - the hl7terser language reads a stream or a file

The `hl7terser` language now reads a stream or a file body as an HL7 message, in the charset of MSH-18 as the HL7
data format does. A body that is not an HL7 message, such as a `Map`, or no body, now fails with an
`ExpressionEvaluationException` that says what the body is, instead of a `NullPointerException`; text that is not a
valid HL7 message fails with an `ExpressionEvaluationException` that has the parse error as its cause.

=== camel-javascript, camel-python3 - bytes and functions in the result of a script

A Python `bytes`, `bytearray` or `memoryview` returned by a `python3` script, and an `ArrayBuffer` returned by a `js`
script, now give a `byte[]` instead of a `List` of numbers. A script that returns a function now fails with an
`ExpressionEvaluationException`; before, it gave a value that failed when it was used later.

=== camel-quickjs - a Set, a Map or a typed array in the result of a script

A JavaScript `Set` returned by a `quickjs` script now gives a `List`, a JavaScript `Map` a `Map` with its keys as text,
and a typed array or an `ArrayBuffer` a `List` of numbers, also when nested. Before, a `Set` and a `Map` gave an empty
`Map`, and a typed array a `Map` of index to value.

=== camel-support - the error of a failed API component call names its arguments only

When the call of an API component fails, such as one of fhir, box, google-drive or twilio, the error message now names
the arguments of the call, as in `Error invoking resourceById with [password, resourceClass, serverUrl, stringId,
username]: ...`, and no longer shows their values. The arguments hold the options of the endpoint, such as a password or
a token, and the message data. The debug log of the call masks the values of the sensitive options.

=== camel-solr - the operation of the header or the endpoint wins over the type of the body

The solr producer now runs the operation of the `CamelSolrOperation` header, or else of the `operation` option, whatever
the body is. Before, the type of the body came first: a Map, a file or a collection that is not all Strings ran an
insert, and a collection of Strings ran a delete by id, even when the header or the option named another operation. The
body decides only when neither is set, and a body that is a request of SolrJ, such as a `SolrQuery` or an
`UpdateRequest`, still runs its own operation. The `CamelSolrQueryString` header makes a search only when no operation
is set, and a `CamelSolrOperation` header with a value that is not an operation now fails.

=== camel-vertx-http - the httpMethod option is not overridden by the CamelHttpMethod header

The `httpMethod` option of the endpoint now wins over the `CamelHttpMethod` header, as its description says and as in
camel-http. Before, the header overrode the option, so a route that received a request through an HTTP consumer, which
sets the header to the method of that request, sent it on with that method instead of the method of the endpoint. To
let the header choose the method, do not set `httpMethod` on the endpoint.

=== camel-as2 - the response to a failed route does not carry the message of the exception

The server/listen and receipt/receive consumers have the new `muteException` option, `true` by default: when the
route fails, the HTTP 500 that the partner gets says only that the message (or the receipt) could not be processed.
Before, its body was the message of the exception of the route. Set `muteException=false` to send the message of the
exception as before.

=== camel-as2 - quoted-printable parts keep their line breaks

Commons Codec 1.23.0 changed how a `quoted-printable` part is decoded. The line breaks (CRLF) that a partner writes into
the part are now kept in the decoded payload, as RFC 2045 says. Before, every CR and LF was dropped. A soft line break
written as `=CR` without the LF is now rejected with a `DecoderException`. Messages that Camel encodes are not affected.

=== camel-file-watch - CamelFileLastModified is the last modified time of the file

The `CamelFileLastModified` header is now the last modified time of the file, as its documentation says.
Before, it was the time of the event. The header is not set when the file no longer exists, for example for a `DELETE` event.
The time of the event is in the `CamelMessageTimestamp` header.

=== camel-whatsapp - the component is no longer marked producer only

The whatsapp component receives events in webhook mode (`webhook:whatsapp:...`), so its metadata no longer marks it as
producer only, and the catalog now describes the body that the webhook route gets. Tools that read the catalog, such as
the YAML DSL validator and the Endpoint DSL, now accept a whatsapp endpoint as a consumer, and the Endpoint DSL builder
of whatsapp gets the consumer options `bridgeErrorHandler`, `exceptionHandler` and `exchangePattern`. A whatsapp
endpoint used directly in `from` still fails at startup: wrap it with the webhook component, as before.

=== camel-cometd - the JSONP transport is disabled by default

The CometD JSONP transport (`callback-polling`) is now disabled by default. Only `long-polling` (and `websocket`,
when the CometD WebSocket transport is on the classpath) is enabled by default. Applications that require JSONP
clients can configure `allowedTransports` on the endpoint to include `callback-polling`
(for example, `allowedTransports=websocket,long-polling,callback-polling`).

=== camel-avro-rpc - the http consumer listens on the host of the endpoint

The `avro:http` consumer now uses the host of the endpoint URI (`avro:http:[host]:[port]`) for its HTTP server, as the `avro:netty` consumer does.

Use `0.0.0.0` as the host to accept connections on all network interfaces.

`AvroRpcHttpServerFactory` has a new method `create(SpecificResponder, String, int)` that receives the host. A custom factory should implement it.

=== camel-core - the simple resume cache follows the ResumeCache.forEach contract

The action passed to `ResumeCache.forEach(BiFunction<K, V, Boolean>)` returns `true` to invalidate (remove) the entry
and `false` to keep it, as documented on `ResumeAction.evalEntry` and as the Caffeine resume cache has always done.
The cache created by `TransientResumeStrategy.createSimpleCache()` previously did it the other way round: it removed
the entries for which the action returned `false`. It now removes the entries for which the action returns `true`.

* A custom `ResumeAction`, or any other action lambda passed to `ResumeCache.forEach`, that was written for the old
  behaviour of the simple cache must swap its return values: `false` keeps the entry, `true` removes it.
* A custom `ResumeCache` implementation must remove an entry when the action returns `true`, and must visit every
  entry (do not stop iterating when the action returns `false`).

*Impact on `KafkaResumeAdapter`:* `KafkaResumeAdapter.resume()` now returns `false` (keep the cache entry), so that
the topic-partition offset remains cached after the first seek and is re-applied on subsequent resumes.
Additionally, `KafkaResumeAdapter` now guards against seeking partitions that are no longer in the current consumer
assignment, avoiding `IllegalStateException` on revoked partitions.

=== camel-plc4x - PLC4X 1.0.0 and the triggered consumer

`camel-plc4x` now uses PLC4X 1.0.0, which requires Java 21, as Camel 4.24 does.

PLC4X 1.0.0 changes the connection strings of its drivers. Several parameters are renamed, for example
`request-timeout` to `request-timeout-ms`, and the S7 driver reads the rack and slot as `cotp.remote-rack` and
`cotp.remote-slot` instead of `remote-rack` and `remote-slot`. A parameter that a driver does not know, such as an old
name, is logged as a warning and does not apply. The OPC UA driver now uses the security policy `Basic256Sha256` by
default instead of `NONE`, `insecure-certificate-verification=true` becomes `tls.verify=false`, and a user name and
password are refused over a channel that is neither signed nor encrypted unless `allow-insecure-credentials` is set.
See the incompatible changes in the https://github.com/apache/plc4x/releases/tag/v1.0.0[PLC4X 1.0.0 release notes]
for the full list.

PLC4X 1.0.0 writes the array selection of a tag address before the data type, and as a range instead of a count. An
address written as `holding-register:1:INT[4]` or `%DB42:28.0:BYTE[4]` for four elements is now written
`holding-register:1[0..3]:INT` or `%DB42:28.0[0..3]:BYTE`; `[4]` before the data type selects the single element at
index 4. Addresses in the old form no longer parse. This applies to the `tag.*` options, to `trigger` and to the
addresses in the body of the producer.

The S7 driver of PLC4X 1.0.0 no longer includes the `s7-light` driver, so `plc4x:s7-light://` endpoints no longer find
a driver. Use `plc4x:s7://` instead.

A consumer with `trigger` now reads the trigger tag and the tags in one request on the connection of the endpoint,
every `period` milliseconds from the start of the route. Before, it sampled the trigger first after about one second,
and read the tags in a later, separate request on another connection. A failed read is now reported to the exception
handler of the consumer (or to the error handler of the route with `bridgeErrorHandler=true`), and polling continues;
before, the consumer stopped triggering after the first failed read of the trigger. After a failed poll the remembered
trigger value is unknown, so a trigger that is still `true` when the PLC returns emits once. Since the consumer now uses
the connection of the endpoint, `autoReconnect` governs its reconnects: without it, a lost connection stops
the endpoint and the polling until the route is restarted. A connection that cannot be set up when the
route starts fails the start of the route, unless `autoReconnect=true`, in which case the polls retry it. A route that
starts with the CamelContext then fails the start of the CamelContext too, unless the route is supervised by the
xref:route-controller.adoc[supervising route controller] or has `autoStartup=false`. As before, `period` must be
positive when `trigger` is set. A trigger that reads as a value that is not a boolean, such as a number or an array,
fails the poll. See the xref:components::plc4x-component.adoc[PLC4X component] for how triggered reading works.

With `autoReconnect=true`, a lost connection is now closed and replaced with a new one from the driver instead of being
reconnected, because the PLC4X 1.0.0 S7 and EtherNet/IP drivers cannot connect a connection again once it is lost. This
applies to the producer and to the consumers alike.

The component now removes its own options (`trigger`, `period`, `tag.*`, `autoReconnect` and the generic consumer
options such as `exceptionHandler`) from the connection string it hands to the driver. Before, the driver received them
as connection string parameters too.

The constant `Constants.PLC_NAME`, which was only used with the PLC4X scraper, was removed.

=== camel-plc4x - the producer checks the tags it writes

The value of each tag in the body of the producer must now be a `Map` of exactly one address to the value to write.
A tag with several addresses now fails the exchange with a `PlcInvalidTagException`, and nothing is written; before,
only one of its addresses was written and the others were ignored. Use one tag for each address. A tag whose value is
not a `Map`, or an empty `Map`, now also fails with a `PlcInvalidTagException` instead of a `ClassCastException` or a
`NoSuchElementException`.

=== camel-stripe - endpoint request options isolate credentials across endpoints

The `camel-stripe` producer now applies `apiKey` and `apiBase` using endpoint-scoped `RequestOptions` on each Stripe
API request, rather than mutating JVM-global Stripe SDK static configuration at startup. Multiple Stripe endpoints
configured in the same application now operate with complete configuration isolation.

Additionally, configuring an `apiKey` on either the endpoint or the component is now validated at startup, throwing
an `IllegalArgumentException` if missing rather than falling back to unauthenticated or globally-mutated SDK state.

Applications whose custom code (such as beans calling `Customer.retrieve(...)` directly) previously relied on
`camel-stripe` setting `Stripe.apiKey` globally in the JVM at startup must now configure `Stripe.apiKey` directly or
pass `RequestOptions` explicitly.

=== camel-lucene - inserted documents are indexed, one document per exchange

The `lucene:...:insert` endpoint now indexes the fields it writes, so a `lucene:...:query` finds the inserted messages
and the files of `srcDir`. Before, the fields were stored but not indexed, and a query never found them.

*Breaking change:* an index directory written by an earlier Camel version cannot be appended to. Lucene does not let a
field change from not indexed to indexed, so the insert fails with `IllegalArgumentException: cannot change field
"exchangeId" from index options=NONE to inconsistent index options=DOCS_AND_FREQS_AND_POSITIONS` (or the same for
another field). Delete the index directory (`indexDir`) and insert the messages again. A query on such an index does not
fail, but it finds none of its documents, as before.

Each exchange is now indexed as one document, with the body in `contents`, the exchange id in `exchangeId` and each
header in a field of the header name. Each file of `srcDir` is one document with the `contents` and `path` fields.
Before, each of these fields was a document of its own. The files of `srcDir` are indexed each time the insert endpoint
is created, such as when the application starts; the document of a file now replaces the one with the same `path`
instead of adding a copy. A query on a header, such as `author:carlin`, now gives a hit
whose data is the body of the exchange, and with `CamelLuceneReturnLuceneDocs` the document of a hit carries all the
fields of its exchange or file.

=== camel-wordpress - requests and responses are no longer logged

The WordPress client no longer logs every HTTP request and response it exchanges with the WordPress server at `INFO`
level (the `org.apache.cxf.services` loggers). The component no longer depends on `cxf-rt-features-logging`, and the
`camel-wordpress` JAR no longer contains a `log4j.properties` file.

=== camel-jbang - camel plugin add selects the plugin release built for the CLI

`camel plugin add <name>` no longer registers a community plugin (such as `forage` or `kit`) with the Maven `LATEST`
version when no version is given. It selects the newest release of the plugin built for the version of the CLI, from
the `camel.jbang.plugin.camel-versions` property in the POM of the plugin, or else from the version of its
`camel-jbang-core` dependency. The exact version is stored in `~/.camel-jbang-plugins.json`, so the plugin no longer
moves to newer releases on its own: run `camel plugin add <name>` again to select the newest matching release.
When the CLI is between the Camel versions of the releases, it selects the newest release for the nearest older Camel
version, with a warning. When the CLI is newer than all the releases, or the releases cannot be read, it warns and uses
`LATEST` as before.

`--version` and `--gav` are used as given, with a warning when the release is built for another Camel version. Plugins
registered before are not changed. See xref:camel-jbang-projects.adoc#_plugin_versions[Plugin versions].

=== camel-spring - a CamelEvent only goes to the consumers of the same name

A `spring-event` producer publishes a body that is not an `ApplicationEvent` as a `CamelEvent`. Before, every
`spring-event` consumer received it, whatever its name. Now only the consumers of the endpoint with the same name
receive it: a message sent to `spring-event:foo` goes to `from("spring-event:foo")`, and no longer to
`from("spring-event:bar")`.

Other application events of the `ApplicationContext`, such as `ContextRefreshedEvent` or an `ApplicationEvent` body
published by a producer, still go to every consumer.

=== camel-paho-mqtt5 - the producer sends a copy of the properties header

The producer used to hand the `MqttProperties` object of the `CamelPahoMqtt5MsgProperties` header to the client as it
was. The client writes the outgoing topic alias into that object, and the parts of a split, multicast, recipient list,
loop or wire tap of a received message share it, so once the broker's Topic Alias Maximum was used up, later messages
were delivered on the wrong topic. The producer now publishes a new `MqttProperties` with the payload format indicator,
message expiry interval, content type, response topic, correlation data and user properties of the header object, which
a publish no longer modifies. A topic alias or subscription identifiers set in the header object are not sent: the
client assigns topic aliases for its connection, and subscription identifiers are sent by the server only.

=== camel-wal - the unconfirmed offsets of the previous run are replayed after a restart

`WriteAheadResumeStrategy` now does what it is documented to do after a restart: when the consumer starts and loads the
resume cache (`loadCache()`), the strategy replays the offsets of the previous run that the wrapped resume strategy did
not confirm, by calling `updateLastOffset` on the wrapped strategy. Before, `start()` truncated the log file before
`loadCache()` read it, so nothing was replayed. Only the last offset of each key in the log counts: it is replayed when
its entry is in state `NEW` or `FAILED`, and an earlier offset of the same key is not replayed. These entries stay in
the log until the wrapped strategy confirms them through its callback; a wrapped strategy that never calls back gets
the last offset of each such key again at every start. The Kafka consumer does not call `loadCache()`, so with it the
entries are kept in the log but not replayed. A replay that fails, or an entry that the resume adapter cannot
deserialize, is logged with a WARN and does not stop the start of the route. If the last record of the log is
incomplete (the JVM stopped while writing it) or invalid, the entries before it are replayed and a WARN is logged; the
route still starts. A file that is not a log of this format is replaced by a new log, as before, with a WARN.

The log keeps 524,288 entries; after that it rolls over and holds the newest entries before the older ones, and the
file does not say which are newer. So a log that holds that many entries is not replayed, as before: a WARN names the
number of unconfirmed entries, which are discarded with the log. Replaying the wrong offset of a key would make the
consumer resume from an older offset. A log that rolled over and was cut short because the system stopped while the
log rewrote itself after the roll-over holds fewer entries and is replayed: the last entry of a key in it can then be
an older offset, so the consumer can process again the messages after that offset.

=== camel-core - a hook before each step of a route

A new SPI `org.apache.camel.spi.StepHook`, added with `ExtendedCamelContext.addStepHook`, can fail or delay each step of
a route. `ErrorHandlerRedeliveryCustomizer` has a new default method `useStepHook(String)`, so existing implementations
need no change. A custom error handler that wants a failure from the hook handled like a failure of the step's
processor (with `onException` and redelivery) can override it.

=== camel-cluster - ClusteredRouteController stops the cluster service once the routes have stopped

When the CamelContext stops, the `ClusteredRouteController` no longer stops the cluster service when the route
controller is stopped (which the CamelContext does before it stops the routes), but once the routes have stopped. A node
being stopped can keep (or still take) the leadership of its namespaces until its routes have finished their in-flight
exchanges, so another node no longer starts the clustered routes at the same time. A graceful stop can therefore hand
over the leadership later than before, by up to the shutdown timeout of the CamelContext (45 seconds by default). To
hand over sooner, lower the shutdown timeout (`camel.main.shutdownTimeout`, or
`CamelContext.getShutdownStrategy().setTimeout`).

When a `ClusteredRouteController` is stopped or shut down while the CamelContext keeps running (for example after it
was replaced by another route controller), it now stops its cluster service only if it started that service itself and
the service is not a service of the CamelContext (Camel Main, Spring Boot and Quarkus add the cluster services they
configure to the CamelContext). Before, it always stopped it, so the cluster route policies, master consumers and route
controllers that share the cluster service of the CamelContext gave up their leadership too. To leave the cluster while
the CamelContext runs, stop the cluster service itself.

=== camel-quartz - a route that keeps its trigger fires again when it is added again or after a restart

With `deleteJob=false` (as with `pauseJob=true`), a route pauses its trigger when it stops, and only the same endpoint
resumed it. A route that was removed and added again (a reload, a route template removed and added), and a route that
started after a restart with a persistent job store that is not clustered, therefore never fired again. The route now
marks the trigger it pauses, with the key `CamelQuartzTriggerPausedByCamel` in the `JobDataMap` of its job, and a
route resumes a paused trigger with this mark when it starts. A trigger that was paused outside Camel (with the Quartz
API or JMX) has no mark and stays paused; this now also holds when the same route is stopped and started again, which
resumed such a trigger before. A trigger that Camel paused before this version has no mark either: resume it once
with the Quartz API. A clustered job store is not changed, as Camel does not pause triggers there.

=== camel-langchain4j-ingest - a media document whose type cannot be told is filtered

With `modality=media` and no `contentType` set, a document whose media type cannot be told from the extension of its
id, such as a README or an editor backup beside the media, is now answered with a `filtered` result, as an id rejected
by `includeId` or `excludeId` is. Before, it failed the exchange with an `IllegalArgumentException`, so such a file in a
polled folder failed again on every poll. The exchange now completes, so a file consumer without `noop` moves such a
file into `.camel` instead of leaving it in place. A hint is logged at INFO. A document whose type is known but is not a
medium, or is one the embedding model does not support, still fails the exchange.

=== camel-langchain4j-web-search - webSearchRequest and additionalParams are not autowired, CONTENT falls back to the snippet

The `webSearchRequest` and `additionalParams` options are no longer autowired. Before, the only `WebSearchRequest` bean
of the registry was used by every `langchain4j-web-search` endpoint, so the search terms in the message body were
ignored, and the only `Map` bean of the registry was sent as the additional parameters of every search. Refer to such a
bean on the endpoints that should use it, for example `webSearchRequest=#myRequest`.

With `resultType=CONTENT` (the default), a result without content now gives its snippet instead of null. Google Custom
Search never gives content, and Tavily only with `includeRawContent(true)`, so with these engines the body was null
for a found result (or a list of nulls with `maxResults` above 1).

=== camel-jbang - camel export boots the integration in the same JVM, no longer through camel run

To find the dependencies, `camel export` boots the integration silently, as before, but no longer goes through
`camel run` for it. Some settings of `camel run` therefore no longer apply to an export:

* `camel.jbang.background` and `camel.jbang.jvmDebug` in `application.properties` are ignored by the export. Before,
  they made the export start a separate `camel run` process (in the background, or with a debugger), which kept running
  after the export, or which the export waited for.
* `--camel-version` is accepted by `camel export` from `camel-launcher` too: it sets the Camel version of the exported
  project. Before, it failed with a usage error.
* `camel export clipboard.yaml` no longer reads the route from the clipboard when there is no such file: it fails with
  exit code 66 (file not found), as for any other missing file. Save the content of the clipboard to a file and export
  that file.
* `camel export --profile=<profile>` boots the integration with that profile to find the dependencies. Before, it always
  booted with the `dev` profile, so a component that only `application-<profile>.properties` uses was missing from the
  exported project: such an export can now have more (or other) dependencies. The security policy of the profile (such
  as `prod`, which fails on an insecure option) does not fail the export, nor does `camel.security.policy=fail`: the
  export only warns. Without `--profile` the dependencies are the same as before.

=== camel-launcher - the distribution has the JARs in lib/

The zip and tar of the Camel CLI (`camel-launcher-<version>-bin`, which Homebrew, SDKMAN, WinGet, Scoop, Chocolatey,
the website installer and `camel self-update` install) no longer have the fat JAR in `bin/`. They have the JARs in
`lib/`, with `lib/camel-launcher-<version>.jar`, whose manifest names the other JARs of `lib/` as its class path. The
scripts in `bin/` run `java -jar lib/camel-launcher-<version>.jar`, so each command starts about 100 ms faster, and
`camel --help` about 250 ms. A script or an image that runs the fat JAR from the extracted distribution runs
`lib/camel-launcher-<version>.jar` instead, or the fat JAR from Maven Central, which has not changed.

The JARs of `lib/` are in the order of the dependencies of `camel-launcher`, which is the class path its tests run
with; the fat JAR had them in alphabetical order. `camel generate` no longer has `swagger-models` 2.2.52 next to
`swagger-models-jakarta` 2.2.36, two JARs with the same classes.

`bin/camel.sh` and `bin/camel.bat` turn on class data sharing: the JVM keeps the classes that a command loads in an
archive, one per command and Camel version, in `${XDG_CACHE_HOME:-~/.cache}/camel-cli/cds` (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\camel-cli\cds`
on Windows). The first run of a command creates its archive and takes longer (up to about a second), and the JVM
creates it again when the JDK changes. `CAMEL_CDS=false` turns it off, and so do CDS or AOT options in `JAVA_OPTS`
(such as `-Xshare:off`). The archives of a Camel version are not needed after an upgrade, and the directory can be
removed at any time.

=== camel-once - the consumer fires on a Camel thread pool

The once consumer now fires its route on a single thread scheduled executor that it creates through the
`ExecutorServiceManager` when the route starts and shuts down when the route stops, so the thread is named
`Camel (<context>) thread #N - <name>` instead of `<name>`. Before, each once consumer created a non-daemon
`java.util.Timer` thread when it was created, and that thread stayed alive after its route was stopped and after the
CamelContext was stopped (after its route was removed, until the consumer was garbage collected). A once route that is
stopped and started again now fires again, and so does a once route after the CamelContext is stopped and started
again; before, the start failed with `IllegalStateException: Task already scheduled or cancelled`.

The protected method `OnceConsumer.scheduleTask(TimerTask, Timer)` is replaced by `scheduleTask()`.

=== camel-bonita - startCase fails with InvalidPayloadException for a body that is not a Map

The `startCase` operation now fails with `org.apache.camel.InvalidPayloadException` when the body is missing or cannot
be converted to a `Map`, such as JSON text. Before, it failed with `IllegalArgumentException: The contract input is
null`, also when a body was sent. Unmarshal JSON text to a `Map` before the bonita endpoint.

The id of the started case is now set in the `CamelBonitaCaseId` header.

=== camel-camunda - a failed operation fails the exchange, and the JSON uses camelCase property names

The producer caught the error of the Camunda client, and gave a response with `success` set to `false` and the error
message, while the exchange completed as if the operation had worked. A failed operation now fails the exchange with a
`CamelExchangeException` that has the error message of Camunda; the response stays in the message body. Routes that
checked `success` in the response can handle the exception instead, for example with
`onException(CamelExchangeException.class).handled(true)`.

The JSON of the producer (the request bodies and, with `formatJSON=true`, the responses) now uses the camelCase property
names of the request and response objects, as Camunda itself and the JSON of the `worker` consumer do. Before, it used
snake_case names. The snake_case names are no longer accepted: a request with them gets the default values, for example
job key `0`. Rename the properties in your JSON:

[cols="1m,1m,2",options="header"]
|===
| Previous name | New name | Used in
| process_id | processId | `ProcessRequest`, `ProcessResponse`
| process_version | processVersion | `ProcessRequest`, `ProcessResponse`
| process_key | processKey | `ProcessRequest`, `ProcessResponse`
| process_instance_key | processInstanceKey | `ProcessRequest`, `ProcessResponse`
| correlation_key | correlationKey | `MessageRequest`, `MessageResponse`
| time_to_live | timeToLive | `MessageRequest`
| message_id | messageId | `MessageRequest`
| message_key | messageKey | `MessageResponse`
| job_key | jobKey | `JobRequest`
| fail_message | failMessage | `JobRequest`
| error_message | errorMessage | `JobRequest` and all responses
| error_code | errorCode | `JobRequest` and all responses
| file_content | fileContent | `DeploymentRequest`
| bpmn_process_id | bpmnProcessId | `ProcessDeploymentResponse`
| process_definition_key | processDefinitionKey | `ProcessDeploymentResponse`
| resource_name | resourceName | `ProcessDeploymentResponse`
|===

The job operations also read the job key from the `key` property of the JSON of a `worker`, so that JSON can be sent to
`completeJob`, `failJob`, `updateJobRetries` and `throwError` as is.

=== camel-support - DefaultThreadPoolFactory: virtual thread pools validate the pool size

With virtual threads enabled, a thread pool now rejects an invalid pool size at startup, as a platform thread pool
always did: `threads(5, 2)` (`maxPoolSize` below `poolSize`) and `threads(-1, 2)` (negative `poolSize`) fail with
`IllegalArgumentException`, where they started before.

A scheduled pool with virtual threads now keeps `poolSize` threads (before: none), and removes a cancelled task from its
queue, the same as a scheduled pool with platform threads. After shutdown, it handles a rejected task with the rejected
policy of its thread pool profile (by default it discards the task) instead of throwing `RejectedExecutionException`.

=== camel-jpa - a TransactionStrategy in the registry is used by default

The `transactionStrategy` option of the JPA component is now autowired. When no `TransactionStrategy` is configured,
the JPA component and endpoint now use the single `TransactionStrategy` bean in the registry, if there is one, before
creating the Spring based `DefaultTransactionStrategy`. Without such a bean, or with `autowiredEnabled=false`, nothing
changes. If there is more than one, none of them is used and a WARN is logged.

The bean applies to every JPA component and endpoint without a configured strategy, including ones using another
`EntityManagerFactory`. If the bean is bound to one `EntityManagerFactory` (for example a `DefaultTransactionStrategy`
created for it), set the `transactionStrategy` option explicitly.

The return type of the protected method `JpaEndpoint.createTransactionStrategy()` is now `TransactionStrategy` instead
of `DefaultTransactionStrategy`. A subclass that overrides it must be recompiled: compiled against the old return type,
its method is no longer called.

=== camel-jpa - JpaKeyValueRepository: putIfAbsent after a constraint violation

When the insert of `JpaKeyValueRepository.putIfAbsent` fails with a constraint violation because another caller stored
the key, the key is read again in a new transaction, which returns the value stored by the other caller. Before, the key
was read again in the failed transaction, which the constraint violation had marked for rollback, so `putIfAbsent`
failed when that transaction was committed (with a `DataIntegrityViolationException` when the transaction strategy is
Spring based). After a constraint violation, `putIfAbsent` now throws a `PersistenceException` only when the insert has
failed three times. A constraint violation is also recognised by its SQL state (class `23`), as the PostgreSQL and SQL
Server drivers report it. When `putIfAbsent` runs in a transaction of the caller, the failed insert marks that
transaction for rollback, so the caller's transaction fails, as before.

=== camel-jbang - the export to Quarkus keeps camel-quarkus-microprofile-health

`camel export --runtime=quarkus`, and `camel run --runtime=quarkus`, which exports the same way, now write
`org.apache.camel.quarkus:camel-quarkus-microprofile-health` to the `pom.xml` when the project asks for it:
`--dep=camel:microprofile-health`, `--dep=org.apache.camel:camel-microprofile-health`,
`--dep=org.apache.camel.quarkus:camel-quarkus-microprofile-health`, or the same in `camel.jbang.dependencies`. Before,
the export dropped it in every spelling, as if the Quarkus POM template contained it. Since Camel 4.14 the template
contains neither the extension nor `camel-quarkus-observability-services`, which brings it along.

A project that lists the dependency but does not want the health extension in the Quarkus export must remove it.
Without the dependency nothing changes: the health extension then only comes with `--observe` (which
`camel kubernetes export` always sets), as part of `camel-quarkus-observability-services`.

=== camel-git - the consumer applies the scheduler and error handler options

The `git` consumer (`type=commit`, `tag` and `branch`) now applies the scheduler options of the endpoint, such as
`delay`, `initialDelay`, `startScheduler`, `greedy` and the `backoff` options, and the `bridgeErrorHandler` and
`exceptionHandler` options. Before, it ignored them and always polled with an initial delay of 1 second and a delay of
500 milliseconds. A route that sets one of these options now gets what it configured: for example, `delay=60000` now
polls once a minute.

=== camel-git - a failed commit, tag or branch is delivered again on the next poll

The `git` consumer now marks a commit, tag or branch consumed only when its exchange completes without a failure. A
failed exchange (an exception that the route did not handle) is reported to the exception handler of the consumer,
which logs it at WARN by default, and the item is delivered again on the next poll. Before, the item was marked
consumed whether the route succeeded or not, so a failed item was never delivered again. The item then comes after
the items that followed it in the same poll, which were already delivered, such as the older commits.

An item that fails every time is now routed again at every poll. Handle the failure in the route, for example with a
dead letter channel or a handled `onException`, so that the item is marked consumed. To retry it less often, raise
`delay`, or set `backoffIdleThreshold` and `backoffMultiplier`: a poll counts only the items that it marked consumed,
so a poll whose items all failed is an idle poll, after which a `greedy` consumer does not poll again at once.

=== camel-jbang - runtime catalogs use the selected Camel version

Spring Boot and Quarkus catalogs now report the resolved Camel core version and read EIP/model schemas from that
version's catalog. Previously, they could combine the selected runtime's component schemas with the running CLI or
MCP server's newer model schemas. A model absent from the selected catalog now remains absent.
Runtime dependency coordinates still use the selected Spring Boot or Camel Quarkus version.

A Quarkus BOM must identify both `org.apache.camel:camel-catalog` and
`org.apache.camel.quarkus:camel-quarkus-catalog`. If either version or the selected catalog cannot be resolved,
catalog loading fails instead of returning the running server's catalog.

Spring Boot retains its existing fallback: if downloading `org.apache.camel:camel-catalog` for the requested
version fails, it tries the running CLI's Camel version. The catalog reports the core version actually resolved.

For Spring Boot, `camel export` and `camel run` without an explicit `--camel-version` now use the resolved core
catalog version for Camel core dependencies in the generated project. For example, `--camel-spring-boot-version=4.8.0`
uses Camel 4.8.0 instead of the running CLI's version when that catalog is available. Set `--camel-version` explicitly
to retain a different core dependency version.

=== camel-jbang - the Quarkus Platform is looked up only for the Quarkus runtime

With a runtime other than Quarkus, `camel run` and `camel export` no longer look up the Quarkus Platform in the Quarkus
Extension Registry. Before, the command stopped when the registry could not be reached and no cached answer existed,
also for an integration that uses nothing from Quarkus. For these runtimes `camel kubernetes export` and
`camel kubernetes run` no longer look it up either; with the Quarkus runtime they no longer ask `registry.quarkus.io`
in addition to the registry or the Quarkus version that is given.

With these runtimes, the dependencies that Camel CLI adds with the version of Quarkus now get the `quarkus-version` of
the `camel-dependencies` POM of the Camel CLI, as before Camel 4.21, instead of the version of the Quarkus Platform that
the registry has for the Camel version:

* `io.quarkus:quarkus-core`, for `jakarta.inject.Inject`, `jakarta.inject.Named`, `jakarta.inject.Singleton`,
  `jakarta.enterprise.context.ApplicationScoped` and `jakarta.enterprise.inject.Produces`: when a source imports one of
  them, or when one of them is loaded at runtime;
* the `io.quarkus:quarkus-jdbc-*` driver of a `quarkus.datasource.db-kind` property.

This is also the version of these dependencies in the `pom.xml` of an export to Camel Main or Spring Boot. With
`camel run` and `camel export`, `--quarkus-version` (or `camel.jbang.quarkusVersion`) still sets it.

The run settings (`.camel-jbang/camel-jbang-run.properties`) of these runs no longer have `camel.jbang.quarkusGroupId`,
`camel.jbang.quarkusVersion` and `camel.jbang.quarkusExtensionRegistryBaseUri`, unless a Quarkus version is given
(`--quarkus-version` or `camel.jbang.quarkusVersion`).

The export to Quarkus now leaves the version of the `io.quarkus` dependencies that Camel CLI adds itself to the Quarkus
Platform BOM, as for the Camel Quarkus extensions that the BOM manages. Before, the `pom.xml` had them with the version
of the run, which can be another one than the platform of the export, for example when the export reuses the data of
an earlier `camel run`. An `io.quarkus` dependency that is given with its version (`--dep` or
`camel.jbang.dependencies`) keeps that version. Nothing else changes for the Quarkus runtime.

=== camel-netty-http - logWarnOnBadRequest warns by default

The `logWarnOnBadRequest` option of a `netty-http` consumer is documented as enabled by default, but its field was
left at `false`, so a request that Netty could not decode got its HTTP 400 returned without a log line. The option now
defaults to `true`, as documented, so such a request logs `Netty request decoder failure due: <reason> returning HTTP
Status 400 to client` at `WARN` level. Set `logWarnOnBadRequest=false` on the consumer to keep the previous silence.

=== camel-azure-functions - invokeFunction uses the Content-Type header of the message

When the `CamelAzureFunctionsHttpHeaders` header does not set a `Content-Type`, `invokeFunction` now sends the
`Content-Type` header of the message, if there is one, before falling back to `application/json`. Before, the
request was always sent as `application/json` in that case.

A route whose message still carries a `Content-Type` header from an earlier step, for example from an HTTP
consumer, now sends that Content-Type. Remove the header, or set the Content-Type in
`CamelAzureFunctionsHttpHeaders`, to keep the old behavior.

=== camel-catalog - an empty deprecationNote is no longer written

The option metadata in the catalog (component, endpoint and header options, and the functions and operators of the
simple language) no longer has `"deprecationNote": ""`. The key is now present only when there is a note, as was
already the case for options without `@Metadata`. Tools that read the catalog JSON get no key (null with
`camel-tooling-model`) instead of an empty string.

=== camel-catalog - no default value for java.lang.Boolean options that declare none

A component or endpoint option of type `java.lang.Boolean` that declares no default value no longer has
`"defaultValue": false` in the catalog, and the javadoc of its Endpoint DSL and Component DSL methods no longer says
`Default: false`. The same holds for the `java.lang.Boolean` parameters of the API-based components. Such an option is
often not `false` when it is not set: another option, a library or the remote service then decides. Routes behave as
before.

The `closeStreamAfterRead` option of `azure-storage-datalake` now has the default value `true` in the catalog, the value
it has when it is not set.

Tools that read the default value from the catalog get none for these options (null with `camel-tooling-model`).

=== camel-langchain4j-embeddings - the producer uses the EmbeddingRequest API of LangChain4j

The producer now calls `EmbeddingModel.embed(EmbeddingRequest)` instead of `embed(TextSegment)` and
`embedAll(List<TextSegment>)`, so the `CamelLangChain4jEmbeddingsResponseModel` header is now set with the
model name of the response. `EmbeddingResponse` has no finish reason, so the
`CamelLangChain4jEmbeddingsFinishReason` header is no longer set.

A custom `EmbeddingModel` must implement `embedAll(List<TextSegment>)` (or `doEmbed(EmbeddingRequest)`), as
the LangChain4j models do. A model that only overrides `embed(TextSegment)` is no longer called, and the
default methods of LangChain4j then fail with a `StackOverflowError`.

=== Jackson 3

Camel 4.24 moves its own code to Jackson 3 (`tools.jackson`, version 3.2). The `camel-jackson`, `camel-jacksonxml`,
`camel-jackson-avro` and `camel-jackson-protobuf` data formats stay on Jackson 2; `camel-jackson3`, `camel-jackson3xml`,
`camel-jackson3-avro` and `camel-jackson3-protobuf` are the Jackson 3 line. The Jackson 3 sections below name the components
that moved and what changes for each; this section has what applies to all of them.

*One data format name, two artifacts.* Both `camel-jackson` and `camel-jackson3` register the `jackson` data format
(`marshal().json()`, `library: Jackson`). When both are on the classpath, the Jackson 3 one is used; before 4.24 the one first
on the classpath was used. Declare only one of them. An `ObjectMapper` in the registry is only found by the data format of the
same Jackson version.

*The Jackson 3 defaults apply.* The `camel-jackson3` data formats (JSON, XML, Avro and Protobuf) use the defaults of Jackson 3,
which differ from Jackson 2. Most visibly:

[cols="2,1,1,3"]
|===
| Feature | Jackson 2 | Jackson 3 | Effect of the Jackson 3 default

| `FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES` | on | off | Unknown properties are ignored, except by REST binding (see below).
| `SORT_PROPERTIES_ALPHABETICALLY` | off | on | Properties are written in alphabetical order.
| `WRITE_DATES_AS_TIMESTAMPS` | on | off | Dates are written as ISO-8601 text, not as numbers.
| `WRITE_DURATIONS_AS_TIMESTAMPS` | on | off | Durations are written as ISO-8601 text.
| `FAIL_ON_TRAILING_TOKENS` | off | on | Content after the first JSON value fails the read.
| `FAIL_ON_NULL_FOR_PRIMITIVES` | off | on | A `null` for a primitive field fails the read.
| `FAIL_ON_EMPTY_BEANS` | on | off | A bean without properties is written as `{}`.
| `USE_GETTERS_AS_SETTERS` | on | off | Collection and Map getters are not used as setters.
| `READ_ENUMS_USING_TO_STRING`, `WRITE_ENUMS_USING_TO_STRING` | off | on | Enums use `toString()`, not `name()`.
|===

Set a feature back with the `enableFeatures` and `disableFeatures` options of the data format, or with a custom
`ObjectMapper`:

[source,java]
----
restConfiguration().bindingMode(RestBindingMode.json)
    .dataFormatProperty("json.out.enableFeatures", "WRITE_DATES_AS_TIMESTAMPS");
----

NOTE: The REST DSL binding of a service enables `FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES` on its Jackson 3 data formats (`json`, and
`jacksonXml` from `camel-jackson3xml`), so a request body with properties that its type does not have is still rejected,
as with Jackson 2. To accept such bodies, set `json.in.disableFeatures` (or `xml.in.disableFeatures`) to
`FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES`. A feature you enable with `enableFeatures` is added to it. A data format with a mapper of
your own keeps the settings of that mapper: the `objectMapper` option or a mapper found with `autoDiscoverObjectMapper`
for JSON, the `xmlMapper` option for XML. The REST producer, which reads the responses of the services it calls, uses
the Jackson 3 default.

*Limits.* Jackson 3 reads and writes with other limits: nesting up to 500 levels (it was 1000), for reading and writing, and
strings up to 100 million characters (it was 20 million). They apply to every component on Jackson 3, including those whose
internal mappers keep the Jackson 2 defaults. To change them for a data format, set its `objectMapper` option to a mapper
with your own `StreamReadConstraints` (and `StreamWriteConstraints` for the nesting depth when writing):

[source,java]
----
JsonMapper mapper = JsonMapper.builder(JsonFactory.builder()
        .streamReadConstraints(StreamReadConstraints.builder()
                .maxNestingDepth(1000)
                .maxStringLength(20_000_000)
                .build())
        .build())
        .build();
----

*Exceptions.* Jackson 3 exceptions extend the unchecked `tools.jackson.core.JacksonException`, and are no longer an
`IOException`. `onException(IOException.class)` does not match them, and `JsonParseException` becomes
`tools.jackson.core.exc.StreamReadException`.

*Internal mappers.* A component that uses Jackson internally, to talk to an external API, parse a secret or write a file,
keeps the Jackson 2 defaults on its mapper, so what it sends and stores does not change unless its section says so.

*Jackson 2 dependency.* A component that moved to Jackson 3 no longer brings `com.fasterxml.jackson.core:jackson-databind`
to your application, unless a library it uses still needs it; at most `jackson-annotations` is left. If your code uses
Jackson 2, for example an `ObjectMapper` it got through `camel-salesforce`, declare `jackson-databind` yourself.

*Components that stay on Jackson 2.* `camel-jackson`, `camel-jacksonxml`, `camel-jackson-avro`, `camel-jackson-protobuf`,
`camel-azure-cosmosdb`, `camel-elasticsearch`, `camel-jq`, `camel-jslt`, `camel-metrics`, `camel-opa`, `camel-openai`,
`camel-openapi-java`, `camel-opensearch`, `camel-quickjs`, `camel-rest-openapi` and `camel-tahu` still use Jackson 2. Where
they take or return Jackson nodes, these are `com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.JsonNode`, not the
`tools.jackson.databind.JsonNode` that `camel-jackson3` produces. In a route that mixes the two, such as a `jq` expression
after `unmarshal().json()` in Camel JBang, pass the body between them as JSON text.

*Jackson 2 nodes.* A component on Jackson 3 accepts JSON text, bytes, streams, Maps, Lists and `tools.jackson.databind.JsonNode`
bodies. A `com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.JsonNode` is an ordinary bean to its mapper: pass it as JSON text, or produce it
with `camel-jackson3`. Where a component reads the whole body, a body that is a Jackson 2 node is read as its JSON text.

=== Jackson 3 - camel-jackson3 is the Jackson line of the catalog, Camel JBang and the Kamelets

The `jackson`, `jacksonXml`, `avroJackson` and `protobufJackson` data formats, and the `application-json`,
`application-x-java-object`, `application-x-struct`, `avro-*` and `protobuf-*` data type transformers, are registered by
two artifacts each: the Jackson 2 line (`camel-jackson`, `camel-jacksonxml`, `camel-jackson-avro`,
`camel-jackson-protobuf`) and the Jackson 3 line (`camel-jackson3`, `camel-jackson3xml`, `camel-jackson3-avro`,
`camel-jackson3-protobuf`). Camel 4.24 makes the Jackson 3 line the one these names stand for:

* The Camel catalog names the Jackson 3 artifacts for these data formats and transformers. Everything that turns a
  data format name into an artifact follows it: `camel run` and `camel export` in Camel JBang, the dependency download
  of `camel-kamelet-main`, the `camel-jbang` catalog commands, and the hint of an error about a missing data format
  (`add camel-jackson3 to the classpath`). A Camel JBang application that uses `marshal().json()` or a `json` data
  format in YAML or XML now runs on `camel-jackson3`, and the Jackson 3 defaults apply to it; `camel export` to Spring
  Boot writes `camel-jackson3-starter`. `camel export` to Quarkus writes the `camel-quarkus-jackson` extension (and
  `camel-quarkus-jacksonxml`, `camel-quarkus-jackson-avro`, `camel-quarkus-jackson-protobuf`), which is on Jackson 2
  until Camel Quarkus moves it to `camel-jackson3`: until then an exported Quarkus application runs the `json` data
  format with the Jackson 2 defaults, and Java code that imports `tools.jackson` needs that dependency added. Declaring `camel-jackson` as a dependency does not change that: the artifact
  the catalog names is downloaded as well, and the Jackson 3 one is used (see the next point). Use the
  `enableFeatures` and `disableFeatures` options of the data format to restore a Jackson 2 default.
* When both lines are on the classpath, the Jackson 3 data format and transformers are used. Before 4.24 the one
  whose service file was found first on the classpath was used. An `ObjectMapper` in the registry is only used by the
  data format of the same Jackson version.
* `camel-kamelet-main` depends on the Jackson 3 line instead of the Jackson 2 one. The `DelegatingSchemaResolver` of the
  Kamelet data type utilities (used by `resolve-pojo-schema-action`) delegates to the Jackson 3 `JsonSchemaResolver`,
