  `AvroSchemaResolver` and `ProtobufSchemaResolver`, or to the Jackson 2 ones when the Jackson 3 artifact is not on the
  classpath.
* A Kamelet that declares `camel:jackson`, `camel:jackson-avro` or `camel:jackson-protobuf` still gets that Jackson 2
  artifact, since the dependency names the artifact, but in Camel JBang its data formats and transformers run on
  Jackson 3. Jackson 2 class names in a Kamelet therefore no longer work there: an `unmarshalType` of
  `com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.JsonNode` fails on each message, and a Jackson 2 module in `moduleClassNames` or a
  Jackson 2 `SchemaResolver` bean fails when the route starts.

*Kamelets.* Camel 4.24 in Camel JBang needs Kamelets 4.24 for the Kamelets that name Jackson 2 classes:
`json-deserialize-action`, `avro-serialize-action`, `avro-deserialize-action`, `protobuf-serialize-action`,
`protobuf-deserialize-action`, `resolve-pojo-schema-action` (for Avro and Protobuf), `azure-storage-blob-changefeed-source`,
`jira-source` and `jira-oauth-source`, `aws-s3-event-based-source`, `ceph-event-based-source` and
`azure-storage-blob-event-based-source` (with `getObject` or `getBlob` enabled), and the eight kafka field actions (see
"Jackson 3 - camel-kafka"). The 4.24.0 launcher ships the 4.23 Kamelets: run it with `--kamelets-version=4.24.0`. Kamelets
that only use the `json` data format by name keep working, with the Jackson 3 defaults: for example, `json-serialize-action`
writes properties in alphabetical order and dates as text. Outside Camel JBang, an application on the Jackson 2 line runs
the 4.23 Kamelets as before, except the kafka field actions.

The `camel-jackson` artifacts are unchanged and stay on Jackson 2. An application that declares them, outside Camel
JBang, keeps using Jackson 2 as long as it does not also have the Jackson 3 line on the classpath.

To keep Jackson 2 for a data format in Camel JBang, add the `camel:jackson` dependency (`--dep=camel:jackson`, or
`camel.jbang.dependencies=camel:jackson` in `application.properties`), define the Jackson 2 data format as a bean, and
refer to it as a custom data format instead of by the `json` name:

[source,yaml]
----
- beans:
    - name: jackson2
      type: org.apache.camel.component.jackson.JacksonDataFormat
- route:
    from:
      uri: direct:start
      steps:
        - marshal:
            custom:
              ref: jackson2
----

=== Jackson 3 - camel generate schema

`camel generate schema` now uses `jsonschema-generator` 5, which is based on Jackson 3. The annotations in
`com.fasterxml.jackson.annotation` still apply, but `@JsonNaming` from `com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.annotation` is ignored:
the schema of such a class has the Java field names. Use `@JsonNaming` from `tools.jackson.databind.annotation` to apply it.

=== Jackson 3 - components that no longer use camel-jackson

`camel-aws-bedrock`, `camel-dhis2`, `camel-google-vertexai`, `camel-mongodb` and `camel-neo4j` now use Jackson 3
(`tools.jackson.core:jackson-databind`) directly, and `camel-mongodb-gridfs` no longer uses Jackson. None of them brings
`camel-jackson` or `camel-jackson3` to your application any more. If a route uses the `jackson` data format
(`marshal().json()`), or relies on its type converters, and got it through one of these components, declare `camel-jackson`
or `camel-jackson3` yourself.

`camel-aws2-ddb` and `camel-google-sheets` no longer use Jackson for their JSON data type transformers
(`aws2-ddb:application-json` and `google-sheets:application-x-struct`), so they need neither `camel-jackson` nor
`camel-jackson3`. A body that is a Jackson `JsonNode` of either line is read as its JSON text.

The data these components send does not change: their internal mappers keep the Jackson 2 defaults.

*camel-aws-bedrock.* A text result (the Anthropic Claude v2, Cohere and Llama models) is now a `String` body; it was a Jackson
`TextNode` that `camel-jackson` converted to the text. The other results stay nodes, now of Jackson 3: the AI21 completions,
the images, and the whole response of the Claude 3, Nova and Mistral models. The type converters of `camel-jackson` do not
apply to them, so converting such a body to a pretty-printed `String`, `byte[]`, `InputStream` or `Map` needs `camel-jackson3`.
Without it, `getBody(String.class)` gives the compact JSON and the other conversions give `null`.

*camel-mongodb.* The conversion of a body to a `Document` or `DBObject` uses Jackson 3. A Jackson `JsonNode` of either line is
read as its JSON text, but a POJO with Jackson 2 databind annotations such as `@JsonNaming` loses them, so the field names
it is stored with can change.

=== Jackson 3 - components that use Jackson internally

`camel-bonita`, `camel-clickup`, `camel-lumberjack`, `camel-ocsf`, `camel-pqc`, `camel-sap-netweaver`, `camel-servicenow`,
`camel-splunk-hec`, `camel-stitch`, `camel-stripe`, `camel-telegram`, `camel-weather`, `camel-whatsapp`, `camel-wordpress`
and `camel-zookeeper-master` now use Jackson 3 (`tools.jackson`). The JSON they send, store or return does not change: their
internal mappers keep the Jackson 2 defaults. You only need to act if your code passes Jackson types to these components.

*`camel-servicenow`.* The `mapper` option is now a `tools.jackson.databind.ObjectMapper`, and must be a
`tools.jackson.databind.json.JsonMapper`, or the endpoint fails with an `IllegalArgumentException`. A custom mapper does not
get the Jackson 2 defaults, nor the `dateFormat`, `timeFormat` and `dateTimeFormat` options. The payload of the metadata
extension is a `tools.jackson.databind.JsonNode`. Request and response models that use `@JsonSerialize` or `@JsonDeserialize`
must import them from `tools.jackson.databind.annotation`; Jackson 3 ignores the ones from
`com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.annotation`.

*`camel-ocsf`.* `setObjectMapper` and the constructor take a `tools.jackson.databind.ObjectMapper`, and only a mapper of that
type is found in the registry. An unmarshal body of type `com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.JsonNode` is no longer recognized:
pass JSON text or `tools.jackson.databind.JsonNode`. With `prettyPrint`, the data format uses a copy of your mapper, and no
longer changes the mapper itself.

*Model classes.* The `camel-telegram` and `camel-clickup` models write their timestamps with Jackson 3 annotations. A Jackson 2
mapper ignores those, so to marshal these models use `camel-jackson3`, not `camel-jackson`.

*Exceptions.* `camel-clickup` webhooks, `camel-ocsf`, `camel-pqc`, `camel-sap-netweaver`, `camel-servicenow`,
`camel-splunk-hec` and `camel-weather` let a JSON failure through as the unchecked `tools.jackson.core.JacksonException`, which
is not an `IOException`, so `onException(IOException.class)` does not match it.

=== Jackson 3 - camel-cbor, camel-jsonata and camel-xj

`camel-cbor`, `camel-jsonata` and `camel-xj` now use Jackson 3 (`tools.jackson`) instead of Jackson 2. Jackson 3 exceptions
are unchecked and are not an `IOException`, so `onException(IOException.class)` does not match them.

*camel-cbor.* A custom `ObjectMapper` must be a `tools.jackson.databind.ObjectMapper`, such as
`tools.jackson.dataformat.cbor.CBORMapper`: a Jackson 2 mapper in the registry is no longer found. `prettyPrint`,
`enableFeatures` and `disableFeatures` now apply to a copy of a mapper from the registry, not to that mapper.

The data format uses the defaults of Jackson 3, listed in the section "Jackson 3": properties in
alphabetical order, dates as text, enums with `toString()`, and content after the first value fails the read. `enableFeatures`
and `disableFeatures` set them back, and now also accept the names of `DateTimeFeature`, `EnumFeature`, `JsonNodeFeature`,
`StreamReadFeature` and `StreamWriteFeature`. In Java, `enableFeature(SerializationFeature.WRITE_DATES_AS_TIMESTAMPS)` no
longer compiles, because that feature is now in `DateTimeFeature`: use `setEnableFeatures("WRITE_DATES_AS_TIMESTAMPS")`.

The mapper that the data format creates keeps the CBOR encoding of Jackson 2, so data sent or stored by earlier versions
decodes to the same values. A custom `new CBORMapper()` uses the encoding of Jackson 3: it follows RFC 8949 for a negative
`BigInteger`, so a value written by Jackson 2 is read as a different number, and it reads CBOR simple values as `CBORSimpleValue`
rather than as numbers. To keep the encoding of Jackson 2 on a custom mapper, disable
`CBORWriteFeature.ENCODE_USING_STANDARD_NEGATIVE_BIGINT_ENCODING`,
`CBORReadFeature.DECODE_USING_STANDARD_NEGATIVE_BIGINT_ENCODING`, `CBORReadFeature.READ_UNDEFINED_AS_EMBEDDED_OBJECT` and
`CBORReadFeature.READ_SIMPLE_VALUE_AS_EMBEDDED_OBJECT` on its `CBORFactory`.

A `com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.JsonNode` body is no longer treated as a tree: use `tools.jackson.databind.JsonNode`.

*camel-jsonata.* With the default `outputType`, the result is now a `tools.jackson.databind.JsonNode` instead of a
`com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.JsonNode`, so code that reads the body as the Jackson 2 type must change. Invalid JSON now
fails with `tools.jackson.core.exc.StreamReadException`. The result of an expression does not change. A body that is a Jackson 2
`JsonNode` is read as its JSON text, but a Jackson 2 node inside a `Map` or `List` body is read as a bean: a component on
Jackson 3 treats a `com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.JsonNode` as an ordinary bean, so pass JSON text or produce the node with
`camel-jackson3`. A `java.time` value in a POJO body no longer fails: it is written as numbers.

WARNING: A Jackson 2 mapper writes a Jackson 3 `JsonNode` as a bean, without any error. If a route marshals the result with
`camel-jackson` (`marshal().json()`), the JSON it produces is wrong. Use `outputType=JsonString`, or use `camel-jackson3`.

*camel-xj.* The output does not change. The cause of a failed transformation is now a Jackson 3 exception, such as
`tools.jackson.core.exc.StreamReadException`. The public classes `XmlJsonStreamWriter`, `JsonXmlStreamReader`,
`JsonSourceHandlerFactoryImpl` and the result handlers now take the Jackson 3 `JsonFactory`, `JsonGenerator` and `JsonParser`.

=== Jackson 3 - vault, cloud and AI components

`camel-aws-secrets-manager`, `camel-google-secret-manager`, `camel-google-firestore`, `camel-ibm-secrets-manager`,
`camel-cyberark-vault`, `camel-knative`, `camel-elasticsearch-rest-client`, `camel-keycloak`, `camel-telemetry-dev`,
`camel-micrometer`, `camel-docling`, `camel-huggingface` and `camel-chatscript` now use Jackson 3 internally. What they read
and write does not change: their mappers keep the Jackson 2 defaults, including that content after the first JSON value of a
secret is ignored.

They no longer bring `com.fasterxml.jackson.core:jackson-databind` to your application (`camel-keycloak` still does, through
the Keycloak client). If your code uses it, declare it yourself.

Changes to API types:

* `Knative.MAPPER` in `camel-knative-api` is a `tools.jackson.databind.ObjectMapper`.
* `MicrometerModule` in `camel-micrometer` is a Jackson 3 module (`tools.jackson.databind.JacksonModule`).

Exceptions: a response that `camel-elasticsearch-rest-client` or a model output that `camel-huggingface` cannot parse, and a
structured CloudEvent that `camel-knative` cannot parse, now fail with the unchecked `tools.jackson.core.JacksonException`, not
an `IOException`. `onException(IOException.class)` does not match it. Loading a `KnativeEnvironment` still fails with an
`IOException`.

=== Jackson 3 - camel-salesforce

`camel-salesforce`, `camel-salesforce-codegen` and `camel-salesforce-maven-plugin` use Jackson 3 (`tools.jackson`). What the
component sends to and reads from Salesforce does not change, and neither does the output of the `schema` goal.

DTOs generated with an earlier version of `camel-salesforce-maven-plugin` must be generated again. They import
`JsonTypeResolver`, `JsonSerialize` and `JsonDeserialize` from `com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.annotation`, and Jackson 3
ignores those annotations, so nested types and multi-select picklists are no longer handled. Generated source that still has
these imports fails to compile, because the classes they name are now Jackson 3 classes. A JAR of DTOs compiled against an
earlier version gives no such error. In hand-written DTOs, import these annotations from
`tools.jackson.databind.annotation`. The annotations in `com.fasterxml.jackson.annotation` do not change.

The following Java API changes:

* The `objectMapper` option and `SalesforceEndpointConfig.setObjectMapper` take a `tools.jackson.databind.ObjectMapper`. A
  custom mapper is used as it is, and Jackson 3 has other defaults than Jackson 2, so start from
  `JsonUtils.createObjectMapper().rebuild()` to keep the format that Salesforce expects.
* `JsonUtils` returns and takes `tools.jackson.databind.ObjectMapper`, and its schema methods use
  `tools.jackson.module.jsonSchema.jakarta.JsonSchema`. They no longer declare `JsonProcessingException`.
* `TimeModule`, `TypeReferences` and the serializers and deserializers in `org.apache.camel.component.salesforce.api` are
  Jackson 3 types.
* `JsonUtils.withNullSerialization` no longer fails on a DTO with a date value set.

A response that cannot be parsed is still reported as a `SalesforceException`. Jackson errors in your own code are the unchecked
`tools.jackson.core.JacksonException`, which is no longer an `IOException`.

=== Jackson 3 - camel-a2a

`camel-a2a` now uses Jackson 3 internally. The JSON it sends does not change: its mapper keeps the Jackson 2 defaults. What
it accepts follows the limits of Jackson 3: nesting up to 500 levels (it was 1000) and strings up to 100 million characters
(it was 20 million).

It no longer brings `com.fasterxml.jackson.core:jackson-databind` or `jackson-datatype-jsr310` to your application. If your
code uses them, declare them yourself.

Changes to API types:

* `A2AJsonMapper.instance()` returns a `tools.jackson.databind.ObjectMapper`. It is immutable, so this is a shared instance
  and not a copy. Use `rebuild()` to derive a customized mapper.
* `PartDeserializer` is a Jackson 3 `ValueDeserializer`, and `Part` and `AgentCard` declare their deserialization with Jackson 3
  annotations. A Jackson 2 `ObjectMapper`, such as the one in the `camel-jackson` data format, can no longer read these types
  or the types that contain them, such as `Message` and `Task`. Use `A2AJsonMapper.instance()` or the `camel-jackson3` data format.
* Objects you put in a `DataPart` or in metadata are written with Jackson 3. It ignores `com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.annotation`
  annotations such as `@JsonSerialize`, and it writes a Jackson 2 `JsonNode` as a bean, with properties such as `nodeType`,
  instead of as its JSON. Use a Jackson 3 `JsonNode` or a `Map` instead.

=== Jackson 3 - camel-jsonpath

`camel-jsonpath` now uses Jackson 3 (`tools.jackson`) instead of Jackson 2, and no longer brings in Jackson 2. Code that got
Jackson 2 through `camel-jsonpath` must declare it. The results of an expression do not change: POJO bodies are still read
and written with the defaults of Jackson 2, so dates are numbers, properties stay in declared order and content after the
first JSON value is ignored. Invalid JSON still fails with `com.jayway.jsonpath.InvalidJsonException`, but its cause is now a
Jackson 3 exception, such as `tools.jackson.core.exc.StreamReadException`.

A custom `ObjectMapper` in the registry must be a `tools.jackson.databind.ObjectMapper`: a Jackson 2 mapper is no longer
found, without any error, and the default is used instead.

A `JsonNode` body of either line works, and `writeAsString` writes a result that is a Jackson 2 node as its JSON. A Jackson 2
node inside a `Map`, `List` or POJO result is written as a bean: a component on Jackson 3 treats a
`com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.JsonNode` as an ordinary bean, so pass JSON text or produce the node with `camel-jackson3`. The
text of a node (`"t"`) is now read as the JSON string it is, where a Jackson 2 text node failed as invalid JSON.

A `java.time` value in a POJO body no longer fails: it is written as numbers. JAXB annotations on a POJO are used when
`tools.jackson.module:jackson-module-jakarta-xmlbind-annotations` is on the classpath; `jackson-module-jaxb-annotations` of
Jackson 2 is no longer used.

The limits on what is parsed are those of Jackson 3: nesting up to 500 levels (it was 1000) and strings up to 100 million
characters (it was 20 million). To change them, register a mapper with your own `StreamReadConstraints`.

=== Jackson 3 - camel-kafka

`camel-kafka` now uses Jackson 3 (`tools.jackson`) instead of Jackson 2, and no longer brings Jackson 2 to your application.
The transforms in `org.apache.camel.component.kafka.transform` (`DropField`, `ExtractField`, `HoistField`, `InsertField`,
`MaskField`, `MessageTimestampRouter`, `ReplaceField` and `ValueToKey`) keep their names and work on a
`tools.jackson.databind.JsonNode`, JSON text or bytes, and need `camel-jackson3` on the classpath for their type converters. A
`com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.JsonNode` body is an ordinary bean to them, and `HoistField` writes it as one without an error:
produce the node with `camel-jackson3`, or pass JSON text.

The Kamelets that use these transforms (`drop-field-action`, `extract-field-action`, `hoist-field-action`,
`insert-field-action`, `mask-field-action`, `message-timestamp-router-action`, `replace-field-action` and
`value-to-key-action`) do so from their 4.24 release, so Camel 4.24 needs Kamelets 4.24 for them, also outside Camel JBang.
See "Kamelets" in the section "Jackson 3 - camel-jackson3 is the Jackson line of the catalog, Camel JBang and the Kamelets".

The kafka producer still sends a Jackson 2 array node as one record per element, and any other Jackson 2 node as one record.

=== Jackson 3 - components with a mapper of their own

`camel-hashicorp-vault`, `camel-google-functions`, `camel-azure-key-vault`, `camel-camunda`, `camel-langchain4j-agent`,
`camel-huaweicloud-dms`, `camel-huaweicloud-functiongraph`, `camel-huaweicloud-iam`, `camel-huaweicloud-obs` and
`camel-huaweicloud-smn`, and the `camel infra` and `camel semantic eval` commands of Camel JBang, now use Jackson 3 internally.
What they read and write does not change: their mappers keep the Jackson 2 defaults. The SDK each component talks to (Spring
Vault, Google Cloud, Azure, Camunda, LangChain4j, Huawei Cloud) is not involved: no Jackson type crosses its API, so these SDKs
stay as they are, and most of them still bring `com.fasterxml.jackson.core:jackson-databind` to your application.
`camel-hashicorp-vault` and `camel-google-functions` no longer bring it: if your code uses it, declare it yourself.

A `java.time` value in a POJO body no longer fails: `callFunction` of `camel-google-functions` sends the POJO as JSON, and
`camel-huaweicloud-smn` publishes it as JSON instead of as its `toString()`. The value is written as numbers.

Exceptions: `camel-huaweicloud-dms` and `camel-huaweicloud-iam` let a failure to write a response as JSON through as the
unchecked `tools.jackson.core.JacksonException`, which is not an `IOException`, so `onException(IOException.class)` does not
match it.

=== Jackson 3 - camel-json-validator

`camel-json-validator` now uses json-schema-validator 3 (`com.networknt`), which is built on Jackson 3
(`tools.jackson`), and no longer brings Jackson 2 to your application.

*Object mapper.* The `objectMapper` option of the endpoint is now a `tools.jackson.databind.ObjectMapper`, and the
`objectMapper` and `useDefaultObjectMapper` options of the component only find a mapper of that type in the registry. A
mapper that the `objectMapper` option of the component names, and that is not found or is a Jackson 2 mapper, now fails
the creation of the endpoint with a `NoSuchBeanException`; it was ignored before. A Jackson 2 mapper that
`useDefaultObjectMapper` found in the registry, such as one a runtime registers, is no longer used, without a warning:
bind a Jackson 3 mapper with the same settings to keep them. With `useDefaultObjectMapper`, a single
Jackson 3 mapper in the registry is now used to read the JSON. With the Jackson auto-configuration of Spring Boot
(`spring-boot-starter-json`, which the web starters include), that is its `JsonMapper`, which uses the Jackson 3 defaults:
content after the JSON value now fails the validation. To accept it again, set `spring.jackson.use-jackson2-defaults=true`,
or `disabledDeserializationFeatures=FAIL_ON_TRAILING_TOKENS` on the endpoint.

*Reading the JSON.* Without a mapper of your own, the body is read with the Jackson 2 defaults, as before: content after the
first JSON value is ignored unless you enable `FAIL_ON_TRAILING_TOKENS` with the `enabledDeserializationFeatures` option.
The limits are those of Jackson 3 (see *Limits* in <<_jackson_3,Jackson 3>>): JSON nested more than 500 levels deep now
fails. A mapper of your own is read with its own settings. Unlike the components in *Jackson 2 nodes*, `camel-json-validator`
still reads only JSON text, bytes and streams: a `Map` or `List` body fails, as before; marshal it to JSON first. The `enabledDeserializationFeatures` and `disabledDeserializationFeatures`
options take the names of `tools.jackson.databind.DeserializationFeature`, and configure a copy of your mapper rather than
the mapper itself. A name that Jackson 3 moved to another type of feature, such as `READ_ENUMS_USING_TO_STRING` (now an
`EnumFeature`) or `ADJUST_DATES_TO_CONTEXT_TIME_ZONE` (now a `DateTimeFeature`), fails the creation of the endpoint.
A body that is not JSON reaches the error handler with a `tools.jackson.core` exception as the cause; see *Exceptions* in
<<_jackson_3,Jackson 3>>.

*Validation.* json-schema-validator 3.0 also changed the outcome of some checks. Where the `format` keyword is asserted,
`date-time` requires a `T` and an explicit offset, and `time` requires the seconds. See the
https://github.com/networknt/json-schema-validator/blob/master/CHANGELOG.md[changelog of json-schema-validator] for the
full list.

*Custom error handlers and schema loaders.* The `JsonValidatorErrorHandler` and `JsonUriSchemaLoader` interfaces keep the
same `com.networknt` types, but an `Error` gives the instance node as a `tools.jackson.databind.JsonNode`. The `mapper`
field that a subclass of `DefaultJsonUriSchemaLoader` inherits is now a `tools.jackson.databind.ObjectMapper`: a subclass
that uses or replaces it must move to Jackson 3.

*YAML DSL validator.* `camel validate yaml` and the YAML DSL validator Maven plugin read the YAML with Jackson 3, which
follows YAML 1.2: `yes`, `no`, `on` and `off` are no longer booleans, and `1_000` and `0b101` are no longer numbers. An
option such as `streaming: yes` is now reported; the route fails to start with it, so write `true` or `false`.

=== Jackson 3 - the MCP JSON module of camel-mcp-server and camel-openai

`camel-mcp-server` and the MCP client of `camel-openai` now use the Jackson 3 JSON module of the MCP Java SDK,
`io.modelcontextprotocol.sdk:mcp-json-jackson3`, instead of `mcp-json-jackson2`. `McpJsonDefaults.getMapper()` returns a
mapper of that module. `camel-mcp-server` no longer brings `com.fasterxml.jackson.core:jackson-databind` to your application;
`camel-openai` still does, for the OpenAI client.

The module moved with `camel-json-validator`: `mcp-json-jackson2` needs json-schema-validator 2, and `mcp-json-jackson3` and
`camel-json-validator` need json-schema-validator 3, which an application can only have one of. If your application declares
`mcp-json-jackson2` itself, replace it with `mcp-json-jackson3`: with json-schema-validator 3, `mcp-json-jackson2` fails with a
`NoSuchMethodError` as soon as it checks the schema of a tool.

The mapper of `mcp-json-jackson3` uses the Jackson 3 defaults (see the table in <<_jackson_3,Jackson 3>>). This mainly
changes the JSON of the SDK's own message records and of POJOs inside a `Map`, such as the order of their properties and
how enums and dates are written.

=== camel-docling - the apiKeyHeader option is deprecated

The `apiKeyHeader` option never had any effect: the name of the header that carries the API key is fixed in the
docling-serve client, which always sends it as `X-Api-Key`, and the producer never read the option. It is now
deprecated, with a `deprecationNote` that says so. Remove `apiKeyHeader` from the endpoint URI or the configuration:
with `authenticationScheme=API_KEY` and `authenticationToken` set, the key is still sent, as `X-Api-Key`.

=== camel-docling - the CamelDoclingEnableOCR and CamelDoclingOCRLanguage headers are read

The `CamelDoclingEnableOCR` and `CamelDoclingOCRLanguage` headers were declared and documented as per-message
overrides of `enableOCR` and `ocrLanguage`, but the component never read them: the message was OCRed the way the
endpoint was configured. They now override those two settings for the message, on the docling CLI path and on the
docling-serve path, as `CamelDoclingUseAsyncMode` already overrode `useAsyncMode`. A header that turns OCR off does
not pass a language on; a language header alone turns OCR on, unless the endpoint has disabled it with
`enableOCR=false`, which keeps winning over the header.

The language itself is passed to docling untouched, in both forms: it is docling's own identifier (language codes such
as `en,de`, BCP-47 tags such as `iso:zh-Hant`, or `script/Latin`), and docling validates it.

=== camel-influxdb2 - the retentionPolicy option and the camelInfluxDB.RetentionPolicy header are deprecated

Neither has ever had any effect. A retention policy is an InfluxDB 1.x concept: its write API takes one per write, and
`camel-influxdb` passes it there. The InfluxDB 2.x write API that this component calls does not -- retention is a
property of the bucket -- so `InfluxDb2Producer` never reads the option or the header, and both were carried over from
`camel-influxdb` along with the header's 1.x spelling, `camelInfluxDB.RetentionPolicy`.

Both are now deprecated with a note that says so. Remove `retentionPolicy` from the endpoint URI and stop setting the
header: they are silently ignored. To control how long the data is kept, set the retention period on the bucket the
endpoint writes to.

=== camel-language - the CamelLanguageScript header is used once and removed

With `allowTemplateFromHeader=true`, a script from the `CamelLanguageScript` header is now used for that message only.
Before, with `cacheScript=true` it could replace the cached script of the endpoint, so later messages without the header
ran it too. The header is now removed after use, so a later language endpoint in the route runs its own script instead
of the header's. Set the header again before each language endpoint that should use it.

=== camel-jbang - camel infra run artemis maps MQTT and AMQP only on request

`camel infra run artemis` maps the core acceptor (61616) and the web console (8161) on the host, and no longer the
MQTT (1883) and AMQP (5672) ports, which are the ports of mosquitto and RabbitMQ: Artemis failed to start next to
`camel infra run mosquitto`. Map them with `--property artemis.ports=mqtt,amqp` (or `all`).
The `withFixedPort()` container of `camel-test-infra-artemis` maps the same ports, and reads the same property.

A service that does not start because a port it maps on the host is taken now fails with one line that names the port,
and the infra service that has it, instead of the stack trace of the container runtime.

=== camel-jbang - camel run loads one version of each library

`camel run` downloads the JARs of each component when it is first used. It now keeps one version of each library: when
a component used later depends on another version of a library that is already loaded, it gets the loaded version, as
with the `dependencyManagement` of a Maven project.

Before, the other version was added as well. The classpath could then hold, for example, three versions of Netty, and a
class came from whichever JAR was first. Use `--verbose` to see the JARs added for each dependency.

=== camel-vertx - the component fails to start when the clustered Vert.x is not created

When the component builds its own clustered Vert.x (the component `host` or `port` option is set and no `vertxOptions` is
given), it waits for the EventBus to be ready before it reports itself started. That wait used to be ineffective: the
result of the bounded wait was discarded and a failed build only logged a warning, so the component started with no
Vert.x instance and the first endpoint, producer or consumer to use it failed with a `NullPointerException`.

The component now fails to start, with the cause attached, when the clustered Vert.x cannot be built or is not ready within
the `timeout` option. An application whose cluster join is slower than `timeout` (60 seconds by default) has to raise it.
A Vert.x that completes after the timeout is closed, rather than being left joined to the cluster with nobody to stop it.

`timeout` must now be a positive number of seconds on that path: `timeout=0` used to mean "do not wait", which left the
component without a Vert.x, and is rejected with a clear message instead.

=== camel-jbang - camel run stops when a dependency it was given cannot be downloaded

`camel run` now stops with exit code 1 when a dependency given with `--dep`, `camel.jbang.dependencies`, a `//DEPS`
line or the dependencies of a Kamelet cannot be downloaded, and prints why on one line, such as
`ERROR: Cannot download dependency: com.acme:foo:1.0 due to: ... Could not find artifact com.acme:foo:jar:1.0 in central (...)`.
Before, it logged the error with the stack trace, started the routes without the dependency, and exited with 0.
In dev mode (`--dev`), a reload with a `//DEPS` line that cannot be downloaded fails, and the routes keep running as
they were. Other commands of the Camel CLI that fail to download a dependency also exit with 1 and print one line,
instead of exiting with 70 and printing the stack trace (use `--verbose` for the stack trace). `camel export` still
writes the dependency to the exported project.

=== camel-docling - html_split_page is rejected by docling-serve

`outputFormat=html_split_page` produced an empty body on the docling-serve path: the request asked docling-serve for
the split pages, whose content it does not return in the response, so every content field of the reply was empty. It
now fails with an `IllegalArgumentException` that names the reason and the way out, `outputFormat=html` to get the
HTML of the whole document.

=== camel-aws2-transcribe - the protocol option is deprecated

The `protocol` option never had any effect. Nothing reads it: the Transcribe client is built by the shared
`AwsClientBuilderUtil`, which takes the region, the endpoint override and the proxy settings, and `getProtocol()` is
called only by the generated configurer. Its description was also the one belonging to `proxyProtocol`, so it read as
if it configured the proxy.

It is now deprecated, with a `deprecationNote` that says so, and its description no longer claims to configure a proxy.
Remove `protocol` from the endpoint URI or the configuration. To send the client's traffic through a proxy, use
`proxyProtocol` together with `proxyHost` and `proxyPort`, which the shared client builder does apply.
